## I / 一

===Original===

The Initiation

===Chinese===

入会仪式

===Original===

It was an hour when most respectable citizens were preparing for bed, and the upper windows of the big, old-fashioned houses in the square showed patches of light, against which the outlines of the leafless trees, bending and swaying under the urge of the gale, were silhouetted. A cold wind was sweeping up the river, and its outriders penetrated icily into the remotest and most sheltered places.

The man who paced slowly by the high iron railings shivered, though he was warmly clad, for the unknown had chosen a rendezvous which seemed exposed to the full blast of the storm.

The debris of the dead autumn whirled in fantastic circles about his feet, the twigs and leaves came rattling down from the trees which threw their long gaunt arms above him, and he looked enviously at the cheerful glow in the windows of a house where, did he but knock, he would be received as a welcome guest.

===Chinese===

这时辰，大多数体面市民都已准备就寝。广场上那些宽大的老式宅子，高层的窗口透出一方一方灯光，映出秃树的轮廓——它们在狂风的驱策下俯仰摇摆。一股寒风沿着河面席卷而来，风的先锋刺骨侵肌，连最幽深、最避风的角落也钻了进去。

在高高的铁栏杆旁缓缓踱步的那个男人打了个寒噤，尽管他衣着厚实——那位不露面的人所选定的碰头地点，似乎偏偏暴露在风暴的正面冲击之下。

枯死秋天的残骸绕着他的脚打着怪异的旋儿，细枝与败叶从头顶簌簌砸落——那些树把又长又瘦的枝条伸在他上方——他羡慕地望着旁边一座宅子窗里融融的暖光：只要他肯上前敲门，那家人准会把他当位受欢迎的客人接待。

===Original===

The hour of eleven boomed out from a nearby clock, and the last stroke was reverberating when a car came swiftly and noiselessly into the square and halted abreast of him. The two headlamps burned dimly. Within the closed body there was no spark of light. After a moment’s hesitation the waiting man stepped to the car, opened the door, and got in. He could only guess the outline of the driver’s figure in the seat ahead, and he felt a curious thumping of heart as he realised the terrific importance of the step he had taken. The car did not move, and the man in the driver’s seat remained motionless. For a little time there was a dead silence, which was broken by the passenger.

“Well?” he asked nervously, almost irritably.

“Have you decided?” asked the driver.

===Chinese===

附近一座钟当当地敲出十一点，最后一声余音未散，一辆汽车已悄然疾驶入广场，停在他身旁。两盏车灯幽幽地亮着。封闭的车厢里没有一星火光。等候的人迟疑片刻，走到车前，拉开车门，钻了进去。前排座位上驾驶者的身形，他只能凭轮廓揣测；待他意识到自己迈出的这一步干系何等重大，心口便异样地怦怦直跳。汽车没有动，驾驶座上的人也纹丝不动。一时间是死一般的沉寂，先开口打破它的，是那位乘客。

“怎么样？”他紧张地问，几乎带着恼意。

“决定了吗？”驾驶者问。

===Original===

“Should I be here if I hadn’t?” demanded the passenger. “Do you think I’ve come out of curiosity? What do you want of me? Tell me that, and I will tell you what I want of you.”

“I know what you want of me,” said the driver. His voice was muffled and indistinct, as one who spoke behind a veil.

When the newcomer’s eyes grew accustomed to the gloom, he detected the vague outline of the black silk cowl which covered the driver’s head.

“You are on the verge of bankruptcy,” the driver went on. “You have used money which was not yours to use, and you are contemplating suicide. And it is not your insolvency which makes you consider this way out. You have an enemy who has discovered something to your discredit, something which would bring you into the hands of the police. Three days ago you obtained from a firm of manufacturing chemists, a member of which is a friend of yours, a particularly deadly drug, which cannot be obtained from a retail chemist. You have spent a week reading up poisons and their effects, and it is your intention, unless something turns up which will save you from ruin, to end your life either on Saturday or Sunday. I think it will be Sunday.”

===Chinese===

“我要是没决定，还会到这儿来？”乘客逼问道，“你以为我是出于好奇才来的？你到底要我做什么？你先说了，我再告诉你我要你做什么。”

“你要我做什么，我清楚。”驾驶者说。他的声音发闷，含混不清，像隔着面纱讲话。

待新来者的眼睛适应了昏暗，他才辨认出驾驶者头上罩着的黑绸兜帽的模糊轮廓。

“你已经站在破产的边缘，”驾驶者接着说，“你动了不属于你的钱，正在盘算自杀。而逼你考虑这条出路的，并不是你的资不抵债。你有一个仇家，摸到了你一件见不得人的事，一件足以把你送进警察手里的把柄。三天前，你从一家制药商行弄到了一种格外致命的药物——商行里有一位成员是你的朋友——这种药在零售药房是买不到的。你花了整整一个星期研读毒药和它们的药性；除非有什么转机把你从毁灭里救出来，你打算在星期六或星期日了结自己的性命。我看，会是星期日。”

===Original===

He heard the man behind him gasp, and laughed softly.

“Now, sir,” said the driver, “are you prepared for a consideration to act for me?”

“What do you want me to do?” demanded the man behind him shakily.

“I ask no more than that you should carry out my instructions. I will take care that you run no risks and that you are well paid. I am prepared at this moment to place in your hands a very large sum of money, which will enable you to meet your more pressing obligations. In return for this I shall want you to put into circulation all the money I send you, to make the necessary exchanges, to cover up the trail of bills and banknotes, the numbers of which are known to the police; to dispose of bonds, which I cannot dispose of, and generally to act as my agent﻿—” he paused, adding significantly, “and to pay on demand what I ask.”

===Chinese===

他听见身后那人猛抽一口气，不由得轻轻笑了。

“那么，先生，”驾驶者说，“你可愿意受一份酬报，替我办事？”

“你要我干什么？”身后那人声音发颤地问。

“我只要求你照我的指示行事。我担保你不必冒任何风险，而且报酬优厚。此刻我就准备把一大笔钱交到你手里，足够你应付那些最紧迫的债务。作为回报，我要你把我寄给你的钱统统投放出去，做必要的兑换，抹掉票据与钞票的来路——它们的编号都已在警察手里备了案；替我出手那些我自己无法脱手的债券；总而言之，充当我的代理人——”他顿了顿，又意味深长地补上一句，“外加一条：我几时要，你就得照数付。”

===Original===

The man behind him did not reply for some time, and then he asked with a hint of petulance:

“What is the Crimson Circle?”

“You,” was the startling reply.

“I?” gasped the man.

“You are of the Crimson Circle,” said the other carefully. “You have a hundred comrades, none of whom will ever be known to you, none of whom will ever know you.”

===Chinese===

身后那人半晌没有作声，然后才带着几分愠色问道：

“红圈（the Crimson Circle）到底是什么？”

“就是你。”这一句答话令人悚然心惊。

“我？”那人倒抽一口气。

“你已是红圈中人，”对方一字一顿地说，“你有一百名同道，他们当中任何一个人，你永远不会认识；他们也永远不会知道你是谁。”

===Original===

“And you?”

“I know them all,” said the driver. “You agree?”

“I agree,” said the other after a pause.

The driver half-turned in his seat and held out his hand.

“Take this,” he said.

===Chinese===

“那你呢？”

“他们我全都认得。”驾驶者说，“你同意吗？”

“我同意。”对方顿了顿，说道。

驾驶者在座位上侧过半个身子，伸出一只手来。

“拿着。”他说。

===Original===

“This” was a large, bulky envelope, and the newly initiated member of the Crimson Circle thrust it into his pocket.

“And now get out,” said the driver curtly, and the man obeyed without question.

He slammed the door behind him and walked abreast of the driver. He was still curious as to his identity, and for his own salvation it was necessary that he should know the man who drove.

“Don’t light your cigar here,” said the driver, “or I shall think that your smoking is really an excuse to strike a match. And remember this, my friend, that the man who knows me, carries his knowledge to hell.”

===Chinese===

这“东西”是一只又大又厚的信封，红圈的新入会者把它塞进了衣袋。

“现在，下车吧。”驾驶者冷冷地说。那人二话没说，照办了。

他砰地摔上车门，走到与驾驶者并排的位置。他对那人的身份依旧好奇难耐，而为了自己身家性命着想，他非弄清这个开车的人是谁不可。

“别在这儿点雪茄，”驾驶者说，“不然我会疑心你抽烟其实是个借口，为的是划火柴。还有，记住这句话，我的朋友：凡认得我的人，都得把这份见识带进地狱。”

===Original===

Before the other could reply the car moved on and the man with the envelope stood watching its red tail light until it disappeared from view.

He was shaking from head to foot, and when he did light the cigar which his chattering teeth gripped, the flame of the match quivered tremulously.

“That is that,” he said huskily, and crossed the road, to disappear in one of the side-turnings.

===Chinese===

对方还来不及答话，汽车已经开动。带信封的人站在原地，望着那盏红色的尾灯，直到它从视野里消失。

他从头到脚抖个不停。待他终于点燃那支雪茄——磕得咯咯作响的牙齿紧紧叼着它——火柴的火苗也簌簌地颤。

“就这样了。”他哑着嗓子说，随即穿过马路，消失在一条岔路里。

===Original===

He was scarcely out of sight before a figure moved stealthily from the doorway of a dark house and followed. It was the figure of a man tall and broad, and he walked with difficulty, for he was naturally short of breath. He had gone a hundred paces in his pursuit before he realised that he still held in his hand the ship’s binoculars through which he had been watching.

When he reached the main street his quarry had vanished. He had expected as much and was not perturbed. He knew where to find him. But who was in the car? He had read the number and could trace its owner in the morning. Mr. Felix Marl grinned. Had he so much as guessed the character of the interview he had overlooked, he would not have been amused. Stronger men than he had grown stiff with fear at the menace of the Crimson Circle.

===Chinese===

他前脚刚从视线里消失，一条人影便从一座黑灯瞎火的宅子门口悄悄闪出，跟了上去。那是个高大魁梧的男人，走起路来颇为吃力，因为他天生气息短促。跟踪了约莫一百步，他才发觉自己手里还握着那副一直用来观察的船用双筒望远镜。

走到大街上时，他的目标已经无影无踪。他早料到会这样，并不着慌。他知道上哪儿去找那人。可车里坐的又是谁？车牌号他已经记下，明天一早便能查出车主。菲利克斯·马尔（Felix Marl）先生咧嘴一笑。哪怕他只猜中一分自己方才在旁窥看的这场会面的性质，他也断然笑不出来了。多少比他更强硬的汉子，都曾在红圈的威胁之下吓得周身僵直。

## II / 二

===Original===
The Man Who Did Not Pay

===Chinese===
不付钱的人

===Original===
Philip Bassard paid, and lived, for apparently the Crimson Circle kept faith; Jacques Rizzi, the banker, also paid, but in a panic. He died from natural causes a month later, having a weak heart. Benson, the railway lawyer, pooh-poohed the threat and was found dead by the side of his private saloon.
Mr. Derrick Yale, with his amazing gifts, ran down the coloured man who had crept into Benson’s private car and killed him before he threw the body from the window, and the coloured man was hanged, without, however, revealing the identity of his employer. The police might sneer at Yale’s psychometrical powers﻿—as they did﻿—but within forty-eight hours he had led the police to the crimp’s house at Yareside and the dazed murderer had confessed.
Following this tragedy many men must have paid without reporting the matter to the police, for there was a long period during which no reference to the Crimson Circle found its way into the newspapers. And then one morning there came to the breakfast table of James Beardmore, a square envelope containing a card, on which was stamped a Crimson Circle.

===Chinese===
菲利普·巴萨尔（Philip Bassard）付了钱，也保住了性命——红圈显然是守信用的；银行家雅克·里齐（Jacques Rizzi）也付了钱，却是在惶恐万状之中付的。他本就心脏衰弱，一个月后死于自然原因。铁路律师本森（Benson）对这份威胁嗤之以鼻，结果人们发现他死在自己那节私人包厢车厢的旁边。
德里克·耶尔先生凭着他的惊人天赋，追捕到了那个潜入本森私人车厢、行凶之后把尸体从车窗抛出去的有色人种男子；此人被处以绞刑，只是到死也没有供出雇他行事的人是谁。警方尽可以嘲笑耶尔触物感应（psychometry）的异能——他们也确实嘲笑过——可不出四十八小时，他已领着警方找到亚尔赛德（Yareside）一处拐子的住所，那个吓懵了的凶手当场招认了罪行。
这场悲剧过后，想必有许多人都悄悄付了钱而没有报官，因为此后很长一段时间，报纸上再没有出现过关于红圈的只言片语。直到一天早晨，一只方形信封送到了詹姆斯·比德莫尔的早餐桌上，里面装着一张卡片，卡上盖着一个猩红的圆圈。

===Original===
“You are interested in the melodrama of life, Jack﻿—read that.”
James Stamford Beardmore tossed the message across the table to his son and proceeded to open the next letter in the pile which stood beside his plate.
Jack retrieved the message from the floor, where it had fallen, and examined it with a little frown. It was a very ordinary letter-card, save that it bore no address. A big circle of crimson touched its four edges and had the appearance of having been printed with a rubber stamp, for the ink was unevenly distributed. In the centre of the circle, written in printed characters, were the words:
One hundred thousand represents only a small portion of your possessions. You will pay this in notes to a messenger I will send in response to an advertisement in the *Tribune* within the next twenty-four hours, stating the exact hour convenient to you. This is the final warning.
There was no signature.

===Chinese===
“你对人生这出情节剧向来有兴趣，杰克——念念这个。”
詹姆斯·斯坦福德·比德莫尔把那张卡片隔着餐桌抛给儿子，随即去拆信堆里的下一封信——那摞信就摆在他的餐盘旁边。
杰克把卡片从掉落的地板上拾起，微微蹙着眉端详起来。那是一张再普通不过的通信卡，只是没有写地址。一个大大的猩红圆圈抵着卡片的四条边，看上去像用橡皮图章盖出来的，因为油墨深浅不匀。圆圈正中以印刷体写着下面几行字：
十万英镑只占你财产的一小部分。你须在二十四小时内于《论坛报》（Tribune）刊登启事，写明于你方便的确切钟点，我将据此派出信使，你须以钞票将此款付给信使。这是最后警告。
没有署名。

===Original===
“Well?”
Old Jim Beardmore looked up over his spectacles and his eyes were smiling.
“The Crimson Circle!” gasped his son.
Jim Beardmore laughed aloud at the concern in the boy’s voice.
“Yes, the Crimson Circle﻿—I have had four of ’em!”

===Chinese===
“怎么样？”
老吉姆·比德莫尔从眼镜上方抬起眼来，眼里含着笑意。
“红圈！”他儿子倒抽一口气。
听到儿子语气里的担忧，吉姆·比德莫尔放声大笑。
“不错，正是红圈——这种玩意儿我已经收过四张了！”

===Original===
The young man stared at him.
“Four?” he repeated. “Good heavens! Is that why Yale has been staying with us?”
Jim Beardmore smiled.
“That is a reason,” he said.
“Of course, I knew that he was a detective, but I hadn’t the slightest idea﻿—”
“Don’t worry about this infernal circle,” interrupted his father a little impatiently. “I’m not scared of them. Froyant is in terror of his life that he will be marked down. And I don’t wonder. He and I have made a few enemies in our time.”

===Chinese===
年轻人直愣愣地瞪着他。
“四张？”他重复了一遍，“天哪！耶尔住到我们家来，就是为了这个？”
吉姆·比德莫尔微微一笑。
“算是一条原因吧。”他说。
“我当然知道他是个侦探，可我万万没有想到——”
“别为这个该死的圈儿操心，”父亲有些不耐烦地打断他，“我不怕他们。弗鲁瓦扬倒吓得魂不附体，唯恐自己被圈定为目标。这我一点也不奇怪——他和我这辈子都结下过几个仇家。”

===Original===
James Beardmore, with his hard, lined face and his stubbly grey beard, might have been mistaken for the grandfather of the good-looking young man who sat opposite to him. The Beardmore fortune had been painfully won. It had materialised from the wreckage of dreams and had its beginnings in the privations, the dangers and the heartaches of a prospector’s life. This man whom Death had stalked on the waterless plains of the Kalahari, who had scraped in the mud of the Vale River for illusory diamonds, and thawed out his claim in the Klondyke, had faced too many real dangers to be greatly disturbed by the threat of the Crimson Circle. For the moment his perturbation was based on a more tangible peril, not to himself, but to his son.
“I’ve got a whole lot of faith in your good sense, Jack,” he said, “so don’t be hurt by anything I’m going to say. I’ve never interfered in your amusements or questioned your judgment﻿—but﻿—do you think that you’re being wise just now?”
Jack understood.

===Chinese===
詹姆斯·比德莫尔生着一张刚硬多皱的脸，颏下是一片斑白的短髭，坐在他对面那个相貌堂堂的年轻人，简直会被错当成他的孙子。比德莫尔家的这份家业，是含辛茹苦挣来的。它是从梦想的残骸里凝成的，发端于一个探矿人生涯中的种种困顿、艰险与椎心之痛。这个曾被死神在卡拉哈里（Kalahari）无水的荒原上穷追不舍、曾在瓦尔河（Vale River）的泥浆里扒摸虚幻钻石、又曾在克朗代克（Klondyke）掘开冻土开采矿地的人，见识过的真危险太多了，红圈的威胁不至于让他心神大乱。眼下叫他挂心的，倒是一个更切实的祸端——不是对他自己，而是对他的儿子。
“我很信得过你的见识，杰克，”他说，“所以待会儿无论我说什么，你都别见怪。你的消遣，我从不干涉；你的判断，我也从不质疑——不过——眼下这件事，你自问做得明智吗？”
杰克听懂了。

===Original===
“You mean about Miss Drummond, father?”
The older man nodded.
“She’s Froyant’s secretary,” began the youth.
“I know she is Froyant’s secretary,” said the other, “and she’s none the worse for that. But the point is, Jack, do you know anything more about her?”
The young man rolled his napkin deliberately. His face was red and there was a queer set look about his jaw which secretly amused Jim.
“I like her. She is a friend of mine. I’ve never made love to her, if that is what you mean, dad, and I rather think our friendship would be at an end if I did.”

===Chinese===
“您是说德拉蒙德小姐的事吧，父亲？”
年长的那位点了点头。
“她是弗鲁瓦扬的秘书。”年轻人开口道。
“我知道她是弗鲁瓦扬的秘书，”对方说，“这一点倒不有损于她。可要害在于，杰克——除了这一层，你还知道她的什么？”
年轻人不紧不慢地卷着餐巾。他涨红了脸，下巴透出一股古怪的执拗劲儿，暗地里看得吉姆直乐。
“我喜欢她。她是我的朋友。要是您指的是那件事——爸爸——我从没向她求过爱，而且我倒觉得，真若那么做，我们的交情也就到头了。”

===Original===
Jim nodded. He had said all that was necessary and now he took up a more bulky envelope and looked at it curiously. Jack saw that it bore French postage stamps and wondered who was the correspondent.
Tearing open the flap, the old man took out a pad of correspondence, which included yet another envelope heavily sealed. He read the superscription and his nose wrinkled.
“Ugh!” he said, and put the envelope down unopened. He glanced through the remainder of the correspondence, then looked across at his son.
“Never trust a man or woman until you know the worst of them,” he said. “I’ve got a man coming to see me today who is a respectable member of society. He has a record as black as my hat and yet I’m going to do business with him﻿—I know the worst!”
Jack laughed. Further conversation was interrupted by the arrival of their guest.

===Chinese===
吉姆点了点头。该说的话都已说尽，他便拿起一只更厚的信封，好奇地端详起来。杰克看见上面贴的是法国邮票，心里纳闷这位通信人是谁。
老人撕开封口，抽出一沓信件，其中还套着另一只封得严严实实的信封。他看了上面的题款，鼻子皱了起来。
“哼！”他说了一声，把那只信封原封不动地搁下了。余下的信件他草草翻过，然后抬眼朝儿子望去。
“不摸清一个人最坏的底细，就千万别信他——男人也好，女人也好，”他说，“今天就有个人要来见我，在社会上是个体面人物。他的老底黑得跟我这顶帽子一样，可我照样要跟他做生意——他最坏是什么样，我一清二楚！”
杰克笑出了声。进一步的交谈，被客人的到来打断了。

===Original===
“Good morning, Yale﻿—did you sleep well?” asked the old man. “Ring for some more coffee, Jack.”
Derrick Yale’s visit had been an unmixed pleasure to Jack Beardmore. He was at the age when romance had its full appeal and the companionship of the most commonplace detective would have brought him a peculiar joy. But the glamour which surrounded Yale was the glamour of the supernatural. This man had unusual and peculiar qualities which made him unique. The delicate aesthetic face, the grave mystery of his eyes, the very gesture of his long, sensitive hands, were part of his uniqueness.
“I never sleep,” he said good-humouredly as he unrolled his serviette. He held the silver napkin ring for a second between his two fingers, and James Beardmore watched him with amusement. As for Jack, his eager admiration was unconcealed.
“Well?” asked the old man.
“Who handled this last has had very bad news﻿—some near relation is desperately ill.”

===Chinese===
“早安，耶尔——昨夜睡得好么？”老人问道，“按铃再叫些咖啡来，杰克。”
德里克·耶尔的做客，对杰克·比德莫尔来说纯粹是一桩乐事。他正当浪漫情怀最动人心魄的年纪，哪怕相伴的是一位再平庸不过的侦探，也会给他带来一种特别的快活。然而萦绕着耶尔的那圈光彩，却是超自然的光彩。这个人身上有着种种异乎寻常的禀赋，使他独一无二。那张清秀儒雅的脸，那双眼睛里深沉的神秘，乃至那双修长灵敏的手的一举一动，都是他这份独一无二的一部分。
“我从不睡觉。”他一面展开餐巾，一面和颜悦色地说。他把那只银质餐巾环在两指之间停了一瞬，詹姆斯·比德莫尔饶有兴味地望着他。至于杰克，那股热切的倾慕更是全写在脸上。
“怎么样？”老人问。
“最后碰过它的人得了极坏的消息——哪位至亲病得性命垂危。”

===Original===
Beardmore nodded.
“Jane Higgins was the servant who laid the table,” he said. “She had a letter this morning saying that her mother was dying.”
Jack gasped.
“And you felt that in the serviette ring?” he asked in amazement. “How do you get that impression, Mr. Yale?”
Derrick Yale shook his head.
“I don’t attempt to explain,” he said quietly. “All that I know is that the moment I took up my serviette I had a sensation of profound and poignant sorrow. It is weird, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
比德莫尔点了点头。
“摆餐桌的女仆是简·希金斯（Jane Higgins），”他说，“今天早上她收到一封信，说她母亲已奄奄一息。”
杰克倒抽一口气。
“这些您是从餐巾环上感应出来的？”他惊异莫名地问，“这种感应您是怎么得来的，耶尔先生？”
德里克·耶尔摇了摇头。
“我不试图解释，”他轻声说，“我只知道，一拿起餐巾，我便感到一阵深切刺骨的悲伤。这事是有点玄，不是么？”

===Original===
“But how did you know about her mother?”
“I traced it somehow,” said the other almost brusquely; “it is a matter of deduction. Have you any news, Mr. Beardmore?”
For answer Jim handed him the card he had received that morning.
Yale read the message, then weighed the card on the palm of his white hand.
“Posted by a sailor,” he said, “a man who has been in prison and has recently lost a great deal of money.”

===Chinese===
“可您怎么会知道是她母亲？”
“总之是循迹追出来的，”对方的话近乎生硬，“这是推理的功夫。您有什么消息么，比德莫尔先生？”
作为回答，吉姆把他那天早晨收到的卡片递了过去。
耶尔读了那封信，然后将卡片搁在白皙的掌心里掂了掂。
“投寄的人是个水手，”他说，“坐过牢，新近又蚀掉了大笔的钱。”

===Original===
Jim Beardmore laughed.
“Which I shall certainly not replace,” he said, rising from the table. “Do you take these warnings seriously?”
“I take them very seriously,” said Derrick in his quiet way. “So seriously that I do not advise you to leave this house except in my company. The Crimson Circle,” he went on, arresting Beardmore’s indignant protest with a characteristic gesture, “is, I admit, vulgarly melodramatic in its operations, but it will be no solace to your heirs to learn that you have died theatrically.”

===Chinese===
吉姆·比德莫尔哈哈一笑。
“这笔蚀掉的钱我当然是决计不补的，”他说着从餐桌旁站起身，“这些警告，您是当真的么？”
“我当真得很，”德里克以他惯有的平缓语气说，“当真到这般地步：若非由我作陪，我劝您不要迈出这所宅子一步。红圈——”他接着说，抬起手做了个特有的手势，止住比德莫尔愤然的抗辩，“我承认，它行事的路数庸俗而耸人听闻，一派情节剧的做派；可您的继承人若是得知您死得轰轰烈烈，那也绝不是什么安慰。”

===Original===
Jim Beardmore was silent for a time, and his son regarded him anxiously.
“Why don’t you go abroad, father?” he asked, and the old man snapped round on him.
“Go abroad be damned!” he roared. “Run away from a cheap Black Hand gang? I’ll see them﻿—!”
He did not mention their destination, but they could guess.

===Chinese===
吉姆·比德莫尔沉默了片刻，他的儿子则忧心忡忡地望着他。
“您干吗不出国避一避，父亲？”他问道。老人霍地转过身来冲着他。
“出国，见鬼去吧！”他吼道，“要我躲开一个三流的黑手帮（Black Hand）匪帮？我倒要会一会他们——！”
他没有点明要在哪儿会他们，但父子俩都猜得到。

## III / 三

===Original===

The Girl Who Was Indifferent

===Chinese===

无动于衷的姑娘

===Original===

A heavy weight lay on Jack Beardmore’s mind as he walked slowly across the meadows that morning. His feet carried him instinctively in the direction of the little valley which lay a mile from the house, and in the exact centre of which ran the hedge which marked the division between the Beardmore and Froyant estates. It was a glorious morning. The storm of wind and rain which had swept the country the night before had blown itself out, and the world lay bathed in yellow sunlight. Far away, beyond the olive-green coverts that crowned Penton Hill, he caught a glimpse of Harvey Froyant’s big white mansion. Would she venture out with the ground so sodden and the grasses soaked with rain, he wondered?
He stopped by a big elm tree on the lip of the valley and cast an anxious glance along the untidy hedge, until his eyes rested on a tiny summer house which the former owners of Tower House had erected﻿—Harvey Froyant, who loathed solitude, would never have been guilty of such extravagance.
There was nobody in sight, and his heart sank. Ten minutes’ walking brought him to the gap he had made in the fence, and he stepped through. The girl who sat in the tiny house might have heard his sigh of relief.

===Chinese===

那天早晨，杰克·比德莫尔（Jack Beardmore）缓步走过草场，心头压着一桩沉甸甸的心事。他的双脚不由自主地把他带向那条小山谷——谷地离宅子约一英里，一道树篱恰恰沿着谷底正中穿过，标出比德莫尔家与弗鲁瓦扬家产业的分界。这是一个灿烂明媚的早晨。昨夜横扫乡野的那场风雨已经发作殆尽，整个世界浸浴在金黄的日光里。远处，在彭顿山（Penton Hill）顶上那片油橄榄色树丛的那一边，他望见了哈维·弗鲁瓦扬（Harvey Froyant）那座白色大宅。地面这般湿软泥泞、草叶饱蘸雨水，她还肯出门吗？他心里暗暗思忖。
他在谷口一株大榆树旁站定，焦灼的目光顺着那道凌乱的树篱一路扫过去，直到落在一座小凉亭上——那是塔楼宅（Tower House）从前的主人们盖的；哈维·弗鲁瓦扬最恨独处，断然不会犯下这等奢侈的罪过。
四下里不见人影，他的心往下一沉。走了十分钟，他来到自己当初在栅栏上破开的那道缺口，一步跨了过去。坐在小凉亭里的那位姑娘，多半听见了他那声如释重负的叹息。

===Original===

She looked round, then rose with some evidence of reluctance.
She was remarkably pretty, with her fair hair and flawless skin, but there was no welcome in her eyes as she came slowly toward him.
“Good morning,” she said coolly.
“Good morning, Thalia,” he ventured, and her frown returned.
“I wish you wouldn’t,” she said, and he knew that she meant what she said. Her attitude toward him puzzled and worried him. For she was a thing of laughter and bubbling life. He had once surprised her chasing a hare, and had watched, spellbound, the figure of this laughing Diana as her little feet flew across the field in pursuit of the scared beast. He had heard her singing, too, and the very joy of life was vibrant in her voice﻿—but he had seen her so depressed and gloomy that he had feared she was ill.

===Chinese===

她回过头来，随即站起身子，神情里分明透着几分不情愿。
她生得极美，金色的头发，无瑕的肌肤；可当她缓缓向他走来时，眼睛里却无半点欢迎之意。
“早安。”她冷冷地说。
“早安，塔莉娅（Thalia）。”他壮着胆子唤了一声。她的眉头又蹙了起来。
“我希望你别这么叫。”她说。他明白她这话是当真的。她对他的态度令他又是困惑又是忧心。因为她本是欢笑与蓬勃生气的化身。他有一回撞见她追赶一只野兔，看得如痴如醉——这位笑声朗朗的狄安娜（Diana），一双小脚飞也似的掠过田野，追赶那只吓坏了的小兽。他也听见过她唱歌，嗓音里颤动着生命本身的欢悦——可他也见过她那样消沉忧郁，害他一度担心她是不是病了。

===Original===

“Why are you always so stiff and formal with me?” he grumbled.
For a second a ghost of a smile showed at the corner of her mouth.
“Because I’ve read books,” she said solemnly, “and poor girl secretaries who aren’t stiff and formal with millionaire’s sons usually come to a bad end!”
She had a trick of directness which was very disconcerting.
“Besides,” she said, “there is no reason why I shouldn’t be stiff and formal. It is the conventional attitude which people adopt toward their fellow creatures, unless they are very fond of them, and I’m not very fond of you.”

===Chinese===

“你干吗对我总是这么拘谨客气？”他嘟嘟囔囔地抱怨。
有那么一秒钟，一丝笑影在她嘴角一闪而过。
“因为我读过书，”她一本正经地说，“穷苦人家的女秘书要是对百万富翁的儿子们不拘谨客气，到头来通常没什么好下场！”
她有这么一种直截了当的脾性，很叫人招架不住。
“再说了，”她说，“我没有理由不拘谨客气。人们对待自己的同类，采用的正是这种合乎惯例的态度——除非他们非常喜欢对方。而我，可谈不上多喜欢你。”

===Original===

She said this calmly and deliberately, and the young man’s face went red. He felt a fool, and cursed himself for provoking this act of cruelty.
“I will tell you something, Mr. Beardmore,” she went on in her even tone. “Something which you haven’t realised. When a boy and girl are thrown together on a desert island, it is only natural that the boy gets the idea that the girl is the only girl in the world. All his wayward fancies are concentrated on one woman and as the days pass she grows more and more wonderful in his eyes. I’ve read a lot of these desert island stories, and I’ve seen a lot of pictures that deal with that interesting situation, and that is how it strikes me. You are on a desert island here﻿—you spend too much time on your estate, and the only things you see are rabbits and birds and Thalia Drummond. You should go into the city and into the society of people of your own station.”

===Chinese===

她语气平静、不慌不忙地说出这句话，年轻人的脸涨红了。他觉得自己像个傻瓜，暗骂自己不该招来这样一通狠心话。
“我来告诉你一件事，比德莫尔先生，”她用她那平稳的语调接着说，“一件你还没有悟出来的事。当一个男孩和一个女孩被一同撂在荒岛上，那男孩自然而然会觉得，这女孩就是天下唯一的女孩。他所有任性不羁的遐想都聚集到一个女人身上，而随着时日一天天过去，她在他的眼里也一天比一天更加美妙出奇。这类荒岛故事我读过许多，演这种有趣情境的影片也看过不少，我的感想便是如此。你在这儿过的就是荒岛日子——你在自家庄园里耽搁的时间太多了，眼面前见到的无非是兔子、鸟儿，外加一个塔莉娅·德拉蒙德。你应该进城去，走进与你身份相当的社交圈子。”

===Original===

She turned from him with a nod, for she had seen her employer approaching, had watched him out of the corner of her eye as he stopped to survey them, and had guessed his annoyance.
“I thought you were doing the house accounts, Miss Drummond,” he said with asperity.
He was a skinny man, in the early fifties, colourless, sharp-featured, prematurely bald. He had an unpleasant habit of baring his long yellow teeth when he asked a question, a grimace which in some curious way suggested his belief that the answer would be an evasion.
“Morning, Beardmore,” he jerked the salutation grudgingly and turned again to his secretary.

===Chinese===

她朝他一点头，转身走开——原来她早已瞧见雇主走近，用眼角余光望着他停下脚步打量他们俩，也猜到了他心头的不快。
“我还以为你在做宅里的账目呢，德拉蒙德小姐。”他尖刻地说。
他是个干瘦的男人，五十出头，面色没有血色，脸上棱角分明，头发早早地秃了。他有个叫人讨厌的习惯：每每一发问，便龇出那口长长的黄牙——这副怪相不知怎的总透着一股意思：他料定对方的回答必定是遁词。
“早啊，比德莫尔。”他不情不愿地蹦出这句招呼，随即又转向他的秘书。

===Original===

“I don’t like to see you wasting your time, Miss Drummond,” he said.
“I am not wasting either your time or mine, Mr. Froyant,” she answered calmly. “I have finished the accounts﻿—here!” She tapped the worn leather portfolio which was under her arm.
“You could have done the work in my library,” he complained; “there is no need to go into the wilderness.”
He stopped and rubbed his long nose and glanced from the girl to the silent young man.

===Chinese===

“我不爱看你浪费光阴，德拉蒙德小姐。”他说。
“我没浪费您的时间，也没浪费我自己的，弗鲁瓦扬先生。”她从容不迫地答道，“账目我已经做完了——就在这儿！”她拍了拍夹在臂弯里那只用旧的皮面文件夹。
“这活儿你本可以在我的书房里做，”他抱怨道，“犯不着跑到这荒郊野地里来。”
他停住脚，揉了揉自己的长鼻子，目光从姑娘身上移到那个一声不吭的年轻人身上。

===Original===

“Very good; that will do,” he said. “I am going to see your father, Beardmore. Perhaps you will walk with me?”
Thalia was already on her way to Tower House, and Jack had no excuse for lingering.
“Don’t occupy that girl’s time, Beardmore, don’t, please,” said Froyant testily. “You’ve no idea how much she has to do﻿—and I’m sure your father wouldn’t like it.”
Jack was on the point of saying something offensive, but checked himself. He loathed Harvey Froyant, and at the moment hated him for his domineering attitude toward the girl.

===Chinese===

“很好；就这样吧。”他说，“我正要去见你父亲，比德莫尔。你或许愿意陪我走一程？”
塔莉娅已经动身回塔楼宅去了，杰克再没有流连的借口。
“别占用那姑娘的时间，比德莫尔，请千万别。”弗鲁瓦扬急躁地说，“你根本想不到她有多少事要做——而且我敢肯定，你父亲也不会乐意的。”
杰克眼看要冲出一句难听的话来，又硬生生咽了回去。他厌恶哈维·弗鲁瓦扬，此刻更是恨他对待姑娘那副专横霸道的做派。

===Original===

“That class of girl,” began Mr. Froyant, turning to walk by the side of the hedge toward the gate at the end of the valley, “that class of girl﻿—” he stood still and stared. “Who the devil has broken through the hedge?” he demanded, pointing with his stick.
“I did,” said Jack savagely. “It is our hedge, anyway, and it saves half a mile﻿—come on, Mr. Froyant.”
Harvey Froyant made no comment as he stepped gingerly through the hedge.
They walked slowly up the hill toward the big elm tree where Jack had stood looking down into the valley.

===Chinese===

“那一类的姑娘嘛，”弗鲁瓦扬先生开了腔，转身顺着树篱边朝山谷尽头的大门走去，“那一类的姑娘——”他陡然站住，直瞪着眼。“到底是哪个家伙把树篱捅破了？”他用手杖一指，质问道。
“是我。”杰克狠狠地说，“反正那是我们家的树篱，走这儿能省半英里路——走吧，弗鲁瓦扬先生。”
哈维·弗鲁瓦扬一声不吭，小心翼翼地从树篱缺口跨了过去。
两人缓缓登上山坡，朝那株大榆树走去——杰克方才就是站在那里俯瞰山谷的。

===Original===

Mr. Harvey Froyant preserved a tight-lipped silence. He was a stickler for the conventions, where their observations benefited himself.
They had reached the crest of the rise, when suddenly his arm was gripped, and he turned to see Jack Beardmore, staring at the bole of the tree. Froyant followed the direction of his eye and took a step backward, his unhealthy face a shade paler. Painted on the tree trunk was a rough circle of crimson, and the paint was yet wet.

===Chinese===

哈维·弗鲁瓦扬先生紧闭着嘴，一路不吭声。他是个拘泥礼俗的人——但凡恪守礼俗于他自己有利的场合。
两人刚走到坡顶，他的胳膊突然被一把攥住。他转过身，只见杰克·比德莫尔正直勾勾地瞪着树干。弗鲁瓦扬顺着他的目光望去，不由得倒退一步，那张病恹恹的脸又白了一层。树干上草草画着一个红圈，颜料还没有干。

## IV / 四

===Original===

Mr. Felix Marl

===Chinese===

菲利克斯·马尔先生

===Original===

Jack Beardmore looked round, scanning the country. The only human being in sight was a man who was walking slowly away from them, carrying a bag in his hand. Jack shouted, and the man turned.

“Who are you?” demanded Jack. Then, “What are you doing here?”

The stranger was a tall, stoutish man, and the exertion of carrying his grip had left him a little breathless. It was some time before he could reply.

“My name is Marl,” he said, “Felix Marl. You may have heard of me. I think you are young Mr. Beardmore, aren’t you?”

“That is my name,” said Jack. “What are you doing here?” he asked again.

“They told me there was a shortcut from the railway station, but it is not so short as they promised,” said Mr. Marl, breathing stertorously. “I’m on my way to see your father.”

===Chinese===

杰克·比德莫尔环顾四周，扫视着这片乡野。视野里唯一的人影，是个提着提包、正慢慢朝远处走去的男人。杰克喊了一声，那人回过头来。

“你是谁？”杰克厉声问。接着又是一句：“你在这儿干什么？”

那陌生人是个高个子、略发福的男人，一路提着提包赶路的辛劳让他有些气喘。过了好一会儿他才答上话来。

“我叫马尔，”他说，“菲利克斯·马尔。你也许听说过我。我想你就是小比德莫尔先生吧？”

“正是这个名字。”杰克说，“你在这儿干什么？”他又问了一遍。

“人家告诉我，从火车站过来有条近路，可它远不像他们许诺的那么近。”马尔先生呼哧呼哧地喘着粗气说，“我是来见你父亲的。”

===Original===

“Have you been near that tree?” asked Jack, and Marl glared at him.

“Why should I go near any tree?” he demanded aggressively. “I tell you I’ve come straight across the fields.”

By this time Harvey Froyant arrived, and apparently recognised the newcomer.

“This is Mr. Marl; I know him. Marl, did you see anybody near that tree?”

The man shook his head. Apparently the tree and its secret was a mystery to him.

“I never knew there was a tree there,” he said. “What﻿—what has happened?”

===Chinese===

“你到过那棵树跟前吗？”杰克问。马尔瞪了他一眼。

“我干嘛要到什么树跟前去？”他气势汹汹地质问，“我告诉你，我是一路穿过田野径直过来的。”

这时哈维·弗鲁瓦扬赶了过来，显然认出了新来的这个人。

“这位是马尔先生，我认识他。马尔，你看见那棵树附近有什么人吗？”

那人摇了摇头。那棵树和树里的秘密，显然对他是个不解之谜。

“我从不知道那儿有棵树，”他说，“出——出什么事了？”

===Original===

“Nothing,” said Harvey Froyant sharply.

They came to the house soon after, Jack carrying the visitor’s bag. He was not impressed by the big man’s appearance. His voice was coarse, his manner familiar, and Jack wondered what association this uncouth specimen of humanity could have with his father.

They were nearing the house when suddenly and for no obvious reason the stout Mr. Marl emitted a frightened squeal and leapt back. There was no doubt of his fear. It was written visibly in the blanched cheeks and the quivering lips of the man, who was shaking from head to foot.

Jack could only look at him in astonishment﻿—and even Harvey Froyant was startled into an interest.

“What the hell is wrong with you, Marl?” he asked savagely.

His own nerves were on edge, and the sight of the big man’s undisguised terror was a further strain which he could scarcely endure.

===Chinese===

“没什么。”哈维·弗鲁瓦扬厉声说。

不多时他们便到了宅子，客人的提包由杰克提着。这大块头的仪表没给杰克留下半点好印象。他嗓音粗鄙，举止轻慢随便，杰克实在纳闷，父亲同这么一个粗野的人物能有什么瓜葛。

快到宅子时，那位胖胖的马尔先生忽然无缘无故地发出一声惊恐的尖叫，猛地朝后跳去。他那份恐惧是做不得假的：刷白的面颊、哆嗦的嘴唇，处处写得分明，整个人从头到脚抖个不停。

杰克只能愕然望着他——就连哈维·弗鲁瓦扬也吃了一惊，不由得上了心。

“你这是见了什么鬼，马尔？”他恶狠狠地问。

他自己的神经本已绷到了边缘，眼见这大个子毫不掩饰的惊怖，更添一重几乎承受不住的煎熬。

===Original===

“Nothin’﻿—nothin’,” muttered Marl huskily. “I’ve been﻿—”

“Drinking, I should think,” snapped Froyant.

After seeing the man into the house Jack hurried off in search of Derrick Yale. He discovered the detective in the shrubbery sitting in a big cane chair, his chin upon his breast, his arms folded, a characteristic attitude of his.

Yale looked up at the sound of the young man’s footsteps.

“I can’t tell you,” he said, before Jack had framed his question, and then, seeing the look of astonishment on his face, he laughed. “You were going to ask me what scared Marl, weren’t you?”

“I came with that intention,” laughed Jack. “What an extraordinary fellow you are, Mr. Yale! Did you see his extraordinary exhibition of funk?”

===Chinese===

“没什么——没什么。”马尔哑着嗓子嘟囔，“我方才——”

“我看你是酒喝多了。”弗鲁瓦扬厉声道。

把那人送进宅子之后，杰克便匆匆去找德里克·耶尔。他发现这位侦探正在灌木丛里，坐在一把大藤椅上，下巴抵在胸前，两臂合抱——这是他特有的姿态。

听见年轻人的脚步声，耶尔抬起了头。

“我可说不上来。”杰克还没把问题在嘴边组织好，他先开了口；随即看见对方一脸愕然，不由笑了。“你正要问我马尔是让什么吓着的，对不对？”

“我正是为这事来的。”杰克笑道，“你可真是个神人，耶尔先生！瞧见他那一出精彩的丧魂失魄了吗？”

===Original===

Derrick Yale nodded.

“I saw him just before he had his shock,” he said. “You can see the field path from here.”

He frowned.

“He reminds me of somebody,” he said slowly, “yet I cannot for the life of me tell who it is. Is he a frequent visitor here? Your father told me he was coming, and I guessed it was he.”

Jack shook his head.

“This is the first time I’ve seen him,” he said. “I remember now, though, that father and Froyant have had some business dealings with a man named Marl﻿—dad mentioned him one day. I think he is a land speculator. Father is rather interested in land just now. By the way, I have seen the mark of the Crimson Circle,” he added, and described the newly-painted *O* he had found on the elm.

===Chinese===

德里克·耶尔点了点头。

“他受惊之前，我还瞧见他来着。”他说，“从这儿能望见那条田间小径。”

他皱起了眉。

“他叫我想起一个人，”他缓缓地说，“可我怎么也想不起究竟是谁。他常来这儿做客吗？你父亲告诉过我他要来，我猜着就是此人。”

杰克摇了摇头。

“我还是头一回见着他，”他说，“不过这会儿我想起来了，父亲和弗鲁瓦扬跟一个姓马尔的有过几桩生意往来——爸爸有一天提起过他。我想他是个做土地投机的。父亲眼下正对地产颇感兴趣。对了，我看见红圈的记号了。”他补上一句，随即讲述了他在那棵榆树上发现的新涂的 *O*。

===Original===

Instantly Yale lost interest in Mr. Marl.

“It was not on the tree when I went down into the valley,” said Jack. “I’ll swear to that. It must have been painted whilst I was talking to﻿—to a friend. The trunk is out of sight from the boundary fence, and it was quite possible for somebody to have painted the sign without being seen. What does it mean, Mr. Yale?”

“It means trouble,” said Yale shortly.

He rose abruptly and began pacing the flagged walk, and Jack, after waiting a little while, left him to his meditations.

===Chinese===

耶尔顿时对马尔先生失去了兴趣。

“我下到谷底去的时候，树上还没有这个记号，”杰克说，“这一点我可以起誓。它准是在我跟——跟一位朋友说话的当儿被人涂上去的。从边界围栏那儿望不见树干，谁要画下这个记号而不被人瞧见，是全然办得到的。这意味着什么，耶尔先生？”

“意味着麻烦。”耶尔简截地说。

他霍然起身，在石板小径上来回踱步。杰克等了一小会儿，便由他去冥想，自顾走开了。

===Original===

In the meantime, Mr. Felix Marl was comparatively a useless third of a conference which dealt with the transfer of lands. Marl was, as Jack had said, a land speculator, and he had come that morning bringing a promising proposition which he was wholly incapable of explaining.

“I can’t help it, gentlemen,” he said, and for the fourth time his trembling hand rose to his lips. “I’ve had a bit of a shock this morning.”

“What was that?”

But Marl seemed incapable of explanation. He could only shake his head helplessly.

“I’m not fit to discuss things calmly,” he said. “You’ll have to put the matter off until tomorrow.”

===Chinese===

与此同时，菲利克斯·马尔先生在一场商谈土地转让的会议里，差不多就是个派不上用场的第三者。正如杰克所言，马尔是个土地投机商，那天早上他带来一项前景可观的提议，可他全然没本事把它讲清楚。

“我没有法子，二位，”他说，发抖的手已是第四回举到唇边，“今天早上我受了一场惊吓。”

“怎么回事？”

可马尔似乎就是讲不明白。他只能无可奈何地摇着头。

“我这副样子，没法心平气和地谈事情，”他说，“这事你们只好推迟到明天了。”

===Original===

“Do you think I’ve come here today for the purpose of listening to that sort of nonsense?” snarled Mr. Froyant. “I tell you I want this business settled. So do you, Beardmore.”

Jim Beardmore, who was indifferent as to whether the matter was settled then or the following week, laughed.

“I don’t know that it is very important,” he said. “If Mr. Marl is upset, why should we bother him? Perhaps you’ll stay here tonight, Marl?”

“No, no, no,” the man’s voice rose almost to a shout. “No, I won’t stay here, if you don’t mind﻿—I would much rather not!”

“Just as you like,” said Jim Beardmore indifferently, and folded up the papers he had prepared for signature.

===Chinese===

“你以为我今天大老远跑来，是专门听这种废话的不成？”弗鲁瓦扬先生咆哮道，“我告诉你，这笔买卖我要今天了结。你也一样，比德莫尔。”

吉姆·比德莫尔对这事是当下办结还是下星期再办全无所谓，笑了笑。

“我看这也没什么了不得的，”他说，“马尔先生既然心里不痛快，我们何必逼他？要不你今晚就在这儿住下，马尔？”

“不，不，不，”那人的嗓门几乎拔高成了喊叫，“不，我不在这儿住，你们别见怪——我实在宁可不住！”

“随你的便。”吉姆·比德莫尔淡然说着，把备好待签的文件折了起来。

===Original===

They walked out into the hall together, and there Jack found them.

Beardmore’s car carried the visitor and his bag back to the station, and from there on Mr. Marl’s conduct was peculiar. He registered his bag through to the city, but he himself descended at the next station, and for a man who so disliked walking, and was by nature so averse from physical exercise, he displayed an almost heroic spirit, for he set forth to walk the nine miles which separated him from the Beardmore estate﻿—and he did not go by the shortest route.

It was nearing nightfall when Mr. Marl made his furtive way into a thick plantation on the edge of the Beardmore property.

He sat down, a tired, dusty but determined man, and waited for the night to close down over the countryside. And during the period of waiting, he examined with tender care the heavy automatic pistol he had taken from his bag in the train.

===Chinese===

他们一道走出来进了门厅，杰克正是在那儿找到他们的。

比德莫尔的汽车把客人连同提包送回了车站，而打那以后，马尔先生的举动便透着古怪。他把提包直接托运到城里，自己却在下一站下了车。对于一个如此厌恶走路、天性懒于体力活动的人来说，他表现出了近乎英勇的精神——他动身徒步走完那把自己同比德莫尔庄园隔开的九英里路，而且走的还不是最短的路线。

临近天黑，马尔先生才鬼鬼祟祟地摸进比德莫尔地产边缘一片茂密的林地。

他坐了下来——一个疲惫不堪、满身尘土却铁了心的男人——等着夜幕笼罩这片乡野。而在等候的当儿，他满怀温存地细细检视那把沉甸甸的自动手枪——那是他在火车上从提包里取出来的。

## V / 五

===Original===

The Girl Who Ran

===Chinese===

逃跑的姑娘

===Original===

“I can’t understand why that fellow hasn’t come back this morning,” said Jim Beardmore with a frown.

“Which fellow?” asked Jack carelessly.

“I’m speaking of Marl,” said his father.

“Was that the large-sized gentleman I saw yesterday?” asked Derrick Yale.

===Chinese===

“我真闹不明白，那家伙今天早上怎么还没回来。”吉姆·比德莫尔皱着眉头说。

“哪个家伙？”杰克漫不经心地问。

“我说的是马尔。”他父亲说。

“就是我昨天见到的那位大块头绅士吗？”德里克·耶尔问。

===Original===

They were standing on the terrace of the house, which, from its elevated position, gave them a view across the country.

The morning train had come and gone. They could see the trail of white smoke it left as it disappeared into the foothills nine miles away.

“Yes. I’d better phone Froyant, and tell him not to come over.”

Jim Beardmore stroked his stubbly chin.

“Marl puzzles me,” he said. “He is a brilliant fellow I believe, a reformed thief I know﻿—at least I hope he is reformed. What upset him yesterday, Jack? He came into the library looking like death.”

===Chinese===

他们正站在宅子的露台上，那露台地势高峻，四下的乡野尽收眼底。

早班火车来了又去了。它消失在九英里外的丘陵脚下，留下的一缕白烟还望得分明。

“正是。我最好给弗鲁瓦扬打个电话，叫他不用过来了。”

吉姆·比德莫尔摩挲着满是胡茬的下巴。

“马尔这人叫我捉摸不透，”他说，“我相信他是个有本事的，我知道他是个改过自新的窃贼——至少我指望他真已改了。他昨天是让什么给吓着了，杰克？他走进书房时面如死灰。”

===Original===

“I haven’t the slightest idea,” said Jack. “I think he has a weak heart, or something of the sort. He told me he gets these spasms occasionally.”

Beardmore laughed softly, and going into the house returned with a walking-stick.

“I’m going for a stroll, Jack. No, you needn’t come along. I’ve one or two things I wish to think out, and I promise you, Yale, I won’t leave the grounds, though I think you attach too much importance to the threats of these ruffians.”

===Chinese===

“我丝毫摸不着头脑。”杰克说，“我想他是心脏不好，或是那一类的毛病。他跟我说过，这种痉挛他偶尔会发作一回。”

比德莫尔轻轻一笑，走进宅子，出来时手里拿了根手杖。

“我出去散散步，杰克。不，你不必跟着。我有一两件事要细细想清楚。我向你保证，耶尔，我不会走出庄园地界——虽说依我看，你把这群恶棍的威胁看得太重了。”

===Original===

Yale shook his head.

“What of the sign on the tree?” he asked.

Jim Beardmore snorted contemptuously.

“It will take more than that to extract a hundred thousand from me,” he said.

He waved a farewell at them as he went down the broad stone steps, and they watched him walking slowly across the park.

===Chinese===

耶尔摇了摇头。

“那树上的记号又怎么说？”他问。

吉姆·比德莫尔轻蔑地哼了一声。

“想凭那个就从我这儿榨走十万，还差得远呢。”他说。

他走下宽阔的石阶时向他们挥手作别，两人望着他缓步穿过庄园园林。

===Original===

“Do you really think my father is in any kind of danger?” asked Jack.

Yale, who had been staring after the figure, turned with a start.

“In danger?” he repeated, and then after a second’s hesitation. “Yes, I believe there is very serious danger for him in the next day or two.”

===Chinese===

“你真觉得我父亲有什么危险吗？”杰克问。

耶尔一直凝望着那远去的身影，这时猛然回过头来。

“危险？”他重复了一遍，随即迟疑了一瞬。“是的，我相信这一两天之内，就有大祸落到他头上。”

===Original===

Jack turned his troubled gaze upon the disappearing figure.

“I hope you’re wrong,” he said. “Father doesn’t seem to take the matter as seriously as you.”

“That is because your father has not the same experience,” said the detective, “but I understand that he saw Chief Inspector Parr, and the inspector thought there was considerable danger.”

===Chinese===

杰克把忧心忡忡的目光投向那个渐渐消失的身影。

“但愿你说错了。”他说，“父亲似乎不像你把这事看得那么重。”

“那是因为你父亲没有这般的阅历，”侦探说，“不过据我所知，他见过帕尔总探长，而探长也认为此中危险非同小可。”

===Original===

Jack chuckled in spite of his fears.

“How do the lion and the lamb amalgamate?” he asked. “I didn’t think that headquarters had much use for private men like you, Mr. Yale?”

“I admire Parr,” said Derrick slowly. “He’s slow, but thorough. I am told that he is one of the most conscientious men at headquarters, and I fancy that the headquarters chiefs have treated him badly over the last Crimson Circle crime. They have practically told him that if he cannot run the organisation to earth he must send in his resignation.”

===Chinese===

杰克尽管满心忧惧，还是失声笑了出来。

“狮子和羔羊是怎么合到一处的？”他问道，“我还当警察总部根本用不上你这样的私家侦探呢，耶尔先生？”

“我敬佩帕尔。”德里克缓缓地说，“他动作迟缓，但办案彻底。我听说他是警察总部里最恪尽职守的人之一；而且我总觉得，在上一起红圈罪案上，总部那班首脑待他颇为不公。他们差不多已经放了话：他若不能把这个组织追查到水落石出，就得递上辞呈。”

===Original===

Whilst they were speaking, the figure of Mr. Beardmore had disappeared into the gloom of a little wood on the edge of the estate.

“I worked with him during the last Circle murder,” Derrick Yale went on, “and he struck me﻿—”

He stopped, and the two men looked at one another.

===Chinese===

两人说话的当儿，比德莫尔先生的身影已经没入庄园边缘一座小树林的幽暗里。

“上一起红圈凶案里，我跟他共过事，”德里克·耶尔接着说，“他给我的印象是——”

他戛然而止，两人面面相觑。

===Original===

There was no mistaking the sound. It was a shot near and distinct, and it came from the direction of the wood. In an instant Jack had leapt over the balustrade and was racing across the meadow, Derrick Yale behind him.

Twenty paces along the woodland path they found Jim Beardmore lying on his face, and he was quite dead, and even as Jack was staring down at his father with horrified eyes, a girl emerged from the wood at the farther end, and stopping only long enough to wipe with a handful of grass something that was red from her hands, she flew along the shadow of the hedge which divided the Froyant estate.

Never once did Thalia Drummond look back until she reached the shelter of the little summer house. Her face was drawn and white, and her breath came gaspingly as she stood for a second in the doorway of the little hut, and looked back to the wood. A swift glance round and she was in the house and on her knees tugging with quivering hands at the end of a floor board. It came up disclosing a black cavity. Another second’s hesitation, and she threw into the hole the revolver she had held in her hand, and dropped the board back into its place.

===Chinese===

那声音错不了。是一声枪响，又近又清晰，正来自小树林的方向。眨眼间杰克已翻过露台石栏，飞奔着穿过草地，德里克·耶尔紧随其后。

沿林间小径走出二十来步，他们发现吉姆·比德莫尔脸朝下趴在地上，早已气绝身亡。就在杰克惊骇万分地俯视着父亲的那一刻，一个姑娘从树林远端走了出来，只略停一停，抓一把草擦净手上那红色的东西，便沿着分隔弗鲁瓦扬家地产的树篱的阴影疾奔而去。

塔莉娅·德拉蒙德一路头也不回，直到那座小凉亭掩住了她的身形。她面容扭曲，脸色惨白，在凉亭门口喘息不定地站了一秒，回头望向那片树林。她飞快地环顾四周，随即进了凉亭，跪下身来，用发颤的双手抠住一块地板的一端猛拽。地板起了上来，露出一方黑洞洞的空穴。她又迟疑了一瞬，便把手一直握着的那把左轮手枪扔进洞里，再把地板放回原处。

## VI / 六

===Original===

“Thalia Drummond Is a Crook”

===Chinese===

塔莉娅·德拉蒙德是个骗子

===Original===

The Commissioner looked down at the newspaper cutting before him and tugged at his grey moustache. Inspector Parr, who knew the signs, watched with an apparently detached interest.

He was a short, thickset man, so lacking in inches that it was remarkable that he had ever satisfied the stringent requirements of the police authorities. His age was something below fifty, but his big red face was unlined. It was a face from whence every indication of intelligence and refinement was absent. The round, staring eyes were bovine in their lack of expression, the big fleshy nose, the heavy cheeks, pouched beneath the jaws, and the half-bald head, were units of his unimpressiveness.

===Chinese===

总监低头望着面前那则剪报，捻扯着自己的灰白八字胡。帕尔探长深谙这些迹象的意味，只摆出一副俨然置身事外的关切，在一旁看着。

他是个五短身材的壮汉，个头矮得出奇，居然能过警务当局那道严格的体格关，实在堪称奇事。他年纪未满五十，那张大红脸却不见一丝皱纹。这张脸上，才智与文雅的痕迹一概寻不见：圆睁的双眼呆滞如牛、毫无表情，肥大的肉鼻子，沉甸甸的两腮——颔下还垂着囊肉——加上那半秃的脑袋，凡此种种，无不为他的其貌不扬贡献了一份。

===Original===

The Commissioner picked up the cutting.

“Listen to this,” he said curtly, and read. It was the editorial of the *Morning Monitor* and it was direct to a point of offensiveness.

“For the second time during the past year the country has been shocked and outraged by the assassination of a prominent man. It is not necessary to give here the details of this Crimson Circle crime, particulars of which appear on another page. But it is very necessary that we should state in emphatic and unmistakable terms that we view with consternation the seeming helplessness of police headquarters to deal with this criminal gang. Inspector Parr, who has devoted himself for the past year to tracking the murdering blackmailers, can offer us nothing more than vague promises of revelations which never materialise. It is obvious that police headquarters needs a thorough overhauling, and the introduction of new blood, and we trust that those responsible for the government of the country, will not hesitate to make the drastic changes which are necessary.”

===Chinese===

总监拿起那则剪报。

“听听这个。”他简截地说，随即念了起来。这是《晨哨报》（Morning Monitor）的社论，笔锋直露得近乎冒犯。

“过去一年之内，显要人物惨遭暗杀、举国为之震惊愤慨，这已是第二回。这桩红圈罪案的细节此处无须赘述，详情另见本报另一版面。但极有必要的是，我们应以斩钉截铁、明白无误的字句声明：警察总部面对这伙匪帮竟似束手无策，我们目睹此情，惊惧交加。帕尔探长一年来专心致志追缉这些杀人的勒索匪徒，如今能拿给我们看的，无非是些许诺破案在即、却从不兑现的空话。显而易见，警察总部需要一番彻底的整顿改组，需要注入新鲜血液；我们相信，负治国之责的诸公，定会当机立断，力行必要的激烈变革。”

===Original===

“Well,” growled Colonel Morton, “what do you think of that, Parr?”

Mr. Parr rubbed his big chin and said nothing.

“James Beardmore was murdered after due warning had been given to the police,” said the Commissioner deliberately. “He was shot within sight of his house, and the murderer is at large. This is the second bad case, Parr, and I’ll tell you candidly that it is my intention to act on the advice which this newspaper gives.”

He tapped the cutting suggestively.

===Chinese===

“怎么样，”莫顿上校低吼道，“你对此有何高见，帕尔？”

帕尔先生摩挲着自己的大下巴，一言不发。

“詹姆斯·比德莫尔遇害之前，警方早已接获正式警告，”总监不紧不慢地说，“他就在自家宅子望得见的地方挨了枪，而凶手至今逍遥法外。这是第二桩恶劣的大案了，帕尔。我坦白告诉你，我打算照这家报纸的劝告行事。”

他意味深长地敲了敲那则剪报。

===Original===

“On the previous occasion you allowed Mr. Yale to get away with all the kudos for the capture of the murderer. You have seen Mr. Yale, I presume?”

The detective nodded.

“And what does he say?”

Mr. Parr shifted uneasily on his feet.

“He told me a lot of nonsense about a dark man with toothache.”

===Chinese===

“上一回，你让耶尔先生轻而易举地独揽了擒凶的全部赞誉。耶尔先生那儿，想必你去见过了？”

侦探点了点头。

“他怎么说？”

帕尔先生脚下不安地挪了挪。

“他跟我讲了一大通胡话，说什么有个害牙疼的黝黑汉子。”

===Original===

“How did he get that?” asked the Commissioner quickly.

“From the shell of the cartridge he found on the ground,” said the detective. “I don’t take any notice of this psychometrical stuff—”

The Commissioner leant back in his chair and sighed.

“I don’t think you take notice of any stuff that is serviceable, Parr,” he said, “and don’t sneer at Yale. That man has unusual and peculiar gifts. The fact that you don’t understand them, does not make them any less peculiar.”

===Chinese===

“他这是从哪儿得来的？”总监飞快地问。

“从他捡到的那枚地上的弹壳。”侦探说，“这种触物感应的玩意儿，我可不放在心上——”

总监往椅背上一靠，叹了口气。

“我看凡是真正派得上用场的东西，你一概不放在心上，帕尔，”他说，“还有，别讥诮耶尔。那个人有着非同寻常的奇异禀赋。你不理解它们，它们也并不因此少了半分奇异。”

===Original===

“Do you mean to say, sir,” said Parr, stirred into protest, “that a man can take a cartridge in his hand and tell you from that the appearance of the person who last handled it and what he was thinking about? Why, it is absurd!”

“Nothing is absurd,” said the Commissioner quietly. “The science of psychometry has been practised for years. Some people, unusually sensitive to impression, are able to tell the most remarkable things, and Yale is one of these.”

“He was there when the murder was committed,” replied Parr. “He was with Mr. Beardmore’s son, not a hundred yards away, and yet he did not catch the murderer.”

The Commissioner nodded.

“Neither have you,” he said. “Twelve months ago you told me of your scheme for trapping the Crimson Circle, and I agreed. We’ve both expected a little too much for your plan, I think. You must try something else. I hate to say it, but there it is.”

===Chinese===

“长官，您这话是说，”帕尔被激得抗辩起来，“一个人把弹壳攥在手里，就能凭它说出最后一个摸过它的人的相貌，连那人当时心里想什么都说得出来？嗐，这简直是荒唐！”

“天下事没有什么是荒唐的，”总监平静地说，“触物感应这门学问已行之有年。有些人对种种印象异乎寻常地敏感，能道出最不可思议的事情，耶尔便是此辈中人。”

“案发的时候他就在那儿，”帕尔回答说，“同比德莫尔先生的儿子在一起，相距不足一百码，可他照样没抓住凶手。”

总监点了点头。

“你也没抓住。”他说，“十二个月前，你向我提出你那套诱捕红圈的计划，我准了。依我看，对你那计划，咱们俩都期望得过了头。你得另想辙。这话我实在不愿出口，可事实就是如此。”

===Original===

Parr did not answer for a time, and then to the Commissioner’s surprise, he pulled up a chair to the desk and sat down uninvited.

“Colonel,” he said, “I’m going to tell you something,” and he was so earnest, so unlike his usual self, that the Commissioner could only look at him in amazement.

“The Crimson Circle gang is easy to get. I can find every one of them, and will find them if you will give me time. But it is the hub of the wheel that I’m after. If I can get the hub the spokes don’t count. But you’ve got to give me a little more authority than I have at present.”

“A little more authority?” said the dumbfounded Commissioner. “What the devil do you mean?”

“I’ll explain,” said the bovine Mr. Parr, and he explained to such purpose that he left the Commissioner a very silent and a very thoughtful man.

===Chinese===

帕尔半晌没有作声。随后，令总监诧异的是，他把一把椅子拉到桌前，不等相请便坐下了。

“上校，”他说，“我要告诉你一件事。”他这般恳切，全然不似平日的那副样子，弄得总监只能愕然望着他。

“红圈那帮喽啰不难拿。他们每一个人我都找得出，只要您肯给我时间，我定能把他们一个个揪出来。可我真正追的是那只轮毂。轮毂一到手，轮辐便不足论了。不过您得给我的权柄，比眼下的再大上一些。”

“再大上一些的权柄？”总监听得目瞪口呆，“你这话究竟是什么意思？”

“我自会解释。”这位一脸牛相的帕尔先生说。他这一解释，竟解释得极见分量，直留下总监一个人久久无言、思虑重重。

===Original===

After he left headquarters, Mr. Parr’s first call was at an office in the centre of the city.

On the third floor, in a tiny suite, which was distinguished only by the name of the occupant, Mr. Derrick Yale was waiting for him, and a greater contrast between the two men could not be imagined.

Yale, the overstrung, nervous, and sensitive dreamer; Parr, solid and beefy, seemingly incapable of an independent thought.

===Chinese===

他离开警察总部之后，头一处拜访的是市中心的一间办公室。

在三楼一套小小的写字间里——它唯一与众不同的地方，就是主人的名字——德里克·耶尔先生正等着他。这两个人之间的反差，大到无以复加。

耶尔，是个一根神经绷得过紧、敏感而易激动的梦幻家；帕尔，则结结实实、肥壮如牛，仿佛连一个自出心裁的念头都生不出来。

===Original===

“How did your interview go on, Parr?”

“Not very well,” said Parr, ruefully. “I think the Commissioner’s got one against me. Have you discovered anything?”

“I’ve discovered your man with the toothache,” was the astonishing reply. “His name is Sibly; he is a seafaring man, and was seen in the vicinity of the house the following day. Yesterday,” he picked up a telegram, “he was arrested for drunken and disorderly conduct, and in his possession was found an automatic pistol, which I should imagine was the weapon with which the crime was committed. You remember that the bullet which was extracted from poor Beardmore, was obviously fired from an automatic.”

===Chinese===

“你同总监那场会面，谈得怎么样，帕尔？”

“不大妙。”帕尔懊丧地说，“我看总监对我存了芥蒂。你有什么发现没有？”

“你那个牙疼的汉子，我找出来了。”回答令人咋舌。“他名叫西布利（Sibly），是个跑海路的人，案发第二天有人在这宅子左近见过他。昨天，”他拿起一封电报，“他因酗酒滋事被捕，随身搜出一把自动手枪——我料想那便是作案的凶器。你总还记得，从可怜的比德莫尔身上取出的那颗子弹，一望便知是自动手枪射出的。”

===Original===

Parr gaped at him in amazement.

“How did you find this out?”

And Derrick Yale laughed softly.

“You haven’t a great deal of faith in my deductions,” he said with a glint of humour in his eyes. “But when I felt that cartridge I was as certain that I could see the man as I am certain I can see you. I sent one of my own staff down to make enquiries, with this result.” He picked up the telegram.

===Chinese===

帕尔张口结舌，惊愕地瞪着他。

“你是怎么查出来的？”

德里克·耶尔莞尔轻笑。

“你对我的推论素来没多少信心，”他说，眼中闪过一丝谐谑，“可我握着那枚弹壳的时候，我确信自己看得见那个人，就如同我确信此刻看得见你一样。我派了自己手下的一个人下去查访，得来的便是这个结果。”他拿起那封电报。

===Original===

Mr. Parr stood, a heavy frown disfiguring what little claim to beauty he might have.

“So they’ve caught him,” he said softly. “Now I wonder if he wrote this?”

He took out a pocketbook, and Derrick Yale saw him extract a scrap of paper which had evidently been burnt, for the edges were black.

Yale took the scrap from his hand.

“Where did you find this?” he asked.

“I raked it out of the ashpan at Beardmore’s place yesterday,” he said.

===Chinese===

帕尔先生站在那儿，眉头拧得紧紧的，把他脸上原本就不多的一点看头毁了个干净。

“这么说，他们把他逮住了，”他轻声说，“如今我倒要问：这个会不会是他写的？”

他掏出一本记事簿，德里克·耶尔看见他从里面抽出一片纸屑——那纸显然经火烧过，四边焦黑。

耶尔把那片纸屑从他手里接了过去。

“你这是在哪儿找到的？”他问。

“昨天从比德莫尔家的炉灰坑里扒出来的。”他说。

===Original===

The writing was in a large scrawling hand, and the scrap ran:

You alone

me alone

Block B

Graft

===Chinese===

字迹写得又大又潦草，那片纸上写着：

只有你

只有我

B区

贪赃

===Original===

“ ‘Me alone… you alone,’ ” read Yale. “ ‘Block B… Graft’?”

He shook his head.

“It is Greek to me.”

He balanced the letter upon the palm of his hand and shook his head.

“I can’t even feel an impression,” he said. “Fire destroys the aura.”

===Chinese===

“‘只有我……只有你，’”耶尔念道，“‘B区……贪赃’？”

他摇了摇头。

“这对我简直是天书。”

他把那片纸平放在掌心上，又摇了摇头。

“我连一点感应都摸不出来了，”他说，“火会把灵光毁掉。”

===Original===

Parr carefully put away the scrap into his case and replaced it in his pocket.

“There is another thing I’d like to tell you,” he said. “Somebody was in the wood who wore pointed shoes and smoked cigars. I found the cigar ashes in a little hollow, and his footprint was on the flowerbeds.”

“Near the house?” asked Derrick Yale, startled.

The solid man nodded.

“My own theory is,” he went on, “that somebody wanted to warn Beardmore, wrote this letter and brought it to the house after dark. It must have been received by the old man, because he burnt it. I found the ashes in the place where the servants dump their cinders.”

===Chinese===

帕尔小心翼翼地把纸屑收回记事簿，又把簿子放回衣袋。

“还有一件事我想告诉你，”他说，“树林里到过一个穿尖头鞋、抽雪茄的人。我在一处小洼地里发现了雪茄灰，花圃上也留着他的脚印。”

“就在宅子近旁？”德里克·耶尔吃了一惊，忙问。

这个敦实的汉子点了点头。

“依我自己的推测，”他接着说，“是有人想给比德莫尔报信，写下这封信，天黑之后送到了宅子里。信必定到了老头子手里，因为他把它烧了。灰烬我是在仆人们倾倒炉灰的地方找到的。”

===Original===

There was a gentle tap at the door.

“Jack Beardmore,” said Yale under his breath.

Jack Beardmore showed signs of the distressing period through which he had passed. He nodded to Parr and came toward Yale with outstretched hand.

“No news, I suppose?” he asked, and turning to the other: “You were at the house yesterday, Mr. Parr. Did you find anything?”

“Nothing worth speaking about,” said Parr.

===Chinese===

门上响起轻轻的一记叩声。

“是杰克·比德莫尔。”耶尔压低了嗓音说。

杰克·比德莫尔眉宇之间，犹见他所经历的那段惨痛时日留下的痕迹。他向帕尔点了点头，朝耶尔伸出一只手迎上前去。

“想必还没有什么消息吧？”他问，又转向另一位：“昨天您到宅子里去过，帕尔先生。可发现了什么？”

“没什么值得一提的。”帕尔说。

===Original===

“I’ve just been to see Froyant, he is in town,” said Jack. “It wasn’t a very successful visit, for he is in a pitiable state of nerves.”

He did not explain that the unsatisfactory part of his call was that he had not seen Thalia Drummond, and only one of the men guessed the reason of his disappointment.

Derrick Yale told him of the arrest which had been made.

“I don’t want you to build any hopes on this,” he said, “even if he is the man who fired the shot, he is certain to be no more than the agent. We shall probably hear the same story as we heard before, that he was in low water and that the chief of the Crimson Circle induced him to commit the act. We are as far from the real solution as ever we have been.”

===Chinese===

“我刚去看过弗鲁瓦扬，他进城来了，”杰克说，“这一趟不算圆满，他的神经糟糕得可怜。”

他没有解释说，这趟拜访之所以不能令人满意，是因为没有见到塔莉娅·德拉蒙德；两人之中，只有一位猜到了他失望的缘故。

德里克·耶尔把那边落网的事说给他听。

“我不愿你在这上头抱什么指望，”他说，“就算他真是开枪的那个人，他也必定不过是个受差遣办事的工具。我们多半会听到和先前一模一样的故事：说他穷困潦倒，是红圈的头目诱使他下的手。离真正的破解，我们还和从前一样遥远。”

===Original===

They strolled out of the office together, into the clean autumn sunlight.

Jack, who had an engagement with a lawyer who was settling his father’s estate, accompanied the two men, who were on their way to catch a train for the town where the suspected murderer was detained. They were passing through one of the busiest streets when Jack uttered an exclamation. On the opposite side of the road was a big pawnbroker’s, and a girl was coming from the side entrance devoted to the service of those who needed temporary loans.

“Well, I’m blessed!” It was Parr’s unemotional voice. “I haven’t seen her for two years.”

===Chinese===

三人一道踱出办公室，走进明净的秋日阳光里。

杰克同一位替他父亲清理遗产的律师有约在身，便陪两位同行了一程——他们正要赶火车，前往羁押那名杀人嫌犯的城镇。正走过一条顶热闹的大街时，杰克忽然喊出了声。马路对面是一家大洋当铺，一个姑娘正从侧门里出来——那道侧门，专供急需短期借款的客人出入。

“嗬，真是稀奇！”说话的是帕尔那副不动声色的嗓音，“我有两年没见着她了。”

===Original===

Jack turned on him open-eyed.

“Haven’t seen her for two years,” he said slowly. “Are you referring to that lady?”

Parr nodded.

“I’m referring to Thalia Drummond,” he said calmly, “who is a crook and a companion of crooks!”

===Chinese===

杰克睁圆了眼睛，霍地转向他。

“两年没见着她了，”他慢条斯理地说，“您说的是那位小姐？”

帕尔点了点头。

“我说的正是塔莉娅·德拉蒙德，”他心平气和地说，“她是个骗子，而且专同骗子们为伍！”

## VII / 七

===Original===
The Stolen Idol

===Chinese===
失窃的佛像

===Original===
Jack heard him and was stunned.

He stood motionless and speechless, as the girl, as though unconscious of the scrutiny, hailed a taxicab and was driven away.

“Now what the dickens was she doing there?” said Parr.

“A crook and a companion of crooks,” repeated Jack mechanically. “Good God! Where are you going?” he asked quickly, as the inspector took a step into the roadway.

===Chinese===
杰克听见了他的话，顿时呆住了。

他一动不动、一言不发地站在原地，眼看着那姑娘仿佛浑然不觉有人打量她，扬手叫了一辆出租汽车，乘车而去。

“她到底在那儿干什么？”帕尔说。

“一个骗子，而且是骗子们的同伙。”杰克木然地重复道。见探长抬脚要走上马路，他赶忙问：“天哪！您这是上哪儿去？”

===Original===
“I intend discovering what she has been doing in the pawnbroker’s,” said the stolid Parr.

“She may have gone there because she was short of money. It is no crime to be short of money.”

Jack realised the feebleness of his defence even as he spoke.

===Chinese===
“我打算查清楚她在那家当铺里做了什么。”不动声色的帕尔说。

“她也许是因为手头缺钱才去那儿的。手头缺钱可不是罪过。”

话一出口，杰克自己也觉出了这番辩护的苍白无力。

===Original===
Thalia Drummond a thief! It was incredible, impossible! And yet he followed unresistingly the detective as he crossed the road; followed him down the dark passage to the loaning department, and was present in the manager’s room when an assistant brought in the article which the girl had pledged. It was a small golden figure of Buddha.

“I thought it queer,” said the manager, when Parr had made himself known. “She only wanted ten pounds and it is worth a hundred if it’s worth a penny.”

“What explanation did she give?” asked Derrick Yale, who had been a silent listener.

“She said she was short of money and that her father had a number of these curios, but wanted to pledge them at a price which would allow him to redeem them.”

“Did she leave her address? What name did she give?”

===Chinese===
塔莉娅·德拉蒙德会是个窃贼！这难以置信，绝无可能！然而侦探穿过马路时，他竟毫无抗拒地跟了上去；跟着他走过昏暗的过道，来到放款部；店员把姑娘典当的那件东西送进经理室时，他也在场——那是一尊小小的黄金佛像。

帕尔表明身份之后，经理开口道：“我当时就觉得蹊跷。她只要了十英镑，可这东西再怎么说也值一百英镑。”

“她是怎么解释的？”一直沉默旁听的德里克·耶尔问道。

“她说她手头缺钱，又说她父亲有好几件这类古玩，但想按一个日后赎得回来的价钱典当。”

“她留没留地址？报的什么名字？”

===Original===
“Thalia Drummond,” said the assistant, “of 29, Park Gate.”

Derrick Yale uttered an exclamation.

“Why that’s Froyant’s address, isn’t it?”

Too well Jack knew it was the address of the miserly Harvey Froyant, and he remembered with a sinking of heart that Froyant made a hobby of collecting these eastern antiquities. The inspector gave a receipt for the idol and slipped it into his pocket.

===Chinese===
“塔莉娅·德拉蒙德，”店员说，“住帕克门（Park Gate）29 号。”

德里克·耶尔失声叫了出来。

“哎呀，那不是弗鲁瓦扬的地址吗？”

杰克心里再明白不过，那正是吝啬鬼哈维·弗鲁瓦扬的住址；他想起弗鲁瓦扬素有收藏这类东方古董的癖好，心不由得往下一沉。探长为这尊佛像开了张收据，随手将佛像揣进了口袋。

===Original===
“We’ll go along and see Mr. Froyant,” he said, and Jack interposed desperately:

“For heaven’s sake, don’t let us get this girl into trouble,” he pleaded. “It may have been some sudden temptation﻿—I will make things right, if money can settle the affair.”

Derrick Yale was eyeing the young man with a grave, understanding look.

“You know Miss Drummond?”

Jack nodded. He was too miserable to speak; he felt an absurd desire to run away and hide himself.

===Chinese===
“我们这就去见弗鲁瓦扬先生。”他说。杰克不顾一切地插了话：

“看在老天的分上，别让这姑娘惹上麻烦，”他恳求道，“那也许只是一时鬼迷心窍——只要钱能了结这件事，我来把它料理妥当。”

德里克·耶尔正打量着年轻人，目光凝重而了然。

“你认识德拉蒙德小姐？”

杰克点了点头。他心里苦得说不出话，只觉出一股荒唐的冲动，想逃开去把自己藏起来。

===Original===
“It can’t be done,” said Inspector Parr definitely. He was the conventional police officer now. “I’m going along to Froyant’s to discover whether this article was pledged with his approval.”

“Then you’ll go by yourself,” said Jack wrathfully.

He could not contemplate being a witness of the girl’s humiliation. It was monstrous. It was beastly of Parr, he said to Yale when they were alone.

“The girl would not commit so mean a theft, the stupid, blundering fool! I wish to heaven I had never called his attention to her.”

“It was he who saw her first,” said Yale, and dropped his hand upon the young man’s shoulder. “Jack, you’re a little unstrung, I think. Why are you so interested in Miss Drummond? Of course,” he said suddenly, “you must have seen a lot of her when you were at home. Froyant’s estate joins yours, doesn’t it?”

===Chinese===
“那可办不到。”帕尔探长斩钉截铁地说。此刻他又成了那个照章办事的警官。“我要去弗鲁瓦扬那里查清，这件东西典当有没有经过他的同意。”

“那您就自己去吧。”杰克怒气冲冲地说。

他没法设想自己亲眼看着那姑娘受辱。这简直骇人听闻。等到只剩他们两人时，他对耶尔说，帕尔这么干实在太狠了。

“那姑娘绝不会干出这么下作的偷窃勾当——这个蠢头蠢脑的大笨蛋！恨不得当初从没让他注意到她才好。”

“最先看见她的可是他自己。”耶尔说着，把手搭上年轻人的肩头。“杰克，我看你的神经有点绷得太紧了。你对德拉蒙德小姐怎么这样上心？哦，对了，”他忽然说道，“你们在家那阵子，你一定常常见到她。弗鲁瓦扬家的地界紧挨着你们家的，不是吗？”

===Original===
Jack nodded.

“If he would give as much attention to the running down of the Crimson Circle as he gives to the hounding of that poor girl,” he said bitterly, “my poor father would be alive today.”

Derrick Yale did his best to soothe him. He took him back to his office and tried to bring his thoughts to a more pleasant channel. They had been there a quarter of an hour when the telephone bell rang. It was Parr who spoke.

===Chinese===
杰克点了点头。

“他要是能把穷追那个可怜姑娘的心思，同样用在追查红圈上，”他悲愤地说，“我可怜的父亲今天就还会活着。”

德里克·耶尔竭力宽慰他。他带他回了自己的办公室，想法子把他的心思引到愉快些的道道上去。两人在那里待了一刻钟，电话铃响了。电话里说话的是帕尔。

===Original===
“Well?” asked Yale.

“I’ve arrested Thalia Drummond, and I am charging her in the morning,” was the laconic message.

Yale put down the receiver gently and turned to the young man.

“She’s arrested?” Jack guessed before he spoke.

===Chinese===
“怎么样？”耶尔问。

“我已经逮捕了塔莉娅·德拉蒙德，明天早上就起诉她。”来话就这么简短几句。

耶尔轻轻搁下听筒，转向年轻人。

“她被捕了？”耶尔还没开口，杰克已先猜着了。

===Original===
Yale nodded.

Jack Beardmore’s face was very white.

“You see, Jack,” said Yale gently, “you have probably been as much deceived as Froyant. The girl is a thief.”

“If she were a thief and murderess,” said Jack doggedly, “I love her.”

===Chinese===
耶尔点了点头。

杰克·比德莫尔的脸色煞白。

“你瞧，杰克，”耶尔温和地说，“你多半和弗鲁瓦扬一样受了骗。这姑娘是个窃贼。”

“就算她是窃贼，是杀人凶手，”杰克执拗地说，“我也爱她。”

## VIII / 八

===Original===

The Charge

===Chinese===

指控

===Original===

Mr. Parr’s interview with Harvey Froyant was a short one. At the sight of the detective, that thin man blanched. He knew him by sight and had met him in connection with the Beardmore tragedy.

“Well, well,” he asked tremulously. “What is wrong? Have these infernal people started a new campaign?”

“Nothing so bad as that, sir,” said Parr. “I came to ask you a few questions. How long have you had Thalia Drummond in your house?”

===Chinese===

帕尔先生与哈维·弗鲁瓦扬的会面十分短促。一见到侦探，那瘦削的男人顿时面无血色。他跟此人有过一面之缘，还因比德莫尔惨案与他打过交道。

“哎呀，哎呀，”他颤声问道，“出什么事了？是那伙该死的家伙又发起新一轮攻势了吗？”

“还没糟到那个地步，先生。”帕尔说，“我来是要问你几个问题。塔莉娅·德拉蒙德在你家做事多久了？”

===Original===

“She has been my secretary for three months,” said Froyant suspiciously. “Why?”

“What wages do you pay her?” asked Parr.

Mr. Froyant mentioned a sum grossly inadequate, and even he was apologetic for its inefficiency.

“I give her her food, you know, and she has evenings off,” he said, feeling that the starvation wage must be justified.

===Chinese===

“她当我的秘书已经三个月了。”弗鲁瓦扬满腹狐疑地说，“问这个干什么？”

“你付她多少薪水？”帕尔问。

弗鲁瓦扬先生报了一个少得可怜的数目，连他自己也为数目的微薄而讪讪致歉。

“要知道，我管她饭食，晚上还放她的假。”他说，觉得这份饿不死人的工钱非得辩白一番不可。

===Original===

“Has she been short of money lately?”

Mr. Froyant stared at him.

“Why﻿—yes. She asked me if I could advance her five pounds yesterday,” he said. “She said she had a call upon her purse which she could not meet. Of course, I didn’t advance the money. I do not approve of advancing money for work which is not performed,” said Froyant virtuously. “It tends to pauperise﻿—”

===Chinese===

“她近来手头拮据吗？”

弗鲁瓦扬先生直愣愣地瞪着他。

“这个——是的。她昨天问我能不能预支给她五镑。”他说，“她说有一笔要她掏的钱，她应付不出来。当然，我没有预支。我不赞成为尚未做出的工作预支款项。”弗鲁瓦扬道貌岸然地说，“那会把人惯成乞丐——”

===Original===

“You have a large number of antiques, I understand, Mr. Froyant, some of them very valuable. Have you missed any lately?”

Froyant jumped to his feet. The very hint that he might have been robbed was sufficient to set his mind in a panic. Without a word he rushed from the room. He was gone three minutes and when he came back his eyes were almost bulging from his head.

“My Buddha!” he gasped. “It is worth a hundred pounds. It was there this morning﻿—”

===Chinese===

“据我了解，你藏有大量古董，弗鲁瓦扬先生，其中几件还相当贵重。近来可曾发现短缺了什么？”

弗鲁瓦扬霍地跳起身来。光是家中可能失窃这一句暗示，便足以叫他方寸大乱。他一言不发地冲出房去。一去三分钟，回来时双眼几乎要从眼眶里暴了出来。

“我的佛像！”他喘着气说，“那东西值一百镑。今天早晨还在原处——”

===Original===

“Send for Miss Drummond,” said the detective briefly.

Thalia came, a cool, self-possessed girl, who stood by her employer’s desk, her hands clasped behind her, scarcely looking at the detective.

The interview was short, and for Mr. Froyant, painful. Upon the girl it had no apparent effect whatever. And yet she must have known, from the steely glare in Froyant’s eyes, that her theft had been detected. For a little time the man found a difficulty in framing a coherent sentence.

===Chinese===

“去把德拉蒙德小姐叫来。”侦探简短地说。

塔莉娅来了，是个神色沉静、从容自持的姑娘，站在雇主的写字台旁，双手交扣在背后，几乎不看那侦探一眼。

这场问话为时甚短，对弗鲁瓦扬先生来说却颇为难堪。而这事落在姑娘身上，似乎没有产生丝毫影响。然而，她必定已从弗鲁瓦扬眼中那钢铁般的凶光里看出，自己的偷窃已然败露。有好一阵子，这个男人连一句囫囵话都说不出来。

===Original===

“You﻿—you have stolen something of mine,” he blurted out. His voice was almost a squeak. The accusing hand trembled in the intensity of his emotion. “You﻿—you are a thief!”

“I asked you for the money,” said the girl coolly. “If you hadn’t been such a wicked old skinflint, you’d have let me have it.”

“You﻿—you﻿—” spluttered Froyant, and then with a gasp﻿—“I charge her, inspector. I charge her with theft. You shall go to prison for this. Mark my words, young woman. Wait﻿—wait,” he raised his hand. “I will see if anything else is missing.”

===Chinese===

“你——你偷了我的东西。”他脱口嚷出，声音尖细得近乎尖叫。他伸出去指控的那只手激动得直发抖。“你——你是个贼！”

“我可是问你要过那笔钱的。”姑娘冷冷地说，“你要不是这么个狠毒的老守财奴，早就该把钱给我了。”

“你——你——”弗鲁瓦扬语无伦次地嚷道，随即倒抽一口气——“探长，我控告她。我控告她偷窃。你要为这事进监狱的。记住我的话，年轻姑娘。等等——等等，”他抬起一只手，“我再去看看还短了别的什么没有。”

===Original===

“You can save yourself the trouble,” said the girl, as he was leaving the room. “The Buddha was the only thing I took, and it was an ugly little beast, anyway.”

“Give me your keys,” stormed the enraged man. “To think that I’ve allowed you to open my business letters!”

“I’ve opened one which will not be pleasant for you, Mr. Froyant,” she said quietly, and then he saw what she was holding in her hand.

She passed the envelope across to him, and with staring eyes he saw the Crimson Circle, but the words written within the hoop were blurred and indistinct. He dropped the card and collapsed into a chair.

===Chinese===

“您就不必费这个事了。”他正要出房，姑娘说道，“我拿的只有那尊佛像，再说，那小东西本来就丑得吓人。”

“把你的钥匙交出来。”怒气冲天的男人咆哮道，“想想看，我竟然容许你拆看我的公务信件！”

“我拆过一封您看了不会痛快的信，弗鲁瓦扬先生。”她不动声色地说。这时他才看清她手里拿的是什么。

她把那只信封递到他面前。他瞪眼一看，见着的正是那个红圈，只是圆环之内写着的字样已然模糊难辨。他手一松，卡片跌落，整个人瘫倒在椅子里。

## IX / 九

===Original===

Thalia in the Police Court

===Chinese===

警务法庭上的塔莉娅

===Original===

The magistrate was a kindhearted man and seemed uncomfortable. He looked from the unemotional Mr. Parr who stood on the witness-stand, to the girl in the steel pen, and she was almost as cool and as self-controlled as the police witness. Her face was one which would have attracted attention in any circumstances, but in the drab setting of the police court, her beauty was emphasised and enhanced.

The magistrate glanced down at the charge-sheet before him. Her age was described as twenty-one, her occupation as secretary.

The man of law, who had had many shocks in his lifetime, and had steeled himself to the most unusual and improbable happenings, could only shake his head in despair.

“Is anything known against this woman?” he asked, and felt it was absurd even to refer to the slim, girlish prisoner as a “woman.”

“She has been under observation for some time, your worship,” was the reply, “but she has not been in the hands of the police before.”

===Chinese===

治安法官是个心肠慈软的人，神色却透着局促。他的目光从证人席上那位不动声色的帕尔先生身上，移到铁栏被告席里的姑娘身上；而她几乎同这位警方证人一般冷静、一般自持。她这张脸，无论置于何种境地都惹人注目，而在警务法庭这灰暗单调的布景之中，她的美貌更被烘托得愈见照人。

治安法官低头瞥了一眼面前的控罪单。单上载明：年龄二十一岁，职业秘书。

这位司法官生平经受过种种震荡，心肠早炼得足以应对最反常、最难以置信的变故，此刻却只能绝望地摇头。

“这个女人可有前科？”他问罢只觉荒唐——竟把这样苗条稚气的女被告唤作“女人”。

“她已被警方留意查访多时，法官大人，”得到的回答是这样，“不过先前从未落进过警察手里。”

===Original===

The magistrate looked over his glasses at the girl.

“I cannot understand how you got yourself into this terrible position,” he said. “A girl who has evidently had the education of a lady, you have been charged with a theft of a few pounds, for although the article you stole was worth a large sum, that was all that your dishonesty realised. Your act was probably due to some great temptation. I suppose the need for the money was very urgent; yet that does not excuse your act. I shall bind you over to come up for judgment when called upon, treating you as a first offender, and I do most earnestly appeal to you to live honestly and avoid a repetition of this unpleasant experience.”

The girl bowed slightly and left the box for the police office, and the next case was called.

===Chinese===

治安法官从镜片上方望着那姑娘。

“我实在不明白，你怎么会把自己弄到这般可怕的境地，”他说，“一个分明受过淑女教养的姑娘，被控偷窃的不过是区区几镑钱——因为你窃取的那件物品虽值一大笔钱，你的不老实实际到手的也就这么些。你的行为，多半是遭了什么重大的诱惑。我料想你当时等钱用等得十分急迫；然而这并不能开脱你的行径。我按初犯对待，判你具结，随传随到、听候发落；我还至诚恳切地奉劝你，往后清白做人，切莫让这不愉快的经历再重演一遍。”

姑娘微微欠身，离开被告席往警察办公室去了，下一桩案子随即被传唤开审。

===Original===

Harvey Froyant rose at the same time and made his way out of the court. He was a rich man to whom money represented the goal and object of life. He was the type of man who counted the contents of his pocket every night before he went to bed, and he would have had his own mother arrested in similar circumstances. Thalia Drummond’s offence was made more heinous in his eyes because her last act of service had been to hand to him the warning of the Crimson Circle, from the shock of which he had not yet recovered.

He was a large, thin man with a permanent stoop. His attitude towards the world was one of acute suspicion; for the moment it was one of resentment, for he held the strongest views on the sacredness of property.

To Parr, who followed him out of the court, he expressed his disappointment that the girl had not been sent to prison.

“A woman like that is a danger to society,” he complained in his high-pitched, peevish voice. “How do I know that she isn’t in league with these blackguards who are threatening me? Forty thousand they ask for! Forty thousand!” He wailed the last words. “It is your duty to see that I come to no harm! Understand that﻿—it is your duty!”

===Chinese===

哈维·弗鲁瓦扬同时起身，出了法庭。他是个有钱人，在他这里，金钱便是人生的目标与旨归。他是那种每晚就寝前必得数一数口袋内容的人物，换作同样的情形，他连自己的亲娘也会扭送究办。塔莉娅·德拉蒙德的过犯在他眼里因此愈发深重——她当差的最后一桩活计，便是把红圈的那封警告书递到他手上，那一场惊吓，他至今不曾缓过来。

他是个瘦高个子，永远伛着背。他对整个世界的姿态是一概的深切疑忌；此刻则是怨怼，因为对于财产的神圣不可侵犯，他抱着最坚定不移的见解。

对尾随他走出法庭的帕尔，他坦言自己的失望——那姑娘竟没有被送进监狱。

“那样的女人是社会的祸害，”他用高亢而乖戾的嗓音抱怨道，“我怎么知道她没同那伙挟制我的恶棍暗中勾结？他们索价四万英镑！四万英镑啊！”末了那几个字，他简直是哀嚎出来的。“保我毫发无损，是你的职责！给我记住——是你的职责！”

===Original===

“I heard you!” said Inspector Parr wearily. “And as to the girl, I don’t suppose she ever heard of the Crimson Circle. She’s very young.”

“Young!” snarled the lean man. “That’s the time to punish them, isn’t it? Catch them young and punish them young, and you may turn them into respectable citizens!”

“I dare say you’re right,” agreed the stout Mr. Parr with a sigh, and then inconsequently, “Children are a great responsibility.”

Froyant muttered something under his breath, and without so much as a nod of farewell, walked rapidly through the court, into the motorcar which was waiting for him at the entrance to the courthouse.

===Chinese===

“听见了！”帕尔探长倦倦地说，“至于那姑娘，红圈的名号她怕是压根没有听说过。她还年轻得很。”

“年轻！”瘦子狞声啐道，“正是该治罪的年纪，不是吗？趁年轻拿下、趁年轻惩治，才调教得出规规矩矩的公民！”

“想来你说得对。”敦实的帕尔先生叹着气表示赞同，随即没头没脑地补了一句：“儿女可是桩天大的责任哪。”

弗鲁瓦扬压着嗓子咕哝了句什么，连一个道别的颔首都欠奉，疾步穿过法院，钻进候在法院大门口的那辆汽车里去了。

===Original===

The inspector watched him depart with a slow smile, and, looking round, caught the eye of a young man who was waiting by the clerk’s door.

“Good morning, Mr. Beardmore,” he said. “Are you waiting to see the young lady?”

“Yes. How long will they keep her?” asked Jack nervously.

Mr. Parr gazed at him with expressionless eyes, and sniffed.

===Chinese===

探长含着慢慢浮起的笑意目送他远去，一转脸，正与守在书记官室门边的一个青年四目相接。

“早上好，比德莫尔先生，”他说，“你是在等那位年轻小姐？”

“是。他们要扣她多久？”杰克神色紧张地问。

帕尔先生面无表情地盯着他望了片刻，吸了吸鼻子。

===Original===

“If you don’t mind my saying so, Mr. Beardmore,” he said quietly, “you are probably taking a greater interest in Miss Drummond than is good for you.”

“What do you mean?” asked Jack quickly. “The whole thing was a plot. That beast Froyant—”

The inspector shook his head.

“Miss Drummond admitted that she took the statuette,” he said, “and, besides, we saw her coming out of Isaacs’. There isn’t any doubt about it.”

“She only made the admission for some reason best known to herself,” said Jack violently. “Do you think a girl like that would steal? Why should she? I would have given her anything she wanted”—he checked himself suddenly. “There is something behind this,” he went on more quietly, “something which I do not understand, and probably you do not understand either, inspector.”

===Chinese===

“恕我直言，比德莫尔先生，”他平静地说，“你对德拉蒙德小姐的关切，恐怕已经超出了于你有益的分寸。”

“你这是什么意思？”杰克急急地问，“整件事就是个圈套。弗鲁瓦扬那头畜生——”

探长摇了摇头。

“德拉蒙德小姐亲口承认拿了那尊佛像，”他说，“况且，我们亲眼看见她从艾萨克斯（Isaacs）当铺里出来。这桩案子没有什么可疑的。”

“她那样承认，自有只有她自己才最明白的缘故，”杰克激烈地说，“你以为那样的姑娘会偷东西？她凭什么要偷？她想要什么我都乐意给——”他猛地煞住了话头。“这背后有名堂，”他接着说，口气平和了几分，“有名堂我不明白，多半你也不明白，探长。”

===Original===

The door opened at that moment and the girl came out. She stopped at the sight of Jack and a faint flush crept into her pale face.

“Were you in court?” she asked quickly.

He nodded, and she shook her head.

“You shouldn’t have come,” she said almost vehemently. “How did you know? Who told you?” She seemed oblivious to the presence of the inspector, but for the first time since her arrest she showed some sign of her pent emotion. The colour came and went, and her voice shook a little as she continued: “I am sorry you knew anything about it, Mr. Beardmore, and am desperately sorry you came,” she said.

===Chinese===

正在这时门开了，姑娘走了出来。一见杰克她便止住步，苍白的脸上悄悄爬上一抹淡淡的红晕。

“你到庭上来了？”她急急地问。

他点了点头，她却摇起头来。

“你本不该来的，”她几乎是激愤地说，“你怎么知道的？谁告诉你的？”她似乎全然不觉探长在场，可自被捕以来，她头一回泄露了几分郁积心头的情绪。红晕来了又褪，她再往下说时，声音微微发颤：“这件事让你知道了，我很抱歉，比德莫尔先生；你还赶到这里来，我更是难过万分。”

===Original===

“But it isn’t true,” he interrupted. “You can tell me that, Thalia? It was a plot, wasn’t it? A plot intended to ruin you?” His voice was almost pleading, but she shook her head.

“There was no plot,” she said quietly. “I stole from Mr. Froyant.”

“But why, why?” he asked despairingly. “Why did you—”

“I am afraid I can’t tell you why,” she said with the ghost of a smile on her lips, “except that I needed the money, and that is good and sufficient reason, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===

“可那不是真的，”他插话道，“这一点你能告诉我吧，塔莉娅？那是个圈套，对不对？一个存心毁掉你的圈套？”他的声音几乎是哀求，她却摇了摇头。

“没有什么圈套，”她静静地说，“我确实偷了弗鲁瓦扬先生的东西。”

“可是为什么，为什么？”他绝望地问，“你为什么要——”

“只怕我没法告诉你为什么，”她说，唇边挂着一丝若有若无的笑影，“只能说我需要那笔钱，而这个理由又正当又充分，不是吗？”

===Original===

“I’ll never believe it.” Jack’s face was set and his grey eyes regarded her steadily. “You are not the kind who would indulge in petty pilfering.”

She looked at him for a long time, and then turned her eyes to the inspector.

“You may be able to undeceive Mr. Beardmore,” she said. “I am afraid I cannot.”

“Where are you going?” he asked as, with a little nod, she was passing on.

“I am going home,” she replied. “Please don’t come with me, Mr. Beardmore.”

===Chinese===

“我永远也不会相信。”杰克脸色绷得紧紧的，灰色的眼睛定定地端详着她。“你不是那种人，会去染指小偷小摸的勾当。”

她望了他许久，随后把目光转向探长。

“您或许能让比德莫尔先生醒过神来，”她说，“只怕我是无能为力了。”

“你要去哪儿？”见她微微颔首便自顾往前走，他问道。

“我回家去，”她答道，“请不要跟着我，比德莫尔先生。”

===Original===

“But you have no home.”

“I have a lodging,” she said with a hint of impatience.

“Then I am going with you,” he said doggedly.

She did not make any remonstrance, and they passed from the court together into the busy street. No word was spoken until they reached the entrance of a tube station.

“Now I must go home,” she said more gently than before.

===Chinese===

“可你并没有家。”

“我有个寄宿的住处。”她的话音里透出一丝不耐。

“那么我陪你一道去。”他倔强地说。

她没有再行劝阻，两人一同走出法院，来到熙熙攘攘的大街上。直到一处地铁站的入口，彼此谁也没有开口。

“这回我真得回家了。”她说，语气比先前温软了些。

===Original===

“But what are you going to do?” he demanded. “How are you going to get your living with this terrible charge against you?”

“Is it so terrible?” she asked coolly. She was walking into the station entrance when he took her arm and swung her round with almost savage violence.

“Now listen to me, Thalia,” he said between his teeth. “I love you and I want to marry you. I haven’t told you that before, but you’ve guessed it. I am not going to allow you to go out of my life. Do you understand that? I do not believe that you are a thief and—”

===Chinese===

“可你往后打算怎么办？”他追问道，“顶着这么一桩可怕的罪名，你拿什么过活？”

“有那么可怕么？”她冷冷地问。她正往站口里走，他一把攥住她的胳膊，带着近乎凶蛮的狠劲把她整个人抡了转来。

“这回你听我说，塔莉娅，”他咬着牙迸出话来，“我爱你，我要娶你。这话我从前没有说过，可你早就猜着了。我不容你就这样走出我的生命。你明不明白？我不信你是个贼，还有——”

===Original===

Very gently she disengaged his grip.

“Mr. Beardmore,” she said in a low voice, “you are just being quixotic and foolish! You have told me what you will not allow, and I tell you that I am not going to allow you to ruin your life through your infatuation for a convicted thief. You know nothing of me except that I am a seemingly nice girl whom you met by accident in the country, and it is my duty to be your mother and your maiden aunt.” There was a glint of amusement in her eye as she took his offered hand. “Some day perhaps we shall meet again, and by that time the glamour of romance will have worn off. Goodbye.”

She had disappeared into the booking hall before he could find his voice.

===Chinese===

她轻轻地挣脱了他的钳制。

“比德莫尔先生，”她低声说，“你这是在逞堂吉诃德式的痴侠之气，又傻又蠢！你说了你不容许什么，我也告诉你，我不容许你为痴迷一个定了罪的窃贼而毁掉自己的一生。你对我的全部所知，不过是乡间萍水相逢、遇上个看似乖巧的姑娘罢了；论本分，我该当你的母亲、你的未婚姑母。”她握住他伸来的手，眼中闪过一丝笑谑。“或许有朝一日你我还会重逢，到那时，这层浪漫的光晕早该磨蚀尽了。再见。”

不等他找回自己的声音，她的人影已没入售票大厅。

## X / 十

===Original===
The Summons of the Crimson Circle

===Chinese===
红圈的召唤

===Original===
Thalia Drummond went back to the lodging she had occupied before she had entered Mr. Harvey Froyant’s service as resident secretary, and apparently the story of her ill-deeds had preceded her, for the stout landlady gave her a chilly welcome, and had she not continued to pay the rent of her one room during the time she was working for Froyant, it was probable that she would not have been admitted.

It was a small room, neatly if plainly furnished, and oblivious to the landlady’s glum face and cold reception, she went to her apartment and locked the door behind her. She had spent a very unpleasant week, for she had been remanded in custody, and her very clothes seemed to exhale the musty odour of Holloway Gaol. Holloway, however, had an advantage which No. 14, Lexington Street, did not possess. It had an admirable system of bathrooms, for which the girl was truly grateful as she began to change.

She had plenty to occupy her mind. Harvey Froyant﻿ ﻿… Jack Beardmore﻿ ﻿… she frowned as though at a distasteful thought, and tried to dismiss him from her mind. It was a relief to go back to Froyant. She almost hated him. She certainly despised him. The time she had spent in his house had been the most wretched period in her life. She had taken her meals with the servants and had been conscious that every scrap of food she ate had been measured and weighed and duly apportioned by a man whose cheque for seven figures would have been honoured.

===Chinese===
塔莉娅·德拉蒙德回到了她进哈维·弗鲁瓦扬先生家当住家秘书之前住的那处寄宿屋。她的种种劣迹显然早已抢先一步传到了这里，因为那位胖房东太太给她的迎接冷冰冰的——若不是她在弗鲁瓦扬家做工期间一直照付那间单人房的房租，人家多半根本不会放她进门。

那是个小房间，陈设虽简朴，倒也收拾得干净整齐。她对房东太太的阴沉脸色和冷淡接待浑不在意，径自回到自己的房间，反手锁上了门。她刚熬过极不痛快的一个星期——一直被还押在监，连身上的衣服都仿佛散发出霍洛韦监狱（Holloway Gaol）的霉味。不过霍洛韦倒有一条长处，是莱克星顿街（Lexington Street）14 号比不了的：那里有一套顶呱呱的浴室设备，姑娘开始更衣时，心里着实为此感恩。

她心里要操心的事多着呢。哈维·弗鲁瓦扬……杰克·比德莫尔……她皱起眉头，像是想到了什么厌烦的事，竭力把后一个人从脑海中赶出去。回到弗鲁瓦扬那里，倒不失为一种解脱。她几乎恨他。她确实看不起他。在他府上度过的那段日子，是她一生中最凄惨的时光。她跟仆役们同桌用饭，心里明白，自己吃进的每一口饭食，都被一个男人量过、称过、按份分派得清清楚楚——而这个人开的七位数支票，走到哪家银行都照付不误。

===Original===
“At least, he didn’t make love to you, my dear,” she said to herself, and smiled. Somehow she couldn’t imagine Harvey Froyant making love to anybody.
