Instead she had produced a small, black, egg-shaped thing, and with a flick of her left hand had pulled out a small pin and dropped the pin on to the table. He knew what it was﻿—he had dabbled in army supplies and had seen many Mills bombs.

“Put it down﻿—no, no, put the pin in, you young fool!” he whimpered.

===Chinese===
“这就对了，亲爱的，你这才有我心目中小姑娘的样——那是什么？”

后半句成了恐惧的尖叫。

她慢慢踱到桌边，拿起了那只锦缎手袋。他一直在看着她，只当她在找手帕。谁知她掏出的是一件小小的、黑色的、蛋一样的东西，左手轻快地一拨，拔出一根小销针，把销针丢在桌上。他认得那是什么——他插手过军需生意，米尔斯手榴弹（Mills bomb）见得多了。

“放下——不，不，把销针插回去，你个年轻的小傻瓜！”他带着哭腔喊道。

===Original===
“Don’t worry,” she said coolly. “I have a spare pin in my bag﻿—open that door!”

His hand shook like a man with palsy as he fumbled at the keyhole. Then he turned and blinked at her.

“A Mills bomb!” he mumbled, and fell back an obese mass of quivering flesh against the delicate panelling.

Slowly she nodded.

“A Mills bomb,” she said softly, and went out, still gripping the lever of the deadly egg-like thing. He followed her to the door and slammed it after her, then went shakily up the stairs to his bedroom.

===Chinese===
“别担心，”她冷冷地说，“我包里还备着一根销针呢——把门打开！”

他的手抖得像害了麻痹症的人一般，摸索着去开锁眼。然后他转过身来，朝她直眨眼。

“米尔斯手榴弹！”他咕哝一声，一团肥硕哆嗦的肉瘫靠在精致的护墙板上。

她缓缓地点了点头。

“是米尔斯手榴弹。”她轻声说，随即出了门，手里仍攥着那致命的蛋形物上的握杆。他跟到门边，在她身后砰地摔上门，然后颤颤巍巍地上了楼，进了自己的卧室。

---

===Original===
“Flush” Barnet, standing in the shadow of a clothespress, heard the click of locks and the snap of a bolt as Mr. Marl entered his room.

The house was still. Through the thick door of Mr. Marl’s bedroom no sound came. There was no transom to the door, and the only evidence that there was somebody in his room was afforded by a fret of light in the ceiling of the passage, which came through a ventilator in the wall of the bedroom.

During the war this house had been used as an officers’ convalescent home, and certain hygienic arrangements had been introduced, which were more useful than beautiful.

“Flush” crept softly in his stockinged feet to the door and listened. He thought he heard the man talking to himself and looked around for some means by which he could obtain a view of the room. There was a small oaken table in the corridor and he placed this against the wall and mounted. His eyes came to the level of the ventilator and he looked down upon Mr. Marl pacing the room in his shirtsleeves, obviously disturbed. Then “Flush” Barnet heard a sound. Just a faint “hush-hush” of feet on a carpet, and he slipped down, walked quickly along the corridor, passing the head of the stairs.

===Chinese===
马尔先生进屋时那几声上锁的咔哒和门闩的脆响，“弗拉什”·巴尼特在大衣柜的阴影里听得真切。

宅子里一片寂静。马尔先生卧室厚厚的门内没有一丝声息。门上没有气窗，屋里有人唯一的确证，是走廊天花板上的一片细碎光纹——光是从卧室墙上的通风口透出来的。

大战期间，这座宅子曾改作军官疗养院，添置了一些卫生设施——实用归实用，美观却谈不上。

“弗拉什”穿着袜子蹑手蹑脚地凑到门边听了听。他仿佛听见那人在自言自语，便四下张望，想找个法子看一眼屋里的情形。走廊里有一张小橡木桌，他把它挪到墙边，踩了上去。眼睛刚够到通风口，他便望见马尔先生只穿着衬衫在屋里来回踱步，分明心绪不宁。接着“弗拉什”·巴尼特听见一点声响——只是地毯上一阵轻微的“沙沙”脚步声，他随即溜下桌子，快步沿走廊走去，走过了楼梯口。

===Original===
The hall below was in darkness, but he felt rather than saw a figure on the stairway. Whether it was man or woman he could not say, and did not stop to discover. It might be one of the servants returning furtively﻿—servants did not always stay away when they were bidden. “Flush” passed to the farther end of the corridor and from an angle in the wall watched. He saw nobody pass the head of the stairs, but there was no background. After a while he crept back again. There was nothing to be gained by forcing the door of Marl’s bedroom, even if it were possible. He had had time to inspect the house at his leisure, and he had already decided upon investigating the little safe in the library, for Mr. Marl’s own room had drawn blank.

The “investigation,” which took two hours and the employment of one of the best sets of tools in the profession, was not unprofitable. But it did not reveal the huge sum of money which he anticipated. He hesitated. The night was too far through to make an attempt on the bedroom, even if he had not already searched it from wall to wall. He folded his kit and slipped it into one pocket, his loot into another, and went upstairs again. There was no sound from Marl’s room, but the light was still on. He tried to look through the keyhole, but the key was still there. The only inducement there was for him to enter the room was the possibility that the money was in the man’s clothes. This likelihood was remote, he thought. Possibly Marl had taken it to some safe deposit﻿—a contingency which Barnet had foreseen.

He went slowly down the stairs, through the hall and the butler’s pantry to the side door, where he had left his boots, his overcoat and his shiny silk hat, for he was in evening dress. Then he stole softly forth along the covered passageway running by the side of the house. Here a door opened into the little forecourt of Marl’s house. He reached the garden and his hand was on the gate when somebody touched him and he spun round.

===Chinese===
下面的大厅一片漆黑，楼梯上有个人影，他与其说是看见，不如说是感觉到的。是男是女他说不上来，也没停下探究。也许是哪个仆人悄悄溜了回来——仆人被吩咐回避时，并不见得就当真躲开。“弗拉什”走到走廊另一头，从一处墙角里守望着。他没看见有人经过楼梯口，不过那边一片死黑，没有衬底可辨。过了一阵，他又蹑手蹑脚地折返。硬撬马尔的卧室门毫无益处，何况也未必撬得开。他早有工夫从容踏看这座宅子，已打定主意去查书房里那只小保险柜，因为马尔自己的房间他已经搜过，一无所获。

这番“搜检”花去两个钟头，还动用了本行当里数一数二的一套工具，倒也并非全无收获。只是他料想中的那笔巨款并未现身。他犹豫了。夜已经太深，即使他先前没有把那间卧室从墙到墙搜个遍，如今也来不及再打它的主意。他把工具折好塞进一只口袋，赃物塞进另一只，又上了楼。马尔房里没有动静，灯却还亮着。他想从锁眼里看一眼，钥匙却还插在里头。唯一诱使他进屋的，无非是钱也许就在那人身上这件衣裳里的可能。他想，这种可能性微乎其微。没准马尔早把钱存进了哪家保管库——这种变数，巴尼特是早料到了的。

他慢慢下楼，穿过大厅和管家的配膳室，来到边门——他的靴子、大衣和锃亮的大礼帽都留在那儿，因为他穿的是夜礼服。随后他放轻脚步，顺着宅子侧面带顶的廊道溜了出去。廊道尽头一扇门通向马尔家的小前院。他走到园子里，手刚搭上园门，忽觉有人碰了他一下，猛地转过身去。

===Original===
“I want you ‘Flush,’ ” said a well-remembered voice. “Inspector Parr. You may remember me?”

“Parr!” gasped the bewildered Barnet, and with an oath wrenched himself free and leapt through the gate, but the three policemen who were waiting for him were not so easy to dispose of, and they marched “Flush” Barnet to the nearest police station, a worried man.

In the meantime Parr conducted a search of his own. Accompanied by a detective he made his way to the hall of the house and up the stairs.

===Chinese===
“我要拿的是你，‘弗拉什’。”一个记忆犹新的声音说，“帕尔探长。还记得我不？”

“帕尔！”慌了神的巴尼特倒抽一口气，咒骂一声挣脱身子，纵身窜出园门；可等在那儿的三名警察就不那么好打发了，他们把“弗拉什”·巴尼特押往最近的警察局——落网的人满腹愁烦。

与此同时，帕尔自己也进行了一番搜查。他带着一名警探进了宅中的门厅，上了楼。

===Original===
“This is the only room occupied apparently,” he said, and knocked at the door.

There was no reply.

“Go along and see if you can rouse any of the servants,” said Parr.

The man came back with the startling information that there were no servants in the house.

===Chinese===
“看样子只有这间屋有人住。”他说着敲了敲门。

无人应门。

“去瞧瞧，能不能叫醒哪个仆人。”帕尔说。

那人回来时带回一个惊人的消息：宅子里根本没有仆人。

===Original===
“There’s somebody here,” said the old inspector, and flashing his lamp along the corridor he saw the table, and with an agility remarkable in one of his age, he leapt up and peered through the ventilator.

“I can just see somebody asleep,” he said. “Hi! Wake up!” he called, but there was no reply.

Hammering on the door did not produce any response.

“Go down and see if you can find a hatchet, we’ll break open the door,” said Parr. “I don’t like this.”

===Chinese===
“这里头有人。”老探长说着，手电沿走廊一扫，看见了那张桌子；随即以他这把年纪罕见的敏捷一跃而上，凑着通风口往里瞧。

“我隐约看见有个人睡着呢。”他说，“喂！醒醒！”他喊道，却无人应声。

捶门也捶不出半点回应。

“下去看看能不能找把斧头来，我们把门劈开。”帕尔说，“这光景我不喜欢。”

===Original===
Hatchet there was none, but they found a hammer.

“Can you show a light, Mr. Parr?” asked the man, and the inspector flashed his lamp on the door. It was a white door﻿—white except for the Crimson Circle affixed to a panel as by a rubber stamp.

“Break in the door,” said Parr, breathing heavily.

For five minutes they smashed at a panel before they finally hammered it through, and the sleeper within gave no sign of consciousness.

Parr reached his hand through the door, turned the key and, by dint of stretching, found the bolt at the top. He slipped into the room. The light was still burning and its rays fell across the man on the bed, who lay upon his back, a twisted smile on his face, most obviously dead.

===Chinese===
斧头没有，倒寻着一把锤子。

“帕尔先生，能给照个亮吗？”那人问，探长便把手电照向门上。那是一扇白门——只是在一块门板上，仿佛拿橡皮图章盖上去似的，印着一个猩红的红圈。

“把门砸开。”帕尔说，呼吸粗重。

他们对着一块门板连砸了五分钟才砸穿，屋里那个睡着的人始终不见一丝知觉。

帕尔把手从门洞里伸进去，拧开锁，又费力地伸长胳膊，够到了顶上的门闩。他闪身进屋。灯还亮着，灯光正照在床上那人身上——他仰面躺着，脸上挂着一丝扭曲的笑，分明已经死了。

## XVII / 十七

===Original===

The Blower of Bubbles

===Chinese===

吹泡泡的人

===Original===

It was long after midnight and Derrick Yale was sitting in his pretty little study﻿—he lived in a flat overlooking the park﻿—when the knock came to the door and he rose to admit Inspector Parr.

Parr related the incident of the evening.

“But why didn’t you tell me?” asked Derrick a little reproachfully, and then laughed. “I’m sorry,” he said. “I always seem to be butting in on your affairs. But how came the murderer to escape? You say you had had the house surrounded for two hours. Did the girl come out?”

“Undoubtedly; she came out and drove home.”

“And nobody else went in?”

“I wouldn’t like to swear that,” said Parr. “Whoever was in the house had probably arrived long before Marl returned from the theatre. I have since discovered that there was a way out through the garage at the back of the house. When I said the house was surrounded that was an exaggeration. There was a way through the back garden which I did not know. I didn’t even suspect there were gardens there. Undoubtedly he went through the garage door.”

===Chinese===

午夜过后已久，德里克·耶尔还坐在他那间精致的小书房里——他住的是一套俯瞰公园的公寓——这时门上响起敲声，他起身开门，迎进来的是帕尔探长。

帕尔把当晚的事讲了一遍。

“可你怎么不告诉我一声呢？”德里克略带埋怨地问，随即又笑了。“对不住，”他说，“我总像是要插一手，管你们的事。不过那凶手是怎么逃脱的？你说那宅子已被你们围了两个钟头。姑娘出来了吗？”

“出来了，这没有疑问；她出来后坐车回了家。”

“再没有别的人进去了？”

“这个我可不敢起誓。”帕尔说，“不管当时屋里的是谁，多半早在马尔从戏院回来之前就到了。我事后查明，宅子后头的车库有一条出路。我说宅子被围住了，那是夸张的说法。后花园里有一条道，我原先并不知道，我连那边有园子都没疑心到。毫无疑问，他是从车库那道门走的。”

===Original===

“Do you suspect the girl at all?”

Parr shook his head.

“But why were you surrounding Marl’s house at all?” asked Derrick Yale seriously.

The answer was as unexpected as it was sensational.

“Because Marl has been under police observation ever since he came back to London,” said Parr. “In fact, ever since I discovered that he was the man who wrote the letter, the scrap of which I found and which I compared last week with his writing﻿—I asked him for the address of his tailor.”

===Chinese===

“你到底疑不疑那姑娘？”

帕尔摇了摇头。

“可你们究竟为什么要围马尔的宅子呢？”德里克·耶尔正色问道。

这答案既出人意料，又耸人听闻。

“因为马尔自打回到伦敦，就一直处在警方监视之下。”帕尔说，“确切地说，打我发现写那封信的人就是他起，便已如此——那封信的残片是我寻着的，上星期我又把它同他的笔迹比对过了——我向他讨过他裁缝铺的地址。”

===Original===

“Marl?” said the other incredulously.

Inspector Parr nodded.

“I don’t know what there was between old man Beardmore and Marl, or what brought him to the house. I’ve been trying to reconstruct the scene. You may remember that when Marl came to the house on a visit he was suddenly seized with a panic.”

“I remember,” nodded Yale. “Jack Beardmore told me about it. Well?”

“He refused to stay at the house, said he was going back to London,” said Parr. “As a matter of fact, he went no farther than Kingside, which is a station some eight or nine miles away. He sent his bag on to London and came back by road. He was probably the person whom the murderer saw in the wood that night. Now why had he come back if he was so scared that he ran away in the first place? And why did he write that letter for delivery in the night when he had every opportunity to tell James Beardmore by day, when he was with him?”

===Chinese===

“马尔？”对方满腹狐疑地说。

帕尔探长点了点头。

“老比德莫尔同马尔之间有过什么瓜葛，又是什么把他引到那座宅子里去的，我都不清楚。我一门心思想把那幕情景重新拼凑出来。你或许记得，马尔上门做客那回，曾陡然惊慌失措起来。”

“我记得。”耶尔点头道，“杰克·比德莫尔同我讲过。后来呢？”

“他死活不肯在宅子里留宿，说是要回伦敦去。”帕尔说，“实际上，他只走到金赛德（Kingside）为止——那是个八九英里外的火车站。他把行李先打发去伦敦，自己又从大路上折了回来。那天夜里凶手在树林里瞧见的人，多半就是他。这就怪了：他既然怕成那样，头一回都吓跑了，干吗还要回来？再者，他白日里同詹姆斯·比德莫尔厮守在一处，有的是机会当面讲，何苦写那封信、专等夜里投递？”

===Original===

There was a long silence.

“How was Marl killed?” asked Yale.

The other shook his head.

“That is a mystery to me. The murderer could not possibly have entered the room. I had an interview with ‘Flush’ Barnet﻿—as yet he knows nothing about the murder﻿—and he admits he broke in for the purpose of burglary. He says he heard the sound of somebody moving about the house, and very naturally hid himself. He also says he heard a strange hissing sound, like air escaping from a pipe. Another remarkable clue was a round wet patch on the pillow, within a few inches of the dead man’s hand. It was exactly circular. At first I thought it was a symbol of the Crimson Circle, until I discovered another patch on the counterpane. The doctor has not been able to diagnose the cause of death, but the motive is clear. According to his banker﻿—I’ve just been talking to Brabazon on the telephone﻿—he drew a large sum of money from the bank yesterday. In fact, Brabazon closed his account. They had a quarrel over something or other. The safe was of course opened by ‘Flush’ Barnet, but there was no money found on him when he was searched at the police station. Curiously enough, we did discover several little oddments that ‘Flush’ had picked up﻿—now, who took the money?”

===Chinese===

长长一阵沉默。

“马尔是怎么被杀的？”耶尔问。

对方摇了摇头。

“这在我也是个谜。凶手根本不可能进过那间屋子。我提审了‘弗拉什’·巴尼特——那桩凶案他还一无所知——他承认自己是闯进去行窃的。他说听见宅子里有人走动的声响，便很自然地躲了起来。他还说听见一种奇怪的嘶嘶声，像管子里漏气。另一条惹眼的线索，是枕头上有一块圆形湿痕，离死者的手不过几英寸，端端正正一个圆。起初我只当它是红圈的记号，后来发现床罩上另有一块湿痕，才断了这念头。医生至今诊断不出死因，作案的动机却清清楚楚。据他的银行家说——我刚在电话上同布拉巴宗谈过——他昨天从银行提取了一大笔钱。说白了，是布拉巴宗把他的账户给销了。两人为着什么事口角过一场。保险柜自然是‘弗拉什’·巴尼特撬的，可他在警局受搜身时，身上并没搜出钱来。说来稀奇，我们倒真搜出了几件‘弗拉什’顺手摸走的小零碎——那么，那笔钱是谁拿的呢？”

===Original===

Derrick Yale paced the floor, his hands behind him, his chin on his breast.

“Do you know anything of Brabazon?” he asked.

The other did not reply immediately.

“Only that he is a banker and does a lot of foreign work.”

“Is he solvent?” asked Derrick Yale bluntly, and the inspector raised his dull eyes slowly until they were on a level with the other’s.

===Chinese===

德里克·耶尔在屋里踱来踱去，双手背后，下巴抵在胸前。

“布拉巴宗这个人，你有所闻吗？”他问。

对方没有立时作答。

“只知道他是个银行家，外国方面的业务做得很不少。”

“他手头殷实么？”德里克·耶尔直截了当地问。探长缓缓抬起那双无神的眼睛，直到与对方平视为止。

===Original===

“No,” he said, “and I don’t mind telling you that we’ve had one or two complaints about his methods.”

“Were they good friends﻿—Marl and Brabazon?”

“Fairly good,” was the hesitating reply. “The impression I have from reports is that Marl had some hold over Brabazon.”

“And Brabazon was insolvent,” mused Derrick Yale. “And this afternoon Marl closes his account. In what circumstances? Did he come to the bank?”

Briefly the detective explained what had happened. It seemed that there was precious little that did happen at Brabazon’s bank that he did not know.

===Chinese===

“不殷实。”他说，“我也不妨对你明言，他那套做派，我们接过一两回告状。”

“他们交情好吗——马尔和布拉巴宗？”

“还算要好。”回答得有些迟疑，“据各项报告给我的印象，马尔捏着布拉巴宗什么把柄。”

“而布拉巴宗已经周转不灵，”德里克·耶尔沉吟道，“今天下午马尔就销了户。是在什么情形下销的？他亲自到银行来了吗？”

侦探简略讲了事情经过。看来布拉巴宗银行里发生的事，几乎没有一件是他不知道的。

===Original===

Derrick Yale was beginning to respect this man, whom at first he had regarded, with a good-natured scorn, as a little stupid.

“I wonder if it would be possible for me to go to Marl’s house tonight?”

“I came to suggest that,” said the other. “In fact, I kept a cab waiting at the door with that idea.”

Derrick Yale did not speak during the journey to Bayswater, and it was not until he stood in the hall of the house in Marisburg Place that he broke the silence.

===Chinese===

德里克·耶尔起初带着好脾气的轻蔑，只当他有些愚钝，如今却渐渐对这人生出敬意来。

“不知道今晚我能不能上马尔的宅子去看看？”

“我就是来提议这个的。”对方说，“实话讲，我在门口留着一辆出租汽车，正是打的这个主意。”

去贝斯沃特的一路，德里克·耶尔一言不发，直到站定在马里斯堡广场那座宅子的门厅里，他才开了口。

===Original===

“We ought to find a small steel cylinder somewhere,” he said slowly.

The policeman standing on duty in the hall came forward and saluted the inspector.

“We found an iron bottle in the garage, sir?” he said.

“Ah!” cried Derrick Yale triumphantly. “I thought so!”

He almost ran up the stairs ahead of the detective and paused in the passage, which was now lighted. The little oak table stood against the ventilator and toward that he moved. Then he went down on his hands and knees and sniffed the carpet. Presently he choked and coughed and got up, red in the face.

===Chinese===

“我们该能在什么地方找到一只小钢瓶。”他慢悠悠地说。

守在门厅里值勤的警察上前一步，向探长敬礼。

“我们在车库里寻着了一只铁瓶，长官。”他说。

“啊！”德里克·耶尔得意地叫出声来，“我料定了！”

他抢在侦探前头，几乎是跑着上了楼梯，在过道里停住——过道如今已掌了灯。那张小橡木桌还抵着通风口，他便朝那边走去。随即他手脚着地趴下去，对着地毯嗅了又嗅。不一时他被呛得咳嗽起来，忙爬起身，满脸通红。

===Original===

“Let me see that cylinder,” he said.

They brought it to him. The policeman’s description of it as a bottle was nearer the truth. It was an iron bottle, at the end of which was a small pipe to which was attached a tiny turnkey.

“And now there ought to be a cup somewhere,” he said, looking round, “unless he brought it in a bottle.”

“There was a small glass bottle in the garage near this, sir,” said the policeman who had found it, “it is broken, though.”

“Bring it to me quickly,” said Yale. “And I can only hope that it isn’t so completely smashed that none of its contents are left.”

===Chinese===

“把那只钢瓶给我瞧瞧。”他说。

他们把它抬了来。那警察管它叫瓶子，倒更贴近实情。那是只铁瓶，末端接一根细管，管上装着一个小小的旋钮。

“这会儿该还有个杯子在什么地方，”他环顾四周说，“除非他是连瓶子一块儿带来的。”

“车库里在这东西旁边还有一只小玻璃瓶，长官。”发现它的那个警察说，“只是已经碎了。”

“快给我拿来。”耶尔说，“我只盼它没有碎得那么彻底，里头的东西竟一点儿不剩。”

===Original===

The stout Mr. Parr was regarding him sombrely.

“What is all this about?” he asked, and Derrick Yale chuckled.

“A new way of committing a murder, my dear Mr. Parr,” he said airily, “now let us go into the room.”

The body of Marl lay on the bed covered by a sheet and the circular patch of wet on the pillow had not dried. The windows were open and a fitful wind kept the curtains fluttering.

===Chinese===

发福的帕尔先生正阴沉沉地打量着他。

“这到底是怎么一回事？”他问。德里克·耶尔只是低低地笑。

“一种新鲜杀人的路数，我亲爱的帕尔先生。”他轻飘飘地说，“走，咱们进屋里去。”

马尔的尸身蒙着床单躺在床上，枕头上的圆形湿痕还没有干。窗子敞着，一阵一阵的风搅得窗帘不住飘动。

===Original===

“Of course you can’t smell it here,” said Yale speaking to himself, and again went on his knees and nosed the carpet. And again he coughed and rose hurriedly.

By this time they had returned with the lower half of a glass bottle. It contained a few drops of liquid, and this Yale poured into his hand.

“Soap and water,” he said; “I thought it would be. And now I’ll explain how Marl was killed. Your thief, ‘Flush’ Barnet, heard a hissing sound. It was the sound of a heavy gas escaping from this cylinder. I may be wrong, but I should imagine there is enough poison gas in that little iron bottle to settle your account and mine. It is still lying on the floor, by the way. It is one of those heavy gases which descend.”

===Chinese===

“在这儿自然是闻不出的。”耶尔自言自语地说，随即又跪下去，用鼻子拱了拱地毯。于是又是一阵咳嗽，他慌忙起身。

这时他们已拿着半截玻璃瓶的下段回来了。里头还剩几滴液体，耶尔把它倒进手心。

“肥皂水。”他说，“我料定就是它。现在我来讲解马尔是怎么被杀的。你那位窃贼，‘弗拉什’·巴尼特，听见的是嘶嘶的响声——那是重质的气体从这只瓶里泄出来的声音。我也许猜错，不过我想，那只小铁瓶里的毒气，足够把你的账和我的账一并结清。顺便说一句，它此刻还躺在地板上呢。它属于那种分量重、往下沉的气体。”

===Original===

“But how did it kill Marl? Did they pump it through the grating on to his head?”

Derrick Yale shook his head.

“It is a much simpler and a much more deadly method which the Crimson Circle employed,” he said quietly. “They blew bubbles.”

“Bubbles!”

Derrick Yale nodded.

===Chinese===

“可它怎么杀得了马尔？莫非他们把它从格栅里打上去，罩到他脑袋上？”

德里克·耶尔摇了摇头。

“红圈用的这个法子，简单得多，也毒辣得多。”他平静地说，“他们吹的是泡泡。”

“泡泡！”

德里克·耶尔点了点头。

===Original===

“The end of this cylinder﻿—you can still feel the slime of the soap upon it﻿—was first dipped into the soap solution, then thrust through the grating. The tap was turned down and a bubble formed, which was shaken off. From the ventilator,” he ran outside and jumped on to the table, “yes, I thought so,” he said, “he could see Marl’s head. Two or three of the bubbles must have been failures. One struck the pillow, but I should imagine that that was blown after his death; one struck the wall, you will find the wet patch, but one, and probably more, burst on his face. He must have been killed almost instantaneously.”

Parr could only gape.

“I thought it all out on the way here. The circular patch on the pillow reminded me of my own boyish exploits and their disastrous effect when I started blowing bubbles in the bedroom. And then when you mentioned the ventilator and the hissing noise, I was perfectly certain that my theory was right.”

“But we smelt no gas when we came into the room,” said Parr.

“The wind may have blown away the fumes,” said Derrick Yale. “But apart from that, the weight of the gas would send it to the floor, and by its own density it would spread evenly﻿—look!” He struck a match, shielded it for a moment until it caught light, and then slowly brought it down to the floor level. An inch from the carpet the match was suddenly extinguished.

===Chinese===

“这只瓶的瓶口——那上头肥皂的滑腻到如今还摸得出来——先在肥皂水里蘸了一蘸，然后从格栅中间捅了进去。旋塞一拧开，便鼓起一个泡泡，再一抖，把它抖落下来。从通风口这儿——”他跑到外面，跳上桌子，“不错，我料到了，”他说，“从这儿望得见马尔的脑袋。想必有两三个泡泡吹砸了。一个落在枕头上，不过我揣想那是他断气之后补吹的；一个碰在墙上，那块湿痕你们去找，准能找到；可有一个——多半不止一个——在他脸上炸开了。他准是几乎顷刻之间便断了气。”

帕尔只能张着嘴干瞪眼。

“这一套我是在来的路上想通的。枕头上那块圆形湿痕，叫我想起自己少年时代的壮举，以及当年我在卧室里吹起泡泡时那灾难性的后果。后来你又提起通风口和那阵嘶嘶声，我便十拿十稳，我这套理论是对的了。”

“可我们进屋的时候，并没有闻见毒气。”帕尔说。

“风也许把毒气吹散了。”德里克·耶尔说，“可撇开这不谈，这气体的分量自会把它们压向地板，又凭着自身的浓度四下摊匀——瞧！”他划着一根火柴，拢起手掌挡了片刻，等它燃稳，然后缓缓往地板的高度放下去。离地毯还有一英寸，火柴忽地灭了。

===Original===

“I see,” said Inspector Parr.

“Now what about searching the place? Perhaps I can be of use,” suggested Yale, but his offer of help did not meet with any very gracious response.

A small police audience, which had listened awestricken whilst Yale had developed his theory, could understand the inspector’s feelings. Apparently Yale did, too, for with a good-humoured laugh he made his excuses and went home. There are moments when the headquarters police should be left alone with their own emotions. Nobody realised this more than Derrick Yale.


===Chinese===

“我明白了。”帕尔探长说。

“现在是不是该搜查搜查这地方了？兴许我还能搭把手。”耶尔提议道，可他这番好意，得到的回应却算不上客气。

一小圈当听众的警察，方才听耶尔铺陈他的理论，听得又敬又怕，此刻倒都能体谅探长的心境。看样子耶尔自己也明白，因为他好脾气地笑了笑，道个不是，便回家去了。警察总部的人自有那样的时刻，该让他独个儿同自己的情绪待在一处。这个道理，没有谁比德里克·耶尔参得更透。

## XVIII / 十八

===Original===
“Flush” Barnet’s Story

===Chinese===
“弗拉什”·巴尼特的故事

===Original===
Inspector Parr, after a further search, proceeded to the nearest police station to interview Mr. “Flush” Barnet.

“Flush,” a depressed and weary man, had no illuminating information to give.

The proceeds of his robbery lay upon the station-sergeant’s table, a miscellaneous collection of rings and watches, a perfectly valueless bankbook﻿—valueless to “Flush,” at any rate﻿—and a silver flask. But the most surprising circumstance was that in “Flush” Barnet’s pocket were two brand new banknotes for a hundred pounds, which he insisted stoutly were his own property.

Now burglars, and particularly the type of burglar that “Flush” Barnet was, are notoriously improvident people. They do not work whilst they have money, and with two hundred pounds in his possession, it is certain that “Flush” Barnet would not have attempted to break into Marisburg Place.

===Chinese===
帕尔探长又搜检了一通，随后前往最近的警察局，去讯问“弗拉什”·巴尼特先生。

“弗拉什”垂头丧气、疲惫不堪，问不出什么有价值的口供。

他行窃的所得摊在警局警长的桌上：戒指、怀表乱七八糟一堆，一本毫无价值的存折——至少对“弗拉什”来说毫无价值——还有一只银酒壶。但最出人意料的是，“弗拉什”·巴尼特口袋里竟搜出两张崭新的百镑钞票，而他还一口咬定那是他自己的财物。

须知窃贼——尤其是“弗拉什”·巴尼特这一路窃贼——出了名地不知储蓄为何物。手里有钱，他们便不干活；身上揣着两百镑，“弗拉什”·巴尼特是断然不会去撬马里斯堡广场的宅子的。

===Original===
“They’re my own, I tell you, Mr. Parr,” he protested, “would I tell you a lie?”

“Of course you would,” said Inspector Parr without heat. “If they are your own, where did you get them?”

“They were given to me by a friend.”

“Why did you light a fire in the library?” asked Parr unexpectedly, and “Flush” Barnet started.

“Because I was cold,” he said after a pause.

“H’m,” said Inspector Parr, and then as though speaking his thoughts aloud, “he has two hundred of his own, he breaks into a house, he burgles a safe and lights a fire. Now, why did he light the fire? Why did he light the fire? To burn something he’d found in the safe!”

===Chinese===
“我告诉您，那是我的东西，帕尔先生，”他抗议道，“我会跟您说谎吗？”

“你当然会。”帕尔探长不愠不火地说，“既然是你自己的，你是从哪儿弄来的？”

“是一位朋友给我的。”

“你干吗在书房里生火？”帕尔冷不丁地问，“弗拉什”·巴尼特吓了一跳。

“因为我冷。”他顿了顿才说。

“哼。”帕尔探长应了一声，随即像是把心里想的说出声来：“他自己有两百镑，还去撬门进宅，撬开保险柜，又生起火来。我问你，他干吗生火？他干吗生火？为的是烧掉他从保险柜里翻出来的东西！”

===Original===
“Flush” Barnet listened without offering any comment, but he was visibly distressed.

“Therefore,” said Parr, “you were paid to break into Marl’s house and you got two hundred for pinching something from his safe and burning it. Am I right?”

“If I died this moment﻿—” began “Flush” Barnet.

“You’d go to hell,” said the inspector dispassionately, “where all liars go. Who is your pal, Barnet? You’d better tell me, because I’m in two minds whether I shall charge you with the murder﻿—”

“Murder!” almost screamed “Flush” Barnet, as he sprang to his feet. “What do you mean? I haven’t committed a murder!”

“Marl’s dead, that’s all; found dead in his bed.”

===Chinese===
“弗拉什”·巴尼特听着，一声不吭，可那份坐立不安明摆着瞒不了人。

“这么说，”帕尔道，“你是收了钱才去撬马尔的宅子，从他的保险柜里偷出东西烧掉，得了两百镑。我说得对不对？”

“我要是这会儿就死——”“弗拉什”·巴尼特刚起了个头。

“你就下地狱去，”探长不动声色地说，“撒谎的人都上那儿。你的同伙是谁，巴尼特？你最好说出来，要不要以凶杀罪控你，我正拿不定主意呢——”

“凶杀！”“弗拉什”·巴尼特几乎尖叫起来，一跃而起，“你这是什么意思？我没杀过人！”

“马尔死了，就这么回事；人死在自家床上。”

===Original===
He left the prisoner in a state of mental prostration, and when he returned in the early hours of the morning to renew his inquisition, “Flush” Barnet told him all.

“I don’t know anything about Crimson Circles, Mr. Parr,” he said, “but this is the truth.”

He added a pious wish that Providence would deal hardly with him if he departed from veracity.

“I’m keeping company with a young lady at Brabazon’s bank. One night when she was working late, I was waiting for her when a gentleman came out of the side entrance of the bank and called me. I was surprised to hear him mention my name, and I nearly dropped dead when I saw his face.”

“It was Mr. Brabazon?” suggested Parr.

“That’s right, sir. He asked me into his private office. I thought he’d got something against Milly.”

===Chinese===
他把犯人撂在那儿，任他精神崩溃；等到凌晨时分他回来重新盘问，“弗拉什”·巴尼特便一五一十全招了。

“红圈的事我一概不知，帕尔先生，”他说，“可我下面说的句句是实话。”

他还虔诚地祈愿：自己若有半句虚言，只求上天重重惩治他。

“我正跟布拉巴宗银行的一位年轻小姐处着对象。有天夜里她加班，我在外头等她，一位先生从银行侧门出来，叫住了我。他一开口就叫出我的名字，我已经够吃惊了，等看清他的脸，险些没背过气去。”

“那是布拉巴宗先生吧？”帕尔提示道。

“正是，长官。他把我让进他的私人办公室。我还当他掌握了米莉什么把柄呢。”

===Original===
“Go on,” said Parr, when the man paused.

“Well, I’ve got to save myself, haven’t I? And I suppose I’d better speak the whole truth. He told me that Marl was blackmailing him, and that Marl had some letters of his which he kept in his private safe, and offered me a thousand if I’d get them. That’s the truth. And then he gave me an idea that Marl kept a lot of money in the house. He didn’t exactly say so, but that is what he hinted. He knew I’d been inside for burglary, he’d made inquiries about me, and said that I was the right kind of man. Well, sir, I went round and took a squint at the place, and it seemed to me that it was a bit difficult. There were always men servants in the house, except when Mr. Marl was entertaining ladies to supper,” he grinned. “I’d have given up the job, only there’s a young lady in the office that Marl was sweet on.”

“Thalia Drummond?” suggested Parr.

===Chinese===
“接着说。”那人住口时，帕尔说道。

“唉，我得保全自己，不是么？所以我想，还是把实话全说了吧。他告诉我，马尔一直在敲诈他，马尔手里有他几封信，锁在他那只私人保险柜里；他出一千镑，只要我把信弄到手。这是实话。后来他又让我觉着，马尔家里存着一大笔钱。他没明说，可话里就是这个意思。他知道我因为盗窃蹲过号子，还打听过我的底细，说我正是他要找的合适人选。于是，长官，我就去转了一圈，把那地方瞄了一眼，瞅着有点难办。宅子里老是有些男仆，只有马尔先生款待小姐们吃夜宵的时候例外。”他咧嘴一笑。“要不是办公室里有位年轻小姐正得马尔的欢心，我早就把这活儿辞了。”

“塔莉娅·德拉蒙德？”帕尔提示道。

===Original===
“That’s right, sir,” nodded “Flush.” “It was what you might call an act of Providence, him being sweet on her, and when I found that he’d invited her to dinner, I thought that was a good opportunity to get in. It seemed money for nothing when I found out that he’d drawn his bank balance. I opened the safe﻿—that was easy﻿—and I found the envelope, but it had no papers, only a photograph of a man and a woman on a rock. I think it was a photograph of some place abroad, for there were lots of mountains in the background, and he seemed to be pushing her over and she was holding on to a bit of tree. Maybe it was one of those cinema pictures. Anyway, I burnt it.”

“I see,” said Inspector Parr. “And that is all?”

“That’s all, sir. I never found any money.”

===Chinese===
“正是，长官。”“弗拉什”点头道，“他偏生看上了她，这您可以说是天意；等我打听出他请了她去吃晚饭，我想那正是进宅的好机会。后来又发现他把银行里的存款都提光了，这买卖简直像白捡钱。我打开了保险柜——那不费事——找到了那只信封，可里面没有信，只有一张照片：一个男人和一个女人在一块山岩上。我瞧那像是在外国什么地方照的，因为背景里群山重重，那男人像是在把她往下推，她抓着一截树枝不放。没准是那种电影里的照片呢。反正，我把它烧了。”

“原来如此。”帕尔探长说，“就这些了？”

“就这些，长官。钱我压根儿没见着。”

---

===Original===
At seven o’clock, with a warrant in his pocket, and accompanied by two detectives, Inspector Parr made a call at the block of flats where Brabazon had his residence.

A servant in night attire opened the door to them and indicated the banker’s room. The door was locked, but Parr kicked it open without ceremony. The room, however, was empty. An open window and a fire escape suggested the method by which the eminent banker had made his getaway, and the fact that the bed had not been slept in and that there was no sign of disorder in the room, showed that he had gone hours before the detective’s arrival.

By the side of the bed there was a telephone, and Parr called the exchange.

“Can you find if any message came through to this number during the night?” he asked. “I am Inspector Parr, of police headquarters.”

“Two,” was the reply. “I put them through myself. One from Bayswater﻿—”

===Chinese===
七点钟，帕尔探长怀里揣着一张逮捕证，带着两名警探，登门造访了布拉巴宗居住的那座公寓楼。

一个身着睡衣的仆役给他们开了门，指了指那位银行家的房间。门锁着，帕尔却毫不客气，一脚踹开了。屋里却是空的。一扇敞开的窗子和一部太平梯，道出了这位显赫的银行家溜走的路数；而床铺无人睡过、屋里毫无凌乱之迹，可见他早在警探到来之前几个钟头就走了。

床边有一部电话，帕尔要通了电话局。

“能不能查一查，昨夜有没有电话接到这个号码上？”他问，“我是警察总部的帕尔探长。”

“两个。”对方答道，“都是我亲手接通的。一个是贝斯沃特打来的——”

===Original===
“That was mine,” said the Inspector. “What was the other?”

“From the Western Exchange﻿—at 2:30.”

“Thank you,” said the inspector grimly, and hung up the telephone.

He looked at his companions and rubbed his big nose irritably.

“Thalia Drummond is going to get another job,” he said.

===Chinese===
“那是我打的。”探长说，“另一个呢？”

“西区电话局（Western Exchange）打来的——两点半。”

“多谢。”探长狠声说道，挂上了电话。

他看了看两个同伴，烦躁地揉了揉自己的大鼻子。

“塔莉娅·德拉蒙德要另谋差事了。”他说。

## XIX / 十九

===Original===
Thalia Accepts an Offer

===Chinese===

塔莉娅受聘

===Original===
It took over a week to settle the preliminaries of Brabazon’s insolvency, and at the end of that time, Thalia walked from the bank with a week’s salary in her little leather bag, and no immediate prospects of employment.

Inspector Parr had not minced his words, which he had addressed to her before an impressed audience.

“Only the fact that I saw you come out of Marl’s house and saw him close the door on you, saves you from a serious charge,” he said.

“If it had only saved me from a lecture also, I should have been pleased,” said Thalia coolly.

===Chinese===

料理布拉巴宗破产的种种前期事宜，足足花了一个多星期。到末了，塔莉娅走出银行，小小的皮手袋里装着一周薪水，眼前却没有什么立时可就的差事。

帕尔探长说起话来毫不留情，这番话还是当着一班听得屏息的旁观者讲给她的。

“要不是我亲眼看见你从马尔的宅子里出来，又亲眼看着他当着你把门关上，你免不了要吃一场重罪的官司。”他说。

“要是它还能连这顿训话也一并免掉，我倒该高兴了。”塔莉娅冷冷地说。

===Original===
“What do you make of her?” asked Parr, as the girl disappeared through the swing doors of the office.

“She rather puzzles me,” it was Derrick Yale to whom he had addressed his question. “And the more I think of her, the more I am puzzled. The woman Macroy says that she has been engaged in pilfering since she has been at the bank, but there is no proof of that. In fact, the only person who could supply the proof is our absent friend, Brabazon. Why didn’t you call her as a witness in the prosecution of Barnet?”

“It would be a case of Barnet’s word against hers,” said the detective, shaking his head, “and the case against Barnet was so clear that I didn’t want any further evidence than my own eyes.”

Yale was frowning thoughtfully.

===Chinese===

“你对她怎么看？”帕尔问。这时姑娘的身影已消失在银行办事厅的弹簧门后。

“她着实让我捉摸不透。”他这一问，问的原是德里克·耶尔。“而且我越想越捉摸不透。麦克罗伊那个女人一口咬定，她进银行以来手脚一直不干净，可拿不出凭据。说实话，唯一能拿出凭据的，只有咱们那位眼下没了踪影的朋友布拉巴宗。公诉巴尼特的时候，你怎么不传她出庭作证？”

“那案子就成了巴尼特一面之词对她的另一面之词。”帕尔摇着头说，“再说对巴尼特的指控本来就清清楚楚，除了我自己这双眼睛，我不需要再多的证据。”

耶尔皱着眉头，若有所思。

===Original===
“I wonder,” he said, half to himself.

“What do you wonder?”

“I wonder if this girl could give us a little more information about the Crimson Circle than we have at present. I’m half inclined to engage her.”

Parr muttered something under his breath.

“I know you think I’m mad, but really I have method in my madness. There is nothing to steal in my office; she would be under my eye all the time, and if she were in communication with the Circle, I should certainly know all about it. Besides, she interests me.”

===Chinese===

“我纳闷。”他半是自语地说。

“你纳闷什么？”

“我纳闷，这姑娘能不能比我们眼下多透出一点红圈的消息来。我简直有点想把她雇下来。”

帕尔压着嗓子咕哝了一句什么。

“我知道你当我疯了，可我这疯里头自有章法。我办公室里没什么可偷的；她随时都在我眼皮子底下；她若与红圈有往来，我必定能把底摸个一清二楚。再说，她让我感兴趣。”

===Original===
“Why did you shake hands with her?” asked Parr curiously, and the other laughed.

“That is why she interests me. I wanted to get an impression, and the impression I had was of some dark, sinister force in the background of her life. That girl is not working independently. She has behind her﻿—”

“The Crimson Circle?” suggested Parr, and there was the suggestion of a sneer in his tone.

“Very likely,” said the other seriously. “Anyway, I’m going to see her.”

===Chinese===

“你为什么要同她握手？”帕尔好奇地问。对方笑了。

“这正是她让我感兴趣的原因。我想取得一个感应，而我得到的感应是：她的生活背后，潜藏着一股阴森邪恶的势力。那姑娘不是在单干。她的背后有一——”

“红圈？”帕尔接了一句，语气里带着一丝讥诮。

“很可能。”对方正色道，“不管怎样，我打算去见她。”

===Original===
He called at Thalia’s flat that afternoon, and her servant showed him into the pretty little drawing-room. A minute after Thalia came in, and there was a smile in her fine eyes as she recognised her visitor.

“Well, Mr. Yale, have you come to give me a few words of warning?”

“Not exactly,” laughed Yale. “I’ve come to offer you a job.”

Her eyebrows rose.

“Do you want an assistant,” she asked ironically, “acting on the principle that to catch a thief you must employ a thief? Or have you views about my reformation? Several people want to reform me,” she said.

===Chinese===

当天下午，他到塔莉娅的公寓登门拜访，她的女仆把他让进那间漂亮的小客厅。不多时塔莉娅走了进来，认出来访者，那双秀目里漾起了笑意。

“哟，耶尔先生，你是来训我几句的？”

“那倒不是。”耶尔笑道，“我是来给你一份差事的。”

她扬起了眉毛。

“你是想雇个帮手，”她讥诮地问，“本着‘要捉贼，先得用贼’的道理？还是打定了主意要改造我？想改造我的人可不止一位呢。”她说。

===Original===
She sat down on the piano stool, her hands behind her, and he knew that she was mocking him.

“Why do you steal, Miss Drummond?”

“Because it is my nature to,” she said without hesitation. “Why should kleptomania be confined to the ruling classes?”

“Do you get any satisfaction out of it?” he demanded. “I’m not asking out of idle curiosity, but as a student of human man and woman.”

She waved her hand round the apartment.

“I have the satisfaction of a very comfortable home,” she said. “I have a good servant, and I am not likely to starve. All these things are particularly satisfying to me. Now tell me about the job, Mr. Yale. Do you want me to be a policewoman?”

===Chinese===

她在琴凳上坐下，双手背在身后。他明白，她是在揶揄他。

“你为什么要偷东西，德拉蒙德小姐？”

“因为我生性如此。”她毫不犹豫地说，“偷窃癖凭什么只该归统治阶级专有？”

“你从中能得到什么满足么？”他追问，“我这样问，并非出于无聊的好奇，而是作为一个研究人间男女的学者。”

她朝寓所四下里摆了摆手。

“我得到了一个十分舒适的家，这便是我的满足。”她说，“我有个好女仆，也不至于挨饿。这一切都叫我格外受用。好了，说说那份差事吧，耶尔先生。你要我去当女警察么？”

===Original===
“Not exactly,” he smiled, “but I want a secretary, somebody upon whom I can rely. My work is increasing at a tremendous rate; my correspondence is much more than I can cope with. I will add, that there is little opportunity in my office for the exercise of your pet vice,” he added good-humouredly, “and anyway, I’ll take that risk.”

She considered a moment, looking at him steadily.

“If you’re willing to take the risk, so am I,” she said at last. “Where is your office?”

He gave her the address.

“I shall be with you at ten o’clock in the morning. Lock up your chequebook and clear away your loose change,” she said.

===Chinese===

“那倒不是。”他微笑道，“不过我要的是一位秘书，一个我靠得住的人。我的事务正以惊人的速度增多；来信多得我应付不过来。我还要补一句，在我的办公室里，你可没多少机会施展你那桩心爱的癖好。”他好脾气地添了一句，“不过无论如何，这个险我甘愿冒。”

她定定地望着他，思量了片刻。

“你既愿意冒这个险，我也愿意。”她终于开口，“你的办公室在哪儿？”

他把地址告诉了她。

“明早十点我准到。把你的支票簿锁好，零钱统统收拾起来。”她说。

===Original===
“A remarkable girl,” he thought as he was going back to the city.

He spoke no more than the truth when he had told Parr that she puzzled him, and yet he had met with every type of criminal, and probably knew more of criminal psychology than did Parr with all his experience.

His mind strayed to Parr, that unhappy individual whom he knew was in disgrace. How much longer would police headquarters tolerate him after this third failure to deal with the Crimson Circle, he wondered.

Mr. Parr was thinking on the same lines that night. A brief official memo, had awaited him on his arrival at headquarters, and he read it with a grimace of pain. And there was worse to follow, he guessed, and he had good reason for that fear.

===Chinese===

“真是个了不得的姑娘。”在回城的路上，他心里想。

他对帕尔说她令他捉摸不透，这话一点不假。何况他各色罪犯无不见识过，论犯罪心理这门学问，恐怕比阅历深厚的帕尔还要更胜一筹。

他的思绪溜到了帕尔身上——他知道，这个失意人眼下正失着宠。对付红圈这已是第三回失手，警察总部还能容他多久呢？他暗自思忖。

当天夜里，帕尔先生转的也是这个念头。他一到总部，一纸简短的公函已在案头候着，他读着，脸痛苦地扭了一扭。他猜更糟的还在后头，而且他有充分的理由这样害怕。

===Original===
The next morning he was summoned to the house of Mr. Froyant, and found Derrick Yale already there.

For all their good relationship, the chase of the Crimson Circle had developed into a duel between these strangely different personalities. It was an open secret in newspaper land that Parr’s impending ruin was due less to the unchecked villainies of the Crimson Circle, than to the superhuman brilliancy of this unofficial rival. To do him justice, Yale did his best to discredit this view, but it was held.

Froyant, for all his meanness and his knowledge of Yale’s heavy fees, had commissioned him immediately after he had received the warning. His faith in the police had evaporated, and he made no attempt to disguise his scepticism.

===Chinese===

第二天早晨，他被召往弗鲁瓦扬先生府上，发现德里克·耶尔已经先到了。

两人交情虽好，追缉红圈却已演成这两个脾性迥异的人物之间的一场对决。帕尔之所以濒临垮台，与其说是因为红圈罪行猖獗、无从遏止，不如说是因为这位编外对手出神入化的才干——这在报界早已是公开的秘密。平心而论，耶尔竭力想驳倒这种说法，无奈人们偏信以为真。

弗鲁瓦扬尽管吝啬，又明知耶尔收费高昂，可一接到那份警告，还是当即委托了他。他对警方的那点信心早已蒸发殆尽，也无意掩饰自己的怀疑。

===Original===
“Mr. Froyant has decided to pay,” were the words which greeted the inspector.

“Eh, of course I shall pay!” exploded Mr. Froyant.

He had aged ten years in the past few days, thought Parr; his face was whiter, and thinner, and he seemed to have shrunk within himself.

“If police headquarters allow this dastardly association to threaten respectable citizens, and cannot even protect their lives, what else is there to be done, but to pay? My friend Pindle has had a similar threat, and he has paid. I cannot stand the strain of this any longer.”

He paced up and down the library floor like a man demented.

===Chinese===

帕尔探长一进门，迎头听见的便是：“弗鲁瓦扬先生决定付钱了。”

“呃，我当然要付！”弗鲁瓦扬先生炸了。

这几天他老了十岁，帕尔心想；他的脸更白了，也更瘦了，整个人仿佛往自身里缩了下去。

“既然警察总部听任这个丧心病狂的组织去威胁安分守己的公民，连他们的性命都保不住，那除了付钱，还有什么路可走？我的朋友平德尔（Pindle）也接到过同样的威胁，他付了。这种煎熬，我再也受不住了。”

他像个失心疯的人，在书房里来回踱步。

===Original===
“Mr. Froyant will pay,” said Derrick Yale slowly. “But this time I think the Crimson Circle have been just a little too venturesome.”

“What do you mean?” asked Parr.

“Have you the letter, sir?” demanded Yale, and Froyant pulled open a drawer savagely and slammed down the familiar card upon his blotting-pad.

“When did this arrive?” asked Parr as he took it up, noting the Crimson Circle.

“By this morning’s post.”

===Chinese===

“弗鲁瓦扬先生会付钱的。”德里克·耶尔缓缓地说，“不过这一回，我觉得红圈未免有点过于胆大妄为了。”

“你这话什么意思？”帕尔问。

“信呢，先生？”耶尔问。弗鲁瓦扬狠狠拉开抽屉，把那张熟悉的卡片往吸墨纸垫上一摔。

“这东西什么时候到的？”帕尔拿起卡片，端详着那个红圈印记，问道。

“今早的邮班送来的。”

===Original===
Parr read the words inscribed in the centre:

We shall call for the money at the office of Mr. Derrick Yale at 3:30 on Friday afternoon. The notes must not run in series. If it is not there for us, you will die the same night.

Three times the inspector read the short message, and then he sighed.

===Chinese===

帕尔读着卡片正中印出的字：

我们将于星期五下午三点半，到德里克·耶尔先生办公室取款。钞票不得连号。届时钱若不候在那里，当天夜里你就得死。

探长把这篇短简读了三遍，然后叹了口气。

===Original===
“Well, that simplifies matters,” he said. “Of course, they will not call﻿—”

“I think they will,” said Yale quietly; “but I shall be prepared for them, and I should like you to be on hand, Mr. Parr.”

“If there is one thing more certain than another,” said the inspector phlegmatically, “it is that I shall be on hand. But I don’t think they will come.”

“There I can’t agree with you,” said Yale. “Whoever the central figure of the Crimson Circle is, he or she does not lack courage. And, by the way,” he lowered his voice, “you will meet an old acquaintance at my office.”

===Chinese===

“唔，这一来事情倒省事了。”他说，“他们当然不会来取——”

“我看他们会来。”耶尔平静地说，“不过我会做好准备，也望你到时在场，帕尔先生。”

“要说天下有什么事比别的更板上钉钉，”探长不动声色地说，“那就是我必定到场。可我认为他们不会来。”

“这一点我可不敢苟同。”耶尔说，“红圈的中心人物不管是谁，无论男女，都不缺胆量。顺便说一句，”他压低了嗓音，“你在我办公室会见到一位老相识。”

===Original===
Parr shot a quick, suspicious glance at the detective, and saw that he was mildly amused.

“Drummond?” he asked.

Yale nodded.

“You are engaging her?”

“She rather interests me, and I fancy that she is going to be a real help in the solution of this mystery.”

Froyant came in at that moment, and the conversation was tactfully changed.

===Chinese===

帕尔飞快而狐疑地朝这位侦探瞥了一眼，看出他正自觉得有趣。

“德拉蒙德？”他问。

耶尔点了点头。

“你要雇她？”

“她让我颇感兴趣，而且我预感，解开这个谜团，她会是个真正得力的帮手。”

恰在这时弗鲁瓦扬走了进来，话题便被得体地岔开了。

## XX / 二十

===Original===
The Key of River House

===Chinese===
河畔宅的钥匙

===Original===
It was arranged that Froyant should draw the necessary money from his bank on the Thursday morning to pay the demand, and that Yale should call for it and meet Parr at the former’s office in ample time to make the necessary preparations for the visitor’s reception.

Mr. Parr’s way to headquarters took him past the big house where Jack Beardmore was living in solitude.

The events of the past few weeks had wrought an extraordinary change in the youth. From a boy he had suddenly become a man, with all a man’s balance and understanding. He had inherited an enormous fortune, but with its coming the incentive of life had, for the most part, fallen away. He could never escape the memory of Thalia Drummond; her face was before him, sleeping or waking, and though he called himself a fool, and could, as he did, argue the matter to a logical conclusion, the sum of all his reasoning faded before the image he carried in his heart.

Between Inspector Parr and he there had grown a curious friendship. There was a time when he was near to hating the stout little man, but his good sense had told him that however large a part sentiment had played in his own life, and in the direction of his own actions, it could have no place in a police officer’s moral equipment.

The inspector stopped before the door of the house, and was for passing on, but, obeying an impulse, he walked slowly up the steps and rang the bell. The footman who admitted him was one of the dozen servants who accentuated the emptiness of the mansion.

===Chinese===
一切已经安排停当：星期四上午，由弗鲁瓦扬从他的银行取出支付那笔索款的现钱，耶尔前去领取，再提前赶到前者的办公室与帕尔会合，好有充裕的时间做好必要布置，恭候来客。

帕尔先生去警察总部的路上，要经过那座大宅——杰克·比德莫尔如今正独自住在那里。

过去几个星期的种种变故，在这青年身上造成了非同寻常的变化。他仿佛在一夜之间从少年变成了男子汉，有了成年人的沉稳与识见。他继承了一笔巨额家产，可财富到手，人生的动力却也大半随之消散。塔莉娅·德拉蒙德的记忆他怎么也摆脱不掉；无论睡着还是醒着，她的面容总在眼前。他骂自己是傻瓜，也的确能把这件事一路推演到合乎逻辑的结论，可全部的道理加在一起，在他心头怀着的那个形象面前，也难免褪得干干净净。

他和帕尔探长之间渐渐生出一段奇特的友谊。他也曾一度几乎恨透这个矮胖的小个子男人，但他的理智告诉他：情感无论在他自己的生活里、在他行事的抉择上占过多大的分量，在一个警官的道德素养里却容不得半点位置。

探长在宅子门前站定，本想径直走过去，却因一时心动，缓步登上台阶，按响了门铃。开门迎他的男仆，是宅中十余名仆役之一——正是这帮人，反倒衬得偌大的公馆愈发空旷。

===Original===
Jack was in the dining-room, pretending to be interested in a late breakfast.

“Come in, Mr. Parr,” he said, rising. “I suppose you breakfasted hours ago. Is there anything new?”

“Nothing,” said Parr, “except that Mr. Froyant has decided to pay.”

“He would,” said Jack contemptuously, and then, for the first time in a long while, he laughed. “I shouldn’t like to be the Red or Crimson Circle, or whatever it calls itself.”

“Why not?” asked Mr. Parr, with a little light of amusement in his eyes, but he could guess the answer.

===Chinese===
杰克正在餐厅里，对一顿迟开的早饭装出兴致勃勃的样子。

“请进，帕尔先生。”他起身说，“您想必几个钟头前就用过早饭了。有什么新鲜事吗？”

“没什么，”帕尔说，“只是弗鲁瓦扬先生已经决定付钱了。”

“他自然会。”杰克轻蔑地说，接着，他许久以来头一回笑出了声。“换了我是那个红圈——或是深红圈，随它自称什么名堂——我可不愿意当它。”

“为什么不愿意？”帕尔先生问，眼里闪着一丝饶有兴味的光，不过答案他多半猜得出来。

===Original===
“My poor father used to say that Froyant fretted over every cent that was taken from him and never rested until he got it back. When Harvey’s panic is over he will go after the Crimson Circle, and will never leave it until every banknote he has handed to them is repaid.”

“Very likely,” agreed the inspector, “but they aren’t holding the money yet.”

He told Jack the contents of the letter which Froyant had received that morning, and his young host was visibly astonished.

“They’re taking a big risk, aren’t they? It would be a clever man who got the better of Derrick Yale.”

===Chinese===
“我可怜的父亲生前常说，弗鲁瓦扬被人拿走一个子儿都要牵肠挂肚，不夺回来绝不消停。等哈维这阵惊慌过去，他就会去追红圈，非等他们把他交出去的每一张钞票尽数吐还，绝不撒手。”

“多半会这样。”探长附和道，“不过那笔钱眼下还没到他们手里呢。”

他把弗鲁瓦扬那天早晨收到的那封信的内容讲给杰克听，这位年轻的主人显见得吃了一惊。

“他们这是在冒天大的险，不是吗？想占德里克·耶尔的上风，可得是个厉害角色才行。”

===Original===
“So I think,” said the inspector, crossing his legs comfortably. “I must take my hat off to Yale. There are things about him that I admire tremendously.”

“His psychometrical powers, for example,” smiled Jack, but the inspector shook his head.

“I don’t know enough about those to admire them. They seem uncanny to me, yet in a certain way I can understand them. No, I am thinking of other of his qualities.”

He was suddenly silent, and Jack sensed his depression.

“You’re having a pretty bad time at headquarters, aren’t you?” he asked. “I don’t suppose they are particularly pleased with the immunity of the Crimson Circle?”

===Chinese===
“我也这么想。”探长说着，舒舒服服地跷起了腿。“我得向耶尔脱帽致敬。他身上有些地方，我佩服得很。”

“比如他那触物感应的本事。”杰克含笑道，探长却摇了摇头。

“那些玩意儿我懂得太少，谈不上佩服。在我眼里它们显得邪门，可从某个角度说，我又能理解。不，我想的是他别的品质。”

他忽然沉默下来，杰克觉察出他的郁郁寡欢。

“您在总部这些日子挺不好过吧？”他问，“红圈至今逍遥法外，上头想必不太痛快吧？”

===Original===
Parr nodded.

“I’m not exactly in a bed of roses just now,” he admitted. “But that doesn’t worry me a bit.” He looked steadily at Jack. “By the way, your young friend is in a new job.”

Jack started.

“My young friend?” he stammered. “You mean Miss﻿—”

“Miss Drummond, I mean. Derrick Yale has engaged her,” he chuckled softly at Jack’s astonishment.

===Chinese===
帕尔点了点头。

“我眼下这光景，确实算不上安乐窝。”他承认道，“可这一点儿也烦不着我。”他定定地望着杰克。“对了，你那位年轻的朋友换新差事了。”

杰克猛地一怔。

“我那位年轻的朋友？”他结结巴巴地说，“您是说——那位小姐……”

“我是说德拉蒙德小姐。德里克·耶尔把她雇下了。”见杰克满脸惊愕，他轻声笑了。

===Original===
“Engaged Miss Thalia Drummond? You’re joking, surely?” said Jack.

“I thought he was joking when he suggested it. He’s a queer bird, is Yale.”

“He ought to be at headquarters, a lot of people think,” said Jack, and realised that he had made a faux pas before the words were out.

But if Mr. Parr was hurt he did not show it.

===Chinese===
“雇下了塔莉娅·德拉蒙德小姐？您准是在开玩笑吧？”杰克说。

“他提这事儿的时候，我还当他在说笑呢。耶尔是个怪人。”

“好些人都觉得，他本该进警察总部当差。”杰克说，话音未落便意识到自己失言了。

不过帕尔先生纵然听着不受用，脸上却分毫不露。

===Original===
“They don’t take them in from outside,” he said with a smile, and the inspector very rarely smiled. “Otherwise, Mr. Beardmore, we should have taken you! No, our friend is clever. I suppose you don’t expect a headquarters’ man to admit that what we call a ‘fancy’ detective can be anything but an interfering fool? But Yale is clever.”

They had strolled together to the window, and were looking out into the sedate street in which Jack Beardmore’s residence was situated.

“Isn’t that Miss Drummond?” he asked suddenly.

Parr had already seen her. She was walking slowly along the other side of the road, looking at the numbers of the houses. Presently she crossed.

===Chinese===
“总部不从外头进人。”他微笑着说——探长可是难得一笑的。“要不然，比德莫尔先生，我们早把您招进去了！不，我们这位朋友确实聪明。我想你也别指望一个总部的人肯承认，我们所谓的‘花架子’侦探能不是个碍事的蠢货吧？可耶尔确实聪明。”

两人一同踱到窗前，望着杰克·比德莫尔宅邸所在的那条幽静的街道。

“那不是德拉蒙德小姐吗？”他突然问。

帕尔早已看见她了。她正沿着马路对面缓步走来，一路看着门牌号码。不多时，她便穿过马路走了过来。

===Original===
“She’s coming here,” gasped Jack. “I wonder what﻿—” He did not wait to finish what he had to say, but rushed out of the room and opened the hall door to her whilst her finger was lingering on the bell push.

“It is good to see you, Thalia,” he said, gripping her warmly by the hand. “Won’t you come in? An old acquaintance of yours is in the dining-room.”

She raised her eyebrows.

“Not Mr. Parr?”

===Chinese===
“她到这儿来了。”杰克倒吸一口气，“不知道她——”他等不及把话说完，便冲出房间，趁她的手指还搭在门铃上，就替她拉开了正门。

“见到你真高兴，塔莉娅。”他热忱地握住她的手说。“进来坐坐好吗？餐厅里有你的一位旧相识。”

她扬起了眉毛。

“不会是帕尔先生吧？”

===Original===
“You’re a wonderful guesser,” laughed Jack as he closed the door behind her. “Did you want to see me alone?” he asked suddenly.

She shook her head.

“No; I’ve only a message for you from Mr. Yale. He wanted you to let him have the key of your riverside house.”

By this time they were in the dining-room, and the girl, meeting the expressionless gaze of Mr. Parr, nodded curtly.

“You evidently do not love my friend, Mr. Parr,” thought Jack.

===Chinese===
“你可真会猜。”杰克一面在她身后关上门，一面笑道。“你是想单独见我吗？”他忽然问。

她摇了摇头。

“不是；我不过是替耶尔先生给你捎个口信。他想请你把河边宅子的钥匙交给他。”

这时他们已进了餐厅，姑娘迎上帕尔先生那毫无表情的注视，淡淡地点了点头。

“你分明不喜欢我这位朋友，帕尔先生。”杰克心里想。

===Original===
He explained the object of the girl’s visit.

“My poor father had a derelict property by the riverside,” he said. “It has not been tenanted for years, and the surveyors tell me it will cost almost as much as the property is worth to put it into repair. For some reason Yale thinks that Brabazon will use this as a hiding-place. Brabazon had it in his hands for some time, trying to sell it. He looked after some of my father’s property. But is he at all likely to be there?”

Mr. Parr pursed his large lips and blinked meditatively.

“The only thing I know about him is that so far he has not left the country,” he said at last. “I should not think he’d go to a house which he must know would be searched.” He stared absently at Thalia. “Yet he might,” he mused. “I suppose he has a key to the place. What is it, a house?”

===Chinese===
接着他说明了姑娘此来的目的。

“我可怜的父亲在河边留下一处废弃的房产，”他说，“多年来无人租住，勘察的人告诉我，修葺的费用几乎抵得上这产业本身的价值。不知为什么，耶尔认定布拉巴宗会拿它当藏身之处。这房子有一阵子攥在布拉巴宗手里，由他张罗代售。我父亲的一部分产业原先也归他照管。可他当真有可能藏在那儿吗？”

帕尔先生抿起厚嘴唇，若有所思地眨了眨眼。

“关于他，我只知道他至今还没有出国。”他终于开口。“我想他不会躲进一幢他明知必遭搜查的宅子。”他心不在焉地盯着塔莉娅。“不过也难说。”他沉吟道，“我料想他有那地方的钥匙。那是个什么样的所在——是宅子吗？”

===Original===
“It is half house and half warehouse,” said Jack. “I have never seen it, but I believe it is one of those dwellings which the old merchants favoured two hundred years ago, in the days when they lived in the places where they carried on business.”

He unlocked his desk and pulled out a drawer full of keys, each bearing a label.

“This is the one, I think, Miss Drummond,” he said, handing the key to her. “How do you like your new job?”

It required some courage to ask the question, for he was almost awestricken in her presence.

===Chinese===
“半是宅子，半是货栈。”杰克说，“我自己从没去看过，不过我想，那是两百年前老商人们中意的那类居所——那时候，人就住在自己做买卖的地方。”

他打开书桌的锁，拉出一个装满钥匙的抽屉，每把钥匙上都系着一块小牌。

“我想就是这把，德拉蒙德小姐。”他说着把钥匙递给她。“新差事干得还合意吗？”

问出这一句需要相当的勇气，因为在她跟前，他几乎心怀敬畏。

===Original===
She smiled faintly.

“It is amusing,” she said, “without being in any way tempting! I cannot tell you very much about it, because I only started this morning.” She turned to the detective. “No, I shan’t trouble you very much, Mr. Parr,” she said. “The only thing of value in the office is a silver paperweight﻿—I don’t even have to post the letters,” she went on mockingly. “The office is built on the American plan, and there is a little shute in Mr. Yale’s private office that drops the letters straight away into the box in the hall below. It is very disappointing!”

Solemn though she was, her eyes were dancing with merriment.

“You’re a queer woman, Thalia Drummond,” said Parr, “and yet I’m sure there is some good in you.”

===Chinese===
她淡淡一笑。

“挺有意思，”她说，“可怎么也谈不上诱人！我没法跟你们讲得太多，因为我今早才上的工。”她转向那位侦探。“放心，我不会怎么去劳您的驾，帕尔先生。”她说，“办公室里唯一值钱的东西是一只银镇纸——连信都用不着我去寄。”她接着揶揄道，“办公室是照美国式样建的，耶尔先生的私人办公室里开着一道小滑槽，信件往里一放，便径直落进楼下门厅的信箱里。实在叫人失望！”

她话说得一本正经，一双眼睛里却闪着戏谑的光。

“你是个古怪的女人，塔莉娅·德拉蒙德。”帕尔说，“可我敢肯定，你身上还有几分好的地方。”

===Original===
The remark seemed to cause her unbounded amusement. She laughed until the tears were in her eyes, and Jack grinned sympathetically.

Parr, on the other hand, showed no sign of amusement.

“Be careful,” he said ominously, and the smile faded from her lips.

“You may be sure I shall be very careful, Mr. Parr,” she said, “and if I am in any kind of trouble, you can be equally sure that I shall send immediately for you!”

“I hope you will,” said Parr, “though I have my doubts.”

===Chinese===
这句话似乎叫她快活得了不得。她笑得眼里都沁出了泪花，杰克也跟着会心地咧嘴直乐。

帕尔那边却纹丝不动，脸上不见半点笑意。

“当心点儿。”他说得声气不祥，她唇边的笑意顿时敛住了。

“您放心，我会非常小心的，帕尔先生。”她说，“真要遇上什么麻烦，您也一样可以放心——我会立刻打发人去请您！”

“希望你做得到。”帕尔说，“尽管我心里存着疑。”

## XXI / 二十一

===Original===
River House
===Chinese===

河畔宅

===Original===
Thalia went straight back to the office and found Derrick Yale sitting in his room reading through a heap of unanswered correspondence.

“Is that the key? Thank you. Put it down there,” he said. “I am afraid you will have to answer most of these yourself. The majority of them are from foolish young people who wish to be trained as private detectives. You will find a form reply, and you can sign the answers yourself. And will you tell this lady,” he handed a letter across to her, “that I am so busy now that I cannot undertake any further commissions?”

He took up the key from the table and held it for a second on his hand.
===Chinese===

塔莉娅径直回到办公室，只见德里克·耶尔正坐在自己的房间里，翻检一大摞没有回复的信件。

“是钥匙吧？谢谢你。搁在那儿就行。”他说，“恐怕这些信大半得由你亲自动笔回复。其中多数是一些犯傻的年轻人写来的，一心想受训当私家侦探。你手头找得到一份现成的格式回函，回信尽可由你自己署名发出。另外，请你告诉这位女士——”他把一封信递了过去，“就说我如今忙得很，再接不了什么新的委托了。”

他从桌上拿起那把钥匙，在掌心里托了一瞬。

===Original===
“You saw Mr. Parr?”

She laughed.

“You’re almost terrifying, Mr. Yale. I did see Mr. Parr, but how did you know?”

He shook his head smilingly.

“It is really very simple, and I should take no credit for my gift,” he said, “any more than you take credit for your good looks and your predisposition to﻿—shall I say ‘take things as you find them’?”
===Chinese===

“你见过帕尔先生了？”

她笑了。

“你简直叫人害怕，耶尔先生。我确实见过帕尔先生，可你是怎么知道的？”

他含笑摇了摇头。

“其实再简单不过，我这点天分没什么可居功的。”他说，“正如你那副好容貌和你那点天性也无可居功一样——那天性，我是不是该说成‘见着什么就拿什么’？”

===Original===
She did not answer at once, then:

“I am a reformed character.”

“I believe you will reform in time. You interest me,” said Yale, and then, after a pause, “immensely!” And with a jerk of his head he dismissed her.
===Chinese===

她没有立刻作答，隔了一会儿才开口：

“我可是个改过自新的人了。”

“我相信你假以时日总会改好的。你让我感兴趣。”耶尔说，随即顿了顿，“——而且是浓厚之极的兴趣！”说罢把头一摆，打发她出去了。

===Original===
She was in the midst of her work and her typewriter was clacking furiously when he appeared at the door of his room.

“Will you try to get Mr. Parr on the telephone?” he said. “You will find his number on the register.”

Mr. Parr was not in his office when she called, but half an hour later she reached him, and switched through the wire to the next room.
===Chinese===

她正埋头忙着手头的活计，打字机敲得噼啪作响，这时他出现在自己房间的门口。

“请你设法接通帕尔先生的电话，好吗？”他说，“他的号码在号簿上能查到。”

她打过去时帕尔先生不在办公室，但半小时后她接通了他，便把电话线转到了隔壁房间。

===Original===
“Is that you, Parr?”

She heard his voice through the door, which was left ajar.

“I am going to Beardmore’s river property to make a search. I have an idea that Brabazon may be hiding there!﻿ ﻿… After lunch; all right. Will you be here at half-past two?”
===Chinese===

“是你吗，帕尔？”

房门虚掩着，他的话音从门缝里传到她耳中。

“我要去搜一搜比德莫尔家那处河畔产业。我总觉得布拉巴宗可能就藏在那里！……午饭以后；好。你两点半到这儿来，行不行？”

===Original===
Thalia Drummond listened and made a shorthand note on her blotting-pad.

At half-past two Parr called. She did not see him, for there was a direct entrance to Yale’s room from the corridor without, but she heard the rumble of his voice, and presently they went out.

She waited until their footsteps had died away, then she took a telegraph form, and addressing it to Johnson, 23, Mildred Street, City, she wrote:

Derrick Yale has gone to search Beardmore’s riverside house.

Thalia Drummond was nothing if not dutiful.
===Chinese===

塔莉娅·德拉蒙德一字不落地听着，在吸墨纸垫上打了一条速记。

两点半，帕尔如约而至。她没有见到他，因为从外面的走廊另有一道门直通耶尔的房间，但她听见他低沉浑厚的语声，不多时两人便一道出去了。

她一直等到两人的脚步声远去，这才抽出一张电报空白单，写明收报人为约翰逊，地址伦敦城米尔德丽德街 23 号，然后落笔：

德里克·耶尔已前去搜查比德莫尔的河边宅子。

塔莉娅·德拉蒙德其人，别的且不论，恪尽职守总归是头一条。

---

===Original===
The house stood upon a little wharf, and was a picture of desolation and neglect. The stone foundation of the wharf was in decay, the parapet broken, the yard a wilderness of weed; rank grasses and nettles formed almost an impenetrable barrier to their progress after they had opened the gate which led from the mean east-end street in which the wharfage was cited.

The house itself might at one time have been picturesque, but now, with its broken lower windows, its weather-stained woodwork and discoloured walls, it was a pitiable piece of architectural wreckage.

At one end was a big, gaunt, stone store, built flush with the wharf’s edge, and apparently communicating with the house. An air-raid during the war had demolished one corner of the wall, and robbed it of a few slates which remained, leaving the skeleton of rotting roof ribs nakedly bare to inspection.
===Chinese===

那宅子立在一座小码头上，满目荒凉，无人照管。码头的石基已经朽坏，护墙断的断、缺的缺，院子成了野草的荒原；那码头只连着一条寒碜的东区小街，两人推开从小街通往码头的院门之后，疯长的荒草和荨麻几乎结成一道无从穿越的屏障，挡住去路。

宅子本身想必也曾有过几分画意，可如今下层窗户尽破，木作被风雨浸得斑驳，墙面黯败发乌，活脱是一副令人恻然的建筑残骸。

宅子一端矗着一座嶙峋的大石库房，与码头边沿齐平而建，看样子与宅子相通。大战期间的一场空袭轰塌了库房一角的墙，又把残存的几块石板瓦掠了去，只剩朽烂的屋顶骨架赤裸裸地袒露着，任人打量。

===Original===
“A cheerful place,” said Yale, as he opened the door. “It is not the sort of setting in which one could imagine the elegant Brabazon, is it?”

The passageway was dusty. Cobwebs hung from the ceiling and the house was silent and lifeless. They made a rapid tour through the rooms, without, however, discovering any sign of the fugitive.

“There is a garret here,” said Yale, pointing to a flight of steps that led to a trapdoor in the ceiling of the upper floor.
===Chinese===

“好一个叫人心情舒畅的地方。”耶尔开门时说，“要说雍容儒雅的布拉巴宗会窝在这么个地方，可真叫人想不出来，是吧？”

过道上积着灰，蛛网从天花板垂挂下来，整座宅子死气沉沉。他们把各个房间飞快巡视一遍，却未见逃亡者的半点踪影。

“这儿有个阁楼。”耶尔指着一段小台阶说——那台阶通向顶层天花板上一扇活板门。

===Original===
He ran up the steps, pushed open the flap and disappeared. Parr heard him walking along and presently he came down.

“Nothing there,” he said as he slammed the trapdoor in its place.

“I never expected that you would find anything,” said Parr as he led the way out of the house.

They crossed the weed-grown path to the outer gate, and from a garret window a white-faced man watched them through the dusty glass; a man with a week’s growth of beard, whom even his most intimate friends would never have recognised as Mr. Brabazon, the well-known banker.
===Chinese===

他奔上台阶，推开板门，人就不见了。帕尔听得他在上面走动，不多时他便下来了。

“那儿什么也没有。”他说着，砰的一声把活板门放回原处。

“我原本就没指望你能搜出什么来。”帕尔说着，领头走出宅子。

两人踏过杂草丛生的小径，走向外侧的大门；阁楼的一扇窗后，一个面无人色的男人正隔着蒙尘的玻璃窥望着他们——那汉子满脸一个星期没刮的胡子，纵然是他最亲密的朋友，也决计认不出这位便是大名鼎鼎的银行家布拉巴宗先生。

## XXII / 二十二

===Original===
The Messenger of the Circle

===Chinese===
红圈的信使

===Original===
“You’re a fool, sir, and an idiot. I thought you were a clever detective, but you’re a fool!”

Mr. Froyant was in his most savage mood, and the neat stack of banknotes which stood upon his desk supplied the reason.

The sight of so much good money going away from him was a cause of unspeakable anguish to the miserly Harvey, and if his eyes strayed away from that accumulation of wealth, they came back again almost instantly.

Derrick Yale was a difficult man to offend.

===Chinese===
“你是个蠢货，先生，一个白痴。我还当你是个精明的侦探，可你竟是个蠢货！”

弗鲁瓦扬先生正处在火气最盛的时候，端端正正摆在他写字台上的那摞钞票，便是缘由。

眼看这么多白花花的好钱要从身边飞走，吝啬的哈维心里有说不出的煎熬；他的目光即便一时从那堆财富上挪开，转瞬之间便又粘了回去。

想得罪德里克·耶尔可不容易。

===Original===
“Perhaps I am,” he said, “but I must run my own business in my own way, Mr. Froyant, and if I think that the girl can lead me to the Crimson Circle﻿—as I do think﻿—then I shall employ her.”

“Mark my words,” Froyant shook his fingers in the detective’s face, “that girl is with the gang. You will discover, my friend, that *she* is the messenger who will call for the money!”

“In which case she will be immediately arrested,” said the other. “Believe me, Mr. Froyant, I have no intention of losing sight of these notes, but if they are taken by the Crimson Circle, the responsibility must be mine not yours. My job is to save your life, and to divert the vengeance of the Circle from you to myself.”

“Quite right, quite right,” said Mr. Froyant hastily, “that is the proper way to look at it, Yale. I see that you are not as unintelligent as I thought. Have it your own way,” he said. He fingered the notes lovingly, and putting them into a long envelope, handed them, with every evidence of reluctance, to the detective, who slipped the package into his pocket.

===Chinese===
“也许我是吧，”他说，“可我的差事得按我自己的路子来办，弗鲁瓦扬先生。倘若我认为那姑娘能领我找到红圈——我恰恰是这么认为的——那我就要雇用她。”

“记住我的话，”弗鲁瓦扬把手指晃到侦探的脸前，“那姑娘跟那伙人是一路的。你终会发现，我的朋友，上门取款的信使，正是*她*！”

“果真那样，她当场就会被逮捕。”对方说。“请相信我，弗鲁瓦扬先生，我绝没有让这些钞票脱离视线的打算；可万一它们真被红圈取走，责任也必定归我，而不归您。我的差事是救您的命，把红圈的报复从您身上引到我本人身上来。”

“很对，很对。”弗鲁瓦扬先生赶忙说，“这么看问题才像话，耶尔。我看得出，你并不像我原先想的那么蠢。就随你自己的意思办吧。”他说。他爱不释手地摩挲着那些钞票，把它们装进一只长信封，万般不舍地递给侦探，侦探随即把那纸包塞进了口袋。

===Original===
“I suppose there is no news of Brabazon? The rascal has robbed me of over two thousand pounds, which I foolishly invested in one of Marl’s rotten concerns.”

“Did you know anything about Marl?” asked the detective, opening the door.

“I only know that he was a blackguard.”

“Did you know anything that isn’t as well known?” asked Yale patiently. “His beginnings, where he came from?”

“He came from France, I believe,” said Froyant. “I know very little about him. In fact, it was James Beardmore who introduced me. There was some story about his having been concerned in land swindles in France, and of having been imprisoned there, but I never take much notice of gossip. He was useful to me, and I made quite a considerable sum out of most of my investments with him.”

The other smiled. In those circumstances, he thought, the miser might very well forgive the erring Marl for his later losses.

===Chinese===
“我想布拉巴宗那边还没有消息吧？那恶棍坑了我两千多镑——都怪我糊涂，把这笔钱投进了马尔的一个烂公司。”

“关于马尔，你可知道些什么底细？”侦探一边开门一边问。

“我只知道他是个恶棍。”

“有没有什么不那么人人皆知的事呢？”耶尔耐心地问，“比如他的出身来历，他是打哪儿来的？”

“我记得他是从法国来的。”弗鲁瓦扬说，“他的底细我知道得很少。其实，还是詹姆斯·比德莫尔引我跟他认识的。有过一些传闻，说他在法国卷进过地产诈骗，还在那边蹲过监狱，不过飞短流长，我向来不大理会。他对我有用处，跟他合伙的多数投资，我都赚到了相当可观的一笔。”

对方微微一笑。他想，念着这样的进项，这位守财奴对后来折损他钱财、误入歧途的马尔，尽可以大度地原谅了。

===Original===
When he got back to his office he found Parr waiting, with Jack Beardmore.

He had not expected a visit from the younger man, and guessed that the real attraction was Thalia Drummond, for whose absence he tactfully apologised.

“I’ve sent Miss Drummond home, Parr,” he said. “I don’t want a girl mixed up in the business of this afternoon. There may be a little rough-and-tumble work.”

He looked keenly at Jack Beardmore.

“For which I hope you are prepared.”

“I shall be disappointed if there isn’t,” said Jack cheerfully.

===Chinese===
他回到办公室，发现帕尔正等在那里，同来的还有杰克·比德莫尔。

他没料到这位年轻人会来访，一猜便知，真正引他前来的是塔莉娅·德拉蒙德；对于她的缺席，他婉转周到地赔了个不是。

“我已经打发德拉蒙德小姐回家了，帕尔。”他说，“我不想让一个姑娘家搅进今天下午的事。说不定免不了要动一番拳脚。”

他目光锐利地打量着杰克·比德莫尔。

“但愿你有备而来。”

“真没有这一场，我倒要失望了。”杰克兴冲冲地说。

===Original===
“What is your plan?” asked Parr.

“I am going into my room a few minutes before the messenger is due to arrive. I shall have both doors locked, that into the passage and that into this outer office. In the case of this door, I will leave the key on your side and ask you to lock me in. My object, of course, is to prevent a surprise. As soon as you hear a knock, and hear me rise and go to the door and unlock it, you will know that the visitor has arrived, and when the door closes again, I want you to station yourself outside in the corridor.”

Parr nodded.

“That seems simple,” he said. He walked to the window, looked out, and waved a handkerchief, and Yale smiled approvingly.

===Chinese===
“你有什么计划？”帕尔问。

“信使预定到达之前几分钟，我就进自己那间屋。两扇门都上锁——一扇通走廊，一扇通这间外办公室。通外办公室的这扇，我把钥匙留在你们那一侧，请你把我锁在屋里。我的用意，当然是防备突袭。你一听见敲门声，听见我起身走到门边拧开锁，就知道来客到了；等门再度关上，我就要你守在外面的走廊上。”

帕尔点了点头。

“这法子看来简单。”他说。他走到窗前，朝外张望了一下，挥了挥一块手帕，耶尔赞许地笑了。

===Original===
“I see you have taken the necessary precautions. How many men have you?”

“I think there are eighty,” said Mr. Parr calmly, “and they will practically surround the place.”

Yale nodded.

“We have to remember,” he said, “that the Crimson Circle may send a very ordinary district messenger, in which case, of course, he must be followed. I am determined that the money shall pass into the hands of the chief of the Crimson Circle himself﻿—that is an essential.”

===Chinese===
“我看你已做了必要的防范。你带了多少人？”

“我想有八十个吧。”帕尔先生从容地说，“他们差不多能把这地方围个水泄不通。”

耶尔点了点头。

“我们得记住，”他说，“红圈也可能只派一个普普通通的本区信差来，果真那样，自然得盯他的梢。我拿定主意，非让这笔钱落进红圈首领本人的手里不可——这是关键。”

===Original===
“I quite agree,” said Parr, “but I have an idea that the gentleman, or whoever he is, will not come himself. May I look at your office?”

He walked in and inspected the room. It was lighted by one window. In a corner was a cupboard, the door of which he opened. It was empty save for a hanging coat.

“If you don’t mind,” Inspector Parr was almost humble, “I want you to stay in the outer office. Thank you, I’ll close the door on you. I get rattled if I am overlooked.”

Laughingly Yale walked from the office, and Mr. Parr closed the door on him. He opened the second door, and looked out into the corridor. Presently they heard him close that also.

“You can come in,” he said, “I’ve seen all I want.”

===Chinese===
“我完全同意。”帕尔说，“不过我有一种感觉，那位绅士——不管他究竟是什么人——不会亲自来。我可以看看你那间办公室吗？”

他走进房间，四处察看。屋里靠一扇窗采光。角落里有一只立柜，他打开柜门，里面空空的，只挂着一件外衣。

“你要是不介意，”帕尔探长的口气近乎谦卑，“我想请你待在外面的办公室里。谢谢，我把门替你关上。有人在一旁盯着，我就沉不住气。”

耶尔笑着走出办公室，帕尔先生随手把门关上。他又打开第二扇门，朝走廊里张望。不多时，他们听见他把那扇门也关上了。

“你可以进来了，”他说，“该看的我都看完了。”

===Original===
The room was simply but comfortably furnished. There was a wide fireplace, in which, however, no fire burnt, although the day was chilly.

“I don’t expect him to get up the chimney,” said Yale, humorously, as he noticed the detective’s inspection, “I never have a fire in this office; I’m one of those hot-blooded mortals who are never really cold.”

Jack, a fascinated observer of the search, picked up the deadly little pistol that lay on the detective’s table, and examined it cautiously.

“Be careful, that trigger is a little sensitive,” said Yale.

He took from his pockets the envelope containing the notes, and laid them by the side of the weapon. Then he looked at his watch.

===Chinese===
这间屋子陈设简单，却也舒适。屋里有一座宽大的壁炉，尽管天有些冷，炉膛里却没有生火。

“我可不指望他从烟囱里下来。”耶尔注意到侦探在察看，诙谐地说，“这间办公室我从不生火；我是那种血气火热的凡人，从来不知道什么叫真正的冷。”

杰克把这场搜查看入了迷，拿起放在侦探桌上的那把凶险的小手枪，小心翼翼地察看。

“当心，那扳机有点儿灵。”耶尔说。

他从口袋里取出装着钞票的信封，把它放在那件家伙旁边，然后看了看表。

===Original===
“Now I think that to be on the safe side we should go to the other office, and lock the door,” he said.

He accompanied his words by locking the door into the corridor.

“It is rather thrilling,” whispered Jack. He felt that a whisper was the fitting tone for that exciting moment.

“I hope it won’t be too thrilling,” said Yale.

They went to the outer office, and turned the key on him, and sat down﻿—Jack unconsciously on Thalia Drummond’s chair, a fact which he realised with a start.

Was she of the Crimson Circle, he wondered? Parr had hinted as much. Jack set his teeth; he could not, and would not believe even the evidence of his own eyes, and his own common sense. So far from her influence waning, it was gathering strength. She was a being apart, and if she was guilty﻿—

===Chinese===
“现在我想，为保险起见，我们该到另一间办公室去，把门锁上。”他说。

他话音落处，顺手锁上了通走廊的门。

“这可真够刺激。”杰克悄声说。他觉得，值此扣人心弦的时刻，窃窃私语才是恰如其分的声调。

“但愿别刺激过了头。”耶尔说。

两人进了外办公室，用钥匙把耶尔反锁在里面，坐了下来——杰克不知不觉坐上了塔莉娅·德拉蒙德的椅子，猛然意识到这一层，心里一惊。

她当真是红圈一伙的吗？他心里犯着嘀咕。帕尔话里就有这层暗示。杰克咬紧了牙关：哪怕是亲眼所见的凭据、自己常识的判断，他也不能、更不肯相信。她的魔力非但没有消退，反倒愈聚愈强。她是超然物外的一个存在，倘若她当真有罪——

===Original===
He looked up, and saw Parr’s eyes fixed upon him.

“I don’t pretend to be psychometrical,” said the detective slowly, “but I’ve an idea you’re thinking about Thalia Drummond.”

“I was,” admitted the young man. “Mr. Parr, do you think she is really as bad as she appears to be?”

“Do you mean, do I think that she stole Froyant’s Buddha, because if that’s what you mean, it is not a question of thinking, I am certain.”

===Chinese===
他抬起头，正撞上帕尔一双定定望着他的眼睛。

“我不敢自诩有触物感应的本事。”侦探慢悠悠地说，“不过我有个感觉——你正在想塔莉娅·德拉蒙德。”

“我确实在想她。”年轻人承认道。“帕尔先生，您认为她真像表面上看起来那么坏吗？”

“你是问，我是不是认为她偷了弗鲁瓦扬的佛像？因为你要是这个意思，那就不是认不认为的问题了——我拿得准是她。”

===Original===
Jack was silent. He could never hope to convince this stolid man of the girl’s innocence and anyway it was madness, he recognised, to think of her as innocent when she had confessed her fault.

“You had better keep quiet in there.” It was Yale’s voice, and Parr grunted a reply.

Thereafter they sat in dead silence. They heard him moving about the room, then he too was quiet, for the hour was approaching. Inspector Parr pulled his watch from his pocket, and laid it on the table; the hands pointed to half-past three. It was now that the messenger was due and he sat, his head strained forward, listening, but there was no sound of attack.

===Chinese===
杰克沉默了。想叫这个刻板顽固的人相信姑娘清白，他是休想了；何况自己也明白，她既已认下过错，再当她是清白无辜，无异于痴人说梦。

“里头二位最好安静些。”是耶尔的声音，帕尔咕哝了一声算是回答。

此后两人便死一般寂静地坐着。他们听见他在屋里走动，随后他也安静下来，因为约定的钟点临近了。帕尔探长从口袋里掏出怀表，放在桌上；指针正指三点半。信使此时该到了，他坐着，脑袋使劲前倾，凝神细听，却听不见丝毫来袭的动静。

===Original===
Presently there was a noise in Yale’s room, a queer bumping noise as though Yale had sat down heavily.

Parr jumped to his feet.

“What was that?”

“It is all right,” said Yale’s voice, “I stumbled over something. Be quiet.”

They sat for another five minutes, and then Parr called:

“Are you all right, Yale?”

===Chinese===
不多时，耶尔的房里传出一声响动——一下古怪的闷响，仿佛耶尔一屁股重重坐倒在地。

帕尔霍地跳起身来。

“那是什么？”

“没事，”是耶尔的声音，“我绊着什么东西了。别作声。”

他们又坐了五分钟，帕尔喊道：

“你没事吧，耶尔？”

===Original===
There was no answer.

“Yale!” he called more loudly. “Do you hear me?”

There was no reply and springing to the door he snapped the lock, and rushed into the room, Jack at his heels.

What they saw might have paralysed even a more experienced officer than Inspector Parr.

Stretched upon the ground, his wrists fastened with handcuffs, his ankles strapped, and a towel over his face lay the prostrate figure of Derrick Yale. The window was open, and there was a strong scent of ether and chloroform. The package of money which had lain upon the table had disappeared. Three seconds later, an aged postman left the hall of the building, carrying his letter-bag on his shoulder, and the police who were watching the house, let him pass without question.

===Chinese===
没有回音。

“耶尔！”他放大嗓门喊道，“你听见我说话没有？”

依旧无人应声。他一跃而起抢到门边，咔哒拧开锁，冲进房去，杰克紧随其后。

眼前的景象，恐怕连比帕尔探长更老练的警官也会吓瘫。

只见德里克·耶尔的身躯直挺挺躺在地上，手腕被手铐铐住，脚踝被皮带捆着，脸上还蒙着一条毛巾。窗户开着，屋里弥漫着乙醚和氯仿的浓烈气味。原先搁在桌上的那包钱已经无影无踪。三秒钟后，一个上了年纪的邮差肩挎邮袋，走出了大楼的门厅——监视这幢房子的警察，未加盘问便放他过去了。

## XXIII / 二十三

===Original===
The Woman in the Cupboard
===Chinese===

柜中的女人

===Original===
Parr bent down, and snatched the saturated towel from the detective’s face, and he opened his eyes, and stared around.

“What is it?” he asked thickly, but the inspector was busy unscrewing the handcuffs. Presently he threw them clanking to the floor, and lifted the man to his feet, as Jack, with trembling fingers, unbuckled the straps about Yale’s legs.

They led him to his chair, and he fell heavily into its depths, passing his hand across his forehead.
===Chinese===

帕尔俯下身，一把从那位侦探脸上扯下浸得透湿的毛巾。他睁开眼睛，四下呆望。

“这是怎么了？”他口齿含混地问。探长却只顾忙着拧开手铐，不多时便把手铐哐啷一声掷在地上，将那人搀扶起来；杰克则手指发颤，解开了绑在耶尔双腿上的皮带。

两人把他扶到他的椅子上，他沉沉地陷进椅深处，抬手在额前抹了一把。

===Original===
“What happened?” he asked.

“That’s what I’d like to know,” said Parr. “Which way did they go?”

The other shook his head.

“I don’t know, I can’t remember,” he said. “Is the door locked?”
===Chinese===

“刚才出了什么事？”他问。

“我正想问呢。”帕尔说，“他们往哪边去了？”

对方摇了摇头。

“不知道，想不起来了。”他说，“门锁上了没有？”

===Original===
Jack ran to the door. The key was turned from the inside. He could not have gone that way, but the window was open. That was the first thing Parr had seen when he entered the room.

He ran to the window, and looked out. There was a sheer fall of eighty feet, and no sign of a ladder or of any means by which Yale’s assailant could have escaped.

“I don’t know what happened,” said Yale, when he had partially recovered. “I was sitting in this chair when suddenly a cloth was pulled across my face, and two powerful hands gripped me with a strength which I shouldn’t have thought possible in any human being. Before I could struggle or cry out I must have lost consciousness.”
===Chinese===

杰克奔到门边。钥匙是从里面转上的。那人不可能打这儿出去，可窗户却开着——帕尔进屋时头一眼瞧见的就是这扇窗。

他奔到窗前，探头外望。窗外是八十英尺的笔直陡落，既不见梯子的踪影，也寻不出袭击耶尔的人可能借以脱身的任何法子。

“我不知道出了什么事。”耶尔缓过几分神之后说，“我当时正坐在这把椅子上，忽然一块布蒙上了我的脸，跟着两只强有力的手把我死死攥住——那股手劲，我原以为血肉之躯谁也不可能有。不等我挣扎或喊出声来，多半就已经人事不知了。”

===Original===
“Did you hear my call?” asked Parr.

The other man shook his head.

“But, Mr. Yale, we heard a noise and Mr. Parr asked if you were all right. You replied that you had only stumbled.”

“It was not me,” said Yale. “I remember nothing from the moment the cloth was put on my face until the moment you found me here.”
===Chinese===

“你听见我喊你了吗？”帕尔问。

对方摇了摇头。

“可是，耶尔先生，我们当时听见了响动，帕尔先生问您要不要紧，您还回话说只是绊了一跤。”

“那回话的可不是我。”耶尔说，“打那块布蒙上我的脸起，直到你们在这儿发现我，中间的事我一概不记得。”

===Original===
Inspector Parr was at the window. He pulled down the sash, and he pushed it up again, and then he looked on the windowsill, and when he turned there was a large smile on his face.

“That is the cleverest thing I’ve ever seen,” he said.

Something of Jack’s old antipathy to the stout detective returned.
===Chinese===

帕尔探长在窗前。他拉下窗扇，又推了上去，再细看窗台；待他转过身来，脸上已堆起一个大大的笑容。

“这是我这辈子见过的最妙的一手。”他说。

杰克对这位发福侦探积年的反感，不觉又上了心头。

===Original===
“I don’t think it is particularly clever. They’ve half-killed Yale, and they’ve got away,” he said.

“I said it was clever, and it was clever,” said Mr. Parr stolidly, “and now I think I’ll go down, and interview the officers I left on duty in the hall.”

But the watching officers had nothing to say. Nobody had entered or left the building except the postman.

“Except the postman, eh?” said Parr thoughtfully. “Why, of course, the postman! All right, sergeant, you can dismiss your men.”
===Chinese===

“我看这有什么特别妙的。他们把耶尔整得只剩半条命，人还叫他跑了。”他说。

“我说它妙，它就是妙。”帕尔先生木然道，“现在我可想下楼去，问问我留在门厅值守的警官们。”

可值守的警官们什么也说不上来。除了邮差，没有一个人进出过这幢楼。

“除了邮差，嗯？”帕尔沉吟道，“哎呀，可不是么，那邮差！好了，警长，你可以把弟兄们撤下去了。”

===Original===
He went up in the elevator and rejoined Yale.

“The money’s gone all right,” he said. “I don’t know what we can do except report the matter to headquarters.”

Yale was now nearly his normal self, and sat at his desk with his head resting on his hands.

“Well, I’m the culprit this time,” he said, “and they can’t blame you, Parr. I’m still trying to puzzle out how they got into that window, and how they reached me without making a sound.”
===Chinese===

他乘电梯上楼，回到耶尔身边。

“钱是真真切切没了。”他说，“除了把这事呈报警察总部，还有什么办法，我可想不出来。”

耶尔这时已差不多恢复如常，坐在办公桌前，双手托着脑袋。

“唉，这回罪在我。”他说，“他们怪不着你，帕尔。我还在纳闷呢——他们是怎么进到那扇窗里去的，又是怎么一点声息没有就摸到了我跟前。”

===Original===
“Was your back to the window?”

Yale nodded.

“I never dreamt of the window. I sat so that I could see both doors.”

“Your back was also to the fireplace?”

“They couldn’t have come that way,” said the other, shaking his head. “No, this is the supreme mystery of my career; more astounding than the identity of the Crimson Circle,” he got up slowly, “I must report this to old man Froyant, and you had better come along and lend me your moral support,” he said. “He will be furious.”
===Chinese===

“你当时背对着窗户？”

耶尔点了点头。

“我压根儿没把窗户放在心上。我坐的位子，两扇门都瞧得见。”

“你的后背也对着壁炉？”

“他们不可能打那边来。”对方摇头道，“不，这是我侦探生涯里最高深的一桩谜案，比红圈的真实身份还要骇人。”他缓缓站起身，“我得把这事报告老弗鲁瓦扬，你最好陪我走一趟，给我一点道义上的声援。”他说，“他准会大发雷霆。”

===Original===
They left the office together, Yale locking both doors and slipping the key into his pocket.

To say that Mr. Froyant was furious is to employ a very mild expression to describe his hectic frenzy.

“You told me, you practically promised me,” he stormed, “that the money would come back to me, and now you have come with a cock-and-bull story of being drugged. It is monstrous! Where were you, Parr?”

“I was on the premises,” said Mr. Parr, “and the story Mr. Yale has told is correct.”
===Chinese===

两人一道离开办公室，耶尔锁上两扇门，把钥匙揣进了口袋。

说弗鲁瓦扬先生大发雷霆，实在还嫌字眼太轻，远不足以形容他那副狂态。

“你亲口跟我说过，几乎是向我打过包票，”他咆哮道，“说那笔钱会回到我手里；如今你倒好，跑来编一套遭人下药的鬼话。真是岂有此理！你当时在哪儿，帕尔？”

“我当时就在这幢楼里。”帕尔先生说，“耶尔先生方才讲的经过，句句属实。”

===Original===
Suddenly Froyant’s rage died down, so suddenly that the calmness of his voice was almost startling after its previous rancour.

“All right,” he said, “nothing can be done. The Crimson Circle have had their money, and that is the end of it. I’m much obliged to you, Yale. Please send your bill to me.”

And with these brusque instructions, he sent them to rejoin Jack, who was waiting in the street outside.

“Well, that beats the band,” said Parr. “I thought at one time he was going to have a fit, and then did you notice how his manner changed?”
===Chinese===

弗鲁瓦扬的怒火陡然息了下去，息得那么突然，方才还是一片恶声恶气，转眼间语气的平静几乎吓人一跳。

“好吧，”他说，“无可挽回了。红圈拿到了他们的钱，这事到此为止。多谢你了，耶尔。请把你的账单寄给我。”

这几句冷硬的吩咐一毕，他便打发他们去与杰克会合——杰克正在外面街上候着。

“嘿，这可真是破天荒。”帕尔说，“有一阵子我还当他要当场气得背过气去，可接着你留意到没有——他的态度怎么陡然变了？”

===Original===
Yale nodded slowly.

At the moment of Froyant’s change of manner a great idea was formed in his mind, a tremendous and startling doubt that was almost paralysing.

“And now,” said Parr good-humouredly, “as I have given you moral support, perhaps you will extend the same service to me. At police headquarters I am not so much persona grata as you. Come along and see the Commissioner and tell him what happened.”
===Chinese===

耶尔缓缓点了点头。

就在弗鲁瓦扬态度陡变的那一瞬，他心里生出了一个重大的念头——一个天大的、惊人骇异的疑问，几乎使他全身僵住。

“好了，”帕尔和颜悦色地说，“我既已给过你道义上的声援，兴许你也肯把这番效劳回敬我一次。在警察总部，我可远不及你那样吃得开。走，一同去见见总监，把事情原原本本讲给他听。”

---

===Original===
Derrick Yale’s office was silent and deserted. Ten minutes had passed since the drone of the elevator announced the departure of the three men. The silence was broken by a click, and slowly the doors in the big cupboard in the corner of Derrick Yale’s office were pushed open and Thalia Drummond came out. She closed the doors behind her and stood for a while contemplating the room, deep in thought. From her pocket she took a key, opened the door and, passing into the corridor, locked the door behind her.

She did not ring for the elevator. At the farther end of the passage was a flight of narrow stairs which communicated with the caretaker’s room, on the top floor, and which were used only by him. Down these she went. At the bottom was a door leading into the courtyard of a building. This, too, she unlocked and soon after had joined the throng of homeward bound clerks that thronged the pavement at this hour.
===Chinese===

德里克·耶尔的办公室里寂静无人。电梯嗡嗡的运行声宣告三人离去，至今已有十分钟。一声轻响打破了寂静——办公室墙角那只大柜的柜门被缓缓推开，塔莉娅·德拉蒙德从里面走了出来。她回身掩上柜门，伫立片刻，凝望着这间屋子，陷入沉思。然后她从口袋里取出一把钥匙，打开房门，走进走廊，又反手把门锁上。

她没有按铃叫电梯。走廊尽头有一道窄楼梯，通顶层看门人的房间，向来只有他本人使用。她顺着楼梯下去。梯脚有一扇门，通向另一幢楼的院子。这扇门她也开了锁，不多时，便汇入了这个钟点挤满人行道的下班办事员的人潮。

## XXIV / 二十四

===Original===
£10,000 Reward
===Chinese===
一万镑悬赏

===Original===
The Associated Merchants Bank are authorised to offer a reward of ten thousand pounds for information which will lead to the arrest and conviction of the leader of what is known as the Crimson Circle Gang. In conjunction with this reward the Secretary of State promises a free pardon to any member of the gang, other than one actually guilty of wilful murder, providing that the said member will furnish the information and evidence requisite to the conviction of the man or woman known as the Crimson Circle.

On every hoarding, in every post office window, on every police station board, the announcement flared in bloodred print.

Derrick Yale, on his way to his office, saw the announcement and read it and passed on, wondering what effect this would have upon the minor members of the gang he had been engaged to hunt.

Thalia Drummond read it from the top of a bus, when that vehicle had pulled up close to a hoarding, to take on a passenger, and she smiled to herself. But the most remarkable effect of the poster was upon Harvey Froyant. It brought a colour to his face and a light to his eye which made him almost youthful. He, too, was on his way to the office when he read the announcement, but hurried back to his house and took from a drawer in his study a long list. They were the numbers of the banknotes which the Crimson Circle had taken, and he had compiled them laboriously, almost lovingly.
===Chinese===
联合商业银行（The Associated Merchants Bank）奉准悬赏一万英镑，奖予凡能提供情报、使俗称“红圈帮”的匪帮首领落网定罪之人。与此赏格相辅，国务大臣（Secretary of State）允诺：凡该帮帮众——确犯蓄意谋杀罪者除外——如能提供必要情报与证据，将人称“红圈”之人（无论其为男为女）定罪，概予赦免。

每一块广告牌上，每一家邮局的橱窗里，每一处警察局的告示板上，这份告示都赫然高张，字迹血红刺目。

德里克·耶尔在去办公室的路上见到这份告示，读了读便走过去了，心里琢磨着：这对他受雇追缉的那伙帮众中的小角色们，将会产生什么影响。

塔莉娅·德拉蒙德是在公共汽车顶层上读到它的——那辆车恰好停在一张广告牌旁载客——她不由得独自莞尔。不过，这张招贴最非同寻常的效果，却落在哈维·弗鲁瓦扬身上。它使他脸上添了血色，眼里放出了光，几乎叫他显出几分年轻。他也是在去办公室的路上读到告示的，却急忙折回家中，从书房的一只抽屉里取出一张长长的单子。单上是红圈取走的那批钞票的号码——当初编录它们，他下的是死功夫，那股劲头几乎称得上一片痴情。

===Original===
With his own hands he now made another copy, a work that occupied him until late in the morning.
