When he had finished he wrote a letter, and enclosing the new list of notes, he addressed it, posting the letter himself, to a firm of lawyers which he knew specialised in the tracing of lost and stolen property.

Heggitts’ had rendered him good service before, and the next morning brought a representative of the firm, Mr. James Heggitt, the senior partner, a wizened little man with a chronic sniff.

The name of Heggitt was not one which was universally respected, nor did lawyers, when they met, speak of it with affection or regard. And yet it was one of the most prosperous firms of lawyers in the city. The majority of its clients were on or over the borderline which separates the lawful from the unlawful, but to the law-abiding also it was very useful, and was frequently consulted by more eminent firms whose clients wished to recover valuable goods which had been taken by the light-fingered gentry. In some mysterious way Heggitts’ could always place their finger upon a “gentleman” who had “heard” of the property which was lost, and, in the majority of cases, the missing article was restored.
===Chinese===
此刻他亲手又誊了一份，这门功课一直占去他大半个上午。誊完之后他写了一封信，把新的钞票号码单附入，抬头写给一家他深知专办查寻遗失和失窃财物的律师行——信还是他亲自去寄的。

赫吉特事务所（Heggitts’）从前替他办过漂亮差事。第二天上午，这家律师行派来一位代表——资深合伙人詹姆斯·赫吉特（James Heggitt）先生，一个干瘦的小个子男人，落下个动不动就吸鼻子的老毛病。

赫吉特这块招牌并非处处受人敬重，律师们见面提起它，也从无半点好感或敬意。然而它却是全城最兴隆的律师行之一。它的主顾大多数正踩在合法与不合法的分界线上，有的干脆越了过去；不过对守法良民它也同样大有用处，较有声望的律师行也常来向它讨教，因为他们的主顾想把落入妙手空空之辈手中的贵重财物追讨回来。凭某种神秘的路数，赫吉特事务所总能把指头点到某一位“听说过”失物下落的“君子”身上，而在大多数案子里，失踪的物件也总能物归原主。

===Original===
“I got your note, Mr. Froyant,” said the little lawyer, “and I can tell you now that none of these notes are likely to go through the usual channels.” He paused and licked his lips, looking past Mr. Froyant. “The biggest ‘fence’ of all has gone, so I’m not doing him any injustice when I mention the fact.”

“Who was that?”

“Brabazon,” was the startling reply, and the other stared at him in astonishment.

“You don’t mean Brabazon of Brabazon’s Bank?”
===Chinese===
“来信收到了，弗鲁瓦扬先生，”小个子律师说，“眼下我可以告诉您：这些钞票，怕是一张也走不了寻常的路子。”他顿了顿，舔舔嘴唇，目光越过弗鲁瓦扬先生望向别处。“头号的大‘窝主’已经销声匿迹了，所以我点破这一节，不算冤枉他。”

“那是谁？”

“布拉巴宗。”这答话出人意料，对方愕然瞪着他。

“您莫不是说，布拉巴宗银行的那个布拉巴宗？”

===Original===
“Yes, I do,” said Heggitt, nodding. “I should say he did a bigger business in stolen money than any other man in London. You see, it could pass through his bank without anybody being the wiser, and as he did a lot of business abroad and was constantly changing and re-changing money for export, he got away with it. We knew who was fencing it. At least, when I say we knew,” he corrected himself, “we had a shrewd suspicion. As officers of the court, we should, of course, have notified the authorities had we been certain. I thought it better to call to explain to you that it is going to be a very difficult job to trace this money. Most stolen notes are passed on racecourses, but quite a considerable number find their way abroad, where it is a much simpler matter to change them, and where they are ever so much more difficult to trace. You say it was the Crimson Circle who did it?”

“Do you know them?” asked Froyant quickly.

The lawyer shook his head.

“I have never had any dealings with them at all,” he said, “but, of course, I knew about them, and enough to know that they are clever people. It is likely that this man Brabazon has been doing their work, consciously or unconsciously. In that case they might find a difficulty in disposing of the stuff, for a banknote ‘fence’ is one of the hardest to find. What am I to do when I track one of these notes and have discovered the person who passed it?”
===Chinese===
“正是他。”赫吉特点着头说，“要我说，经手贼赃钞票的生意，全伦敦数他做得最大。您想，这些钱打他自家银行里过账，谁也瞧不出破绽；他海外生意又做得不少，为了出口汇兑，成天把银钱兑来换去，他这才一路瞒天过海。窝赃的是谁，我们心里有数。至少，”他自行更正道，“我说有数，其实是八九不离十的疑心。身为法庭官员，倘若真拿实了，我们自然早该报知当局。我想还是亲自登门，向您说明这桩差事何等棘手为好。贼赃钞票多半在赛马场上脱手，可也有相当一批流到了国外——在那边兑换起来容易得多，追查起来也就难上加难。您说，干这票的是红圈？”

“您了解他们这伙人？”弗鲁瓦扬紧着问。

律师摇了摇头。

“我跟他们素来没有任何往来，”他说，“不过他们的事我自然有所耳闻，而且知道得足够清楚——那是一伙精明人。这位布拉巴宗，很可能一直在替他们办差，有心也罢，无意也罢。若果真如此，他们要出手这批货色就难免遇到难处，因为钞票‘窝主’属最难寻访的一类。等我追到其中一张钞票、查明经手把它花出去的人，我该怎么办？”

===Original===
“I want you to notify me at once,” said Froyant, “and nobody else. You understand that this is a matter on which my life may hang, and if by any chance the Crimson Circle get to know that I am trying to recover the money it will be a very serious thing for me.”

The lawyer agreed.

The Crimson Circle apparently interested him, for he lingered, and skilfully plied his employer with questions without Mr. Froyant realising that he was being pumped.

“They are something new in criminals,” he said. “In Italy, where the Black Hand thrives, the demand for money, followed by a threat of death, is quite a common occurrence, but I should not have thought it possible in this country. The most amazing thing of all is that the Crimson Circle holds together. I should imagine,” he said thoughtfully, “that there is only one man in it, and that he employs a very considerable number of people unknown to one another and each having his particular job to perform. Otherwise he would have been betrayed a long time ago. It is only the fact that the people serving him do not know him that makes it possible for him to carry on.”
===Chinese===
“我要你立刻通知我，”弗鲁瓦扬说，“而且只通知我一个。你要明白，这件事也许系着我的性命；万一红圈得知我在设法追回这笔钱，对我就是一件非同小可的祸事。”

律师依允了。

红圈显然引起了他的兴味，因为他磨磨蹭蹭不肯告辞，又手段老到地拿话去套雇主，弗鲁瓦扬先生全然不觉自己正被人盘出了底细。

“这伙罪犯是桩新事物。”他说，“在黑手帮横行的意大利，先索钱财、继以死胁，本是司空见惯的事；可在咱们这个国家，我原以为绝无可能。最令人称奇的是，红圈竟能抱成一团、始终不散。依我推想，”他若有所思地说，“这里头其实只有一个人，他手下雇着为数甚多的一批人马，彼此互不相识，各人各有各人的差事。不然，他早就被人出卖了。正因为替他办事的人都不认得他，他这营生才做得下去。”

===Original===
He took up his hat.

“By the way, did you know Felix Marl? A client of ours is under charge of burgling his house. Mr. Barnet. You may not have heard of him.”

Mr. Froyant had not heard of “Flush” Barnet, but he knew Marl, and Marl interested him almost as much as the Crimson Circle interested the lawyer.

“I knew Marl. Why do you ask?”

The lawyer smiled.
===Chinese===
他拿起帽子。

“对了，您认识菲利克斯·马尔吗？我们有位主顾，眼下正被控盗窃他的住宅。巴尼特先生。您也许没听说过此人。”

弗鲁瓦扬先生确乎没听说过“弗拉什”·巴尼特，可马尔他是认识的；而马尔勾起他的兴味，几乎不亚于红圈勾起这位律师的兴味。

“我认识马尔。你问这个做什么？”

律师莞尔一笑。

===Original===
“A strange character,” he said. “A remarkable character in many ways. He was a member of the gang engaged in frauds on French banks. I suppose you didn’t know that? His lawyer came to see me today. Apparently a Mrs. Marl has turned up to claim his property, and she has told the whole story. He and a man named Lightman made a fortune in France until they were caught. Marl would have been sent to the guillotine, only he turned State’s evidence. Lightman, I believe, went to the knife.”

“What a charming man Mr. Marl must have been!” said Mr. Froyant ironically.

The little lawyer smiled.

“What charming people we all are when our lives are laid bare!” he said, and Mr. Froyant resented the implied censure, for it was his boast that his life was a book. He might have added in truth a bankbook.
===Chinese===
“一个怪人。”他说，“一个在许多方面都了不得的人物。他曾是那个专对法国银行行骗的团伙中的一员。我想您原先并不知道吧？他的律师今天来见过我。看样子，一位马尔太太现身出来，要认领他的财产，还把全部底细和盘托出。他跟一个名叫利特曼（Lightman）的人在法国发了一注大财，直到东窗事发。马尔本来要被送上断头台，只因他倒戈作了国王的证人。至于利特曼，我记得是上了铡刀。”

“马尔先生当年准是位可爱的人物！”弗鲁瓦扬先生讥诮道。

小个子律师微微一笑。

“把一生的底细统统摊开来，咱们谁不是体面人物啊！”他说。弗鲁瓦扬先生对这话里含沙射影的责难老大不痛快，因为他向来夸口，自己的一生是本书。其实他满可以如实再补上一句——还是本银行存折。

===Original===
So Brabazon was a dealer in stolen notes and Marl a convicted murderer! Mr. Froyant wondered how Marl managed to escape from his term of imprisonment, which must have been a severe one, and he inwardly rejoiced that his business relationships with the deceased had not ended even more disastrously than they had.

He dressed and went to his club to dine, and his car was running into Pall Mall when a hoarding poster showed under the light of a lamp and reminded him of the unpleasant fact that he was a fifty-thousand pounds poorer man that night than he had been in the morning.

“Ten thousand reward!” he muttered. “Bah! Who is going to turn King’s evidence? I don’t suppose even Brabazon would dare.”

But he did not know Brabazon.
===Chinese===
原来布拉巴宗是个买卖贼赃钞票的贩子，马尔是个定过罪的凶犯！弗鲁瓦扬先生纳闷，马尔当年是怎么从服刑中脱身的——那刑期必定不轻；他又暗自庆幸，自己同这位亡故者的生意往来，收场总算没有更惨。

他换好衣裳，上俱乐部进晚餐。汽车正驶入蓓尔美尔街（Pall Mall）时，一张广告牌上的招贴在路灯灯影里映入眼帘，提醒他一桩败兴的事实：这天夜里，他已比早晨出门时穷了五万英镑。

“一万镑的赏格！”他咕哝道，“呸！谁肯倒戈去作国王的证人？我看连布拉巴宗也没这个胆子。”

可是，他并不了解布拉巴宗。

## XXV / 二十五

===Original===
The Tenant of River House
===Chinese===

河畔宅的房客

===Original===
Mr. Brabazon sat in a chill upper room of River House, eating slowly a large portion of bread and cheese. He wore the dress suit he was wearing when the warning came to him, and he was a ludicrous figure in the smartly-fitting, but now soiled and dusty garb. His white shirt was grey with the grime of the house, he was collarless, and his general air of dissipation was heightened by the stubbly beard that decorated his face.

He finished his repast, opened the window carefully and threw out the remnants of bread, and passing through the trapdoor, he descended the ladder and made his way to the big kitchen at the back of the house. He had neither soap nor towel, but he made some attempt to wash himself without their aid, utilising one of the two handkerchiefs he had brought with him to the house in his flight. With the exception of the clothes he stood up in, an overcoat and the soft felt hat he had seized when he made his escape, he was quite unequipped for this undesirable adventure.

The provisions which the mystery man had brought the night after he had reached his hiding place were almost exhausted (he had spent twenty-four hours without any food whatever, but in his agitation had not realised the fact until the stranger arrived carrying a basket of foodstuffs). As to his nerves, they were almost gone. A week spent in that hovel without communion with man, with the knowledge that the police were searching for him, and that a long term of imprisonment would automatically follow his capture, had played havoc with his placid features, and to the solitude had been added the terror of a search.
===Chinese===

布拉巴宗先生坐在河畔宅一间阴冷的顶楼房间里，慢条斯理地啃着一大块面包加干酪。他身上还是接到警告那晚穿的那套晚礼服；这身原本裁剪合体的行头如今又脏又蒙尘，穿在他身上活脱脱一副滑稽相。白衬衫叫屋里的污垢染成了灰色，领子也没了一条，满脸胡茬点缀着那张脸，愈发衬出他一身落魄潦倒的气息。

他吃完了这顿饭，小心推开窗户，把剩下的面包屑扔了出去，然后钻过活板门，顺梯子下来，走到宅子后部的大厨房里。他既没有肥皂也没有毛巾，只得凑合着洗了洗——用的是他仓皇出逃时随身带进宅子的两条手帕中的一条。除了身上这一套，外加逃命时顺手抓来的一件大衣和一顶软毡帽，对于这场谁也不情愿的历险，他可说是毫无装备。

那个神秘人在他躲进藏身处的第二天夜里送来的吃食，已经快见底了（他整整二十四个钟头粒米未进，可直到那陌生人提着一篮食物出现，心慌意乱的他才意识到这一点）。至于他的神经，也几乎垮光了。在那间破屋里困了整整一个星期，与世隔绝，又明知警察正在四处搜捕、一旦落网少不得一场长期监禁——这早已把他那副安详的面容折腾得不成样子；孤寂之外，又添上了随时可能遭人搜查的恐怖。

===Original===
He had shrunk in a corner behind a door which opened to the inner room leading to the garret whilst the detective had explored the room. The memory of Derrick Yale’s visit was a nightmare.

He settled himself down in the old chair that he had found in the house, to spend yet another night. The man whose warning had sent him flying to cover must come soon, and must bring more food. Brabazon was dozing when he heard the sound of a key put into the lock below and jumped up. He tiptoed carefully to the trapdoor and lifted it and then he heard the booming voice of the stranger.

“Come down,” it said, and he obeyed.
===Chinese===

那位侦探搜查屋子的时候，他缩在一扇门背后的角落里——那扇门通向里屋，里屋再通阁楼。德里克·耶尔来访的那一幕，回想起来仍是一场噩梦。

他在宅子里寻着的那把旧椅子上安顿下来，准备再熬一个夜晚。那个一句话吓得他仓皇躲藏的人，想必很快就会再来，也必定会再带吃食来。布拉巴宗正打盹，忽听得楼下钥匙插进锁孔的声响，霍地跳了起来。他蹑手蹑脚地摸到活板门边，掀开门板，随即听见了那陌生人低沉洪亮的嗓音。

“下来。”那声音说。他照办了。

===Original===
The previous interview had been in the passage where the darkness seemed thicker than anywhere else in the house. He had accustomed himself to the darkness and walked down the rickety stairs without mishap.

“Stay where you are,” said the voice. “I have brought you some food and clothing. You will find everything you need. You had better shave yourself and make yourself presentable.”

“Where am I going?” asked Brabazon.

“I have taken a berth for you on a steamer leaving Victoria Dock tomorrow for New Zealand. You will find your passport papers and ticket in the grip. Now listen. You are to leave your moustache, or what there is of it unshaven, and shave your eyebrows. They are the most conspicuous features of your face.”
===Chinese===

上一次会面是在过道里，那儿的黑暗仿佛比宅子里任何地方都更浓稠。他早已习惯了黑暗，稳稳当当地走下那道摇摇晃晃的楼梯，没出半点岔子。

“站在原地别动，”那声音说，“我给你带了些吃的和衣裳。你要的东西一应俱全。你最好把脸刮一刮，把自己拾掇得像个人样。”

“我要上哪儿去？”布拉巴宗问。

“我给你在一艘轮船上订了铺位，明天从维多利亚码头（Victoria Dock）启航去新西兰。护照文件和船票都在提包里。现在听着。你的小胡子——或者说嘴上留着的那几根——不要刮，眉毛倒要刮掉。它们是你脸上最扎眼的特征。”

===Original===
Brabazon wondered when this man had seen him. Mechanically his hand stole up to his shaggy eyebrows and mentally he agreed with the mysterious visitor.

“I have not brought you any money,” the voice went on. “You have sixty thousand which you stole from Marl—you closed his account, forging his name to a cheque, believing that I would settle with him—as I did.”

“Who are you?” asked Brabazon.

“I am the Crimson Circle,” was the reply. “Why do you ask that question? You have met me before.”

“Yes, of course,” Brabazon muttered. “I think this place is driving me mad. When may I leave this house?”
===Chinese===

布拉巴宗纳闷，这人究竟是什么时候见过自己的。他的手机械地摸上那两道浓密的眉毛，心里倒也暗暗承认这位神秘访客说得在理。

“我没有给你带钱来，”那声音接着说，“你自己手里有六万镑，那是你从马尔那儿偷的——你伪造他的签名开了张支票，结清了他的账户，满以为我会替你把这笔账料理干净——我也确实料理了。”

“你是谁？”布拉巴宗问。

“我就是红圈。”对方答道，“问这个做什么？你从前见过我。”

“是啊，那当然。”布拉巴宗咕哝道，“我看这地方快把我逼疯了。我什么时候才能离开这宅子？”

===Original===
“You may leave tomorrow. Wait until nightfall. Your ship leaves on the following morning, but you can get on board tomorrow night.”

“But they will be watching the ship,” pleaded Brabazon. “Don’t you think it is too dangerous?”

“There is no danger for you,” was the reply. “Give me your money.”

“My money?” gasped the banker, turning pale.

“Give me your money.” There was an ominous note in the voice that spoke in the darkness, and tremblingly Brabazon obeyed.
===Chinese===

“明天就可以走。等到天黑再动身。船是后天早上开，不过明天夜里你就能上船了。”

“可是他们会盯住那条船的。”布拉巴宗哀求道，“你不觉得这太危险了吗？”

“你不会有什么危险。”对方答道，“把你的钱给我。”

“我的钱？”银行家倒吸一口冷气，脸色发白。

“把你的钱给我。”黑暗中响起的声音里带着一丝凶兆，布拉巴宗哆哆嗦嗦地照办了。

===Original===
Two large packets of money passed into the gloved hand of the visitor, and then:

“Here, take this.”

“This” was a thinner wad of notes, and the sensitive fingers of the banker told him that they were new.

“You can change them when you get abroad,” said the man.

“Couldn’t I leave tonight?” Brabazon’s teeth were chattering now. “This place gives me the horrors.”
===Chinese===

两大包钞票递进了来客戴手套的手心，接着便是：

“喏，拿着这个。”

“这个”是一沓薄些的钞票；银行家的指头灵敏，一捻便知都是新票。

“到了国外你再把它们兑开。”那人说。

“难道今晚就走不成？”布拉巴宗的牙齿这时已格格打颤，“这地方叫我心里直发毛。”

===Original===
The Crimson Circle was evidently thinking, for it was some time before he spoke.

“If you wish,” he said, “but remember you are taking a risk. Now go upstairs.”

The order was sharp and peremptory, and meekly Brabazon obeyed.

He heard the door close, and peering through the dusty windows, he saw the dark shadow stalk along the path and disappear into the darkness. Presently he heard the gate click. The man was gone.
===Chinese===

红圈显然在思量，隔了好一会儿才开口。

“随你的便，”他说，“不过记住，你这是在冒险。上楼去吧。”

这道吩咐又急又专断，布拉巴宗乖乖地照办了。

他听见底下大门合上，便凑到积尘的窗前窥望，只见一条黑影大步沿着小径走去，没入黑暗之中。不多时，他听见园门咔哒一响。人走了。

===Original===
Brabazon groped for the bag which the other had left and, finding it, carried it to the kitchen. Here he could show a light without fear of detection, and he lit one of the scraps of candle he had discovered in his search of the house during the week.

The stranger had not exaggerated when he said that the bag contained all that Brabazon required. But the banker’s first thought was to examine the money which the other had put into his hand. They were notes of all series and all numbers. His own had been in a series, and yet they were new. He looked at them curiously. He knew that new banknotes were not usually issued higgledy-piggledy, and then he guessed the reason. The Crimson Circle had blackmailed somebody and had asked that the notes should not be numbered consecutively. He put the money down and began to change.

It was a very smart Brabazon who stepped cautiously through the gates carrying his bag an hour later, and yet so remarkable was the change which the shaved eyebrows had made, that when, at eleven o’clock that night, he passed one of the many detective officers who were looking for him, he was unrecognised.
===Chinese===

布拉巴宗摸黑去找那人留下的提包，找着以后拎进了厨房。在这儿点灯不怕被人察觉，他便点起一支蜡烛头——那是他这个星期翻遍全屋搜寻出来的。

那人说提包里布拉巴宗用得着的东西一应俱全，倒没有夸张。但银行家头一件事，还是查验那人塞到他手里的那笔钱。各种版别、各种号码的钞票都有。他自己那批本是连号的，可这沓也一样是新钞。他好奇地端详着。他知道新钞票发行时向来不会这样乱七八糟不成套，随即便猜出了缘故：红圈准是勒索过什么人，还特意要求交来的钞票不要连号。他把钱放下，动手更衣。

一个钟头之后，一个衣冠楚楚的布拉巴宗提着提包，小心翼翼地迈出了园门。刮掉眉毛带来的变化实在非同小可——当晚十一点，他从四下搜捕他的众多探员中一位身边走过时，竟没有被人认出来。

---

===Original===
He had engaged a room in a small hotel near Euston Station, and went to bed. It was the first night of untroubled sleep he had enjoyed for over a week.

The next day he spent in his room, not caring to trust himself abroad in daylight, but in the evening, after a solitary meal served in his sitting-room, he went out to take the air. He was gaining in confidence, and was now satisfied that he could pass the scrutiny of the ship detective. He chose the less frequented streets and was passing near the Museum when he saw a bill newly pasted on the hoarding, and stopped to read it.

As he read, an idea took shape. Ten thousand pounds and a free pardon! It was by no means sure that he would escape in the morning; more likely was it that he would be detected, and at best what would his life be? The life of a hunted dog, for which even his money would not compensate him. Ten thousand pounds and freedom! And nobody knew about the money that he had tricked from Felix Marl’s estate. He would put that in a safe deposit in the morning, go straight to police headquarters with information which he felt sure must lead to the Crimson Circle’s undoing.
===Chinese===

他在尤斯顿车站（Euston Station）附近一家小旅馆里开了个房间，上床睡了。这是一个多星期以来，他头一回睡了个安稳觉。

第二天他整天待在房间里，白天不敢抛头露面；到了晚上，起居室里独自用过一餐饭，便出门透透气。他的胆气渐渐壮了，这时已笃信自己过得了船上侦探那一关盘查。他专拣行人稀少的街道走，路过博物馆附近时，瞧见围板上新贴了一张告示，便停下脚步读了起来。

读着读着，一个主意在他心里成形了。一万镑赏金，外加特赦！明天一早出逃，未必就一定能脱身；更大的可能是被当场识破。退一万步说，就算逃了出去，往后的日子又算什么？无非是丧家之犬的日子——他的钱再多，也补不回这个。一万镑，外加自由！何况他从菲利克斯·马尔遗产里坑来的那笔钱，还没有一个人知道。明天早上先把那笔钱存进银行保险库，再直奔警察总部，献上一条情报——他笃信这条情报必然送红圈走上末路。

===Original===
“I’ll do it,” he said aloud.

“I think you’re very wise.”

The voice was at his elbow and he swung round.

A little, stocky man had walked noiselessly behind him in his rubber-soled shoes, and Brabazon recognised him instantly.

“Inspector Parr,” he gasped.

“That’s right,” said the inspector. “Now, Mr. Brabazon, will you come a little walk with me, or are you going to make trouble?”
===Chinese===

“就这么办。”他出声说道。

“我看你这样想是很明智的。”

话音就在他肘边响起，他猛地转过身去。

一个矮壮敦实的人穿着胶底鞋，悄没声儿地走到了他背后，布拉巴宗一眼就认出了他。

“帕尔探长。”他倒吸一口气道。

“正是。”探长说，“那么，布拉巴宗先生，你是肯乖乖陪我走一段呢，还是要给我添麻烦？”

===Original===
As they went into the police-station, a woman came out, and the pallid Brabazon failed to recognise his former clerk. He stood in the steel pen whilst the story of his iniquities was told in the cold, official language of the warrant.

“You can save yourself a lot of trouble, Mr. Brabazon,” said Inspector Parr, “by telling me the truth. I know where you are staying—at Bright’s Hotel in the Euston Road. You arrived there late last night and your passage is booked in the name of Thomson to New Zealand by the *Itinga*, which is due to leave Victoria Dock tomorrow morning.”

“Good God!” said the startled Brabazon. “How did you know that?”

But here Inspector Parr did not inform him.
===Chinese===

他们走进警察局时，一个女人正从里面出来，面无人色的布拉巴宗竟没认出这位从前自己手下的女职员。他站在铁栅囚栏里，听任人家用逮捕令上冷冰冰的公文腔调，历数他的种种罪状。

“你跟我说实话，布拉巴宗先生，”帕尔探长说，“就能省去自己许多麻烦。我知道你落脚在哪儿——尤斯顿路的布赖特旅馆（Bright’s Hotel）。你是昨天深夜到的，舱位是用‘汤姆森’（Thomson）的名字订的，去新西兰，搭“伊廷加”号（*Itinga*），定于明晨从维多利亚码头启航。”

“天哪！”布拉巴宗大惊失色，“你是怎么知道的？”

这一点，帕尔探长倒没有说给他听。

===Original===
Brabazon did not intend lying. He told everything he knew. All that had happened from the moment he was called by telephone and told to make a getaway, until he was arrested.

“So you were in the house all the time?” said the inspector thoughtfully. “How did you come to escape Mr. Yale’s search?”

“Oh, was it Yale?” said Brabazon. “I thought it was you. There was an inner room—just a little storehouse, I think it was in the old times—I got behind the door and hid. He came almost to the door. I nearly died with fright.”

“So Yale was right again. You were there!” said the inspector speaking half to himself. “Now, what are you going to do about it, Brabazon?”
===Chinese===

布拉巴宗没打算撒谎。他把知道的一切都倒了出来：从接到电话、被吩咐赶紧脱身逃走的那一刻起，直到被捕为止，桩桩件件，一五一十。

“这么说，你自始至终都藏在那宅子里？”探长若有所思地说，“你是怎么躲过耶尔先生那番搜查的？”

“哦，那是耶尔吗？”布拉巴宗说，“我还当是你呢。那儿有间里屋——看那样子，从前八成只是个小库房——我躲到门背后藏了起来。他差不多走到那扇门跟前了。我差点没吓死。”

“这么说，又是耶尔说对了。你果然在那儿！”探长半是自语道，“好了，往后你打算怎么办，布拉巴宗？”

===Original===
“I’m going to tell you all I know about the Crimson Circle, and I think I can give you information which will lead to his arrest. But you’ll have to be smart.”

He was recovering something of his old pomposity, Parr observed.

“I told you that he exchanged my notes for his, and his notes for mine. I’m sure he did that because he was afraid of the numbers being taken, but my notes were in a series—series E.19, and I can give you the number of every one of them,” he went on easily. “He wouldn’t change the stuff he got.”

“That was Froyant’s money, I think,” said the inspector. “Yes, go on.”

“He dare not change that, but he will change mine. Don’t you see what a chance this gives to you?”
===Chinese===

“我打算把我知道的关于红圈的一切统统告诉你，我想我能供出一条情报，管保能把他捉拿归案。不过你们手底下可得麻利点。”

帕尔瞧在眼里：他往日那副妄自尊大的架子，正一点点回到身上。

“我不是跟你说了嘛，他拿他的票子换了我的，把我的票子换了去。我敢肯定，他这么干是怕号码被登记在案。可我那批票子是连号的——E.19 字头——每一张的号码我都报得出来。”他接着从容不迫地说，“他自己到手的那批票子，是绝不肯出手的。”

“我看，那就是弗鲁瓦扬的那笔钱。”探长说，“嗯，接着讲。”

“他不敢动那笔，可我那批他迟早要出手。你还看不出这是给你们送上门的机会吗？”

===Original===
The inspector was a little sceptical. Nevertheless, after Brabazon had been locked in the cell, he called up Froyant on the phone and told him as much of what had happened as was necessary for him to know.

“You’ve got the money?” said Froyant eagerly. “Come up to the house at once.”

“I’ll bring it up to the house with pleasure,” replied Parr, “but I feel I ought to warn you that this is not your money, although it is the actual cash that was transferred by you to the Crimson Circle.”
===Chinese===

探长将信将疑。不过，等布拉巴宗被关进牢房之后，他还是给弗鲁瓦扬挂了电话，把事情的经过拣必要的说了说。

“钱到你手里了？”弗鲁瓦扬急切地说，“马上到塔楼宅来一趟。”

“我很乐意把它送到塔楼宅来。”帕尔答道，“不过我觉得应当提醒你一句：这已经不是你的钱了——尽管它实实在在就是你交到红圈手里的那笔现款。”

===Original===
Later on, in Mr. Froyant’s presence, he explained the situation. That spare man made no attempt to hide his disappointment, for he seemed to think that in whatever circumstances the money was recovered, he was entitled to claim. After a while Inspector Parr got him into a more reasonable frame of mind. Froyant was talking quite calmly on the matter, when he suddenly broke off with the question:

“Have you the numbers of the notes which Brabazon handed to him?”

“They are easy to remember,” said Parr, “they belong to a series,” and he recited the numbers, Mr. Froyant making a rapid note on his desk-pad.
===Chinese===

稍后，他当着弗鲁瓦扬先生的面把情况解释了一番。这位清瘦的人毫不掩饰自己的失望，他似乎认为，那笔钱不论是在什么情形下追回的，他都有权索取。过了一阵子，帕尔探长才把他说得心平气和了些。弗鲁瓦扬正平心静气地谈着这件事，忽然话锋一转，问道：

“布拉巴宗交给他的那些钞票，号码你都有吗？”

“好记得很，”帕尔说，“都是连号的。”接着他便一一报出号码，弗鲁瓦扬先生飞快地记在案头的便笺簿上。

## XXVI / 二十六

===Original===
The Bottle of Chloroform
===Chinese===
一瓶氯仿

===Original===
Thalia Drummond was writing a letter when her visitor arrived, and of the many people whom Thalia expected to call, Millie Macroy was the last. The girl looked ill and tired, but she was not so far from human that she could not stand and admire the dainty drawing-room into which Thalia showed her, her servant having gone home for the night.

“Why this is a palace, kid,” she said, and regarded Thalia with reluctant admiration. “You know how to do it all right, better than poor ‘Flush.’”

“And how is the elegant ‘Flush’?” asked Thalia coolly.

Millie Macroy’s face darkened.

“See here,” she said roughly, “I don’t want any kind of talk about ‘Flush’ in that tone, do you understand? He is where *you* ought to be. You were in it as well as him.”
===Chinese===
塔莉娅·德拉蒙德正写着信，客人到了。在塔莉娅料想会登门的许多人当中，米莉·麦克罗伊是最不可能来的一个。这姑娘面带病容、一脸倦色，但还没有不近人情到那个地步——望着塔莉娅引自己进来的这间精巧雅致的客厅，她仍能站在那里暗自称羡。女仆已经回家过夜去了。

“嗬，这可真是座宫殿哪，丫头，”她说，带着几分不情愿的艳羡打量塔莉娅。“这一套你可真会经营，比可怜的‘弗拉什’强多了。”

“那位风雅的‘弗拉什’可好？”塔莉娅冷冷地问。

米莉·麦克罗伊的脸沉了下来。

“你听着，”她粗声粗气地说，“我不爱听谁用这种腔调谈论‘弗拉什’，明白吗？他待的地方，本该是*你*待的。那桩买卖，你跟他一样有份。”

===Original===
“Don’t be silly. Take off your hat and sit down. Why, it’s like old times seeing you, Macroy.”

The girl grumbled something under her breath, but accepted the invitation.

“It is about ‘Flush’ I want to see you,” she said. “There’s some talk of framing a murder charge against him, but you know he didn’t commit any murder.”

“I know? Why should I know?” asked Thalia. “I didn’t even know that he was in the house until I read the newspapers in the morning—how wonderfully clever they are on the Press to get news so red-hot.”
===Chinese===
“别犯傻。摘了帽子坐下吧。嗬，见到你可真像回到了从前，麦克罗伊。”

姑娘嘴里咕哝了句什么，但还是依言坐下了。

“我来找你，就是为了‘弗拉什’的事，”她说。“外面有话，要给他罗织一桩凶杀罪名。可你知道，他没有杀过人。”

“我知道？我凭什么要知道？”塔莉娅问。“早晨看了报，我才知道他当时就在那宅子里——报界那些人真是神通广大，这么滚烫火辣的新闻也能弄到手，妙极了。”

===Original===
Milly Macroy had not come to discuss the enterprise of the Press. She drove straight into her subject, which was, as Thalia had expected, “Flush” Barnet and his immediate prospects.

“Drummond, I’m not going to quarrel with you,” she said.

“I’m glad of that,” said Thalia. “I can’t exactly see what there is to quarrel about, anyway.”

“That may or may not be,” said Miss Macroy ironically. “The point is, what are you going to do for ‘Flush’? You know all these swells, and you’re working for that swine Yale,” she almost hissed. “It was Yale who put Parr up to the Marisburg Place job; Parr hadn’t got brains enough to think it out for himself. Were you working with Yale all the time?”
===Chinese===
米莉·麦克罗伊可不是来探讨报界那番事业的。她单刀直入切进正题——果然不出塔莉娅所料，正题就是“弗拉什”·巴尼特和他眼下的处境。

“德拉蒙德，我不想跟你吵架。”她说。

“那我很高兴，”塔莉娅说。“反正我实在看不出咱们有什么可吵的。”

“也许是，也许不是。”麦克罗伊小姐讥讽道。“要紧的是，你打算为‘弗拉什’做点什么？你认识这一帮子大人物，又替那头猪猡耶尔当差——”她说这话时几乎是咬牙切齿。“马里斯堡广场那一出，就是耶尔给帕尔出的点子；靠帕尔自己那个脑子，可想不出这一手。你从头到尾都在跟耶尔搭伙办事吗？”

===Original===
“Don’t make me laugh,” said Thalia scornfully. “It’s certainly true I am working for Yale, if writing his letters and tidying his desk is work. But what swells are you talking about? And what can I do for ‘Flush’ Barnet?”

“You can go to Inspector Parr and tell him the old, old story,” said Macroy. “I’ve got it all worked out; you can say that ‘Flush’ was sweet on you, saw you go into the house and followed, and couldn’t get out.”

“What about my young reputation?” asked the girl coolly. “No, Milly Macroy, you’ve got to think up something prettier and, anyway, I don’t think they’re making a charge for murder against him, from what Derrick Yale said this morning.”
===Chinese===
“别逗我笑了。”塔莉娅不屑地说。“我确实在替耶尔做事——如果替他写写信、理理桌子也算做事的话。可你说的是哪些大人物？我又能为‘弗拉什’·巴尼特做些什么？”

“你可以去找帕尔探长，把那套老掉牙的说辞讲给他听，”麦克罗伊说。“我都替你编排好了：你就说‘弗拉什’早就迷上了你，看见你进了那宅子便跟了进去，结果脱不开身。”

“那我年轻姑娘家的名声还要不要了？”姑娘冷冷地问。“不行，米莉·麦克罗伊，你得另编一套体面点儿的。再说了，照德里克·耶尔今天早晨的话听来，我看他们并不打算以凶杀的罪名起诉他。”

===Original===
She rose and walked slowly across the room, her hands clasped behind her.

“Besides, what interest have I in your young man? Why should I take the trouble of speaking for him?”

“I’ll tell you why.”

Miss Macroy rose, her hands on her hips, and glared at the girl.

“Because when the Brabazon case comes on, there’s nothing to prevent me going into the box and saying a few plain words about what you did in the way of quick money-getting when you were Brab’s secretary. Ah! That’s made you jump, miss!”
===Chinese===
她站起身来，双手交叠在背后，缓缓踱过房间。

“再说了，你那位年轻情郎与我有什么相干？我何必费这个口舌替他说话？”

“我告诉你为什么。”

麦克罗伊小姐站起身，双手叉腰，恶狠狠地瞪着姑娘。

“等布拉巴宗案开庭的时候，谁也拦不住我上证人席，就你当布拉布的秘书那阵子怎样捞快钱的勾当，直截了当说上几句。啊哈！这一下可叫你跳起来了，小姐！”

===Original===
“When the Brabazon case comes on!” said the girl slowly. “Why? Have they caught Brabazon?”

“They pinched him tonight,” answered the girl triumphantly. “Parr did it: I was up at the police station making inquiries about some money that ‘Flush’ left over for me, when they brought him in.”

“Brabazon a prisoner,” said Thalia slowly. “Poor old Brab!”

Macroy was watching her through her half-closed lids. She had never liked Thalia Drummond, and now she hated her. She feared her too, for there was something sinister in her very coolness. Presently Thalia spoke.
===Chinese===
“等布拉巴宗案开庭！”姑娘缓缓地说。“怎么？他们抓住布拉巴宗了？”

“今晚把他逮进去了。”那姑娘得意洋洋地答道。“是帕尔动的手：我正在警察局打听‘弗拉什’留给我的那笔钱，他们就把他押进来了。”

“布拉巴宗成了阶下囚。”塔莉娅缓缓地说。“可怜的老布拉布！”

麦克罗伊眯缝着眼打量她。她从来就不喜欢塔莉娅·德拉蒙德，如今更是恨透了她。她也怕她，因为这姑娘那份冷静本身就透着一股阴森。过了一会儿，塔莉娅开口了。

===Original===
“I’ll do what I can for ‘Flush’ Barnet,” she said. “Not because I’m scared of your going into the box—that’s the part of the police court where you’ll be least at home, Macroy—but because the poor little wretch was innocent of the murder.”

Miss Macroy swallowed something at this description of her lover.

“I’ll talk to Yale in the morning. I can’t be sure it will do any good, but I’ll get a heart-to-heart talk with him if he gives me a chance.”

“Thank you,” said Miss Macroy, a little more graciously, and proceeded to admire the flat in conventional language.
===Chinese===
“‘弗拉什’·巴尼特的事，我会尽力而为，”她说。“倒不是怕你上证人席——在警务法庭上，那一处可是你最待不自在的地方，麦克罗伊——而是因为那个可怜的小东西确实没有犯下那桩凶杀。”

听着人家这样形容自己的情郎，麦克罗伊小姐把涌上来的话咽了回去。

“明天早晨我会跟耶尔谈谈。不敢保准有什么用处，不过只要他给我机会，我就跟他推心置腹地谈一回。”

“谢谢你。”麦克罗伊小姐的口气客气了几分，接着便用一套惯常的客套话夸赞起这所公寓来。

===Original===
Thalia showed her from room to room.

“What’s this place?”

“The kitchen,” said Thalia, but made no attempt to open the door. The girl looked at her suspiciously.

“Have you got a friend?” she asked, and before Thalia could stop her she had opened the door and walked in.

The kitchen was a small one and empty. The electric light was burning, which suggested to Miss Macroy that the girl had left the kitchen to answer her knock.
===Chinese===
塔莉娅领着她一间屋一间屋地看。

“这是什么地方？”

“厨房。”塔莉娅说，却没有要开门的意思。姑娘狐疑地打量她。

“你这儿藏着个相好吧？”她问。塔莉娅还没来得及拦阻，她已经推开门走了进去。

厨房很小，空无一人。电灯还亮着——这情形落在麦克罗伊小姐眼里，说明姑娘是刚离开厨房去应门的。

===Original===
Thalia could have smiled at the obvious disappointment on Milly Macroy’s face, but her inclination to amusement departed as Macroy walked to the sink and picked up a bottle.

“What is this?” said she, and read the label.

It was half-filled with a colourless liquid, and Miss Macroy did not attempt to take out the stopper. The label told her all she wanted to know.

“‘Chloroform and Ether,’” she read, looking at the girl. “Why have you been using chloroform?”
===Chinese===
瞧着米莉·麦克罗伊脸上那份明摆着的失望，塔莉娅本可以笑上一笑，可她那点想笑的意思，随着麦克罗伊走向水槽、拿起一只瓶子，顿时烟消云散了。

“这是什么？”她说着，念起瓶上的标签来。

瓶里盛着半瓶无色的液体。麦克罗伊小姐并没有去拔瓶塞——标签已经把想知道的一切都告诉她了。

“‘氯仿和乙醚。’”她念道，眼睛盯着姑娘。“你要氯仿做什么用？”

===Original===
Only for a second was Thalia taken aback, and then she laughed.

“Well, do you know, Milly Macroy,” she drawled, “when I think of poor ‘Flush’ Barnet in Brixton Gaol, I just have to sniff something to put him out of my mind.”

Macroy banged down the bottle on the table with a snort.

“You’re a bad lot, Thalia Drummond, and one of these days they’ll be waking you at eight o’clock, and ask you if you have any message for your friends.”

“And I shall reply,” said Thalia sweetly, “bury me next to ‘Flush’ Barnet, the eminent crook.”

Miss Milly Macroy did not think of a suitable retort until she was in the Marylebone Road, and then it came to her with annoying force that, for all her interview, Thalia Drummond had promised nothing.
===Chinese===
塔莉娅只错愕了一秒钟，随即笑了起来。

“唉，你说是不是，米莉·麦克罗伊，”她懒洋洋地说，“一想到可怜的‘弗拉什’·巴尼特蹲在布里克斯顿监狱（Brixton Gaol）里，我就非得嗅点什么，才能把他从脑子里赶出去。”

麦克罗伊哼了一声，砰地把瓶子掼在桌上。

“你是个坏坯，塔莉娅·德拉蒙德。早晚有一天，人家会清早八点钟把你叫醒，问你要不要给朋友们留句话。”

“那我就回答，”塔莉娅甜甜地说，“把我埋在‘弗拉什’·巴尼特旁边——那位大名鼎鼎的惯窃身边。”

米莉·麦克罗伊小姐直到走在了玛丽勒本路上，才想出一句合适的回敬话；可这时她也才带着恼人的力道恍然醒悟：这一场会面从头到尾，塔莉娅·德拉蒙德其实什么也没有答应。

## XXVII / 二十七

===Original===
Mr. Parr’s Mother
===Chinese===
帕尔先生的母亲

===Original===
Jack Beardmore had heard of Brabazon’s arrest, and went straight to police headquarters to see Mr. Parr.

He found that excellent gentleman had gone home.

“If it is important, Mr. Beardmore,” said the police clerk on duty, “you will find him at home in his house at Stamford Avenue.”
===Chinese===
杰克·比德莫尔听说了布拉巴宗被捕的消息，便径直赶到警察总部去见帕尔先生。

却发现那位可敬的先生已经回家了。

“要是事情要紧，比德莫尔先生，”值班的警务书记员说，“您去斯坦福德大道他家里，准能找到他。”

===Original===
Beyond his natural interest in the Crimson Circle and all that pertained thereto, Jack had no particular wish to see the inspector, and Derrick Yale had telephoned all that was known or could be told.

“Parr thinks this arrest may have an important development,” he said. “No, I haven’t seen Brabazon, but I accompany Parr tomorrow morning when he visits him.”
===Chinese===
杰克对红圈及其种种关涉本就怀有一份天生的关切，除此之外，他并无特别要见探长的理由，何况德里克·耶尔已经来过电话，把知道的、能说的统统说了。

“帕尔认为这次逮捕说不定会有重大进展，”他说，“不，我还没有见到布拉巴宗，不过明天上午他去看他时，我陪他一道去。”

===Original===
Yale, too, was apparently un-get-at-able; he had hinted that he had a theatre party that night, and Jack bent his steps homeward. He had sent his car away, for he felt he needed exercise to dissipate his energies, and as he crossed the gloomy park, taking a shortcut to his house, he found himself wondering what sort of a home life a man like Parr could have. He had never spoken about his family, and his mode of living outside of the police headquarters was almost as much of a mystery as that which he was trying to unravel.

Where was Stamford Avenue, he wondered. He had reached a deserted spot of the park, when he thought he heard footsteps behind him, and turned his head. He was not a nervous type, and ordinarily the sound of somebody walking in his rear would not have interested him sufficiently to make him turn. The path here skirted a dense thicket of rhododendrons. There was nobody in sight. Jack went on, quickening his pace.
===Chinese===
耶尔那边，显然也是寻不见人；他露过口风，当晚要招待一班人去看戏。杰克便抬步往家走。他已经把汽车打发走了，只觉得需要活动活动，把浑身的劲头发散一番。抄近道穿过那座黑沉沉的公园时，他不由得暗自琢磨：像帕尔这样的人，过的会是怎样一种家庭生活？他从没提起过自己的家人，他在警察总部之外的起居做派，几乎同他竭力侦破的那桩谜案一样神秘。

斯坦福德大道究竟在哪儿呢？他走到公园里一处人迹全无的地方，忽觉身后有脚步声，便回过头去。他不是那种神经过敏的人，寻常若有人跟在身后走路，还不足以引得他回头张望。这里的小径傍着一片茂密的杜鹃花丛，四下里不见一个人影。杰克继续前行，脚下加快了步子。

===Original===
He heard no more footsteps, but looking round he thought he saw a man walking on the grass by the side of the path. As Jack stopped he too halted. He was doubtful as to what he should do. To challenge the man might put him into an absurd position; there was no reason in the world why any good citizen should not walk in the park at night, or, for the matter of that, why they should not walk behind him anywhere at a respectable distance.

And then ahead of him he made out a slowly strolling figure, and heard the unmistakable “beat walk” of a policeman.

To his own amazement he felt relieved, and when he looked round, the figure that had followed him had disappeared. He tried to reconstruct his impression; whoever his tracker had been, he was smally made. At first Jack had thought it was a boy; perhaps some poor park beggar who was mustering up courage to approach him for the price of a night’s bed. It seemed absurd that he was glad to be out of the park, and to step into the well-lighted street, but it was the case.
===Chinese===
脚步声再没有响起，可他回头望去，仿佛瞧见一个人走在小径旁的草地上。杰克一停下，那人也站住了。他拿不定主意该怎么办。上前喝问，只怕会闹得自己下不来台；天底下本没有哪条道理，不许哪个正派的公民夜里在公园里散步；同样也没有哪条道理，不许人家在随便什么地方，隔着体面的距离跟在他身后走。

就在这时，他前方现出一个缓缓踱步的人影，他还听出了那绝不会认错的巡警“巡道”脚步声。

连他自己都暗暗称奇，心里竟松了一口气；待他再回过头，那个尾随他的人影已经无影无踪。他极力追想方才的印象：不管盯他梢的是谁，那人生得矮小。起先杰克还当是个男孩，也许是个穷酸的公园乞丐，正攒着勇气想凑上前去，讨几个钱好寻一夜的宿处。走出公园、踏上灯火通明的大街竟会让他觉得高兴——这看上去未免荒唐，可偏偏就是如此。

===Original===
He made an inquiry of a policeman.

“Stamford Avenue, sir? That bus you see over there will take you, or you can get there in a taxi in ten minutes.”

Jack stood for a long time before he called the taxicab. Mr. Parr would rightly resent this intrusion into his domestic privacy, and really he had no excuse to offer. But making up his mind of a sudden, he called a cab, and in a very short time was experiencing exactly the same doubts and misgivings before the door of Inspector Parr’s maisonette.
===Chinese===
他向一名警察问路。

“斯坦福德大道吗，先生？您瞧见的那辆公共汽车就到那儿；要不坐出租汽车，十分钟也就到了。”

杰克站了老半天，才招呼出租汽车。这样贸然搅扰人家的居家清净，帕尔先生完全有理由着恼，而他自己实在也拿不出什么登门的由头。可他忽然拿定了主意，叫来一辆车；没多大工夫，他已经站在帕尔探长那套独门小公寓（maisonette）门前，心里又翻腾起方才那一番疑虑不安，分毫不差。

===Original===
It was Parr himself who opened the door.

His face was naturally free from expression, and he neither showed surprise nor annoyance at the arrival of his late visitor.

“Come in, Mr. Beardmore,” he said. “I have just arrived, and am having supper. I suppose you’ve had your evening meal a long time ago.”

“Don’t let me interrupt you, Mr. Parr, only I was rather interested to hear that you had caught Brabazon, and I thought I’d come along.”
===Chinese===
开门的正是帕尔本人。

他那张脸天生不挂表情，对这位深夜来客的登门，既不见惊讶，也不见不快。

“请进，比德莫尔先生，”他说，“我也刚到家，正吃着晚饭。您想必早就用过晚餐了吧。”

“别让我搅了您，帕尔先生。只是听说你们抓住了布拉巴宗，我心里搁不下这事，就想过来一趟。”

===Original===
The inspector was showing him into the dining-room, when suddenly he stopped.

“Good Lord!” he said.

Jack could only wonder what had startled him.

“Do you mind waiting here?”
===Chinese===
探长正引他往饭厅去，忽然收住了脚。

“天哪！”他说。

杰克只能纳闷，是什么叫他吃了一惊。

“您在这儿稍候片刻，行吗？”

===Original===
For the first time since Jack had known the police officer, Parr was embarrassed.

“I must first tell an old aunt of mine who is staying here who you are,” he said. “She’s not used to visitors. I’m a widower, you know, and my aunt keeps house for me.”

He entered the dining-room hurriedly, closing the door behind him, and Jack felt something of his host’s embarrassment.
===Chinese===
自打杰克结识这位警官以来，帕尔头一回露出了窘态。

“我得先进去告诉一位住在我这儿的上了年纪的姑母，说您是哪位，”他说，“她不惯见客。您知道，我是个鳏夫，家里全凭姑母替我操持。”

他急匆匆走进饭厅，随手把门带上，杰克也多少体味到了主人那份局促。

===Original===
A minute, two minutes passed. He heard a hurried movement in the room, and Parr opened the door.

“Come in, sir.” His red face was even a deeper red. “Sit you down, and please forgive me for keeping you waiting.”

The room in which he found himself was well and tastefully furnished. Jack was annoyed with himself for expecting anything else.
===Chinese===
一分钟，两分钟过去了。他听见屋里一阵忙乱的响动，帕尔开了门。

“请进，先生。”他那张红脸红得愈发深了。“您请坐。让您久等，千万担待。”

他置身其间的这间屋子，陈设讲究而雅致。杰克不免暗恼自己，先前竟然还料想它会是别的什么样子。

===Original===
Mr. Parr’s aunt was a faded lady with an absent manner, and she seemed to cause Mr. Parr a considerable amount of anxiety. He scarcely took his eyes from her as she moved about the room, and she hardly spoke before he jumped in to interrupt her, always politely, but always very definitely.

The inspector’s supper was set upon a tray; he had just about finished when Jack had knocked at the door.

“I hope you’ll excuse our untidiness, Mr.﻿—er﻿—”

“Beardmore,” said Jack.
===Chinese===
帕尔先生的姑母是位风华褪尽的老妇人，一副心不在焉的神气，而她似乎让帕尔先生悬了十足的心。她在屋里走动时，他的目光几乎片刻不离她的身影；她话才起头，他便赶忙插进去截住话头，回回客气，却回回斩钉截铁。

探长的晚饭摆在一只托盘上；杰克敲门那阵子，他差不多刚用完。

“家中零乱，还望您担待，这位——呃——先生贵姓？”

“比德莫尔。”杰克说。

===Original===
“She’ll never remember it,” murmured the inspector.

“I can’t keep the place as mother kept it,” she said.

“Of course not, of course not, auntie,” said Mr. Parr hurriedly. “A little absent,” he murmured. “Now what did you want to know, Mr. Beardmore?”
===Chinese===
“她是怎么也记不住的。”探长低声道。

“这宅子，我怎么也收拾不出母亲当年那个样子。”她说。

“那自然，那自然，姑妈。”帕尔先生忙不迭地说。“她有点儿心不在焉。”他低声道。“好了，您想打听的是什么来着，比德莫尔先生？”

===Original===
Jack laughingly excused himself for his call.

“The Crimson Circle is such a complicated business that I suspect every new agent to be the central figure,” he said. “Do you think that the arrest of Brabazon is going to help us?”

“I don’t know,” replied Parr slowly. “There is just a chance that Brabazon will be a very big help indeed. By the way, I’ve put one of my own men to look after him, and I have given instructions that the jailer is not to go into the cell under any circumstances.”
===Chinese===
杰克笑着为自己这趟登门赔了不是。

“红圈这桩案子实在千头万绪，每冒出一个新的党羽，我都疑心他就是幕后正主，”他说，“您觉得拿下布拉巴宗，对我们能有所帮助吗？”

“说不准，”帕尔缓缓答道，“布拉巴宗也确实有一线可能帮上大忙。对了，我已经派了自己手下的一个人看守他，并且下了指令：看守在任何情况下都不得走进牢房。”

===Original===
“You’re thinking of Sibly, the sailor, who was poisoned?”

Parr nodded.

“Don’t you think, Mr. Beardmore, that that was one of the greatest mysteries of all the mysterious Crimson Circle murders?”
===Chinese===
“您是想起那个遭了毒手的水手西布利了吧？”

帕尔点了点头。

“比德莫尔先生，您不觉得吗？在红圈种种离奇的凶案里头，那一桩要算最大的谜团之一了。”

===Original===
He asked this question very soberly, but there was a little glint in his eye which Jack did not fail to notice.

“You’re laughing. Why? I think it was mysterious, don’t you?”

“Very,” said the inspector. “In some respects, and the poisoning of Sibly will, to my mind, be a much more important factor in the eventual capture of the Crimson Circle than is the arrest of our friend Brabazon.”
===Chinese===
他这一问问得一本正经，眼里却闪过一丝微光，杰克看在了眼里。

“您在笑。笑什么？我可是觉得那案子神秘得很，您不觉得吗？”

“神秘得很，”探长说，“在某些方面确是如此。而且依我看，西布利遭毒杀这一节，对于到头来擒获红圈，将比拿下咱们那位朋友布拉巴宗重要得多。”

===Original===
“I wish you wouldn’t talk about crime and criminals,” said his aunt fretfully; “really, John, you are very trying. It may have suited mother﻿—”

“Yes, of course, auntie; I’m sorry,” said Parr hurriedly, and when she had left the room, Jack Beardmore’s curiosity got the better of his discretion.

“Mother seems to have been rather a paragon,” he smiled, and wondered if he had made a faux pas.
===Chinese===
“我劝你们别张口闭口罪行呀罪犯呀的，”姑母烦躁地说，“说真的，约翰，你真叫人受不了。这种话题母亲当年或许受用——”

“是，是，姑妈，对不住。”帕尔连忙说。等她出了屋，杰克·比德莫尔的好奇心到底压过了他的分寸。

“那位母亲，看来倒是位十全十美的贤人。”他含笑说道，心里又捉摸着：这话是不是失言了？

===Original===
The answering laugh reassured him.

“Yes, rather a paragon; she is not staying with us just now.”

“Is she your mother, Mr. Parr?”

“No, my grandmother,” said Mr. Parr, and Jack looked at him in astonishment.
===Chinese===
帕尔报以一笑，他这才安下心来。

“是啊，十全十美的贤人；她眼下没跟我们住在一块儿。”

“她是您的母亲吗，帕尔先生？”

“不是，是我的祖母。”帕尔先生说。杰克愕然望着他。

## XXVIII / 二十八

===Original===
A Shot in the Night
===Chinese===

夜半枪声

===Original===
The inspector must have been nearly fifty, and he made a rapid calculation as to the age of this wonderful grandmother who took an interest in crime, and kept the house tidy.

“She must be a wonderful old lady,” he said, “and I suppose she’d even be interested in the Crimson Circle.”

“Interested!” Mr. Parr laughed. “If mother was on the track of that gang with the same authority as I have, they would be high and dry in Cannon Street police station tonight. As it is,” he paused, “they are not.”
===Chinese===

探长怎么也有五十上下了；杰克随即飞快地心算起这位了不得的祖母的年纪——她老人家对罪案兴味十足，还把家收拾得干干净净。

“她准是位了不起的老太太，”他说，“我猜她连红圈的事都会感兴趣。”

“岂止感兴趣！”帕尔先生笑了起来，“要是‘妈妈’手里握着我这样的权柄去追这帮家伙，今晚他们早就插翅难逃，蹲在坎农街警察局（Cannon Street police station）里了。只可惜——”他顿了顿，“并没有。”

===Original===
All the time they were talking Jack was puzzling his head as to why, in spite of its order, the room gave him an impression of untidiness. But he was not left to his own thoughts for very long, for Mr. Parr was in an unusually communicative mood. He even went so far as to tell Jack some of the unpleasant things said to him by the Commissioner.

“Naturally police headquarters are rather rattled by the continuance of these crimes,” he said. “We haven’t had anything like this for fifty years. In fact, I don’t think since the Ripper murders there has been such an orgy of destruction. It may interest you, too, Mr. Beardmore, to know that the Crimson Circle, whoever he is, is the first real organising criminal we have had to deal with for nearly fifty years. Criminal organisations are loose affairs, and as they depend for their safety upon that sense of honour which every thief is supposed to possess, but which I have never met with, the game doesn’t last very long. The Crimson Circle, however, is a man who obviously trusts nobody. He cannot be betrayed because nobody is in a position to betray him. Even the minor members of the gang cannot betray one another, because it is just as clear to me that they do not know one another by name or by sight.”

He went on to discuss interestingly cases in which he had been concerned, and it was nearly half-past eleven when Jack rose with a further apology.
===Chinese===

两人说话的当儿，杰克一直在纳闷：这屋子分明收拾得井井有条，怎么却给人一种凌乱的印象。不过他没能独自琢磨多久，因为帕尔先生今晚一反常态，格外健谈，甚至把总监数落他的一些难听话都对杰克讲了。

“这一连串案子没完没了，警察总部自然有些心慌。”他说，“这样的事，我们五十年没遇上过了。说实在的，自打开膛手凶案（the Ripper murders）之后，还没闹过这样一场疯狂肆虐的大破坏。比德莫尔先生，有件事说出来你也许感兴趣：红圈——不管他究竟是什么人——是我们近五十年来碰上的头一个真正善于组织犯罪的罪犯。犯罪组织向来是松散的营生；它们要保得安全，全凭那种据说每个贼都该有的江湖义气——可我干这一行，还从没见识过——所以这种把戏玩不长。可红圈这个人，显然谁也不信。没人出卖得了他，因为谁也没资格出卖他。连帮里的外围角色想互相出卖都办不到，因为据我看再明白不过，他们彼此连姓名、连长相都对不上号。”

他接着饶有兴味地谈起自己经手的旧案。等杰克再次起身告罪，已经快十一点半了。

===Original===
“I’ll take you to the front door; your car is here, isn’t it?”

“No,” said Jack. “I came by taxi.”

“H’m,” said the inspector. “I thought I saw a car drawn up in front of the door. We are not a motorcar owning neighbourhood; probably it is a doctor’s machine.”

He opened the door, and, as he had said, a black car was drawn up at the kerb.

“I seem to have seen that before,” said the inspector, and took a step forward. As he did so a pencil of flame leapt from the dark interior of the car; there was a deafening report, and Inspector Parr fell into Jack’s arms and slid to the ground. A second later and the car was speeding up the street; it showed no light and vanished round the corner as the doors in the street began to open and to let out the alarmed residents.

A policeman came running along the pavement, and together they lifted the detective and carried him into the dining-room. Happily the aunt had gone to bed, and had apparently heard and noticed nothing.
===Chinese===

“我送你到大门去。你的车已经到了吧？”

“没有，”杰克说，“我是坐出租汽车来的。”

“唔，”探长说，“我好像看见门口停着一辆车。我们这一带，买得起汽车的人家可不多；八成是哪位大夫的车。”

他打开门，果然如他所说，一辆黑色汽车停在路缘石旁。

“这车我好像在哪儿见过。”探长说着，向前跨了一步。就在这一刹那，一道火舌从车内的黑暗里蹿出；一声震耳欲聋的枪响，帕尔探长栽进杰克怀中，滑倒在地。转眼间，那辆汽车已沿着大街飞驰而去；车没点灯，拐过街角便没了影，这时街上一扇扇门陆续打开，惊惶的住户走了出来。

一名警察沿着人行道跑来，两人合力抬起这位侦探，把他抬进餐厅。所幸姑母已经上床睡了，显然什么也没听见、什么也没发觉。

===Original===
Inspector Parr opened his eyes and blinked.

“That was a nasty one,” he said with a wince of pain. He felt gingerly in his waistcoat and brought out a flat piece of lead. “I’m glad he didn’t use an automatic,” he said, and then, seeing the blank amazement on Jack’s face, he grinned.

“The Crimson Circle gentleman is only one of three who wear a bulletproof waistcoat,” he said. “I am the second, and﻿—” he paused, “Thalia Drummond is the third, as I happen to know.”

He did not speak again for some time, and then he said to Jack:

“Will you telephone to Derrick Yale? I think he is going to be considerably startled.”

The prophecy understated the case.
===Chinese===

帕尔探长睁开眼，眨了眨。

“这一枪可够受的。”他说着，疼得一咧嘴。他小心翼翼地探手到背心里摸了摸，摸出一块打扁的铅弹。“幸亏他用的不是自动手枪。”他说。见杰克一脸茫然错愕，他咧嘴一笑。

“世上穿防弹背心的眼下统共三位，红圈那位先生便是其中之一。”他说，“我是第二个，还有——”他顿了顿，“塔莉娅·德拉蒙德是第三个。这我可碰巧知道。”

好一阵子他没有再开口，随后他对杰克说：

“请你给德里克·耶尔打个电话，好吗？我想他准会大吃一惊。”

这句预言还说轻了。

===Original===
Derrick Yale arrived half an hour after the shooting in such haste that his appearance suggested that he had dressed over his pyjama suit. He listened to Parr’s story, and then:

“I don’t want to be uncomplimentary, inspector,” he laughed, “but you’re the last person in the world I should have thought they would have wanted to shoot.”

“Thank you,” said Parr, who was gingerly fixing a lint pad over his bruised chest.

“I don’t mean that as uncomplimentary; I merely mean that such a definite challenge to the police is the last thing in the world I expected them to deliver.” He frowned heavily. “I don’t understand it,” he said as though speaking to himself. “I wonder why she wanted to know. I’m talking about Thalia Drummond. She asked me this morning what was your address,” he said. “I understand your name is not even in the telephone book or in the local directory.”

“What did you say?”

“I gave her some evasive answer, but I’ve just remembered that my private address book is accessible, and she could easily have discovered it without troubling to ask me. I wonder she didn’t.”
===Chinese===

枪响后半小时，德里克·耶尔赶到了。那份仓促劲儿，看他那副模样就知道，简直像是把衣裳直接套在睡衣外面就出了门。他听帕尔讲完事情经过，随即说道：

“探长，我无意冒犯，”他笑道，“可天底下我最想不到他们会想枪杀的，头一个就是你。”

“多谢。”帕尔说。他正小心地在挫伤的胸口上敷一块亚麻纱布垫。

“我这么说不是不敬；我的意思只是，像这样明刀明枪地向警察挑战，是我万万料想不到他们会干出来的事。”他眉头紧锁。“我弄不明白。”他像是自言自语地说，“真怪她当时为什么要打听。我说的是塔莉娅·德拉蒙德。今天早上，她还问我你住在哪儿。”他说，“据我所知，你的名字连电话簿、连本地住址录上都查不到。”

“你怎么回答的？”

“我含糊其辞地应付了两句。不过我这才想起，我的私人住址簿她是翻得着的——根本犯不着费口舌问我，一查便知。奇怪的是她竟没有去查。”

===Original===
Jack gave a weary sigh.

“Really, Yale, you’re not suggesting that Miss Drummond fired that shot, are you? Because, if you are, it’s a ridiculous suggestion. Oh, I know what you’re going to say: she’s a bad lot and has been guilty of all sorts of miserable little crimes, but that doesn’t make her a murderess!”

“You’re quite right,” replied Yale after a pause. “I’m being unjust to the girl, and it doesn’t seem that I’m starting fair if I am sincere in my desire to give her a chance. I wanted to see you tonight, by the way, Parr.” He took from his pocket a card and laid it on the table before the inspector. “How does that strike you for nerve?”

“When did you get it?”

“It was waiting in the letter-box for me, but I didn’t see it, curiously enough, until I was rushing out to find a taxi to bring me here. Isn’t it colossal?”
===Chinese===

杰克厌烦地叹了口气。

“说真的，耶尔，你总不会是说那一枪是德拉蒙德小姐开的吧？你要真这么想，这念头可荒唐透顶。哦，我知道你要说什么：她是个坏坯子，犯过种种不上台面的小罪——可这就能把她定成杀人凶手吗！”

“你说得很对。”耶尔顿了顿，答道，“我这是亏待了这姑娘；我要是真心想给她一个机会，开头就不该这样有失公允。对了，帕尔，我今晚本来正要找你。”他从口袋里掏出一张卡片，摆在探长面前的桌上。“你说说，这份胆气够不够大？”

“什么时候收到的？”

“它就在我家信箱里等着我。说也奇怪，直到我冲出门去找出租汽车上这儿来，才瞧见它。你说这气焰狂不狂？”

===Original===
The card bore a symbol familiar enough to the two men, but at the very sight of that Crimson Circle, Jack shuddered. Within the hoop was written:

You are serving the losing side. Serve us instead and you shall be rewarded tenfold. Continue your present work, and you die on the fourth of next month.

“That gives you about ten days,” said Parr seriously, and it might have been the pain he had suffered, or excitement, but he seemed suddenly to lose his colour. “Ten days,” he muttered.
===Chinese===

卡片上印着一个符号，两人都再熟悉不过；可一望见那个红圈，杰克便打了个寒噤。圈内写着：

你在为败局已定的一方卖命。改投我方，十倍厚赏；若仍执迷于眼前的差事，下月四日便是你的死期。

“算下来，你还有十天左右。”帕尔正色道。也许是因为方才受的痛楚，也许是因为激动，他的脸色忽然间褪尽了血色。“十天。”他喃喃道。

===Original===
“Of course, I take not the slightest notice of that threat,” said Derrick Yale cheerfully. “I must confess that after my unpleasant experience at the office I almost credit them with supernatural gifts.”

“Ten days,” repeated the detective. “Have you made any plans? Ordinarily, where would you be on the fourth of next month?”

“It is curious that you should ask that,” said Yale, “but I had arranged to go down to Deal for some fishing. A friend of mine has lent me a motor-launch, and I thought of spending the night in the Channel; in fact, I had arranged to go on that day.”

“You can make what arrangements you like, but you are not going alone,” said Parr emphatically. “And now you can all clear out. Thank your lucky stars that my aunt has not wakened, and that mother isn’t here!”

The last he said was intended for Jack, and Jack smiled understandingly.
===Chinese===

“这种恐吓，我自然一丝一毫也不放在心上。”德里克·耶尔轻快地说，“不过我必须承认，自打在办公室遭了那回不愉快的经历，我几乎要相信他们身怀超自然的本领了。”

“十天。”探长又重复了一遍，“你订过什么计划没有？照平日的安排，下月四号那天你会在哪儿？”

“你问得真巧，”耶尔说，“我正打算去迪尔（Deal）钓几天鱼。一位朋友借了我一艘汽艇，我本想夜里就宿在英吉利海峡上；说实在的，我定下的启程日子，正是那一天。”

“你爱怎么安排悉听尊便，只是不许独个儿去。”帕尔斩钉截铁地说，“好了，你们现在都给我出去吧。真是谢天谢地——幸亏我姑妈没被吵醒，也幸亏‘妈妈’不在这儿！”

最后那句话是说给杰克听的，杰克会心地笑了。

## XXIX / 二十九

===Original===
“The Red Circle”
===Chinese===
“红圈”

===Original===
It was Harvey Froyant’s boast that he trusted nobody completely. He trusted the lawyer up to a point, but his known connection with questionable people would have been alone sufficient to prevent Harvey from trusting implicitly to his agent.

Two nights after the shooting of Inspector Parr the little lawyer called on his employer, and he was all a-quiver with excitement. He had traced one of the new series of banknotes which the Crimson Circle had taken from Brabazon.

“Now, we’ve got a good line on this, Mr. Froyant, and if we continue in the direction we are going, we can certainly pick up the original changer.”
===Chinese===
哈维·弗鲁瓦扬素来引以自夸的一条，是他从不彻底信任任何人。对这位律师，他只信到某一点为止；何况此人与形迹可疑之徒的往来人所共知，单凭这一条，便足以叫哈维不肯放心大胆地信托自己的代理人。

帕尔探长遇枪击两夜之后，这位小个子律师登门来见雇主，浑身上下兴奋得直打颤。红圈从布拉巴宗手里夺去的那批新钞，他已经追出了其中一张的踪迹。

“弗鲁瓦扬先生，这回线索我们捏得很牢了；只要照眼下这个方向一路查下去，准能把头一个兑票子的人逮出来。”

===Original===
But here Mr. Harvey Froyant was firm. He could not and would not place the case completely in the hands of this man. So far might the knowledgeable firm of Heggitt take him, but he would carry on the rest through another agency. He said so in as many words.

“I’m sorry you won’t let me go on with it,” said the disappointed Heggitt. “I have undertaken this search personally, and I can assure you that there are only a few steps now between the man we discovered with the money and the man you are looking for.”

Harvey Froyant knew that as well as the lawyer.
===Chinese===
可是在这一点上，哈维·弗鲁瓦扬先生寸步不让。他不能、也不愿把这桩案子整个交到此人手里。精明老到的赫吉特事务所可以送他到这一步，余下的路，他要另托一家机构查下去。他把这层意思明明白白说了出来。

“您不肯让我办下去，实在遗憾。”大失所望的赫吉特说。“这桩查访是我亲自经手的，我可以向您担保：从我们查出的那个持款人，到您要找的那个人，眼下只隔着几步路了。”

这个底细，哈维·弗鲁瓦扬跟那位律师一样心里有数。

===Original===
Jack Beardmore had spoken a great truth when he said that this mean man would never be satisfied until he had recovered the money he had lost. It was a goad and an irritation, a source of thought which kept him awake at night and woke him in the morning with a sense of blank despair.

And Harvey was well equipped to carry the investigations to their final stage now that he had the ground clear for him. He had derived his fortune from buying and selling land in every country in the world. Beginning with practically no capital, he had, by personal application to his business, built up a seven figure fortune. And this had not been accomplished by sitting in an office and trusting to subordinates. It had involved considerable travel, restless inquiry and relentless probing into the private circumstances of negotiators, a peculiarity he had shared with James Beardmore, though this he did not know.

He took up his own case with alacrity, and informed neither Yale nor Parr of his intentions.
===Chinese===
杰克·比德莫尔说过一句大实话：这个吝啬鬼不把丢掉的那笔钱追回来，一辈子不得安生。那笔钱是一根鞭子、一根刺，是心头甩不脱的一桩心事——夜里搅得他合不上眼，清早一睁眼，又是一阵茫然的绝望。

如今道路已经扫清，哈维也正有本事把这番追查一竿子插到底。他的家财是在世界各地买地卖地里挣下的。起步时几乎身无分文，全凭事事亲力亲为，他攒起了七位数的家业。而这份家业，可不是坐在写字间里、把事情尽交给下属就能挣来的——那少不了四处奔波，少不了无休无止的查访，少不了对交易对手的私事穷追猛挖。这一点上，他与詹姆斯·比德莫尔如出一辙，只是他自己并不知情。

他兴冲冲地把案子接到自己手上，打算如何，对耶尔和帕尔一个字也没有透露。

===Original===
As Heggitt had said, it was a fairly simple matter to trace the note, for at least three stages. His investigations brought Mr. Froyant successively to a money-changer’s in the Strand, a tourist office and finally to a highly respectable bank. And here he was particularly favoured, for it was a branch of one of the banks which conducted his business.

For three days he pried and questioned, searched books﻿—which he had no right to search﻿—and slowly but surely he came to a conclusion. He was not, however, satisfied to leave the matter with the discovery of the original passer of the note. Not even the bank manager, who gave him facilities for examining private accounts, and was afterwards reprimanded by his superiors for doing so, knew exactly what object he had, or against whom his investigations were directed.

On the morning of the first day Froyant left hurriedly for France. He spent only two hours in Paris, and the night found him on his way to the south. Toulouse he reached at nine o’clock in the morning; here again luck was with him, for an important official of the city had been an agent of his in a purchase he had made a few years before.
===Chinese===
果然如赫吉特所言，这张钞票的来路并不难查，至少头三站是如此。一路追下来，弗鲁瓦扬先生先到斯特兰德街的一家兑换铺，接着到一家旅行社，最后到了一家口碑极好的银行。到了这里他格外顺手，因为这家银行正是承办他自家业务的那家银行下属的一家分行。

整整三天，他连探带问，翻查账册——那些账册他本无权翻查——一步一个脚印，慢慢得出了一个结论。可是，单查出最初出手这张钞票的人，他还不肯罢休。就连那位银行经理——此人给他调阅私人账户行过方便，事后为此挨了上司一顿申斥——也说不准他葫芦里究竟卖的什么药，这番调查到底是冲着谁来。

三天一满，头一个早晨，弗鲁瓦扬便匆匆动身去了法国。在巴黎只逗留了两个钟头，入夜时分，他已在南下途中。翌晨九点，他抵达图卢兹；在这里运气又眷顾了他——城里有一位显要的官员，几年前他置办产业时，此人曾做过他的代理人。

===Original===
Monsieur Brassard offered his guest an emphatic welcome, which Mr. Froyant discounted on the ground that his former agent was under the impression that a new deal and a new commission was in prospect. This seemed to be the case, for he was less enthusiastic when he learnt the object of the visit.

“I do not trouble myself with these matters,” he said, shaking his head, “for although I am a lawyer, my dear Mr. Froyant, my practice does not touch the criminal court.” He stroked his long beard thoughtfully. “I remember Marl very well indeed﻿—Marl and another man, an Englishman, I think.”

“A man named Lightman?”
===Chinese===
布拉萨尔先生（Monsieur Brassard）对客人报以十二分热烈的欢迎。弗鲁瓦扬先生对此打了折扣——他揣想，这位旧日代理人多半以为眼前又是一笔新生意、一笔新佣金。看来也确是如此，因为一听说来意，对方的热乎劲儿便淡了下去。

“这类事我是不过问的。”他摇着头说。“亲爱的弗鲁瓦扬先生，我虽说也是个律师，我的业务可从不沾刑事法庭的边。”他若有所思地捋着长长的胡子。“马尔这个人我确实记得一清二楚——马尔，还有另一个人，我记得是个英国人。”

“一个名叫利特曼的？”

===Original===
“Yes, that was the fellow. Good gracious, yes!” He made a grimace of disgust. “Of course, that is common history,” he went on. “They were scoundrels, those men. One shot the cashier and the watchman of the Nimes Bank, and there were two murders here in Toulouse with which their names were associated. I remember their names very well﻿—and the terrible incident!” He shook his head.

“What terrible incident?” asked Mr. Froyant curiously.

“It was when Lightman was led to execution. I think our executioners must have been drunk, for the knife did not work; twice, three times it fell, but only just touched his neck. And when the horrified spectators interfered﻿—you know our French people are very emotional﻿—there would have been a riot if they had not taken the prisoner back to gaol. Yes, the Red Circle escaped the knife.”
===Chinese===
“对，就是那个家伙。天哪，没错！”他做出一副嫌恶的鬼脸。“当然喽，这都是尽人皆知的旧事了。”他接着往下说。“那两个家伙是地道的恶棍。其中一个枪杀了尼姆银行（Nimes Bank）的出纳和看门人，就在咱们图卢兹，还有两桩命案也挂着他们俩的名字。这两个名字我记得清清楚楚——还有那桩可怕的事！”他摇了摇头。

“什么可怕的事？”弗鲁瓦扬先生好奇地问。

“就是利特曼被押去处刑的那一遭。我看我们那班刽子手准是喝醉了，铡刀不听使唤；落了两回、三回，都只擦着他的脖子皮。吓坏了的看客们当即闹了起来——您知道，我们法国人最容易动感情——若不是赶紧把犯人押回监狱，眼看就要酿成一场暴乱。是啊，红圈就这样逃过了铡刀。”

===Original===
Mr. Froyant, who was sipping a cup of coffee, leapt to his feet, overturning the cup and its contents.

“The what?” he almost shouted.

Mr. Brassard looked at him open-mouthed.

“Why, what is wrong, m’sieur?” he asked, one eye on the damaged carpet.
===Chinese===
正在啜着咖啡的弗鲁瓦扬先生霍地跳起身来，杯子带翻，咖啡泼了一地。

“什么圈？”他几乎是喊出来的。

布拉萨尔先生张着嘴直瞪着他。

“哎呀，您这是怎么了，先生？”他问，一只眼睛还盯着那块遭了殃的地毯。

===Original===
“The Red Circle! What do you mean?” demanded Froyant, trembling with excitement.

“That was Lightman,” nodded Brassard, astonished at the effect his words produced. “It was his public name. But my clerk will know more, for he was interested in the matter, which I was not.”

He rang the bell, and an elderly Frenchman came in.

“Do you remember the Red Circle, Jules?”
===Chinese===
“红圈！你这话是什么意思？”弗鲁瓦扬追问，激动得浑身发颤。

“那就是利特曼啊。”布拉萨尔点点头，对自己这番话引起的反应大感惊讶。“红圈是他公开的名号。不过我的书记员知道得更多——那桩案子他有兴致，我可没有。”

他按了铃，进来一个上了年纪的法国人。

“红圈的事你还记得吗，儒勒？”

===Original===
The aged Jules nodded.

“Very well, m’sieur. I was at the execution. What horror!” He raised his two hands in an expressive gesture.

“Why was he called the Red Circle?” demanded Froyant.

“Because of a mark.” The man drew his long finger about his neck. “Around his throat, m’sieur, was a red circle; it was the colour of his skin, and it was a legend long before the execution that no knife would ever touch him, for such marks are said to be charmed. I think it was a birthmark, but I know that on the way to the execution I met a great number of people﻿—my friend Thiep, for example﻿—who were sure that the execution would not take place. If they were as sure that the executioner and his assistants would be drunk,” added Jules, “and that they had put up the guillotine in the morning so badly that the knife would not work, I think they would have been more intelligent.”
===Chinese===
上了年纪的儒勒点了点头。

“记得清楚得很，先生。行刑那天我在场。多骇人啊！”他双手一举，比了个生动的手势。

“他为什么叫红圈？”弗鲁瓦扬追问。

“因为他身上有个印记。”老头用长长的手指在自己脖颈上绕了一圈。“先生，他喉咙四周有一圈红印，那是他皮肤本来的颜色；早在行刑之前许久，便有传说讲任何铡刀都永远伤他不着，因为这样的印记，据说有法力护着。我想那是个胎记。不过我知道，押赴刑场的那一路上，我遇见许许多多的人——比如我的朋友蒂普（Thiep）——个个都咬定这场处决行不成。”儒勒又补上一句，“他们要是同样咬定刽子手和他的帮手们灌醉了酒，咬定那天早晨断头台搭得马虎、铡刀落不下来，我看他们可就聪明多了。”

===Original===
Mr. Froyant was now breathing quickly.

Little by little the truth was being revealed, and now he saw the whole thing clearly.

“What happened to the Red Circle?” he asked.
===Chinese===
这时，弗鲁瓦扬先生呼吸急促起来。

真相正一点一点地揭开，此刻他已把全案看得清清楚楚。

“红圈后来怎么样了？”他问。

===Original===
“I do not know,” shrugged Jules. “He was sent to one of the island settlements, but Marl was released because he had given evidence for the Republic. I heard some time ago that Lightman had escaped, but I don’t know how true that is.”

Lightman had escaped, as Froyant had already guessed. He passed that day in a feverish search of all available documents, in a visit to the Public Prosecutor, and he ended a strenuous twelve hours in the bureau of the prison governor, examining photographs.

It may be said that Mr. Harvey Froyant went to bed that night in the Hotel Anglaise with a feeling of complete satisfaction, and with the added pleasure that he had succeeded where the cleverest police had failed. The secret of the Crimson Circle was no longer a secret.
===Chinese===
“这我就不知道了。”儒勒耸了耸肩。“他被送到一处海岛流放地去了；马尔倒是放了出来，因为他替共和国倒戈作过证。前些日子我听说利特曼越狱逃了，只是不知这话有几分真。”

利特曼确实逃了，这一点弗鲁瓦扬原已料到。那一整天，他如痴如狂地翻检一切找得到的案卷，又登门拜访了检察官，最后在监狱长的办公处里翻看照片，给这紧张的十二个钟头画上了句号。

可以说，那天夜里，哈维·弗鲁瓦扬先生是在英吉利旅馆（Hotel Anglaise）里就寝的，心头怀着十足的快意，更添一分得意：最精明的警察都没有办成的事，叫他办成了。红圈的秘密，从此不再是秘密。

## XXX / 三十

===Original===
The Silencing of Froyant

===Chinese===
弗鲁瓦扬遭灭口

===Original===
Harvey Froyant’s visit to France had not escaped attention, and both Derrick Yale and Inspector Parr knew that he had gone; so also did the Crimson Circle, if Thalia Drummond’s telegram reached its destination.

Curiously enough these telegrams and messages which Thalia was sending was the excuse for Derrick Yale’s call at police headquarters, on the very evening that Mr. Froyant was returning triumphantly from France.

Parr, returning to his office, found Yale sitting at the inspector’s table, delighting a small but select audience of police officials with an exhibition of his curious power.

His ability in this direction was amazing. From a ring which a police inspector handed him he told the mystified hearer not only his known history but, to his confusion, a little secret history of the man’s life.

===Chinese===
哈维·弗鲁瓦扬这趟法国之行没有逃过众人的耳目，德里克·耶尔和帕尔探长都知道他出国了；只要塔莉娅·德拉蒙德的电报送到了目的地，红圈也同样知情。

说来也怪，塔莉娅接连拍发的这些电报和口信，恰成了德里克·耶尔造访警察总部的由头——而那一个晚上，正是弗鲁瓦扬先生从法国得胜归来的时候。

帕尔回到自己的办公室，发现耶尔正坐在探长的办公桌前，向一小拨经过挑选的警务官员展示他的奇异本领，逗得众人兴味盎然。

他这方面的能耐着实惊人。有位探长递给他一枚戒指，他不但向那位听得满头雾水的听客道出了此人尽人皆知的来历，还顺带抖出他生平中的一段小小秘辛，教那人好生狼狈。

===Original===
As Parr came in his assistant gave him a sealed envelope. He glanced at the typewritten address, and then laid it on Yale’s outstretched hand.

“Tell me who sent that?” he said, and Yale laughed.

“A very small man with an absurd yellow beard; he talks through his nose and keeps a shop.”

A slow smile dawned on Parr’s face.

Yale added:

“And that isn’t psychometry, because I happen to know it is from Mr. Johnson of Mildred Street.”

===Chinese===
帕尔进门时，他的助理递给他一只封了口的信封。他扫了一眼打字机打出的地址，便把它搁到耶尔摊开的掌心上。

“说说看，这是谁寄来的？”他说。耶尔笑了。

“一个小个子男人，蓄着一撮滑稽的黄胡子；说话带鼻音，开着一家铺子。”

帕尔的脸上慢慢绽开一丝笑意。

耶尔又补上一句：

“这可不是触物感应，因为我碰巧知道，信是米尔德丽德街的约翰逊先生寄来的。”

===Original===
He chuckled at the inspector’s blank expression, and when they were alone, explained.

“I happen to know that you discovered the place to which all the Crimson Circle messages were sent. I, on the contrary, have known of its existence for a long time, and every message which has been sent to the Crimson Circle has been read by me. Mr. Johnson told me you were making inquiries, and I asked him to give you a very full explanation in the addressed envelope which you sent to him.”

“So you knew it all the time?” asked Parr slowly.

Derrick Yale nodded.

===Chinese===
他见探长一脸茫然，不由得暗自发笑；等屋里只剩他们两个，他才把原委讲明。

“我碰巧知道，您已经查出了所有红圈信件的投递去处。而我呢，恰恰相反，早就知道有这么个地方，凡寄给红圈的信，每一封我都过目了。约翰逊先生告诉我，说您正在查问，我便请他在您寄给他的那只写明地址的信封里，给您一个十分详尽的交代。”

“这么说，这一切您从头到尾都知道了？”帕尔缓缓问道。

德里克·耶尔点了点头。

===Original===
“I know that messages intended for the Crimson Circle have been addressed to this little newsagent, and that every afternoon and evening a small boy calls to collect them. It is a humiliating confession to make, but I have never been able to trace the person who picks the boy’s pocket.”

“Picks his pocket?” repeated Parr, and Yale enjoyed the mystery.

“The boy’s instructions are to put the letters in his pocket, and to walk into the crowded High Street. Whilst he is there somebody takes them from his pocket without his being any the wiser.”

===Chinese===
“我知道，凡是寄给红圈的信件，收件地址都写在这家小小的报刊铺，每天下午和傍晚，会有一个小男孩上门取信。这话承认出来实在难堪，可我始终没能查出那个掏男孩口袋的人。”

“掏他的口袋？”帕尔重复道。耶尔很享受这份悬念。

“那男孩领到的吩咐，是把信件揣进口袋，走进人潮拥挤的大街。趁他人在那儿，有人便把信从他口袋里取走，他自己却丝毫不知情。”

===Original===
Inspector Parr sat down on the chair which Yale had vacated, and rubbed his chin.

“You’re an amazing fellow,” he said. “And what else have you discovered?”

“What I have all along suspected,” said Yale, “that Thalia Drummond is in communication with the Crimson Circle and has given him every scrap of information which she has been able to gather.”

Parr shook his head.

“What are you going to do about that?”

===Chinese===
帕尔探长在耶尔让出的椅子上坐下，揉着下巴。

“你这个人，真是个奇才，”他说，“你还查出了什么？”

“查实了我一贯的疑心，”耶尔说，“塔莉娅·德拉蒙德与红圈有联络，而且她把自己能搜罗到的一星半点情报，全都递到了红圈手里。”

帕尔摇了摇头。

“这事你打算怎么处置？”

===Original===
“I told you all along that she would lead us to the Crimson Circle,” said Yale quietly, “and sooner or later I am sure my predictions will be justified. It is nearly two months since I induced our friend who keeps a small newsagent’s shop to which letters may be addressed, to give me the first look over all letters addressed to Johnson. He wanted a little inducing, because our newsagent is a very honest, straightforward man, but it is my experience, and probably yours, that the mere suggestion that a man is assisting the cause of justice will induce him to commit the most outrageous acts of disloyalty. I took the liberty of suggesting, without stating, that I was a regular police officer; I hope you don’t mind.”

===Chinese===
“我早就说过，她会领着我们找到红圈，”耶尔平静地说，“而且我敢肯定，我的预言迟早要应验。快两个月前，我就说动了咱们那位开着一家可以代收信件的小报刊铺的朋友，凡寄给约翰逊的信，都让我先过目一遍。他倒费了些口舌才说动，因为咱们这位报贩为人十分诚实耿直。不过依我的经验——多半也是您的经验——只要向一个人暗示他是在相助正义的事业，就足以诱使他干出最出格的背信弃义的勾当来。我斗胆让人误以为我是名正式警官，却并没有明说；还望您不要见怪。”

===Original===
“There are times when I think you should be a regular police officer,” said Parr. “So Thalia Drummond is in communication with the Crimson Circle?”

“I shall continue to employ her, of course,” said Yale. “The closer she is to me, the less dangerous she will be.”

“Why did Froyant go abroad?” asked Parr.

===Chinese===
“有些时候我倒觉得，你实在该去当一名正式警官，”帕尔说，“这么说，塔莉娅·德拉蒙德果真与红圈有联络？”

“我当然还要继续雇用她，”耶尔说，“她离我越近，危险性就越小。”

“弗鲁瓦扬为什么要出国？”帕尔问。

===Original===
The other shrugged his shoulders.

“He has many business connections abroad, and probably is engaged in a deal. He owns about a third of the vineyards in the Champagne. I suppose you know that?”

The inspector nodded. Then, for some reason or other, a silence fell upon them. Each man was busy with his own thoughts, and Mr. Parr particularly was thinking of Froyant, and wondering why he had gone to Toulouse.

===Chinese===
耶尔耸了耸肩。

“他在国外有许许多多生意往来，多半正谈着一桩买卖。香槟区约莫三分之一的葡萄园都归他所有，这个您想必知道吧？”

探长点了点头。接着不知怎的，两人都沉默下来。各人忙着各人的心思，帕尔先生尤其惦着弗鲁瓦扬，琢磨他为什么要去图卢兹。

===Original===
“How did you know he had gone to Toulouse?” asked Derrick Yale.

The question was so unexpected, such a startling continuation of his own thoughts, that Parr jumped.

“Good heavens!” he said, “can you read a man’s mind?”

“Sometimes,” said Yale, unsmilingly. “I thought he had gone to Paris.”

“He went to Toulouse,” said the inspector shortly, and did not explain how he came to know.

===Chinese===
“您怎么知道他去了图卢兹？”德里克·耶尔问道。

这一问来得如此突兀，又如此惊人地接上了他自己心里的念头，帕尔不由得吓了一跳。

“天哪！”他说，“你能看透人的心思？”

“偶尔能。”耶尔说，脸上不见一丝笑意。“我还当他去了巴黎呢。”

“他去了图卢兹。”探长简短地说，并没有解释自己是怎么知道的。

===Original===
Possibly nothing Derrick Yale had ever done, no demonstration he had given of his gifts, had so disconcerted this placid inspector of police as that experiment in thought transference. It alarmed, indeed, frightened him, and he was still shaken in his mind when Harvey Froyant’s telephone call came through.

“Is that you, Parr? I want you to come to my house. Bring Yale with you. I have a very important communication to make.”

Inspector Parr hung up the receiver deliberately.

“Now, what the devil does he know?” he said, speaking to himself, and Derrick Yale’s keen eyes, which had not left the inspector’s face all the time he was speaking, shone for a moment with a strange light.

===Chinese===
大概德里克·耶尔从来没有哪一桩作为、哪一次施展异能，像这一场传心术的试验那样，搅得这位素来沉静的警界探长如此心神不宁。这事叫他惊惶，甚至叫他害怕；哈维·弗鲁瓦扬的电话打进来时，他心里还没有平静下来。

“是你吗，帕尔？我要你到我家来一趟，把耶尔也一并带来。我有一件要事相告。”

帕尔探长慢条斯理地挂上了听筒。

“哼，他到底知道些什么？”他自言自语道。他讲这话的当口，德里克·耶尔那双锐利的眼睛始终没有离开探长的脸，此刻倏地闪过一道异样的光。

---

===Original===
Thalia Drummond had finished her simple dinner and was engaged in the domestic task of darning a stocking. Her undomestic task, which was of greater urgency, was to prevent herself thinking of Jack Beardmore. There were times when the thought of him was an acute agony, and since such moments of quietness and solitude as these were favourable for such meditation, she had just put down her work and turned to something new for distraction, when the door bell rang.

It was a district messenger, and he carried in his hand a square parcel that looked like a boot box.

It was addressed to her in pen-printed characters, and she had a little flutter at her heart as she realised from whom it had come.

Back in her room she cut the string and opened the box. On the top lay a letter which she read. It was from the Crimson Circle, and ran:

===Chinese===
塔莉娅·德拉蒙德用完了她那顿简便的晚饭，正做着一桩家常活计——补一只长袜。她那桩不家常的活计更要紧，就是别让自己去想杰克·比德莫尔。有时候，一想起他来便是阵阵尖锐的苦痛，而眼下这种清静孤寂的时刻，恰恰最宜滋生这类冥想。她刚放下手里的活计，转向一件新鲜事来散心，门铃便响了。

来的是一名本区信差，手里捧着一只四四方方的包裹，看去像个靴盒。

包裹上的收件字样是用钢笔描成的印刷体，写着她的名字；她一明白这是打哪儿来的，心口便不由得轻轻一颤。

回到自己房里，她铰断捆绳，打开了盒子。最上面搁着一封信，她读了起来。信是红圈寄来的，写道：

===Original===
You know the way into Froyant’s house. There is an entrance from the garden into the bombproof shelter beneath his study. Gain admission, taking with you the contents of this box. Wait in the underground room until I give you further instructions.

She lifted out the contents of the box. The first article was a large gauntlet glove that reached almost to her elbow. It was a man’s glove, and left-handed. The only other thing in the box was a long, sharp-pointed knife with a cup-like guard. She handled it carefully, feeling the edge; it was as sharp as a razor. For a long time she sat looking at the weapon and the glove, and then she got up and went to the telephone and gave a number. She waited for a long time, until the operator told her there was no answer.

At nine o’clock.

She looked at her watch. It was past eight already, and she had no time to lose. She put the glove and the knife in a big leather handbag, wrapped herself in her cloak, and went out.

===Chinese===
你认得进弗鲁瓦扬宅子的路。从花园有一道入口，通向他书房底下的防炸密室。设法进去，随身带上盒中之物。在那间地下密室里候着，直到我给你进一步的指令。

她把盒中之物取了出来。头一件是一只长大的护腕手套，几乎长及她的肘弯，是男式手套，左手用的。盒里仅有的另一件东西，是一把尖刃长刀，护手形如杯盏。她小心地托起它，试了试刀锋，快得如同剃刀。她久久坐在那里，望着这凶器和这只手套，然后起身走到电话机前，报了一个号码。她等了很久，直到话务员告诉她，对方无人接听。

九点钟。

她看了看表。已经过了八点，她不能再耽搁了。她把手套和刀放进一只大皮手袋，裹上斗篷，出了门。

===Original===
Half an hour later, Derrick Yale and Mr. Parr ascended the steps of Froyant’s residence and were admitted by a servant. The first thing Derrick Yale noticed was that the passage was brilliantly illuminated; all the lights in the hall were on, and even the lamps on the landing above were in full blaze, a curious circumstance, remembering Mr. Harvey Froyant’s parsimony. Usually he contented himself with one feeble light in the hall, and any room in the house that was not in use was in darkness.

The library was a room opening from the main hall; the door was wide open, and the visitors saw that the room was as brilliantly lighted as the hall.

Harvey Froyant was sitting at his desk, a smile on his tired face, but for all his weariness there was self-satisfaction in every gesture, every note in his voice.

“Well, gentlemen,” he said almost jovially, “I’m going to give you a little information which I think will startle and amuse you.” He chuckled and rubbed his hands. “I have just called up the Chief Commissioner, Parr,” he said, peering up at the stout detective. “In a case like this one wants to be on the safe side. Anything may happen to you two gentlemen after you leave this house, and we cannot have too many people in our secret. Will you take your overcoats off? I am going to tell you a story which may take some time.”

===Chinese===
半小时后，德里克·耶尔和帕尔先生登上弗鲁瓦扬宅邸门前的台阶，由一名仆人引进门去。德里克·耶尔注意到的头一件事，是门道里灯火通明；大厅里所有的灯全亮着，连楼上楼梯平台上的灯也一片通明——念及哈维·弗鲁瓦扬先生的悭吝，这可是桩稀奇事。往常他大厅里点一盏昏灯便满足了，宅中凡是不用的房间，一概黑着。

书房是从正厅开出的一间屋子；房门大敞着，客人看见屋里也同大厅一样亮堂堂的。

哈维·弗鲁瓦扬坐在书桌前，疲惫的脸上带着笑意，但尽管一身倦色，他的举手投足、他嗓音里的每一个音节，都透着志得意满。

“二位，”他几乎可以说是兴致勃勃地开了口，“我要告诉你们一桩小事，我想准会让你们又吃惊又好笑。”他轻声笑着，搓着手。“我刚才给警察总监挂了个电话，帕尔，”他抬眼瞅着这位发福的侦探说，“办这么一桩案子，总得求个稳妥。你们两位先生离开这宅子之后，什么事都可能碰上，咱们这个秘密，知道的人多多益善。把大衣脱了吧？我要讲一个故事，兴许要费些时间。”

===Original===
At that moment the telephone bell trilled, and they stood watching him as he took down the receiver.

“Yes, yes, colonel,” he said. “I have a very important communication to make; may I call you up in a second or two? You will be there? Good.” He replaced the instrument. They saw him frown undecidedly, and then:

“I think I’ll talk to the colonel now, if you don’t mind stepping into another room and closing the door. I don’t want to anticipate the little sensation which I am creating.”

“Certainly,” said Parr, and walked from the room.

===Chinese===
正在这时，电话铃声尖利地响起，他们站在一旁，看他摘下听筒。

“是，是，上校，”他说，“我有一件要事相告；过一两分钟我再打给您行吗？您在那儿等着？好。”他放回听筒。他们看见他犹疑不定地皱了皱眉，接着说：

“我想我现在就同上校谈，两位若不介意，就请到别的房间去，随手把门关上。我可不想把我精心炮制的这点轰动，提前漏了风声。”

“当然可以。”帕尔说着，走出了房间。

===Original===
Derrick Yale hesitated.

“Is this communication about the Crimson Circle?”

“I will tell you,” said Mr. Froyant. “Just give me five minutes and then you shall have your thrill of sensation.”

Derrick Yale laughed, and Parr, who had reached the hall, smiled in sympathy.

“It takes a lot to thrill me,” said Derrick.

===Chinese===
德里克·耶尔迟疑了一下。

“这件要事是关于红圈的吗？”

“我这就奉告，”弗鲁瓦扬先生说，“只消给我五分钟，然后管保让你们惊骇一番。”

德里克·耶尔笑了起来，已经走到大厅的帕尔也含笑附和。

“想叫我惊骇，可没那么容易。”德里克说。

===Original===
He came out of the room, stood for a moment with the door edge in his hand.

“And afterwards I think I shall be able to tell you something about our young friend Drummond,” he said. “Oh, I know you’re not interested, but this little fact will interest you perhaps as much as the story you are going to tell us.”

Parr saw him smile, and guessed that Froyant had growled something uncomplimentary about Thalia Drummond.

Derrick Yale closed the door softly.

“I wonder what his sensation is, Parr,” he mused thoughtfully. “And what the dickens has he to tell your colonel?”

===Chinese===
他走出房间，手扶着门边站了片刻。

“过后，我想我也能告诉你们一点关于咱们那位年轻朋友德拉蒙德的事，”他说，“哦，我知道你不感兴趣，可这桩小小的事实，说不定会同你将要讲给我们听的故事一样有意思。”

帕尔瞧见他笑了笑，猜到弗鲁瓦扬方才必是为着塔莉娅·德拉蒙德，咕哝了句什么不好听的话。

德里克·耶尔轻轻带上了门。

“不知他那个轰动是什么名堂，帕尔，”他若有所思地沉吟道，“他到底要跟你那位上校讲什么呀？”

===Original===
They walked into the front drawing-room, which was equally well lighted.

“This is unusual, isn’t it, Steere?” said Derrick Yale, who knew the butler.

“Yes, sir,” said the stately man. “Mr. Froyant is not as a rule extravagant in the matter of current. But he told me that he’d want all the lights tonight, and that he was not taking any risks, whatever that might mean. I’ve never known him to do such a thing. He’s got two loaded revolvers in his pocket﻿—that is what strikes me as queer. He hates firearms, does Mr. Froyant, as a rule.”

“How do you know he has revolvers?” asked Parr sharply.

“Because I loaded them for him,” replied the butler. “I used to be in the Yeomanry, and I understand the use of weapons. One of them is mine.”

===Chinese===
他们走进前客厅，那里同样灯火通明。

“这可少见，是不是，斯蒂尔（Steere）？”德里克·耶尔认得这位管家。

“是的，先生，”这位气度威严的人说。“弗鲁瓦扬先生平日用电向来不铺张。可他吩咐过我，今晚要所有的灯都亮着，说他不愿冒半点风险——不管这话是什么意思。我从没见他这么干过。他口袋里揣着两把上了子弹的左轮枪——这才是我觉得蹊跷的地方。弗鲁瓦扬先生向来是厌恶枪械的。”

“你怎么知道他揣着左轮枪？”帕尔厉声问。

“因为子弹是我替他上的，”管家答道，“我从前在义勇骑兵队待过，懂得怎么摆弄武器。其中一把还是我自己的呢。”

===Original===
Derrick Yale whistled and looked at the inspector.

“It looks as if he not only knows the Crimson Circle, but he expects a visit,” he said. “By the way, have you any men on hand?”

Parr nodded.

“There are a couple of detectives in the street; I told them to hang around in case they were wanted,” he said.

===Chinese===
德里克·耶尔吹了声口哨，看了看探长。

“看这架势，他不但认得红圈，还料到会有客来访呢，”他说，“对了，你手边有人吗？”

帕尔点了点头。

“街上守着两名侦探；我吩咐他们在附近转悠，以防用得着。”

===Original===
They could not hear Froyant’s voice at the telephone, for the house was solidly built, and the walls were thick.

Half an hour passed, and Yale grew impatient.

“Will you ask him if he wants us, Steere?” he said, but the butler shook his head.

“I can’t interrupt him, sir. Perhaps one of you gentlemen would go in. We never go in unless we are rung for.”

===Chinese===
弗鲁瓦扬在电话里的说话声，他们一句也听不见，因为这宅子造得敦实，墙壁厚实。

半小时过去了，耶尔渐渐沉不住气。

“斯蒂尔，你去问问他要不要我们进去？”他说。管家却摇了摇头。

“我不敢去打搅他，先生。要不二位先生哪位亲自进去看看。不是打铃叫我们，我们是从来不去的。”

===Original===
Parr was halfway out of the room, and in an instant had flung open the door of Harvey Froyant’s study. The lights were blazing, and he had no doubt of what had happened from the second his eyes fell upon the figure huddled back in his chair. Harvey Froyant was dead. The handle of a knife projected from his left breast, a knife with a steel cup-like guard. On the narrow desk was a bloodstained leather gauntlet.

===Chinese===
帕尔已经走到门口，转眼间一把推开了哈维·弗鲁瓦扬书房的门。满室灯火大亮，他的目光一落到那个瘫陷在椅中的人影上，便立刻明白了出了什么事。哈维·弗鲁瓦扬死了。刀柄从他左胸戳出来，一把带着钢制杯形护手的刀。窄窄的书桌上，搁着一只血迹斑斑的皮护腕手套。

===Original===
It was the startled cry of Parr that brought Derrick Yale rushing into the room. Parr’s face was as white as death as he stared at the tragic figure in the chair, and neither man spoke a word.

Then Parr spoke.

“Call my men in,” he said. “Nobody is to leave this house. Tell the butler to assemble the servants in the kitchen and to keep them there.”

He took in every detail of the room. Across the big windows which looked on to a square of green at the back of the house, heavy velvet curtains were drawn. He pulled them aside. Behind these were shutters and they were securely fastened.

===Chinese===
帕尔那一声惊叫，引得德里克·耶尔疾奔进屋。帕尔瞪着椅中那具惨状的人影，脸白得像死人一般，两人半晌说不出一句话来。

随后帕尔开了口。

“把我的人叫进来，”他说，“这宅子里任何人不得离开。叫管家把仆人们都聚到厨房里，看住他们。”

他把屋里的每一个细节都看在眼里。几扇大窗对着宅子后头一方绿地，窗上拉着厚重的丝绒帘。他把帘子拉开，帘后是窗板，全都闩得严严实实。

===Original===
How had Harvey Froyant been killed?

His desk was opposite the fireplace, and the desk was a narrow Jacobean affair which would have distracted any ordinary man by its lack of width, but it was a favourite of the dead financier.

From which way had the murderer approached him? From behind? The knife was thrust in a downward direction, and the theory that his assailant came upon him unawares was at least plausible. But why the glove? Inspector Parr handled it gingerly. It was a leather gauntlet, such as a chauffeur uses, and had been well worn.

His next move was to call the Police Commissioner and, as he had suspected, the colonel was waiting for a communication from Harvey Froyant.

===Chinese===
哈维·弗鲁瓦扬是怎么被杀的？

他的书桌正对着壁炉，那是张詹姆斯一世时代式样的窄桌，那份窄劲儿足以叫任何一个寻常人分神，却偏偏是这位死去的金融家偏爱的物件。

凶手是从哪个方向逼近他的？从背后？刀是自上而下捅进去的，说他遭人乘其不备下手，这推测至少说得通。可那只手套又作何解释？帕尔探长小心翼翼地摆弄着它。那是一只皮护腕手套，汽车司机用的那一种，已经用得很旧了。

他的下一步是打电话给警察总监；果不出他所料，上校正等着哈维·弗鲁瓦扬的消息。

===Original===
“Then he did not telephone to you?”

“No. What has happened?”

Parr told him briefly, and listened unmoved to the almost incoherent fury of his chief at the other end of the wire. Presently he hung up the receiver and went back to the hall, to find his men already posted.

“I am searching every room in the house,” he said.

===Chinese===
“这么说，他没有打电话给您？”

“没有。出什么事了？”

帕尔简要地告诉了他，听着电话那头上司那番几乎语无伦次的雷霆之怒，不动声色。不多时他挂上听筒，回到大厅，他手下的人已经各就各位。

“这宅子里的每一间屋，我都要搜一遍。”他说。

===Original===
He was gone half an hour, and returned to Derrick Yale.

“Well?” asked Yale eagerly.

Parr shook his head.

“Nothing,” he said. “There is nobody here who has no right to be here.”

===Chinese===
他去了半小时，回到德里克·耶尔身边。

“怎么样？”耶尔急切地问。

帕尔摇了摇头。

“毫无所获，”他说，“宅子里没有一个不该在这儿的人。”

===Original===
“How did they get into the room? The hallway was never empty except when Steere came into the drawing-room.”

“There may be a trap in the floor,” suggested Yale.

“There are no traps in drawing-room floors in the West End of London,” snapped Parr, but a further search had a surprising result.
