Turning up one corner of the carpet, a small trapdoor was discovered, and the butler explained that in the days of the war, when air raids were a nightly occurrence, Mr. Froyant had had a bombproof shelter constructed of concrete in a lower wine cellar, ingress to which was gained by means of a flight of stairs leading from his study.

===Chinese===
“那凶手是怎么进屋的？除了斯蒂尔进客厅那一会儿，门厅里始终没有断过人。”

“地板下面说不定有道活板门。”耶尔提醒道。

“伦敦西区客厅的地板下，可没有那种活板门。”帕尔没好气地说。然而进一步的搜查，却有了惊人的发现。

掀开地毯的一角，下面露出一扇小小的活板门。管家解释说，大战那些年头，空袭夜夜不落，弗鲁瓦扬先生便在底下一间酒窖里造了一座混凝土的防炸密室，入口是书房里通下去的一段楼梯。

===Original===
Parr went down the stairs with a lighted candle and discovered himself in a small, square, cell-like room. There was a door, which was locked, but, searching the body of Harvey Froyant, they found a master key. Beyond the first door was a second of steel and this brought them into the open.

The houses in the street shared a common strip of lawn and shrubbery.

“It is quite possible to get into here through the gate at the end of the garden,” said Yale, “and I should say that the murderer came this way.”

===Chinese===
帕尔举着一支点亮的蜡烛走下楼梯，发觉自己置身在一间四四方方、囚室般的小屋里。有一道门，锁着；他们在哈维·弗鲁瓦扬身上搜出了一把万能钥匙。头一道门之外还有第二道钢门，出了钢门便是露天。

这条街上的宅子，共用一条草坪与灌木相间的狭长绿带。

“从花园尽头那扇门进来，完全可以走到这儿，”耶尔说，“依我看，凶手走的就是这条路。”

===Original===
He was flashing his electric lamp along the ground. Suddenly he went down on to the ground and peered.

“Here is a recent footprint,” he said, “and a woman’s!”

Parr looked over his shoulder.

“I don’t think there is any doubt about that,” he said. “It is recent.”

===Chinese===
他用手电筒沿着地面照过去。忽然他俯下身去，趴在地上细看。

“这儿有个新踩下的脚印，”他说，“还是个女人的！”

帕尔从他肩后望过去。

“这个我看没有疑问，”他说，“是新踩的。”

===Original===
And then suddenly he stepped back.

“My God!” he gasped in awestricken tones. “What a devilish plot!”

For it came upon him with a rush that this was the footprint of Thalia Drummond.

===Chinese===
紧接着他猛地退了一步。

“我的天哪！”他用又惊又惧的声气喘道，“好一个毒辣的计谋！”

因为他猛然间省悟过来：这是塔莉娅·德拉蒙德的脚印。

## XXXI / 三十一

===Original===

Thalia Answers a Few Questions

===Chinese===
塔莉娅答了几问

===Original===

Derrick Yale sat with his head on his hands, reading a newspaper. He had read a dozen that morning, and one by one he had cast them aside to open another.

“Under the eyes of the police,” he quoted. “Incompetence at Police Headquarters.” He shook his head. “They are giving our poor friend Parr a bad time in this morning’s press,” he said as he threw the paper aside, “and yet he was as incapable of preventing that crime as you or I, Miss Drummond.”

Thalia Drummond looked a little peaked that morning. There were dark circles about her eyes, and an air of general listlessness which was in contrast to her usual cheerful buoyancy.

“If you’re in that game you expect to get kicks, don’t you?” she asked coolly. “The police can’t have it all their own way.”

He looked at her curiously.

===Chinese===
德里克·耶尔双手支着头坐在那里看报。那天早上他已经读过了十几份，读完一份便丢开一份，又拆开新的一份。

“‘在警察的眼皮底下’，”他引着报上的话，“‘警察总部昏聩无能’。”他摇了摇头。“今天早上的报界，正拿咱们可怜的朋友帕尔狠狠开刀呢。”他说着把报纸扔到一旁，“可要说阻止那桩罪案，他跟你我一样无能为力啊，德拉蒙德小姐。”

那天早上，塔莉娅·德拉蒙德的脸色略显憔悴。她眼圈发黑，周身透着一股无精打采的倦意，同她平日轻快明朗的神气判若两人。

“干上那一行，就少不了要挨踢，不是吗？”她冷冷地问，“总不能让警察事事都称心遂意吧。”

他好奇地打量着她。

===Original===

“You aren’t a particular admirer of police methods, are you, Miss Drummond?” he asked.

“Not tremendously,” she replied, as she laid a stack of correspondence before him. “You aren’t expecting me to get up testimonials to the efficiency of headquarters, are you?”

He laughed quietly.

“You’re a strange girl,” he said. “Sometimes I think that you were born without compassion. And you worked for Froyant, too, didn’t you?”

“Yes,” she said shortly.

===Chinese===
“你对警察的那套办法，算不上怎么佩服，对吧，德拉蒙德小姐？”他问。

“佩服得很有限。”她答道，一面把一摞信函摆到他面前，“你总不见得指望我写篇颂词，称道总部办事有方吧？”

他轻轻地笑了。

“你真是个古怪的姑娘。”他说，“有时候我想，你生来就缺一副怜悯的心肠。再说，你从前还给弗鲁瓦扬做过事，是不是？”

“做过。”她答得干脆。

===Original===

“You lived some time in the house?”

She did not reply, but her grey eyes met his steadily.

“I did live some time in the house,” she admitted. “Why do you ask that?”

“I wondered if you knew of the existence of this underground room?” said Derrick Yale carelessly.

“Of course I knew of the room. Poor Mr. Froyant made no secret of his cleverness. He has told me a dozen times how much it cost,” she added with a faint smile.

===Chinese===
“你还在那宅子里住过些日子？”

她没有作声，一双灰眼睛却稳稳地迎住了他的目光。

“我确实在那宅子里住过些日子。”她承认道，“你问这个做什么？”

“我纳闷，那间地下密室的事，你原先知道不知道？”德里克·耶尔漫不经心地说。

“那间屋子我当然知道。可怜的弗鲁瓦扬先生对自己的高明从来不惜张扬，那屋子花了多少钱，他跟我说过十几回了。”她含着一丝浅笑，又加了一句。

===Original===

He cogitated a moment.

“Where were the keys usually kept that opened the door of the bombproof room?”

“In Mr. Froyant’s desk. Are you suggesting that I have had access to them, or that I was concerned in last night’s murder?”

He laughed.

“I am not suggesting anything,” he said. “I am merely inquiring, and as you seem to know a great deal more about the house than most of the people who live in it, my curiosity is natural. Would it be possible, do you think, to push up that trap without making a noise?”

===Chinese===
他沉吟了片刻。

“开那间防炸密室房门的钥匙，平日都收在哪儿？”

“收在弗鲁瓦扬先生的写字台里。你这是暗指我动过那些钥匙呢，还是暗指我同昨夜的凶案有牵连？”

他笑了。

“我什么也不暗示。”他说，“我不过问一问。这宅子里的情形，你好像比住在里头的大多数人知道得还多，我有点好奇也是自然的。依你看，把那扇活板门顶上去，能不能不出一点声响？”

===Original===

“Quite,” she said. “The trapdoor works on counterbalances. Are you going to answer any of those letters?”

He pushed the pile of letters aside.

“What were you doing last night, Miss Drummond?”

This time his method was more direct.

“I spent my evening at home,” she said. Her hands went behind her, and that curious rigidity which he had noticed before stiffened her frame.

===Chinese===
“完全可以。”她说，“那活板门是靠平衡锤开合的。这些信你到底回不回？”

他把那摞信推到一旁。

“昨天晚上你在做什么，德拉蒙德小姐？”

这一回，他的问法可直截得多了。

“我在家里过的晚上。”她说。她双手背到身后，他先前就留意过的那种说不出的僵硬，又把她整个身子绷紧了。

===Original===

“Did you spend the whole of the evening at home?”

She did not answer.

“Isn’t it a fact that about half-past eight you went out, carrying a small parcel?”

Again she made no reply.

“One of my men accidentally saw you,” said Derrick Yale carelessly, “and then lost sight of you. Where did you spend the evening﻿—you did not return to your flat until nearly eleven o’clock at night.”

===Chinese===
“整个晚上都在家里？”

她不作答。

“八点半前后，你带着一个小包出了门——这不是事实吗？”

她还是不作声。

“我手底下有个人碰巧瞧见了你，”德里克·耶尔漫不经心地说，“随后又把你跟丢了。你那个晚上是在哪儿打发的——直到夜里快十一点，你才回到公寓。”

===Original===

“I went for a walk,” said Thalia Drummond coolly. “If you will give me a map of London, I will endeavour to retrace my footsteps.”

“Suppose some of them have already been traced?”

Her eyes narrowed.

“In that case,” she said quietly, “I am saved the bother of telling you where I went.”

“Now look here, Miss Drummond,” he leant across the table. “I am perfectly sure that you are not, in your heart of hearts, a murderess. That word makes you wince, and it is an ugly one. But there are suspicious circumstances which I have not yet revealed to Parr about your movements last night.”

===Chinese===
“我出去散步了。”塔莉娅·德拉蒙德冷冷地说，“你若肯给我一张伦敦地图，我就勉为其难，把走过的脚印重描一遍。”

“要是其中几步早就有人描过了呢？”

她的眼睛眯细了。

“那样的话，”她静静地说，“倒省了我告诉你去处的这番口舌。”

“你听着，德拉蒙德小姐。”他隔着桌子俯过身来，“我心里完全有数：你骨子里并不是个杀人凶手。这个词让你听了心里发怵——它也确实是个难听的字眼。可是关于你昨晚的行踪，还有几处可疑的地方，我至今没有透露给帕尔。”

===Original===

“Being under suspicion is a normal condition with me,” she said, “and since you know so much, it is quite unnecessary for me to tell you more.”

He looked at her, but she returned his gaze without faltering, and then with a shrug of his shoulders, he said:

“Really, I don’t think it matters where you were.”

“I’m almost inclined to agree with you,” she mocked him, and went back to her office and her typewriter.

“An amazing personality,” thought Derrick Yale.

===Chinese===
“受人猜疑，在我本是家常便饭。”她说，“既然你知道得这么多，我再多说也就不必了。”

他望着她，她却毫不躲闪地回望着。随后，他耸了耸肩，说道：

“说实在的，你昨晚在哪儿，我看无关紧要。”

“我几乎要赞成你的高见了。”她嘲弄了他一句，便回她自己的办公室、回她那台打字机跟前去了。

“真是个奇人。”德里克·耶尔心想。

===Original===

Women did not ordinarily interest him, but Thalia Drummond was beyond and outside of the general run. Her beauty had no appeal for him; he knew she was pretty, just as he knew his office door was painted brown and that the colour of a penny stamp was red.

He took up the paper again and reread some of the comments upon the inefficiency of police headquarters, and soon after, as he had expected, Parr came into the room with a certain briskness and dropped into a chair.

“The Commissioner has asked for my resignation,” he said, and to the other’s surprise, his voice was almost cheerful. “I’m not worrying. I intended to retire three years ago when my brother left me his money.”

This was the first intimation Derrick Yale had received that Inspector Parr was a comparatively rich man.

===Chinese===
女人素来引不起他多大的兴趣，可塔莉娅·德拉蒙德绝非寻常一流。她的美貌对他毫无吸引力；他知道她漂亮，正如他知道办公室的门漆的是棕色、便士邮票的颜色是红的——不过是知道一桩事实而已。

他重新拿起报纸，把几段讥评警察总部无能的文字又读了一遍。过了不久，果然不出他所料，帕尔迈着利落的步子走进屋来，一屁股坐进椅子里。

“总监已经要我辞职了。”他说。令对方诧异的是，他的声音里几乎带着喜气。“我才不犯愁呢。三年前我兄弟把遗产留给我的时候，我就打算退了。”

帕尔探长竟还相当有钱——这个底细，德里克·耶尔还是头一回听说。

===Original===

“What are you going to do?” he asked, and Parr smiled.

“In Government offices when you are asked to resign, you resign,” he said drily. “But my resignation will not take effect until the end of next month. I must wait and see what happens to you, my friend.”

“To me?” said Derrick in surprise. “Oh, you mean the warning that I am to be polished off on the fourth? Let me see, there are only two or three days of life left for me,” he laughed ironically as he glanced at the calendar. “I don’t think you need wait for that. But, joking apart, why resign at all? Do you think if I saw the Commissioner﻿—”

“He’d take much less notice of you than he would of a row of beans if they started articulating,” said Mr. Parr. “As a matter of fact, he isn’t taking me off the case until my resignation comes into effect, and I have you to thank for that.”

===Chinese===
“那你打算怎么办？”他问。帕尔笑了。

“在官署里当差，人家要你辞职，你就辞职。”他干巴巴地说，“不过我的辞呈要到下月底才生效。我还得等着瞧瞧，你会出什么事呢，我的朋友。”

“我？”德里克愕然道，“哦，你是说那份要我四号归天的警告吧？让我算算，我这条命也就剩两三天好活了。”他瞥了一眼日历，讥诮地笑道，“依我看，你大可不必等那么久。不过玩笑归玩笑，干吗非要辞职不可？你说，要是我去见见总监——”

“他理会你的那份心思，还比不上理会一排突然开口说起话的豆子。”帕尔先生说，“实情是，我的辞呈一日不生效，他就一日不把我从这桩案子上撤下来——这一点，我倒得谢谢你。”

===Original===

“Me?”

The stout inspector was laughing silently.

“I told him that your life was so precious to the country that it was necessary I should remain on duty until I had got you over the fatal date,” he said.

Thalia Drummond came in at that moment with another batch of correspondence.

===Chinese===
“我？”

敦实的探长正无声地笑着。

“我对他说，你的性命对国家何等宝贵，我非得留任到保你平安闯过那个致命的日子不可。”他说。

正在这时，塔莉娅·德拉蒙德抱着一摞新的信函走了进来。

===Original===

“Good morning, Miss Drummond.”

The inspector raised his eyes to the girl.

“I’ve been reading about you this morning,” said Thalia coolly. “You’re becoming quite a public character, Mr. Parr.”

“Anything for the sake of a little advertisement,” murmured the inspector without resentment. “It is a long time since I saw your name in the paper, Miss Drummond.”

His reference to her appearance in a police court seemed to afford Thalia a great deal of amusement.

===Chinese===
“早上好，德拉蒙德小姐。”

探长抬起眼来望着姑娘。

“今天早上，我正在报上拜读你的事迹呢。”塔莉娅冷冷地说，“你简直快成风云人物了，帕尔先生。”

“只要能出点风头，怎么都好。”探长毫无愠意地喃喃道，“德拉蒙德小姐，好久没在报上见到你的大名了。”

他提起她当年上警务法庭的那桩旧事，似乎倒让塔莉娅觉得大为有趣。

===Original===

“I shall have my share in time,” she said. “What is the latest news about the Crimson Circle?”

“The latest news,” said Mr. Parr slowly, “is that all correspondence addressed to the Crimson Circle of Mildred Street must in future be sent elsewhere.”

He saw her face change; it was only a momentary flash, but the effect was very gratifying to Inspector Parr.

“Are they opening offices in the city?” she asked, recovering herself rapidly. “I don’t see why they shouldn’t. They seem to do almost as much as they like, and I don’t see why they should not live in a very handsome block with elevators and electric signs﻿—no, I don’t think they’d better have electric signs, because even the police would see them!”

“Sarcasm in a young woman,” said Mr. Parr severely, “is not only unbecoming, it is indecent!”

===Chinese===
“我那一份，迟早也会有的。”她说，“红圈最近有什么新消息？”

“最新的消息嘛，”帕尔先生慢条斯理地说，“是今后凡写米尔德丽德街红圈收的一切函件，一律得改投别处。”

他看见她的脸色变了一变；虽不过一刹那的工夫，这效果却实在叫帕尔探长心满意足。

“他们这是要在城里开办公处了？”她问，随即很快恢复了常态，“我看没有什么不该的。他们差不多是想干什么就干什么了，住进一幢带电梯、装电灯招牌的漂亮大楼，我看也没什么不可以——不，电灯招牌还是别装的好，不然连警察都要瞧见他们了！”

“年轻姑娘耍刻薄，”帕尔先生板起面孔说，“不仅有失体统，简直有伤风化！”

===Original===

Yale was listening to this exchange with a delighted smile. If the girl surprised him, there were moments when Inspector Parr surprised him as much. This heavy man had a very light malicious touch when he wished.

“And where were you last night, Miss Drummond?” asked Parr, his eyes on the ground.

“In bed and dreaming,” said Thalia Drummond.

“Then you must have been walking in your sleep when you were loafing about at the back of Froyant’s house about half-past nine,” suggested the inspector.

“So that is it, eh?” said Thalia. “You found my dainty footsteps in the garden? Mr. Yale has hinted as much already. No, inspector, I went for a walk in the park at night. The solitude is very inspiring.”

===Chinese===
耶尔满面含笑地听着这一场交锋。这姑娘固然叫他惊奇，帕尔探长也时有叫他同样惊奇的时候。这个沉甸甸的大块头，兴之所至，挖苦起人来那一手轻巧刁钻得很。

“还有，你昨晚在哪儿，德拉蒙德小姐？”帕尔问道，眼睛望着地面。

“在床上做梦。”塔莉娅·德拉蒙德说。

“那么，九点半前后在弗鲁瓦扬宅子后头闲晃的那位，准是你在梦游了。”探长提了一句。

“原来症结在这儿，嗯？”塔莉娅说，“你们在园子里发现我那双秀气的脚印了？耶尔先生早就这么点过我了。不，探长，我夜里是上公园散步去了。那份清幽最能启发人的心思。”

===Original===

Still Parr regarded the carpet attentively.

“Well, when you walk in the park, young lady, keep at some distance from Jack Beardmore, because the last time you trailed him, you scared him!”

He had hit truly this time. Her face flushed crimson and her delicate eyebrows met in a frown.

“Mr. Beardmore isn’t easily scared,” she said, “and besides﻿—besides﻿—”

===Chinese===
帕尔仍旧一心一意地端详着地毯。

“哦，是吗。姑娘家在公园里散步的时候，还是离杰克·比德莫尔远着点儿好——上一回你盯他的梢，可把他吓着了！”

这一回他真说中了。她的脸涨得绯红，两道纤秀的眉毛蹙拢了起来。

“比德莫尔先生可不是轻易吓得着的，”她说，“再说——再说——”

===Original===

Suddenly she turned and went from the room, and when Parr, after a little further conversation, also went into the outer office, she looked up at him and scowled.

“There are times, inspector, when I positively hate you!” she said vehemently.

“You surprise me,” said Inspector Parr.

===Chinese===
她忽然一转身，出了房间。待帕尔又略谈了几句、也走进外间办公室时，她抬起头，恶狠狠地瞪了他一眼。

“探长，有些时候我是当真恨你！”她狠狠地说。

“你这话可叫我吃惊。”帕尔探长说。

## XXXII / 三十二

===Original===

A Trip to the Country

===Chinese===
乡间之行

===Original===

Police headquarters was on its trial. The uncomfortable amount of space which the newspapers were giving to the latest of these tragedies which were associated with the name of Crimson Circle, the questions which were on the paper to be asked in Parliament, no less than the conferences behind closed doors at headquarters, and the aloofness of all who were ordinarily connected with Inspector Parr in his work, were ominous signs which he did not fail to appreciate.

There was hardly a newspaper which did not publish a very complete list of the outrages for which the Crimson Circle was responsible, and not one which did not mention pointedly the damning fact that from the very beginning of the Circle’s activity, Inspector Parr had had charge of the various cases.

He asked for, and was granted, leave to make enquiries in France. During his few days’ absence, his superiors arranged for his successor. He had only one friend at headquarters, and that curiously and strangely enough was Colonel Morton, the Commissioner in control of Parr’s department.

===Chinese===
警察总部这回自己坐上了被告席。各家报纸把这些与红圈之名相连的最新惨案炒得沸沸扬扬，占了多得叫人难受的版面；质询已列入议会的议事单；总部里闭门会议接连不断；平日同帕尔探长共事的人们又一齐对他敬而远之——凡此种种，都是不祥之兆，他看得再清楚不过。

几乎家家报纸都登载了一份红圈所犯暴行的完整清单，而且没有一家不特意点破那个叫人无从回护的事实：打从红圈开始活动起，各桩案子就一直由帕尔探长经手。

他请假赴法国查访，获得批准。趁他离开的那几日，上峰已把接替他的人选安排停当。他在总部里只有一位朋友，说来也怪，此人偏偏是主管帕尔所在部门的总监——莫顿上校。

===Original===

Morton fought his case, but knew that it was a hopeless one from the beginning. In this he had the assistance of Derrick Yale. Yale made an early call at headquarters and gave the fullest particulars with the object of exonerating his official colleague.

“The mere fact that I was on the spot, and that I had been specially engaged to protect Froyant, must take a lot of responsibility from Parr’s shoulders,” he urged.

The Commissioner leant back in his chair and folded his arms.

“I don’t want to hurt your feelings, Mr. Yale,” he said bluntly, “but officially you have no existence, and I am afraid that nothing you will say is going to help Mr. Parr. He has had his chance﻿—in fact, he has had several chances, and he has missed them.”

===Chinese===
莫顿替他力争，心里却明白，这案子从一开始就毫无指望。在这件事上，他得了德里克·耶尔之助。耶尔一早赶到总部，提供了最详尽的细节，想为这位官方同僚开脱干系。

“当初我人就在现场，而且我是受专门延聘去保护弗鲁瓦扬的——单凭这两条，就该从帕尔肩上卸去一大半责任。”他力陈道。

总监往椅背上一靠，双臂抱在胸前。

“我不想叫你心里不好受，耶尔先生，”他直截了当地说，“可是就公事而论，你根本不存在；恐怕无论你说什么，都帮不了帕尔先生。他的机会已经给过——说实在的，给过不止一回，他都错失了。”

===Original===

Just as Yale was going the Commissioner beckoned him to remain.

“You can throw light upon one subject, Mr. Yale,” he said. “It has reference to the killing of the man who shot James Beardmore: you remember Sibly, the sailor.”

Yale nodded, and resumed the seat he had vacated.

“Who was in the cell when you were taking this man’s evidence?”

===Chinese===
耶尔正要走，总监又招手叫他留步。

“有一件事，你或能提供些线索，耶尔先生，”他说，“就是枪杀詹姆斯·比德莫尔的那个汉子被人害死的事——你还记得吧，那个水手西布利。”

耶尔点了点头，重新坐回方才腾出的位子。

“你们录取那人供词的时候，牢房里还有谁？”

===Original===

“Myself, Mr. Parr and an official shorthand writer.”

“Man or woman?” asked the Commissioner.

“A man. I think he was a member of your staff. And that was all. The jailer came in once or twice; in fact he came in while we were there, and brought the water, which was found afterwards to contain the poison.”

===Chinese===
“有我，有帕尔先生，还有一名官方速记员。”

“是男是女？”总监问。

“是个男的。我想他是您署里的人。此外就没有别人了。狱卒进来过一两回；说实在的，我们待在那儿的时候他就进来过，送来了那水——后来发现，水里就带着毒。”

===Original===

The Commissioner opened a folder and selected from many documents a sheet of foolscap.

“Here is the jailer’s statement,” he said. “I’ll save you the preliminaries, but this is what he says,” said the Commissioner; he fixed his glasses and read slowly:

“The prisoner sat on his bed. Mr. Parr was sitting facing him and Mr. Yale was standing with his back to the cell door, which was open when I went in. I took a tin mug half full of water which I drew from a faucet which had been fixed for the purpose of supplying drinking water. I remember putting the tin down whilst I attended a bell call from another cell. So far as I know it was impossible that this tin could be tampered with, though it is true that the door into the yard was open. When I went into the cell Mr. Parr took the tin from my hand, and set it on a ledge near the door and told me not to interrupt them.”

===Chinese===
总监打开一个卷宗，从许多文件当中抽出一张大裁纸。

“这就是狱卒的书面陈述，”他说，“开头的套话我给你省了，他讲的是这么一番话。”总监说罢，扶正眼镜，缓缓念道：

“犯人坐在自己床上。帕尔先生面对着他坐，耶尔先生背朝牢门站着——我进去的时候，牢门开着。我端进一只盛了半勺水的锡皮水勺，水是从专供饮水的龙头里接的。我记得，因为要应另一间牢房的摇铃，我把这把勺子搁下过一阵子。据我所知，这把勺子是不可能被人做了手脚的，虽说通往院子的那扇门确实开着。待我走进牢房，帕尔先生从我手里接过勺子，把它搁在门边的一格壁架上，还吩咐我不要打搅他们。”

===Original===

“You notice that no reference is made to the shorthand-writer. Was he obtained locally, do you think?”

“I’m almost sure he was from your office.”

“I must ask Parr about that,” said the Commissioner.

===Chinese===
“你留意到没有，这里头只字没提那个速记员。依你看，他是不是在当地找来的？”

“我差不多可以断定，他就是您办公室里的人。”

“这一点我得问问帕尔。”总监说。

===Original===

Mr. Parr (who had returned from France) when questioned on the telephone, admitted that the shorthand-writer was a local man whom he had secured by making enquiries in the little town. In the confusion which had followed the discovery that Sibly was dead, he had not thought to enquire about the man’s identity.

A typewritten transcript of Sibly’s statement had been given to him, and he remembered indistinctly paying the writer for his trouble. That was as far as he could help the Commissioner, whose information on the subject was not greatly increased.

Derrick Yale waited whilst this telephonic communication was in progress, and when the colonel had finished, he gathered from his dissatisfied expression that Parr’s information was of no particular value.

===Chinese===
帕尔先生（这时已从法国归来）在电话里受了盘问，承认那速记员是个本地人，是他在那座小镇上四处打听寻来的。发现西布利毙命之后，一阵忙乱之中，他没有想到去查问那人的身份。

人家把西布利供词的打字誊本交到了他手里，他依稀记得曾付给那速记员一笔辛苦钱。他能帮总监的忙，也就到此为止；而总监手头关于此事的情况，也没有因此添出多少。

这通电话进行的当口，德里克·耶尔在一旁候着。上校通话完毕，他从那张愠愠不快的脸色里看出：帕尔提供的情况没有多大价值。

===Original===

“You don’t remember the man yourself?”

Yale shook his head.

“His back was to me, most of the time,” he said, “and he sat by the side of Parr.”

The Commissioner muttered something about gross carelessness, and then:

===Chinese===
“那人的模样，你自己也记不起来了？”

耶尔摇了摇头。

“他多半时候背对着我，”他说，“再说，他就坐在帕尔身边。”

总监咕哝了几句，只说这疏忽大得离谱，接着便道：

===Original===

“I shouldn’t be surprised if your shorthand-writer was an emissary of the Crimson Circle,” he said. “It was a piece of criminal neglect to have taken a man whose identity cannot be established for such an important piece of work. Yes, Parr has failed.” He sighed. “I am sorry, in many ways. I like Parr. Of course, he’s one of the old-fashioned police officers whom you bright outside men affect to despise, and he hasn’t any extraordinary gifts, although he has been, in his time, a remarkably good officer. But he’ll have to go. That is decided. I may tell you this, because I have already made the same intimation to Parr himself. It is a thousand pities.”

It was no news to Yale: nor was it news to the youngest officer at police headquarters.

===Chinese===
“你们那位速记员，若真是红圈派来的密使，我是一点也不会吃惊的。”他说，“这么要紧的一桩差事，居然用上了一个身份都查无实据的人，这疏忽简直是犯罪。是的，帕尔失职了。”他叹了一口气。“从许多方面说，我都感到遗憾。我是喜欢帕尔的。当然，他是你们这些机灵的局外能人所惯于佯装瞧不起的老派警官，身上也没有什么异禀奇才——虽说在他得意的年月里，他也算得上一名相当出色的警官。可是他非走不可了。这是定局。这话我可以告诉你，因为我已经向帕尔本人作过同样的暗示。真是万分可惜。”

这在耶尔已不是新闻；便是警察总部里最年轻的警官，也早有所闻。

===Original===

But the person who seemed least concerned was Inspector Parr himself. He went about his routine work as though unconscious that any extraordinary change in his position was contemplated, and even when he met his successor, who came to look at the office he was shortly to occupy, was geniality itself.

One afternoon he met Jack Beardmore by accident in the park, and Jack was struck by the stout little man’s good spirits.

“Well, inspector,” said Jack, “are we any nearer the end?”

Parr nodded.

===Chinese===
可看上去最不把它当回事的，偏偏是帕尔探长本人。他照旧办他的日常公务，仿佛全然不觉自己的位置正酝酿着什么非常之变；就连那位即将接任的人前来察看他不久便要坐上的办公室时，他也一团和气得无以复加。

一天下午，他在公园里同杰克·比德莫尔不期而遇，杰克见这个敦实的小个子那么兴头十足，不由得暗暗纳罕。

“喂，探长，”杰克说，“咱们离收场是不是近了些？”

帕尔点了点头。

===Original===

“I think we are,” he said. “The end of me.”

This was the first definite news Jack had received of the inspector’s retirement.

“But surely you’re not going? You have all the threads in your hands, Mr. Parr. They can’t be so foolish as to dispense with you at this very critical moment unless they have given up all hope of capturing the scoundrel.”

Mr. Parr thought “they” had given up all hope long ago, but the attitude of headquarters was a subject which he did not care to pursue.

===Chinese===
“我看是近了，”他说，“我自个儿的收场。”

探长要告退，这是杰克听到的头一个确切消息。

“可你总不至于真的要走吧？如今线索头绪全攥在你手里呢，帕尔先生。他们断不会糊涂到在这个万分紧要的关头把你撒开——除非逮住那个恶棍的事，他们已经彻底死了心。”

帕尔先生心想，“他们”早就彻底死了心了；不过总部的态度这个题目，他可懒得深究。

===Original===

Jack was going down to his country house. He had not visited the place since his father’s death, and he would not have gone now but the necessity had arisen for revising a number of farm leases, and since the business could not be done in town, and there were other matters which needed local attention, he decided to spend a night in a place which had, in addition to the memory of this tragedy, memories almost as distasteful.

“Going down into the country are you?” said Mr. Parr thoughtfully. “Alone?”

“Yes,” said Jack, and then as he guessed the other’s thoughts, he asked eagerly, “You would not care to come down as my guest, would you, Mr. Parr? I should be delighted if you could, but I suppose this Crimson Circle investigation will keep you in town.”

===Chinese===
杰克要下到自家的乡间宅子去。自父亲亡故，他还未曾再踏进那地方；若不是一批田庄租约到了非改订不可的地步，他这回原也不会去。这桩事在城里办不成，另有几件事务又需就地料理，他便决定在那宅子里宿一夜——那地方除了这桩惨案的记忆之外，还留着几段几乎同样叫人败兴的往事。

“要下乡去，是吗？”帕尔先生若有所思地问，“一个人去？”

“一个人。”杰克答道。随即他猜到了对方的心思，便热切地问：“你不愿意做我的客人，同我一道去一趟么，帕尔先生？你要是能来，我再高兴不过——只是我猜想，红圈这桩案子，怕要把你拴在城里吧。”

===Original===

“I think they’ll get on very well without me,” said Mr. Parr grimly. “Yes, I think I should like to come down with you. I haven’t been to the house since your poor father’s death, and I should like to go over the grounds again.”

He asked for an additional two days’ leave, and headquarters, which would have willingly dispensed with him for the remainder of his lifetime, agreed.

As Jack was leaving that night the inspector went home, packed a small Gladstone bag, and met him at the station.

Neither the weather nor the roads were conducive to a long motorcar journey, and on the whole the inspector agreed that travelling by train was more comfortable.

===Chinese===
“我看，缺了我，他们照样办得顺顺当当。”帕尔先生冷冷地说，“去——我看我是乐意同你走一趟的。自打你可怜的父亲去世，我就没有再到那宅子里去过，我很想再到宅园里走上一遭。”

他又续请了两天假。总部巴不得下半辈子再也用不着他，一口应允。

杰克动身的那天夜里，探长回了趟家，收拾起一只小巧的格拉德斯通旅行袋（Gladstone bag），到车站上去同他会合。

论天气，论路况，都不宜于坐汽车跑长途；总的说来，探长也承认，还是坐火车舒服些。

===Original===

He had left a little note addressed to Derrick Yale, telling him where he was going, and added at the foot:

It is possible circumstances may arise which would need my presence in town. Do not hesitate to send for me if this should be the case.

Remembering this postscript, Mr. Parr’s subsequent conduct was not a little odd.

===Chinese===
他留了一张写给德里克·耶尔的小字条，告诉他自己去往何处，又在纸条末尾添了几句：

局势或有变化，需要我本人回城，也未可知。果真如此，切勿迟疑，即刻派人召我。

想到这几句附言，帕尔先生随后的行止便着实有些古怪了。

## XXXIII / 三十三

===Original===
The Posters

===Chinese===
告示

===Original===
Jack did not find him a pleasant travelling companion; the inspector had brought with him a whole bundle of newspapers, in each of which he read religiously the comments upon the Crimson Circle. His host saw what he was reading, and was astonished that the man, phlegmatic as he was, could find any pleasure in the uncomplimentary references to himself which filled the journals. He said as much.

The inspector put down a paper on his knees, and took off his steel-rimmed pince-nez.

“I don’t know,” he said. “Criticism never did anybody any harm; it is only when a man knows he is wrong that this kind of stuff irritates him. As I happen to know I am right, it doesn’t matter to me what they say.”

“You really think you are right? In what respect?” asked Jack curiously, but here Parr was not offering any information.

===Chinese===
杰克觉得这位旅伴实在算不上惬意；探长随身带来了一大沓报纸，每一张他都一丝不苟地读过上面关于红圈的评论。做主人的瞧见他在读些什么，不由得暗暗称奇：这个素来不动声色的人，竟然能从满纸对他本人不着好话的报道里读出趣味来。他便直言相告。

探长把一份报纸摊在膝头，摘下那副钢丝边的夹鼻眼镜（pince-nez）。

“说不好，”他说，“批评从来伤不了人；只有当一个人自知理亏的时候，这种东西才会叫他冒火。我恰好知道自己是对的，随他们怎么说去，与我无干。”

“您当真觉得自己是对的？在哪一点上？”杰克好奇地问。可帕尔在这个话头上不肯透露半点口风。

===Original===
They arrived at the little station and drove the three miles which separated the line from the big gaunt house which had been James Beardmore’s delight.

Jack’s butler, who had come down to superintend arrangements for his master’s comfort, handed a telegram to Inspector Parr almost as soon as he put his foot across the threshold.

Parr looked at the face of the envelope and then at the back.

“How long has this been here?”

“It arrived about five minutes ago; a cyclist messenger brought it up from the village,” he said.

===Chinese===
两人到了那座小站，又驱车走了三英里，那是铁路线与那座高大冷清的宅子之间的距离——这宅子曾是詹姆斯·比德莫尔生前的得意所在。

杰克的管家先一步赶了来，替主人张罗安顿事宜；帕尔探长前脚刚跨进门槛，他便把一封电报递了上来。

帕尔看了看信封正面，又看了看背面。

“这电报到了多久了？”

“约莫五分钟前到的；村里一个骑自行车的信差送上来的。”他说。

===Original===
The inspector tore open the envelope and extracted the form. It was signed “Derrick Yale,” and read:

Come back to London at once; most important development.

Without a word he handed the message to the young man.

“Of course you’ll go. It’s rather a nuisance; there isn’t a train until nine o’clock,” said Jack, who was disappointed at the prospect of losing his companion.

“I’m not going,” said Parr calmly. “Nothing in the wide world would make me take another train journey tonight. It must wait.”

===Chinese===
探长撕开信封，抽出电报纸。落款是“德里克·耶尔”，电文写道：

立即回伦敦；事态有重大进展。

他一言不发，把电报递给了年轻人。

“您当然得走。真是有点伤脑筋；要到九点钟才有火车呢。”杰克说。想到要失去这位同伴，他心里好生失望。

“我不走。”帕尔平平静静地说，“天底下什么事也别想叫我今夜再坐一趟火车。让它等着去吧。”

===Original===
This attitude toward the summons did not somehow go with Jack’s perception of the inspector’s character. He was, if the truth be told, secretly disappointed, although he was glad enough that Parr would share his first night in the house, every corner, every room of which, seemed to have its own especial ghost.

Parr looked at the telegram again.

“He must have sent this within half-an-hour of our leaving the station,” he said. “You have a telephone, haven’t you?”

Jack nodded, and Parr put through a long distance call. It was a quarter of an hour before the tinkle of the bell announced that he had been connected.

Jack heard his voice in the hall, and presently the detective came in.

===Chinese===
这种对待召唤的态度，不知怎么同杰克心目中探长的性子对不上号。说句实话，他心里暗暗失望，尽管他又着实乐意帕尔陪他在这宅子里度过第一夜——这宅子的每个角落、每间屋子，似乎都各有一个专属的鬼魂。

帕尔又把电报看了一遍。

“咱们离站之后不出半个钟头，他准就拍发了这封电报，”他说，“你家有电话，对吧？”

杰克点了点头，帕尔便去接长途电话。过了一刻钟，铃声叮铃一响，才算接通了。

杰克听见他在门厅里讲话的声音，不多时，这位侦探走了进来。

===Original===
“As I thought,” he said, “the wire was a fake. I’ve just been on to friend Yale.”

“And did you guess it was a fake?”

Mr. Parr nodded.

“I’m getting almost as good a guesser as Yale,” said the detective good-humouredly.

He spent the evening initiating the young man into the mysteries of picquet, of which Parr was a past-master. There is probably no more fascinating card game for two in the world than this, and so pleasantly was the evening passed, that it was with a shock that Jack looked at the clock and found it was midnight.

===Chinese===
“不出我所料，”他说，“那电报是假的。我刚跟咱们的朋友耶尔通过电话。”

“那您当时就猜出它是假的？”

帕尔先生点了点头。

“我猜东西的本事，都快赶上耶尔了。”这位侦探和颜悦色地说。

这一个晚上，他把皮克牌（picquet）的种种奥妙传授给年轻人——帕尔正是此道高手。世上两人对局的牌戏，恐怕再没有比这更引人入胜的了；这一晚过得如此惬意，待杰克一抬眼看钟、发现已是午夜时分，不由得吓了一跳。

===Original===
The room to which the inspector was shown was that which had been occupied by James Beardmore in his lifetime. It was a roomy apartment, lofty and expansive. There were three long windows, and at night the room, as the rest of the house, was lighted by means of an acetylene-gas plant which James Beardmore had installed.

“Where are you sleeping, by the way?” he said as he paused at the entrance of his room, after saying good night.

“I’m in the next room,” said Jack, and Parr nodded, closed the door, locking it behind him.

He heard Jack’s door shut, and proceeded to divest himself of part of his clothing. He made no attempt to undress, but taking from his battered suitcase an old silk dressing-gown, he wrapped it about him, turned out the light and, walking to the windows, pulled up the three blinds.

===Chinese===
安排给探长住的那间屋子，正是詹姆斯·比德莫尔生前的居室。这是间宽敞的屋子，高朗轩豁，开着三扇长窗。入夜后，这间屋子同整座宅子一样，靠詹姆斯·比德莫尔装设的一套乙炔气照明装置来照明。

“对了，你睡在哪儿？”道过晚安后，他在自己房门口站住，问道。

“我就在隔壁一间。”杰克说。帕尔点了点头，关上房门，随手落了锁。

他听见杰克的房门关上了，便动手脱去一部分衣裳。他并没有解衣就寝的意思，只从那只破旧的小提箱里取出一件旧丝质晨衣裹在身上，熄了灯，走到窗前，把三扇百叶窗一一拉了上去。

===Original===
The night was fairly light; there was sufficient to enable him to find his way back to the bed, on which he lay, pulling the eiderdown over him. There is a method by which the worst cases of insomnia-haunted patients may obtain sleep, though it is one which I believe is very little known. It is to attempt deliberately to keep one’s eyes open in the dark.

Mr. Parr succeeded only by turning on his side and staring out of the nearest window, which he had opened a little.

Towards morning he rose suddenly and stepped noiselessly towards the nearest window; he had heard a faint whirr of sound, a noise which a smoothly-running motorcar makes, but now there was a profound silence. He went to the washstand, and rubbed his face with cold water, drying it leisurely. Then he walked back to the window, pulled up a chair and sat so that he commanded whatever view there was of the avenue leading to the front of the house.

He had to wait nearly half an hour before he saw a dark figure steal from the shadow of the trees, only to disappear again in a deeper shadow. He momentarily glimpsed it again as it passed out of his range of vision into the shadow of the house itself.

===Chinese===
夜色尚算明亮；那点光亮足够他摸回床边。他躺上床，把鸭绒被往身上一拉。有一种法子，能让受失眠纠缠最深的病人也得以入睡，不过据我所知，这法子知者甚少——就是在黑暗里刻意撑着不闭眼睛。

帕尔先生只有侧过身去，睁眼望着最近的那扇窗——那扇窗他先前开了一道缝——这法子才算见了效。

将近清晨，他猛地起身，悄没声儿地走向最近的那扇窗；他方才听见一阵轻微的嗡嗡声，正是运转顺滑的汽车发出的那种声响，可眼下又是一片死寂。他走到盥洗架前，用冷水搓了搓脸，慢条斯理地擦干。然后他踱回窗边，拖过一把椅子坐下，坐的位置恰好把通向宅子正面的林荫车道上可望见的景致一览无余。

他等了将近半个钟头，才看见一条黑影从树影里闪出，转眼又没入更浓的暗影中。那人影随后又在他的视野边缘一闪，随即隐入宅子本身的阴影之中。

===Original===
The inspector moved softly from the room and, crossing the landing, went down the stairs. The main door of the house was bolted and locked, and it was some time before he could open it. When he stepped out into the night there was nobody in sight. He crept stealthily along the path which ran parallel with the house, but found no intruder, and he had reached the main entrance again when he heard the sound of the motor fading gradually﻿—the midnight visitor had gone.

He closed and bolted the door and went back to his room. This visit puzzled him. It was clear that the man, whoever he was, had not seen Parr, nor could he have been certain that he was under observation. He must have come and gone almost immediately.

It was not until he came down to breakfast in the morning that the mystery of the visitation was revealed.

Jack was standing before the fire reading a crumpled paper which looked as if it had been posted up and torn. It was the size of a small poster and hand-printed. Before he saw its contents, Parr knew that it was a message from the Crimson Circle.

===Chinese===
探长轻手轻脚地出了房间，穿过楼梯平台，下了楼。宅子正门上了闩、锁了锁，他费了好一会儿才打开。待他跨进夜色里，四下不见一个人影。他蹑手蹑脚地沿着那条与宅子平行的甬道摸索过去，却没有发现来人；等他又绕回正门，才听见汽车声渐渐远去——那位午夜来客已经走了。

他关上门、落了闩，回到自己房里。这一趟夜访叫他纳闷。事情很清楚：那人不管是谁，都没有瞧见帕尔，也未必能肯定自己正被人盯着。他必定是来了就走，前后几乎没耽搁。

直到第二天早晨下楼用早餐，这趟夜访的谜底才揭开。

杰克站在壁炉前，读着一张揉皱的纸，那纸看去像是张贴过又扯下来的，有小幅告示那么大，字是手描的印刷体。还没看清内容，帕尔就知道这是红圈送来的信。

===Original===
“What do you think of this?” asked Jack, looking round as the detective came in. “We found half a dozen of these posters pasted or tacked on to the trees of the drive, and this one was stuck up under my window!”

The detective read:

Your father’s debt is still unpaid. It will remain unpaid if you persuade your friends Derrick Yale and Parr to cease their activity.

Underneath was written in smaller characters, and evidently added as an afterthought:

We shall make no further demands upon private individuals.

===Chinese===
“您瞧瞧这个？”侦探进来时，杰克回头说道，“我们发现车道两旁的树上贴着、钉着六张这样的告示，而这一张，就贴在我窗子底下！”

侦探念道：

你父亲的债至今未偿。只要你说动你的朋友德里克·耶尔和帕尔收手，这笔债便永不追讨。

底下还有一行小字，一望而知是随后添上的：

我们今后不再向私人提出任何要求。

===Original===
“So he was bill-posting,” said Parr thoughtfully. “I wondered why he came and left so early.”

“Did you see him?” asked Jack in surprise.

“I just glimpsed him. In fact, I knew he would call, though I expected a more startling consequence,” said the detective.

He sat through breakfast without saying a word, except to answer the questions that Jack put to him, and then only in the briefest fashion, and it was not until they were walking across the meadows that Parr asked:

===Chinese===
“原来他是来贴告示的，”帕尔若有所思地说，“我还纳闷，他怎么来去都赶得那么早。”

“您看见他了？”杰克惊讶地问。

“只瞥见一眼。其实我早知道他会来，只是原以为会有一番更惊人的动静。”侦探说。

整顿早餐他一言不发，只回答杰克问他的话，且句句简短到不能再简；直到两人走过草场时，帕尔才开口问道：

===Original===
“I wonder if he knows you’re fond of Thalia Drummond?”

Jack went red.

“Why do you ask that?” he said a little anxiously. “You don’t think they will take their vengeance on Thalia, do you?”

“If it would serve his purpose, he would wipe out Thalia Drummond like that.” The detective snapped his fingers.

He put an end to further conversation by stopping and turning about in his tracks.

===Chinese===
“不知他知不知道，你爱慕着塔莉娅·德拉蒙德？”

杰克涨红了脸。

“您问这个做什么？”他有点不安地说，“您不是认为他们会找塔莉娅报仇吧？”

“只要合他的用处，他除掉塔莉娅·德拉蒙德，就会像这样干脆。”侦探打了个响指。

他收住脚步，原地转过身去，就此打住了话头。

===Original===
“This will do,” he said.

“I thought you wanted to go to the station gate﻿—the way Marl came to the house that morning?”

Parr shook his head.

“No, I wished to be sure how he approached the house. Can you point out the spot where he suddenly became so agitated?”

“Why, of course,” said Jack readily, but wondering what it was all about. “It was much nearer the house; in fact, I can give you the exact spot, because I particularly remember his stepping aside from the path and ruining a young rose tree on which he put his foot. There is the tree﻿—or one the gardener has put in its place.”

===Chinese===
“就在这儿吧。”他说。

“我还当您要去车站便门——就是那天早上马尔到宅子来的那条路？”

帕尔摇了摇头。

“不，我要弄清楚的是他是怎么逼近宅子的。你能指给我看，他当时突然变得那样慌乱的地方吗？”

“哦，当然行。”杰克爽快地说，心里却不知所以。“那地方离宅子近得多；说实在的，我能指给您确切的位置，因为我清清楚楚记得，他当时从小径旁边跨出去，一脚踩坏了一株小玫瑰树。喏，那棵树就在那儿——要么是园丁后来补栽的一棵。”

===Original===
He pointed, and Parr nodded his large head several times.

“This is very important,” he said. He walked to where the ruined tree had been. “I knew he was lying,” he said half to himself. “You cannot see the terrace from here at all. Marl told me that he saw your father standing on the terrace at the very moment he had his seizure, and my first impression was that it was the sight of your father which was responsible for his scare.”

He gave Jack details of the conversation he had had with Felix Marl before his death.

“I could have corrected that,” said Jack. “My father was in the library all the morning, and he did not come out of the house until we were ascending the steps of the terrace.”

===Chinese===
他用手一指，帕尔那颗大脑袋连点了几下。

“这一点十分重要。”他说着，走到那株被毁的玫瑰树原先所在的地方。“我早知道他在撒谎，”他半是自语地说，“从这儿根本望不见露台。马尔告诉我，他发病的那一刻，亲眼看见你父亲站在露台上；我起初的印象是，正是瞧见了你父亲，才吓得他失魂落魄。”

他把菲利克斯·马尔死前那次谈话的详情讲给了杰克。

“这一点我本可以纠正的，”杰克说，“我父亲一早上都待在书房里，直到我们正走上露台阶梯的时候，他才出了宅门。”

===Original===
Parr, notebook in hand, was making a rough sketch. On his left front was the solid block of Sedgwood House, immediately before him were the gardens, enclosed by light iron railings to prevent the cattle straying on to the flower beds, and broken by the gate through which Marl must have passed. On the right was a patch of bushes, in the midst of which showed the gay top of a garden umbrella.

“Dad was very fond of the shrubbery,” explained Jack. “We get high winds here even on the warmest days, and the shrubbery affords shelter. Dad used to sit there for hours reading.”

Parr was slowly turning on his heel, taking in every detail of the view. Presently he nodded.

“I think I have seen all there is to be seen,” he said.

As they were walking back to the house he reverted to the midnight bill-poster, and to Jack’s surprise:

===Chinese===
帕尔手里拿着记事本，画着一幅简略的草图。他左前方是塞奇伍德宅（Sedgwood House）敦实的主体，正前方是花园，四周围着轻巧的铁栏杆，为的是不让牛群闯进花坛，栏杆中开着一道门，马尔必是打那儿进来的。右手边是一丛灌木，丛中露出庭院遮阳伞艳丽的伞顶。

“爸爸顶喜欢那片灌木丛，”杰克解释道，“这儿哪怕在最暖和的日子里也刮大风，灌木丛正好挡风。爸爸从前总在那儿一坐几个钟头看书。”

帕尔缓缓地原地转身，把眼前景致的每一处细节都收进眼里。不多时，他点了点头。

“该看的大概都看齐了。”他说。

两人往宅子走回去的路上，他又提起了那位午夜贴告示的人，而且说出的话叫杰克吃了一惊：

===Original===
“That was the only false move that the Crimson Circle have made, and I think it was very much an afterthought. That was not their original intention, I’ll swear.”

He sat down on the steps of the terrace and stared out over the landscape. Jack could not but think that a more uninspiring figure than Mr. Parr he had never met. His lack of inches, his rotundity, his large placid face, did not somehow fit in with Jack’s conception of a shrewd criminal investigator.

“I’ve got it,” said Parr at last. “My first idea was right. He was coming down to blackmail you for the money your father did not pay. On his way he conceived this new idea, which is hinted at in the postscript of his message. He has decided upon some big coup, so that the reference to myself and Yale may be genuine; and he really does want us out of the game, though he’d be a fool if he did not know that the likelihood of his wishes being fulfilled in that respect are pretty remote. Let me see the poster again.”

Jack brought it and the inspector spread it upon the pavement of the terrace.

===Chinese===
“那是红圈唯一的一步错棋，而且我看十有八九是临时起意。那绝不是他们原先的打算，我敢起誓。”

他在露台的石阶上坐下，凝望着眼前的景色。杰克不禁暗想，像帕尔先生这样不起眼的人物，他生平还是头一回见到。个子矮小，身躯滚圆，一张大脸安安然然——这些怎么也同杰克心目中精明的刑侦高手对不上号。

“我明白了，”帕尔终于开口，“我最初的想法没错。他原是来敲诈你那笔你父亲没付的钱的。半路上他起了这个新念头，也就是他告示附言里暗示的那层意思。他已经打定主意要干一票大买卖，所以提到我和耶尔的那句未必是虚言；他也确实真心想让我们出局——不过，倘若他不知道他这愿望实现的指望微乎其微，那他可就是傻瓜了。把那张告示再给我看看。”

杰克取了来，探长把它摊在露台的石铺地面上。

===Original===
“Yes, this has been written in a hurry; probably written in his car, and it is a substitute for the poster he originally intended.” He rubbed his chin impatiently. “Now, what is the new scheme?”

He was to learn almost immediately, for the butler came hurrying out to say that the telephone bell had been ringing in Jack’s study for five minutes.

“It is you they want,” said Jack, handing the receiver to the detective.

Mr. Parr took the instrument in his hands, and recognised immediately Colonel Morton’s voice.

===Chinese===
“不错，这是匆匆忙忙写成的；多半是在他汽车里写的，顶替了他原先打算张贴的那张告示。”他不耐烦地搓着下巴。“那么，这套新计划究竟是什么？”

答案几乎立刻就见分晓：管家急匆匆地跑出来，说杰克书房里的电话铃已经响了五分钟。

“找的是您。”杰克说着，把听筒递给侦探。

帕尔先生接过听筒，立刻听出是莫顿上校的声音。

===Original===
“Come back to London at once, Parr; you are to attend a meeting of the Cabinet this afternoon.”

Mr. Parr put down the receiver, and a smile spread over his big face.

“What is it?” asked Jack.

“I’m joining the Cabinet,” said Mr. Parr, and laughed as Jack had never seen him laugh before.

===Chinese===
“马上回伦敦来，帕尔；今天下午你要出席一次内阁会议。”

帕尔先生放下听筒，一张大脸上绽开了笑容。

“什么事？”杰克问。

“我要入阁了。”帕尔先生说，随即大笑起来，杰克从没见他这样笑过。

## XXXIV / 三十四

===Original===

Blackmailing a Government

===Chinese===
敲诈政府

===Original===

When they reached London the evening newspapers were filled with the new sensation.

The Crimson Circle had indeed decided upon an ambitious programme.

Briefly the story, as related in an official communique to the Press, was as follows:

That morning every member of the Government had received a typewritten document, bearing no address and no other indication of its origin save a Crimson Circle stamped on every page. The document ran:

===Chinese===
他们抵达伦敦时，各家的晚报已经把这桩新的轰动新闻登得满满当当。

红圈果然定下了一项野心勃勃的计划。

简而言之，官方公报向新闻界披露的内情如下：

当天早晨，政府的每一位成员都收到一份打字文件，不落地址，也不见任何表明来历的标记，唯独每一页上都盖着一个红圈。文件写道：

===Original===

Every effort of your police, both official and private, the genius of Mr. Derrick Yale, and the plodding efforts of Chief Inspector Parr, have failed to check Our activity. The full story of Our success is not known. It has been unfortunately Our unpleasant duty to remove a number of people from life, not so much in a spirit of vengeance, as to serve as a salutary warning to others, and only this morning it has been Our unhappy duty to remove Mr. Samuel Heggitt, a lawyer, who was engaged by the late Harvey Froyant on particular work, in the course of which he came unpleasantly close to Our identity.

Fortunately for the other members of his firm, he undertook that task personally. His body will be found by the side of the railway between Brixton and Marsden.

Since the police are unable to hold Us, and since We are in complete agreement with those in authority who say that We are the most dangerous menace to society that exists, We have agreed to forego Our activities on condition that the sum of a million pounds sterling is placed at Our disposal. The method by which this money shall be transferred will be detailed later. This must be accompanied by a free pardon in blank, so that We may, if occasion necessitates, or hereinafter Our identity is disclosed, avail Ourselves of that document.

Refusal to agree to Our terms will have unpleasant consequences. We name hereunder twelve eminent Parliamentarians, who must stand as hostages for the fulfilment of Our desire. If, at the end of the week, the Government have not agreed to Our terms, one of these gentlemen will be removed.

===Chinese===
贵方警察的种种努力——官方的也罢，私家的也罢——连同德里克·耶尔先生的高才、帕尔总探长一步一个脚印的苦干，都未能遏止我方的活动。我方全胜的内情，外界至今未尽知悉。不幸得很，把若干人从人世间除名，一向是我方一桩不愉快的义务——与其说是出于报复之心，毋宁说是给旁人立下一个有益的儆戒。而就在今天早晨，我方又不得不担起一桩不快的义务：除掉了律师塞缪尔·赫吉特（Samuel Heggitt）先生。此人受已故的哈维·弗鲁瓦扬延聘，经办一件特殊差事，办差之中把底细摸得离我方的身份太近，令人不快。

于他事务所的其他同仁而言，所幸那桩差事是他亲自经手的。他的尸体，将在布里克斯顿与马斯登（Marsden）之间的铁路旁被人发现。

鉴于警方无力将我方捉拿归案，又鉴于当权诸公皆谓“我方乃现存于世最危险的社会祸害”，而我方对此说完全赞同，我方遂同意在此条件下罢手：将一百万英镑之数交由我方自由支配。此款如何移交，办法容后详告。随款必须附上一纸空白特赦令，以便一旦事势所需，或日后我方身份败露，我方即可援用该文书。

拒绝应允我方条款，将有不愉快的后果。我方在下面点名十二位显赫议员，他们须充当人质，担保我方愿望的兑现。倘若到本周末，政府仍未应允我方条款，此十二位先生中即有一人将被除名。

===Original===

The first person that Parr met on his arrival at Whitehall was Derrick Yale, and for once the famous detective looked worried.

“I was afraid of this development,” he said, “and the queer thing is that it has come at a moment when I thought I was in a position to lay my hand on the chief offender.”

He took Parr’s hand in his, and walked him along the gloomy corridor.

“This spoils my day’s fishing,” he said, and Inspector Parr remembered.

“Of course, today is the day you die! But I suppose you are reprieved under the general amnesty which the Crimson Circle have issued,” he said drily, and his companion laughed.

“I want to tell you, before we go into this meeting, that I am willing to place myself unreservedly at your disposal,” he said quietly. “I think you ought to know, Parr, that the present wishes of the Cabinet are to give me an official status and place the whole of the investigations in my charge. I have been sounded on the matter, and have given them point-blank refusal. I am convinced that you are the best man for the job, and I will serve under no other chief.”

===Chinese===
帕尔一到白厅（Whitehall），迎面遇见的头一个人便是德里克·耶尔，而这位大名鼎鼎的侦探这一回竟面有忧色。

“这一步发展我早怕它会来，”他说，“蹊跷的是，它偏偏挑上这么个当口——我原以为，眼看就能把首恶一举擒到手里了。”

他把帕尔的手握在自己手里，陪他沿着阴暗的走廊走去。

“这一来，我今天的钓鱼算是泡汤了。”他说。帕尔探长这才想起。

“可不是嘛，今天正是你的死期！不过我寻思，红圈既已颁下普遍大赦，你算是奉赦缓决了。”他干巴巴地说。他的同伴哈哈一笑。

“进会场之前，我想先告诉你：我情愿把自己毫无保留地交你调遣。”他平静地说。“帕尔，我认为你应当知道，内阁眼下的意向，是要授我官方的名分，把全部侦查工作交我主持。他们就此事探过我的口风，被我当面一口回绝了。我确信，这差事你是最合适的人选；换了别的上司，我概不奉命。”

===Original===

“Thank you,” said Parr simply. “Perhaps the Cabinet will take another view.”

The Cabinet meeting was held in the Secretary of State’s office; all the recipients of the Crimson Circle’s memo were present from the beginning, but it was some time before outsiders were called in.

Yale was summoned first, and a quarter of an hour later the messenger beckoned the inspector.

Inspector Parr knew most of the illustrious gathering by sight, and being on the opposite side in politics, had no particular respect for any. He felt an air of hostility as he came into the big room, and the chilly nod which the white-bearded Prime Minister gave him in response to his bow, confirmed this impression.

“Mr. Parr,” said the Prime Minister icily, “we are discussing the question of the Crimson Circle, which, as you must realise, has become almost a national problem. Their dangerous character has been emphasised by a memorandum which has been addressed to the various members of the Cabinet by this infamous association, and which, I have no doubt, you have read in the newspapers.”

“Yes, sir,” said the inspector.

===Chinese===
“谢谢你，”帕尔平平淡淡地说。“内阁或许会另有一番见解吧。”

内阁会议在国务大臣的办公室里召开；红圈那封函件的全体收件人自始在座，过了好一阵，才传外界的人进去。

耶尔先被召了进去；一刻钟之后，传信的听差才向探长招手。

满座显贵，帕尔探长大半是见过面熟的；他政见又与在座诸公相左，对哪一位都谈不上格外敬重。一走进那间大屋子，他就觉出一股敌意；白须垂胸的首相对他的一躬只报以冷冷一颔首，更坐实了这种感觉。

“帕尔先生，”首相冷冰冰地说，“我们正在商讨红圈问题。你必定明白，这几乎已经成了一个国家的问题。这个恶名昭彰的组织致内阁各位成员一份备忘录，其危险性由此凸显无遗——那份东西，想必你已经在报纸上读到了。”

“是的，先生。”探长说。

===Original===

“I will not disguise from you the fact that we are profoundly dissatisfied with the course which your investigations have taken. Although you have had every facility and every power granted you, including,” he consulted a paper before him, but Parr interrupted him.

“I should not like you to tell the meeting what powers I have received, Prime Minister,” he said firmly, “or what particular privileges have been granted me by the Secretary of State.”

The Prime Minister was taken aback.

“Very well,” he said. “I will add that, although you have had extraordinary privileges, and opportunities, and you have even been present when the outrages have taken place, you have not succeeded in bringing the criminal to justice.”

The inspector nodded.

“It was our original wish to place the matter in the hands of Mr. Derrick Yale, who has been especially successful in tracing two of the murderers, without, however, being able to bring the prime culprit to justice. Mr. Yale, however, refuses to accept the commission unless you are in control. He has kindly expressed his willingness to serve under you, and in this course we are agreed. I understand that your resignation is already before the Commissioners, and that it has been formally accepted. That acceptance, for the time being, is reserved. Now remember, Mr. Parr,” the Prime Minister leant forward and spoke very earnestly and emphatically: “It is absolutely impossible that we can accede to the Crimson Circle’s demands: such a course would be the negation of all law, and the surrender of all authority. We rely upon you to afford to every member of the Government who is threatened, that protection which is his right as a citizen. Your whole career is in the balance.”

===Chinese===
“我不瞒你：对你调查工作一路走来的情形，我们深为不满。虽说你要什么便利有什么便利，要什么权力有什么权力，包括——”他低头查看了面前的一纸文书，帕尔却打断了他。

“首相先生，我并不希望您向与会诸公讲出我得到过哪些权力，”他语气坚决地说，“也不希望您讲出国务大臣特意给了我哪些特权。”

首相一时愕然。

“很好，”他说，“那我就再补一句：虽说你享有过非同寻常的特权和机会，甚至几桩暴行发生的当口，你就人在现场，你却始终未能把罪犯罪之依法惩办。”

探长点了点头。

“我们最初的意愿，原是把此事交到德里克·耶尔先生手里——追查两名凶手，他成绩斐然，只是未能把首犯绳之以法。然而耶尔先生拒不接受这项委任，除非由你主持。他慨然表示愿在你麾下效力，我们也就一致照此定局。我了解到，你的辞呈已经呈报警务委员会（Commissioners），并且已获正式核准。这项核准，眼下暂且保留不发。你要记住，帕尔先生，”首相俯身向前，说得极其恳切、极其郑重，“答应红圈的要求，是绝对不可能的：那样办，无异于否定一切法律、交出全部权柄。政府里每一位受威胁的成员，都有他作为公民应得的保护，而这保护，我们就全指望你了。你的整个前程，都悬在这一案上。”

===Original===

The inspector, thus dismissed, rose slowly.

“If the Crimson Circle keeps its word,” he said, “I guarantee that not a hair of one member of your Government shall be harmed in London. Whether I can capture the man who describes himself as the Crimson Circle, remains to be seen.”

“I suppose,” said the Prime Minister, “there is no doubt that this unfortunate man, Heggitt, has been killed.”

It was Derrick Yale who answered.

“No, sir; the body was found early this morning. Mr. Heggitt, who lives at Marsden, left London last night by train, and apparently the crime was committed en route.”

“It is deplorable, deplorable.” The Prime Minister shook his head. “A terrible orgy of murder and crime, and it seems that we are not at the end of it yet.”

===Chinese===
探长就此被打发出来，缓缓起身。

“只要红圈说话算话，”他说，“我担保贵政府的成员在伦敦地界之内一根毫毛也伤不着。至于那个自称红圈的人我擒不擒得住，还得走着瞧。”

“我想，”首相说，“这位不幸的赫吉特先生确已遇害，这一点没有疑问吧。”

答话的是德里克·耶尔。

“没有疑问，先生；尸体今天一早找到了。赫吉特先生家住马斯登，昨晚乘火车离开伦敦，罪行看来是在半路上犯下的。”

“可叹哪，可叹。”首相摇了摇头。“一场凶杀与罪恶的狂欢，而且看样子，我们还没走到头呢。”

===Original===

When they came out into Whitehall, Yale and his companion found that a large crowd had gathered, for news had leaked out that a meeting was being held to discuss this new and extraordinary problem which confronted the Government.

Yale, who was recognised, was cheered, but Inspector Parr passed unnoticed through the crowd﻿—to his intense relief.

Undoubtedly the Crimson Circle was the sensation of the hour. Some of the evening newspaper placards bore a crimson circle in imitation of the famous insignia of the gang, and wherever men met, there the possibility of the Circle carrying their threat into effect was discussed.

Thalia Drummond looked up as her employer came in. The evening newspaper was in front of her, and her chin rested on her clasped hands, and she read every line, word by word.

Derrick noticed the interest, and observed, too, her momentary confusion as she folded the paper and put it away.

“Well, Miss Drummond, what do you think of their last exploit?”

===Chinese===
耶尔和同伴走出来到白厅街上，发觉那里已经聚起一大群人——政府正开会商讨这桩摆在眼前的离奇新难题，风声早已漏了出去。

有人认出了耶尔，向他欢呼；帕尔探长却在人丛中无人识得，悄然穿行而过——这叫他大大松了一口气。

无疑，红圈正是此时此刻最耸动视听的话题。有的晚报招贴牌上印着一个深红色的圆圈，模仿这帮派出名的那枚标记；无论人们在哪里聚头，议论的都是红圈会不会把威胁付诸实行。

雇主走进来时，塔莉娅·德拉蒙德抬起了头。晚报就摊在她面前，她双手交握托着下巴，一行一行，逐字逐句地读。

德里克注意到了这份专注，也注意到她折起报纸收开时一闪而过的慌乱。

“怎么样，德拉蒙德小姐，他们这最新的一手，你怎么看？”

===Original===

“It is colossal,” she said. “In some respects, admirable.”

He looked at her gravely.

“I confess I can see little to admire,” he said. “You take rather a queer, twisted view of things.”

“Don’t I?” she said coolly. “You must never forget, Mr. Yale, that I have a queer, twisted mind.”

He paused at the door of his room and looked back at her, a long, keen scrutiny, which she met without so much as an eyelid quivering.

“I think you should be very grateful that Mr. Johnson, of Mildred Street, no longer receives your interesting communications,” he said, and she was silent.

===Chinese===
“真是大手笔，”她说，“某些方面，堪称高明。”

他神情凝重地望着她。

“恕我直言，我看不出有多少高明之处，”他说，“你看事情的眼光，未免有点古怪乖张。”

“可不是么？”她冷冷地说，“千万别忘了，耶尔先生，我这个人天生一副古怪乖张的脑筋。”

他在自己房间的门口停住脚，回头朝她望去——那是长久而锐利的一瞥审视；她迎着这一瞥，连眼皮也不曾颤动一下。

“我想，米尔德丽德街的约翰逊先生再也收不到你那些有趣的信件了，你实在该谢天谢地。”他说。她没有作声。

===Original===

He came out again soon after.

“I am probably going to establish my offices at police headquarters,” he said, “and realising that that atmosphere is one in which you will not flourish, I am leaving you here in control of my ordinary business.”

“Are you accepting the responsibility for capturing the Crimson Circle?” she asked steadily.

He shook his head.

“Inspector Parr is in control,” he said, “but I am going to help him.”

He made no further reference to his new task, and the rest of the morning was spent in routine work. He went out to lunch and said he would not be back that day, giving her instructions regarding letters he wished despatched.

===Chinese===
没过多久，他又走了出来。

“我多半要把办事处安到警察总部去，”他说，“而我知道，那号空气你滋润不起来，所以把你留在这里，替我掌管日常业务。”

“你是要把缉拿红圈的责任接到自己肩上了？”她不动声色地问。

他摇了摇头。

“主事的还是帕尔探长，”他说，“不过我要从旁帮他一把。”

他没有再提这项新差事，那个上午剩下的时光都耗在日常事务上。他出门去吃午饭，说当天不再回来，又交代了她几桩要发出的信件。

===Original===

He had hardly gone before his telephone bell went, and at the sound of the voice at the other end, she nearly dropped the receiver.

“Yes, it is I,” she said. “Good morning, Mr. Beardmore.”

“Is Yale there?” asked Jack.

“He has just gone out: he will not be back today. If there is anything important to tell him, I may be able to find him,” she said, steadying her voice with an effort.

“I don’t know whether it’s important or not,” said Jack, “but I was going through my father’s papers this morning, a very disagreeable job, by the way, and I found a whole bunch of papers relating to Marl.”

“To Marl?” she said slowly.

===Chinese===
他前脚刚走，电话铃就响了；一听出那头说话的声音，她险些把听筒跌落在地。

“是的，是我，”她说，“早上好，比德莫尔先生。”

“耶尔在吗？”杰克问。

“他刚出去，今天不会回来了。要是有要紧事对他讲，我或许能找到他。”她说，一面竭力稳住自己的嗓音。

“要紧不要紧，我也说不好，”杰克说，“不过今天上午我在整理我父亲的文件——顺带说一句，这差事真叫人腻味——翻出了一大批跟马尔有关的材料。”

“跟马尔有关？”她缓缓地说。

===Original===

“Yes, apparently poor Dad knew a great deal more about Marl than we imagined. He had been in prison: did you know that?”

“I could have guessed it,” said Thalia.

“Father always put through an inquiry about people before he did business with them,” Jack went on, “and apparently there is a lot of explanation about Marl’s early life, collected by a French agency. He seems to have been a pretty bad lot, and I wonder the governor had dealings with him. One curious document is an envelope which is marked ‘Photograph of Execution’: it was sealed up by the French people, and apparently the governor didn’t open it. He hated gruesome things of that kind.”

“Have you opened it?” she asked quickly.

“No,” he answered in a tone of surprise. “Why do you jump at me like that?”

“Will you do me a favour, Jack?”

===Chinese===
“是啊，可怜的爸爸生前对马尔的底细，显然远比我们料想的知道得多。他蹲过大牢——这事你知道吗？”

“这个我本来就猜得着。”塔莉娅说。

“爸爸跟人做生意，事先总要把对方的底细查个明白，”杰克接着说，“看来这批材料里有大量关于马尔早年经历的底档，是一家法国征信所收集的。这家伙似乎是个十足的坏种，我真纳闷老爷子当年怎么会跟他有来往。有一样稀罕东西，是一只信封，封皮上写着‘行刑照片’（Photograph of Execution）的字样——是法国那边封的口，老爷子显然没有拆过。他平生最厌恶那类阴森可怖的东西。”

“你拆开了吗？”她急忙问。

“没有。”他答道，语气里透着诧异。“你干嘛这么劈头盖脸地冲我来？”

“杰克，你肯帮我一个忙吗？”

===Original===

It was the first time she had ever called him by name, and she could almost see him redden.

“Why﻿—why, of course, Thalia, I’d do anything for you,” he said eagerly.

“Don’t open the envelope,” she said intensely. “Keep all the papers relating to Marl in a safe place. Will you promise that?”

“I promise,” he said. “What a queer request to make!”

“Have you told anybody about it?” she asked.

“I sent a note to Inspector Parr.”

===Chinese===
她从来没有叫过他的名字，这是头一回；她几乎能想见他涨红了脸的样子。

“怎——怎么，当然肯，塔莉娅，为你我什么都干。”他忙不迭地说。

“别拆那只信封，”她急切地说。“把所有跟马尔有关的文件收存在稳妥的地方。你能答应我吗？”

“我答应。”他说。“这要求可真够古怪的！”

“这件事你对谁说起过没有？”她问。

“我给帕尔探长递过一张条子。”

===Original===

He heard her exclamation of annoyance.

“Will you promise me not to tell anybody, especially about the photograph?”

“Of course, Thalia,” he answered. “I’ll send it along to you, if you like.”

“No, no, don’t do that,” she said, then abruptly she finished the conversation.

She sat for a few minutes breathing quickly, and then she rose, and putting on her hat, she locked up the office, and went to lunch.

===Chinese===
他听见她恼怒地惊呼了一声。

“你还得答应我，别告诉任何人，尤其是那张照片的事。”

“当然，塔莉娅。”他答道。“你若愿意，我就把它给你送来。”

“不，不，千万别送，”她说，随即唐突地结束了通话。

她坐着定了几分钟，呼吸急促，随后站起身，戴上帽子，锁好办公室的门，吃午饭去了。

## XXXV / 三十五

===Original===
Thalia Lunches with a Cabinet Minister

===Chinese===
塔莉娅与一位内阁大臣共进午餐

===Original===
The fourth of the month had passed, and Derrick Yale was still alive. He commented on the fact as he came into the office which he and Inspector Parr jointly occupied.

“Incidentally,” he said, “I have lost my fishing.”

Parr grunted.

“It is better that you lost your fishing than that we lost sight of you,” he said. “I am perfectly convinced that if you had taken that trip, you would never have returned.”

Yale laughed.

“You have a tremendous faith in the Crimson Circle, and their ability to keep their promises.”

===Chinese===
本月四号已经过去了，德里克·耶尔还活着。他走进自己和帕尔探长合用的办公室，对这一事实发了句议论。

“顺带说一句，”他说道，“我那趟垂钓算是告吹了。”

帕尔哼了一声。

“你告吹了垂钓，总好过你从我们眼皮底下失踪，”他说，“我完完全全相信，你那趟要是真去了，就再也回不来了。”

耶尔笑了。

“你对红圈可真是信心十足，笃信他们说到做到。”

===Original===
“I have﻿—to a point,” said the inspector, without looking up from the letter he was writing.

“I hear that Brabazon has made a statement to the police,” said Yale, after an interval.

“Yes,” said the inspector. “Not a very informative one, but a statement of sorts. He has admitted that for a long time he was changing the money which the Crimson Circle extracted from their victims, though he was unaware of the fact. He also gives particulars of his joining the Circle, after which, of course, he acted as a conscious agent.”

“Are you charging him with the murder of Marl?”

Inspector Parr shook his head.

“We haven’t sufficient evidence for that,” he said, blotted his letter, folded it and enclosed it in an envelope.

===Chinese===
“我有信心——不过有个限度，”探长说，眼睛没有离开他正写着的那封信。

隔了片刻，耶尔说道：“听说布拉巴宗向警方交代了一份供词。”

“是啊，”探长说，“交代不出多少东西，好歹算是份供词。他承认，有很长一段时间，红圈从受害者身上榨取的钱财都经他的手兑换，只是他自己蒙在鼓里。他还交代了入伙的细节；打那以后，他自然就心知肚明地充当帮凶了。”

“你们要指控他谋杀马尔吗？”

帕尔探长摇了摇头。

“这一条，我们还拿不出足够的证据，”他说着，用吸墨纸吸了吸信上的墨迹，把信折好，装进信封。

===Original===
“What did you discover in France? I have not had an opportunity of talking to you about that,” asked Yale.

Parr leant back in his chair, felt for his pipe, and lit it before he answered.

“About as much as poor old Froyant discovered,” he said. “In fact, I followed very closely the same line of investigation that he had. It was mostly and mainly about Marl and his iniquities. You know that he was a member of a criminal gang in France, and that he and his companion, Lightman﻿—I think that was the name﻿—were condemned to death. Lightman should have died, but the executioners bungled the job, and he was sent off to Devil’s Island, or Cayenne, or one of those French settlements, where he died.”

“He escaped,” said Yale quietly.

“The devil he did.” Mr. Parr looked up. “Personally, I wasn’t so interested in Lightman as I was in Marl.”

“Do you speak French, Parr?” asked Yale suddenly.

===Chinese===
“你在法国查出了什么？我一直没机会同你细谈这件事。”耶尔问。

帕尔朝椅背上一仰，摸出烟斗，点着了才开口。

“跟可怜的老弗鲁瓦扬查出的差不多，”他说，“实际上，我循的正是他当年那条调查路线，主要也是围着马尔和他的种种罪孽打转。你知道，他在法国参加过一伙匪帮，同他的同伙利特曼——我记得是这个名字——一起被判了死刑。利特曼本该死的，刽子手却把差事办砸了，他被发配去了魔鬼岛（Devil’s Island），要么就是卡宴（Cayenne），反正进了法国那一类流放地，死在了那儿。”

“他越狱了。”耶尔轻声说。

“嘿，竟有这等事。”帕尔先生抬起头来。“就我本人而论，上心马尔，多过上心利特曼。”

“你会讲法语吗，帕尔？”耶尔冷不丁问道。

===Original===
“Fluently,” was the reply, and the inspector looked up. “Why do you ask?”

“I have no reason, except that I wondered how you pursued your inquiries.”

“I speak French﻿—very well,” said Parr, and would have changed the subject.

“And Lightman escaped,” said Yale softly. “I wonder where he is now.”

“That is a question I have never troubled to ask myself.” There was a note of impatience in the inspector’s voice.

“You were not the only person interested in Marl, apparently. I saw a note on your desk from young Beardmore, saying that he had discovered some papers relating to the late Felix. His father had also made inquiries about the man. Of course, James Beardmore would. He was a cautious man.”

===Chinese===
“讲得流利。”探长答了一句，抬起头来。“你问这个做什么？”

“没什么缘故，不过是纳闷你怎么开展查访的。”

“我法语讲得——很好。”帕尔说着，便想岔开话头。

“而利特曼越了狱，”耶尔轻声道，“不知他如今人在何处。”

“这个问题，我可从来没劳神问过自己。”探长的语气里透出一丝不耐烦。

“看样子，上心马尔的不止你一个。我在你桌上见到小比德莫尔写来的便条，说他发现了同已故的菲利克斯有关的一些文件。他父亲生前也打听过这个人。詹姆斯·比德莫尔当然会这么做——他是个谨慎人。”

===Original===
He was lunching with the Commissioner, Mr. Parr learnt, and was not at all hurt that he was excluded from the invitation. He was very busy in these days, selecting the men who were to form the bodyguard of the Cabinet, and he could well afford to miss engagements which invariably bored him.

As it happens, his company would have been a great embarrassment, for Yale had something to communicate to the Commissioner, something which it was not well that Inspector Parr should hear. It was near to the end of the meal that he dropped his bombshell, and it was so effective that the Commissioner fell back in his chair and gasped.

“Somebody at police headquarters,” he said incredulously. “Why, that is impossible, Mr. Yale.”

Derrick Yale shook his head.

“I wouldn’t say anything was impossible, sir,” he said, “but doesn’t it seem to you that all the evidence tends to support that idea? Every effort that we make to bring about the undoing of the Crimson Circle is anticipated. Somebody having access to the cell of Sibly, killed him. Who but a person having authority from headquarters? Take the case of Froyant: there were a number of detectives on duty round and about the house; nobody apparently came in and nobody went out.”

===Chinese===
帕尔探长这才得知，耶尔要同总监共进午餐；自己没在受邀之列，他丝毫不觉得委屈。这些日子他忙得很，正挑选将组成内阁卫队的人手；这种一成不变叫他发闷的饭局，错过去了他正求之不得。

而事情偏偏是，他若在座，只会叫人为难得紧，因为耶尔有话要对总监交代——有些话，是不宜让帕尔探长听见的。饭快吃到头了，他才把那颗炸弹丢出来；这一手实在灵验，总监一听，仰倒在椅背上，直倒抽凉气。

“‘警察总部里有内线’……”他满腹狐疑地说，“哎呀，这不可能，耶尔先生。”

德里克·耶尔摇了摇头。

“我不敢说什么事绝无可能，先生，”他说，“可您不觉得，桩桩件件的证据都在往这个想法上指吗？我们为搞垮红圈使出的每一招，都被人先一步料中。有人能进西布利的牢房，把他杀了——除了一个手里握着总部授权的人，还能有谁？再拿弗鲁瓦扬一案来说：宅子里外围布了不少当值的侦探；明摆着没人进去，也没人出来。”

===Original===
The Commissioner was calmer now.

“Let us have this thing clear, Mr. Yale,” he said. “Are you accusing Parr?”

Derrick Yale laughed and shook his head.

“Why, of course not,” he said. “I cannot imagine Parr having a single criminal instinct. Only if you will think the matter out,” he leant over the table and lowered his voice, “and will go into every detail and every crime that the Crimson Circle has committed, you cannot fail to be struck by this fact: that, hovering in the background all the time was somebody in authority.”

“Parr?” said the Commissioner.

Derrick Yale bit his lower lip thoughtfully.

===Chinese===
总监此刻镇静下来了。

“咱们把这桩事摆明了说，耶尔先生，”他说道，“你这是在指控帕尔吗？”

德里克·耶尔笑了，摇着头。

“哦，当然不是，”他说，“帕尔身上有一星半点的犯罪天性，这我没法想象。只是，您若肯把这事细细想一遍——”他朝桌面俯过身去，压低了嗓音，“把红圈犯下的每一桩罪行、每一处细节都推敲到，有一件事您不可能不为之悚然：始终有一个身居权位的人，在幕后徘徊不去。”

“帕尔？”总监说。

德里克·耶尔若有所思地咬住了下唇。

===Original===
“I don’t want to think of Parr,” he said. “I would rather think of him as being victimised by a subordinate he trusts. You quite understand,” he went on quickly, “that I should not hesitate to accuse Parr if my discoveries took me in that direction. I would not even free you, sir, from suspicion, if you gave me cause.”

The Commissioner looked uncomfortable.

“I can assure you that I know nothing whatever about the Crimson Circle,” he said gruffly, and realising the absurdity of his protest, laughed.

“Who is that girl over there?” he pointed to a couple who were dining in a corner of the big restaurant. “She keeps looking across toward you.”

“That girl,” said Mr. Derrick Yale carefully, “is a young lady named Thalia Drummond, and her companion, unless I am greatly mistaken, is the Honourable Raphael Willings, a member of the Government and one who has been threatened by the Crimson Circle.”

“Thalia Drummond?” The Commissioner whistled. “Isn’t she the young person who was in very serious trouble some time ago? She was Froyant’s secretary, was she not?”

===Chinese===
“我不愿意往帕尔身上想，”他说，“我宁可把他看作遭了自己信任的某个下属的暗算。您务必要明白，”他紧接着又说，“倘若我的查访把我引向那个方向，我指控帕尔绝不会迟疑。就连您本人，先生，只要让我抓到由头，我也一样不放过。”

总监显出了不自在。

“我向您保证，红圈的种种，我一概不知。”他粗声粗气地说，随即意识到这抗议实在可笑，自己先笑了。

“那边那位姑娘是谁？”他指了指正在大餐馆一角用饭的一对男女。“她一个劲儿地朝您这边张望。”

“那位姑娘，”德里克·耶尔先生字斟句酌地说，“是一位名叫塔莉娅·德拉蒙德的小姐；她那位同伴，除非我大错特错，正是拉斐尔·威林斯阁下——政府的一员，也是受过红圈威胁的人。”

“塔莉娅·德拉蒙德？”总监打了个呼哨。“她不就是前些日子闯了大祸的那个年轻女子吗？她当过弗鲁瓦扬的秘书，对不对？”

===Original===
The other nodded.

“She is an enigma to me,” he said, shaking his head, “and the greatest mystery of all is her nerve. At this precise moment she is supposed to be sitting in my office answering telephone calls and dealing with any correspondence which may arrive.”

“You employ her, do you?” asked the astonished Commissioner, and then with a little smile, “I agree with you about her nerve, but how does a girl of that class come to be acquainted with Mr. Willings?”

Here Derrick Yale was not prepared to supply an answer.

He was still sitting with the Commissioner when he saw the girl rise and, followed by her companion, walk slowly down the room. Her way led her past his table, and she met his enquiring glance with a smile and a little nod, and said something over her shoulder to the middle-aged man who was following her.

===Chinese===
对方点了点头。

“她于我是桩难解之谜，”他摇着头说，“而其中最大的谜，莫过于她的胆量。此时此刻，她本该坐在我的办公室里接听电话、处理随时寄到的函件。”

“你雇着她？”总监愕然问，随即微微一笑，“说到她的胆量，我同意；可她那种身份的姑娘，又是怎么结识威林斯先生的？”

这一问，德里克·耶尔就接不上了。

他还陪着总监坐着，忽见那姑娘起身，由同伴跟着，缓步穿过店堂走来。她走的路线恰好经过他这张桌子；迎上他探询的目光，她嫣然一笑，微微颔首，又回过头去，对跟在身后的中年男子说了句什么。

===Original===
“How is that for nerve?” asked Derrick.

“I should imagine you’d have something to say to the young lady,” was the Commissioner’s only comment.

Derrick Yale was very seldom conventional, either in his speech or his behaviour, but for once he found it difficult to deal with a painful situation other than in the time-honoured way.

The girl had reached the office a few minutes before him, and she was taking off her hat when he came in.

“One moment, Miss Drummond,” he said. “I have a few words to say to you before you continue your work. Why were you away from the office at lunch time? I particularly asked you to be here.”

“And Mr. Willings particularly asked me to go to lunch,” said Thalia with an innocent smile, “and as he is a member of the Government, I am sure you would not have liked me to refuse.”

===Chinese===
“瞧瞧这份胆量，如何？”德里克问。

“我看，你该同这位小姐有话要说。”总监只应了这么一句。

德里克·耶尔无论谈吐还是举止，都难得循规蹈矩；可这一回，面对这桩难堪的局面，他发现除了按老规矩办，竟没有别的法子可想。

姑娘比他早几分钟到了办公室；他进门时，她正在摘帽子。

“稍等，德拉蒙德小姐，”他说，“你接着做事之前，我有几句话要讲。午饭时分你为何不在办公室？我特意叮嘱过，要你守在这儿。”

“威林斯先生也特意请我去吃午餐呀，”塔莉娅带着一脸天真的笑容说，“他既是政府里的人，我笃定您总不至于乐意我回绝吧。”

===Original===
“How did you come to know Mr. Willings?”

She looked at him up and down with that cool, insolent glance of hers.

“There are many ways one may meet men,” she said. “One may advertise for them in the matrimonial newspapers, or one may meet them in the park, or one may be introduced to them. I was introduced to Mr. Willings.”

“When?”

“This morning,” she said, “at about two o’clock. I sometimes go to dances at Merros Club,” she explained. “It is the relaxation which my youth excuses. That is where we became acquainted.”

===Chinese===
“你是怎么认识威林斯先生的？”

她把他上上下下打量了一遍，用的还是她那冷淡而放肆的目光。

“结识男人的门道多得很，”她说，“可以在征婚报上登广告钓他们，可以在公园里撞见他们，也可以经人介绍认识他们。我同威林斯先生，就是经人介绍的。”

“什么时候？”

“今天早上，”她说，“两点来钟吧。我有时去梅罗斯俱乐部（Merros Club）跳舞，”她解释道，“年轻人贪这么点消遣，情有可原。我们就是在那儿认识的。”

===Original===
Yale took some money from his pocket and laid it on the desk.

“There is your week’s wages, Miss Drummond,” he said without heat. “I shall not require your services after this afternoon.”

She raised her eyebrows.

“Aren’t you going to reform me?” she asked him so seriously that he was taken aback. Then he laughed.

“You’re beyond reformation. There are many things I will excuse, and had there been a serious shortage in the petty cash, I could have overlooked that. But I cannot allow you to leave my office when I give you explicit instructions to stay here.”

She picked up the money and counted it.

===Chinese===
耶尔从口袋里掏出些钱来，搁在写字台上。

“这是你这个礼拜的工钱，德拉蒙德小姐，”他不愠不火地说，“今天下午过后，我就不需要你效力了。”

她扬起了眉毛。

“您不打算改造改造我了吗？”她问得那样一本正经，倒叫他怔了一下。随后他笑了。

“你已经无可救药了。许多事我都可以不计较；哪怕零用现金真短了一大笔，我也能睁一只眼闭一只眼。可我明明白白吩咐你守在此地，你却擅自离开我的办公室，这个我容不下。”

她拿起钱来，点了一遍。

===Original===
“Exactly the sum,” she mocked. “You must be Scottish, Mr. Yale.”

“There is only one way that you could be reformed, Thalia Drummond.” His voice was very earnest, and he seemed to experience a difficulty in finding the right words.

“And what is that, pray?”

“For a man to marry you. I’m almost inclined to make the experiment.”

She sat on the edge of the desk and rocked with silent laughter.

===Chinese===
“分毫不差，”她讥诮道，“您准是苏格兰人，耶尔先生。”

“能让你改邪归正的，只有一条路，塔莉娅·德拉蒙德。”他的语气极其恳切，一时仿佛找不着恰当的字眼。

“哦，那敢情是条什么路？倒要请教。”

“就是让一个男人娶了你。我简直跃跃欲试，想亲身做一做这个试验。”

她一屁股坐上写字台的边沿，无声地笑得前仰后合。

===Original===
“You are funny,” she said at last, “and now I see that you are a true reformer.” She was solemnity itself now. “Confess, Mr. Yale, that you only look upon me as an experiment, and that you have no more affection for me than I have for that aged and decrepit bluebottle crawling up the wall.”

“I’m not in love with you, if that is what you mean.”

“I did mean something of the sort,” she said. “No, on the whole, I think I’ll take my dismissal and my week’s wages, and thank you for giving me the opportunity of meeting and serving such a brilliant genius.”

He ended the conversation as though he had made some business proposal which had been declined, and said something about giving her a reference, and there the matter ended for him. He went into his office, and did not even do her the honour of slamming the door after him.

===Chinese===
“你这个人真逗，”她终于说道，“这下我看明白了，你是个不折不扣的改造家。”这会儿她一脸庄重。“招认吧，耶尔先生，你不过是把我看成一件试验品；你对我的情意，并不比我对那只正往墙上爬、老态龙钟的绿头苍蝇多上一丝一毫。”

“我并不爱你——倘若你指的是这个。”

“我指的正是这层意思，”她说，“罢了，权衡下来，我想我还是领了这份辞退、收下这一礼拜的工钱，再谢谢您给了我这个机会，得以结识并侍奉这样一位光彩照人的天才。”

他把这场谈话了结了，那副神情，倒像是一桩生意提议遭到了回绝；又说了几句可以给她写封推荐信之类的话，这桩事在他这儿就算过去了。他走进自己的办公室，甚至连朝她身后砰地把门摔上这点“敬意”，都没赏给她。

===Original===
And yet her dismissal was a serious matter for Thalia. It meant one of two things. Either that Derrick Yale seriously suspected her﻿—and that was the gravest possibility to her﻿—or else that her discharge was only a ruse, part of a deeper plan to bring about her undoing.

On her way home she recalled his reference to Johnson of Mildred Street. There might be something behind that beyond the revelation of the fact that he knew she was associated with the Crimson Circle, and he wanted her to know he knew.

When she reached her flat there was a letter waiting for her, as there had been on the previous night. The controlling spirit of the Crimson Circle was an assiduous correspondent as far as she was concerned. In the privacy of her own room she tore open the envelope.

You did well [the letter ran]. You have carried out my instructions to the letter. The introduction to Willings was well managed and, as I promised you, there was no difficulty. I wish you to know this man thoroughly and discover what are his little weaknesses. Particularly do I wish to know his attitude of mind and the real attitude of the Cabinet towards my proposal. The dress you wore at lunch today was not quite good enough. Do not spare expense in the matter of costume. Derrick Yale is dismissing you this afternoon, but that need not trouble you, for there is no further need for you to stay in his office. You are dining tonight with Willings. He is particularly susceptible to feminine charms. If possible, let him invite you to his house. He has a collection of ancient swords of which he is very proud. You will then be able to discover the lay of the house.

She looked into the envelope. There were two crisp notes for a hundred pounds, and as she put them into her little handbag her face was very grave.

===Chinese===
然而对塔莉娅来说，遭到辞退却是件非同小可的事。这意味着两种可能：要么，德里克·耶尔当真起了疑心——这在她看来是最凶险的一种；要么，这场解雇不过是个幌子，是一整套更深的图谋的一环，为的是把她彻底毁了。

回家的路上，她又想起他提过的米尔德丽德街的约翰逊。那话背后或许另有文章，不止是亮明他知道她与红圈有牵连，也不止是要她知道他心里有数。

她回到寓所，一封信正等着她，同前一天晚上一模一样。就她而论，红圈的幕后主使倒是书信殷勤。她躲进自己房里避了人，撕开信封。

你干得不错[信上写道]。我的指示，你一字不差地照办了。引荐给威林斯一事办得很妥帖，正如我答应过你的，毫无波折。我要你把这个人摸个透，探明他有什么小小的弱点。我尤其想弄清他的心思，以及内阁对我的提议的真实态度。你今天午餐穿的那身衣裳还不够好。置办行头，切勿吝惜花销。德里克·耶尔今天下午就要辞退你，但你不必为此烦心，因为你已无需再留在他办公室里。今晚你将同威林斯共进晚餐。他对女性魅力尤其把持不住。如有可能，让他邀你去他府上。他收藏了一批古剑，引以为豪。到那时，你便能摸清那座宅子的格局了。

她朝信封里看了看。里面是两张挺括的新钞，共一百镑；她把钱收进小手袋时，脸色十分凝重。

## XXXVI / 三十六

===Original===

The Circle Meets

===Chinese===

红圈集会

===Original===

Mr. Raphael Willings was a product of his age. Though he was still in the early forties, he had pushed himself into Cabinet rank by the sheer force of his character. To describe him as a popular Minister would be to stretch the truth beyond permissible bounds. He was neither popular with his colleagues, nor with the country who, whilst recognising his remarkable powers and acclaiming him as the greatest of the parliamentary orators, nevertheless distrusted him. He had given so many proofs of his insincerity that it was remarkable that he should have attained to the position he occupied.

But he had a number of followers. Men who were unwavering in their faith, who could be depended upon to vote steadily at the lift of his finger, and the Government majority was too small to risk the exclusion of the Willings’ bloc.

Amongst his colleagues he had a bad name. It is not necessary to particularise the circumstances which produced his reputation, but it is a notorious fact that he escaped appearing in an unsavoury divorce case by the skin of his teeth. So unpopular was he that twice Merros Club and a fashionable night club of which he was a member and an habitué, were raided by the police in the hope of compromising this flighty politician. The raid had been planned by the wife of one of his colleagues, and that Willings was not unaware of the fact, was proved when the newspaper he owned aimed a bitter attack on the lady’s unfortunate husband, an attack so worded, so framed, that the Minister retired from public life.

===Chinese===

拉斐尔·威林斯先生是他那个时代的产物。他年纪不过四十出头，却全凭一股性格上的狠劲，硬是把自己挤进了内阁的行列。若说他是一位深孚众望的大臣，那就把实话拉伸到许可的限度之外了。他既不得同僚之心，也不得国人之心——国人虽承认他才干出众、奉他为议会第一雄辩家，对他却始终不能放心。他言不由衷的凭据给了人太多，居然能爬到今日占据的位置，倒真是一桩奇事。

但他也有一批追随者。这些人信念坚定、绝无动摇，只要他指头一抬，就可以指望他们稳稳当当投下票来；而政府的多数席位本就单薄，冒不起把威林斯这一派排挤出局的风险。

在同僚中间，他名声不佳。落下这名声的种种情由，不必一一细表；但有桩事实路人皆知：他曾在一宗声名狼藉的离婚案里，险之又险地逃过了出庭的命运。他的人缘坏到这般地步，以致梅罗斯俱乐部和一家时新夜总会——他既为其会员，又是其常客——两度遭警察突击搜查，指望抓住这位轻浮政客的把柄。搜查出自他一位同僚太太的策划；而威林斯对此并非不知，证据便是：他名下的报纸向那位太太倒霉的丈夫发起恶毒攻击，措辞之刻毒、构陷之周密，竟叫那位大臣从此退出了政界。

===Original===

A well-built man inclined to plumpness, slightly bald, there was no gainsaying his personal charm. He was under the impression that his introduction to Thalia Drummond had been skilfully manoeuvred by himself. He would have been horrified to know that the lady who introduced him had received instructions that morning from the Crimson Circle to bring the introduction about. The Crimson Circle had its agents in all branches of life and in all classes. There were bookkeepers, there was at least one railway director, there was a doctor and three *chefs d’hotel* amongst the hundred who obeyed the call of the Crimson Circle. They were well paid and their duties were not onerous. Sometimes, as in this case, they had no more to do than to bring about an introduction between two people whom the Crimson Circle desired to meet, but in every case their instructions came to them in exactly the same form.

The organisation of this great force was extraordinarily complete. In some uncanny way the chief of the Crimson Circle had smelt penury and disaster almost as soon as the suffering recipients of these two evil factors were aware that they were present. One by one they had been absorbed, each ignorant of the other’s identity, and profoundly ignorant of their master. He had come to them in strange places and circumstances. Each had his own function to perform, and generally the part which was played by the subordinate members of the league was ludicrously simple and unimportant.

A few members of the Circle had, in a panic, made statements to police headquarters, and from them it was learned how simple were some of the tasks which were given out by the mystery man.

===Chinese===

他体格结实，略见发福，头顶微秃，而其个人的魅力却是不容抹杀的。他满以为自己与塔莉娅·德拉蒙德的结识，全是他一手巧妙运筹的结果。他若是知道，那位替他引见的女士当天早晨刚接到红圈的指令、奉命促成这番引见，只怕会惊骇不已。红圈的密探遍布各行各业、各个阶层。在听红圈号令的那百来人里，有账房，有至少一位铁路公司董事，有一名医生，还有三位饭店经理。他们报酬优厚，差事也不繁重。有时，比如这一回，他们无非是把红圈想要会面的两个人引见到一处罢了；但无论哪一桩，指令送到他们手里，格式都一模一样。

这支庞大势力的组织严密得出奇。红圈头子自有一手神鬼莫测的本领：穷困和灾祸刚落到哪个人头上，那个受这两样祸害煎熬的当事人还没察觉它们已经临身，他几乎就已嗅出了气味。这些人被他一个个收罗进来，彼此不知对方是谁，对主子更是一无所知。他曾在种种离奇的地点、离奇的境况之下找上他们。每人都有一份职司，而这个组织的下层成员所担当的角色，一般都简单得、无足轻重得近乎可笑。

有那么几个红圈成员慌了手脚，向警察总部作了供述；从他们口中才得知，那位神秘人分派下去的差使，有些竟是何等简单。

===Original===

From fear of the tragic consequences of disloyalty, the majority of the Crimson Circle remained loyal to their unknown chief, and it was a remarkable tribute to his system of espionage, that when he sent forth his summons, as he did on the day Derrick Yale lunched with the Commissioner, calling every member of the Crimson Circle to the first meeting they had ever held, giving them the most explicit instructions as to the garb they should wear, and the means they should adopt to avoid disclosing themselves to their fellows, he omitted the waverers and the malcontents as though their very thoughts were written plainly before him.

To Thalia Drummond that meeting will always remain the most vivid and poignant memory of her association with the Crimson Circle.

The city contains many old churches, but none anterior in date to the church of St. Agnes on Powder Hill. It had escaped the ravages of the Great Fire, only to be smothered under by the busy city which had grown up about it. Enclosed by tall warehouses, so that its squat steeple was absent from the skyline, it had a congregation which might be numbered on the fingers of two hands, although it supported a vicar who preached punctiliously every week to a congregation which was practically paid to attend. Once a churchyard had surrounded it, and the bones of the faithful had been laid to peace within its shadow, but the avaricious city, grudging so much waste building land, had passed Acts which had removed the bones to a more salubrious situation and had covered the place of family vaults with office buildings.

===Chinese===

红圈的大多数成员慑于叛变的下场之惨，始终效忠于他们那位无名的主子；而他的谍报体系何等了得，有一桩事足以为证——德里克·耶尔与总监共进午餐的那一天，他果然发出了召集令，把红圈的每一名成员召来参加他们有生以来的第一次集会，连各自该穿什么装束、该用什么法子避免在同伴面前暴露身份，都交代得毫厘不爽；而那些意志动摇之徒、心怀怨望之辈，他一概撇在一边，仿佛他们的心思就明明白白摊在他眼前。

对塔莉娅·德拉蒙德来说，那次集会将永远是她与红圈这段瓜葛中最鲜明、最刺心的一段记忆。

伦敦城里老教堂为数不少，论年头却没有一座比得上粉山（Powder Hill）上的圣阿格尼斯教堂（St. Agnes）。它逃过了伦敦大火的浩劫，到头来却被在它四周滋生起来的繁忙市井活活埋没。高大的货栈把它围在当中，连它那矮墩墩的尖顶也不曾在天际线上占得一席之地；它的会众扳着两手指头就数得尽，却养着一位教区牧师，每周一板一眼地向一批差不多算是花钱雇来听道的会众布道。教堂四周原有一片墓园，虔诚信徒的骸骨曾安息在它的荫庇之下；无奈这座贪得无厌的都市舍不得偌大地皮白白闲置，接二连三通过法令，把骸骨迁往更有益卫生的所在，又在家族墓穴的原址上盖起了办公楼。

===Original===

Entrance to the church was up an alley which led from a side passage and the figures which slunk along the unlighted way seemed to melt through the almost invisible doors into a gloom even thicker than the night.

For in the church of St. Agnes the Crimson Circle held the first and last meeting of his servitors.

Here, again, his organisation was marvellous. Every member of his company had received explicit orders telling him to the very minute when he must arrive, so that no two came together. How he obtained the keys of the church; what careful manoeuvring he must have planned to bring the hour of meeting and the dispersal between the two periods when the lane would be patrolled by the City police, Thalia Drummond could only guess.

===Chinese===

进这教堂要先走一条小巷，小巷又从一条侧道拐进去；那些沿着无灯路径潜行的人影，仿佛从几不可辨的门口渗了进去，没入一片比夜色更浓的幽暗。

因为红圈就是在圣阿格尼斯教堂里，召集他的党羽举行了第一次、也是最后一次集会。

在这一点上，他的组织功夫又是神乎其神。他的麾下每一名成员都接到明白无误的指令，几点几分必须到场都规定到分，保证没有两个人会撞在一处。教堂的钥匙他如何到手；为把开会的时刻和散场的时间都掐在伦敦城警察巡巷的两段间隙之内，他又筹划过何等细密的调度——这一切，塔莉娅·德拉蒙德只能凭空猜测。

===Original===
