She came into the alleyway punctually, went up the two steps to a door which opened as she approached and was closed immediately she entered the lobby. There was no light of any kind, save for the faint light of night which filtered through a stained-glass window.

“Go straight ahead,” whispered a voice. “You will take the end of the second pew on the right.”

There were other people in the church. She could just distinguish them, two in each pew, a silent, ghostly congregation, none speaking to the other. Presently the man who had admitted her came into the church and walked to the altar rails, and at the first words she knew that the servants of the Crimson Circle sat in the presence of their master.

===Chinese===

她准时走进小巷，登上两级台阶，来到一扇门前；她人还没到，门就开了，待她跨进门厅，门又随即关上。里面没有一星灯火，只有夜的微光透过一扇彩色玻璃窗渗进来。

“一直往前走，”一个声音悄声道。“你坐右边第二排长椅的尽头。”

教堂里还有旁人。她只能勉强辨出轮廓：每排长椅上两个，满堂会众鸦雀无声、鬼影幢幢，谁也不与谁交谈。不多时，放她进来的那个男人走进教堂，径直走到圣坛栏杆前；一开腔她就明白：红圈的仆从们，此刻正坐在他们主子的面前。

===Original===

His voice was low and muffled and hollow; she guessed he wore the veil she had seen over his head the first night she had met him.

“My friends,” he said, and she heard every word, “the time has come when our society will be dispersed. You have read my offer in the public press; and you are interested to this extent, that I intend distributing at least twenty percent of the money which the Government must eventually give me amongst those who have served me. If there are any here who are nervous that we shall be interrupted, let me assure them that the police patrol does not pass for another quarter of an hour, and that it is quite impossible for the sound of my voice to reach outside the church.”

He raised his voice a little, and there was a hard note in it when he added:

===Chinese===

他的嗓音低沉、喑哑、发空；她猜他头上蒙着那块面纱——她头一夜见到他时见过的那块。

“朋友们，”他说——他说的每一个字她都听得真切——“我们这个团体解散的时刻到了。我在报端公布的开价，诸位已经读到；诸位与这件事的利害所关，无非是这一点：政府最终不得不交到我手里的那笔款项，我打算至少提出两成，分给为我效过力的人。这里若有人担心我们会被人搅了局，让我向他保证：警察巡逻队再过一刻钟不会经过，而我的声音也绝无可能传出教堂之外。”

他把嗓音略微抬高了一点，语气里添了一层硬意，接着说道：

===Original===

“And to those who may have treachery in their hearts, and imagine that so widely announced a meeting might bring about my undoing, let me say that it is impossible that I shall be captured tonight. Ladies and gentlemen, I will not disguise from you that we are in considerable danger. Facts which may enable the police to identify me have on two occasions almost come to light. I have upon my tracks, Derrick Yale, who I will not deny is a source of considerable anxiety to me, and Inspector Parr”﻿—he paused﻿—“who is not to be despised. In this supreme moment I do not hesitate to call upon every one of you for an extraordinary effort of assistance. Tomorrow you will each receive operation orders prepared in such detail that it will be impossible for you to misunderstand any particular requirement I have made known. Remember that you are as much in danger as I,” he said more softly, “and your reward shall be correspondingly great. Now you will pass out of the church one by one, at thirty seconds interval, beginning with the first two on the right, continuing with the first two on the left. Go!”

===Chinese===

“至于那些心里也许包藏叛逆、以为一场如此大张旗鼓宣布的集会或许能扳倒我的人，我要说：今夜我绝无被捕之理。女士们，先生们，我不向诸位隐瞒：我们眼下的处境相当危险。有两回，足以让警方认出我来的事实几乎败露。在我背后穷追不舍的，有德里克·耶尔——此人之于我，我不否认，是相当大的一桩心事；还有帕尔探长”——他顿了顿——“此人也不容小觑。值此存亡关头，我不客气地要你们每一个人拿出非常的气力，助我一臂。明天，你们每人都将收到行动指令，编写得详之又详，我公布的任何一项具体要求，你们绝无误解之虞。”他说得更柔和了些，“记住：你们的危险和我一样大，而你们的报酬也将同样丰厚。现在，你们一个接一个退出教堂，间隔三十秒，从右首前两位开始，接着左首前两位。走！”

===Original===

At intervals these dark figures glided along the aisle and vanished through the door to the left of the pulpit.

The man at the chancel rails waited until the church was empty and then he, too, passed through the door into the lobby and into the passage.

He locked the outer door and slipped the key into his pocket. The church clock was booming the half-hour when he called a taxicab and was driven westward.

===Chinese===

那些黑影按着间隔沿中央通道鱼贯滑行，一个个消失在布道坛左侧的门后。

圣坛栏杆前的那个人一直等到教堂空无一人，才也从那扇门里穿出去，进了门厅，进了通道。

他锁上外门，把钥匙悄悄揣进衣袋。待他叫来一辆出租汽车、坐车向西驶去时，教堂的钟正呜呜地敲响半点。

===Original===

Thalia Drummond had preceded him by a quarter of an hour, and in the taxi which carried her to the same end of the town she brought about a lightning transformation of her appearance. The old black raincoat which covered her to the throat, the heavy-veiled black hat, were taken off. Beneath it she wore a cloak of delicate silk tissue, covering an evening dress which would have satisfied her apparently exigent master.

She took off her hat and tidied her hair as well as she could, and when she stepped down at the flashing entrance of Merros Club and handed a small attaché case to the bowing attendant, she was a picture of radiant loveliness.

So Jack Beardmore thought. He was supping with some friends much against his will, for he hated the night side of life, when he saw her come in, and scowled jealously at her debonair escort.

===Chinese===

塔莉娅·德拉蒙德比他早走了一刻钟；在载她前往城区同一头的出租汽车里，她给自己的仪表来了个闪电式的大改造。那件一直裹到喉咙口的老式黑雨衣、那顶垂着厚厚面纱的黑帽子，都脱了下来。底下她穿的是一领轻薄的纱绸斗篷，罩着一身晚礼服——这身装束，足以叫她那位要求显然苛刻得出奇的主子满意。

她摘下帽子，尽力把头发理了理；待她在梅罗斯俱乐部灯火炫目的门前下车，把一只小提箱交给躬身相迎的侍者时，她已是光彩照人、明艳如画。

杰克·比德莫尔心里正是这么想的。他正百般无奈地陪几位朋友吃夜宵——他素来厌恶夜生活这一套——一眼望见她走进来，顿时朝她那位风流倜傥的男伴妒意十足地沉下了脸。

===Original===

“Who is he?”

Jack’s companion glanced across lazily.

“I don’t know the lady,” he said, “but the man is Raphael Willings. He is a big pot in the Government.”

===Chinese===

“他是谁？”

杰克的同伴懒洋洋地朝那边瞟了一眼。

“那位小姐我不认识，”他说，“不过那男的是拉斐尔·威林斯。他在政府里可是个大人物。”

===Original===

Thalia Drummond had seen the young man before he had seen her, and she groaned inwardly. Half of what her host said she missed; her mind was completely absorbed in other directions, and it was not until a familiar phrase reached her ear that she turned her interest toward the Minister.

“Antique swords,” she said with a start. “I’m told you have a wonderful collection, Mr. Willings.”

“Are you interested?” he smiled.

“A little. In fact, quite a lot,” she said awkwardly, and it was not like Thalia to be at a loss for a reply.

===Chinese===

塔莉娅·德拉蒙德早在那青年望见她之前就看见了他，心里暗暗叫苦。男主人说的话，她足足漏听了一半；她的心神整个牵在别处，直到一个耳熟的词儿飘进耳朵，她才把心思转回到这位大臣身上。

“古董刀剑，”她一惊，脱口说道。“听说您藏有一批绝妙的藏品，威林斯先生。”

“您对这个有兴趣？”他微笑道。

“有一点。其实，兴味大得很。”她说得局促不安——塔莉娅素来不是答不上话的人。

===Original===

“Could I ask you to come along to tea one day and see them?” said Raphael. “One doesn’t often find a woman who is interested in such things. Shall we say tomorrow?”

“Not tomorrow,” said Thalia hastily. “Perhaps the next day.”

He made the appointment then and there, writing it ostensibly on his cuff.

===Chinese===

“可否请您哪天过来喝杯茶，看一看它们？”拉斐尔说。“对这类东西感兴趣的女人可不多见。就定明天，怎么样？”

“明天不成，”塔莉娅急忙说，“也许后天吧。”

他当场把日子敲定，还装模作样地把它写在了袖口上。

===Original===

She saw Jack leave the club without a look in her direction, and she felt absurdly miserable. She did so want to talk to him and was praying that he would come over to their table.

Mr. Willings insisted upon driving her home in his car, and she left him with a sigh of relief. He did not harmonise with her mood that night.

There was a little forecourt to the flats in which she lived, and she had dismissed her admirer (he made no secret of this relationship) in the street outside. She had to walk a dozen paces to reach one of the two entrances, and even before she had sent her escort away, she was aware that a man was waiting for her in the darkened court. She stood on the pavement until Willings’s car had moved on, and then she came slowly toward the waiting man. He spoke for a minute in a voice that was a little above a whisper, and she responded in the same tone.

===Chinese===

她眼看着杰克离开俱乐部，连朝她这边瞥一眼都没有，心里没来由地一阵凄惶。她多么想同他讲讲话，一路只暗暗祷告他能走到他们这一桌来。

威林斯先生执意要用自己的汽车送她回家；离开他时，她如释重负地舒了一口气。那天夜里，他同她的心绪怎么也合不上拍。

她住的公寓楼前有一小片前院；她已把那位倾慕者（他对这层关系从不讳言）在街面上打发走了。她还要走上十来步，才能到达两个入口中的一个；甚至还没送走男伴，她已觉察到昏暗的院子里有个男人在等她。她站在人行道上，直到威林斯的汽车开走，才慢慢向那个等候的人走去。他用比耳语高不了多少的声调说了一分钟话，她也以同样的声调作答。

===Original===

The conversation was of very short duration. Presently the man turned without sign or word of farewell, and walked quickly away and the girl entered her flat.

Though the man made no sign, he knew he was being followed. He had been waiting for ten minutes in the dark of the forecourt and had seen the stealthy figure in the doorway of a closed shop opposite the flats. Apparently, however, he was oblivious of the fact that somebody was walking behind him, somebody who he knew would presently overtake him and look into his face. He turned into a side thoroughfare where the street lamps were few and far between, and as he did so he slackened his pace. Presently the spy overtook him, choosing for the point of passing, a place within the radius of a lamp. He had bent his head to peer into the first man’s face when suddenly the quarry turned and sprang at him. The trailer was taken by surprise; before he could shout, a grip of iron was around his throat and he was flung half-senseless to the stone pavement. And then from nowhere in particular, appeared as by magic three men, who pounced upon the prostrate tracker and jerked him to his feet.

===Chinese===

这场交谈为时极短。不多时，那男人一转身，没有告别的表示，也没有告别的话，快步走开；姑娘则进了自己的公寓。

那男人虽不动声色，却知道自己正被人跟踪。他已在前院的暗处候了十分钟，看见了公寓对面一家歇业店铺门洞里那个鬼鬼祟祟的人影。不过看样子，他仿佛浑然不觉身后正走着一个人——那人，他知道，转眼就要赶上来端详他的脸。他拐进一条路灯稀稀拉拉的侧街，一拐进去便放慢了脚步。不多时，那密探果然赶上前来，还特意挑了一盏路灯的光晕之内作为超身而过的地方。他刚低下头去，要看前头那个人的脸，那猎物突然回身，朝他猛扑过来。跟踪者猝不及防；还没来得及喊出声，一只铁钳般的手已经扼住他的喉咙，他被打得半昏，掼倒在石头路面上。跟着，不知打哪儿，仿佛变戏法一般钻出三个人来，扑向趴在地上的盯梢人，一把将他拽了起来。

===Original===

He glared round, dazed and shaken, and his eyes fell upon the man he had been set to watch.

“My God!” he gasped. “I know you!”

The other smiled.

“You will never be able to employ your information, my friend,” he said.

===Chinese===

他晕头转向、惊魂未定，怒目四顾，眼光落在了他奉命监视的那个人身上。

“天哪！”他倒抽一口气。“我认得你！”

对方微微一笑。

“你那点消息，永远也派不上用场了，我的朋友。”他说。

## XXXVII / 三十七

===Original===

“I Will See You﻿—If You Are Alive”

===Chinese===

“我会来见你——如果你还活着”

===Original===

Jack Beardmore went home savage and sick at heart. Thalia Drummond was an obsession to him, and yet he had every reason to believe the worst of her. He was a fool, a thrice-condemned fool, he told himself as he paced the library, his hands thrust into his pockets, his handsome young face clouded with the gloom of despair. He felt at that moment he would like to hurt her, punish her as she unconsciously had punished him. He flung himself down into his chair and sat for an hour with his head on his hands, covering the old ground which reason had so often trodden that it had left a worn and familiar track.

He got up sick and weary, and, opening a safe, took out a packet of documents and flung them on the table. It was the sealed envelope addressed to his father and unopened which interested him most, and he had a childish desire to open it if only to spite Thalia.

Why was she so anxious that he should not see the photograph which it contained? Was she so interested in Marl? He remembered with a scowl that she had spent the evening with that man on the night he died so mysteriously. He rose, and gathering the papers together, he carried them to his bedroom. He was so tired that he had not even the curiosity to probe into the mystery which attached to the photograph of an execution. He shivered at the thought of the grisly contents, and he dropped the package on his dressing-table with a little grimace and began leisurely to undress.

He quite expected that he would pass a sleepless night; his emotion and the state of his mind seemed to call for such an end to a miserable day, but youth, if it has its anguish, has also its natural reaction. He was asleep almost as soon as his head touched the pillow. And then he began to dream. To dream of Thalia Drummond; and in his dream, Thalia was in the power of an ogre whose face was remarkably like Inspector Parr’s. He dreamt of Marl, a grotesque terrifying figure, whom he somehow associated with Inspector Parr’s grandmother﻿—that “mother” of whom he stood in such awe.

===Chinese===

杰克·比德莫尔回到家，心里怒火烧灼、苦涩难当。塔莉娅·德拉蒙德成了他心头挥不去的魔怔，可他又偏偏有一切理由把她往最坏处想。他是个傻瓜，一个该死三遍的傻瓜——他一边这样数落自己，一边在书房里踱来踱去；双手插在口袋里，那张英俊的年轻脸膛蒙着一层绝望的阴翳。那一刻他真想伤害她，想惩罚她，就像她无意间惩罚了他那样。他一屁股跌坐进椅子，双手抱头，一坐就是一个钟头，把那片老地界又从头走了一遍——理智在那上面来回踩过太多遭，早已踩出一条磨得熟透的旧辙。

他站起身来，只觉发闷作呕、疲惫不堪，便打开保险柜，取出一扎文件，摔在桌上。最让他心心念念的，是那只写明给他父亲、至今原封未动的密封信封；他心里生出一种孩子气的渴望，只想拆开它，哪怕单单为了气一气塔莉娅。

她为什么那样急切，不让他看见里面装的照片？她对马尔竟就这样上心么？他皱着眉头想起：那个人死得那般蹊跷的当晚，她恰恰是同他一起消磨的。他起身把文件归拢到一处，抱进了卧室。他累到了极点，连探一探那张行刑照片所附谜团的好奇心都提不起来。一想到封套里那点阴森可怖的内容，他就不寒而栗；他把那包东西往梳妆台上一撂，微微做了个苦相，慢条斯理地宽起衣来。

他满以为自己要彻夜无眠了；以他这份激动、这副心绪，这愁苦的一天似乎合该这样收场。然而青春纵有它的苦楚，也自有它天然的反弹。头一挨枕头，他就睡熟了。跟着便做起梦来。梦见塔莉娅·德拉蒙德；梦里，塔莉娅落在一个妖魔的掌控之中，那张脸像极了帕尔探长。他还梦见了马尔，一个奇形怪状、骇人的身影，不知怎么竟同帕尔探长的祖母纠缠到了一处——就是那位令他敬畏有加的‘妈妈’。

===Original===

What woke him was the reflection of a light from the dressing-table mirror. The light had been extinguished when he sat up in bed, but, half-asleep as he was, he was certain that there had been a flash of some kind﻿—it was hardly the season for lightning.

“Who is there?” he asked, and put out his hand to reach for the lamp. But the lamp was not there; somebody had moved it. Now he saw, and was out of bed in a second.

He heard a movement toward the door and ran. Somebody was in his grip, somebody who squirmed and struggled, and then he released his hold with a gasp. It was a woman﻿—instinct told him that it was Thalia Drummond.

Slowly he put out his hand, groping for the electric switch, and the room was flooded with light.

===Chinese===

把他惊醒的，是梳妆台镜子里反射过来的一道亮光。待他从床上坐起，那光已经熄了；可他虽半睡半醒，却拿得准方才有什么东西闪了一下——眼下这个节令，总不至于打雷闪电。

“谁在那儿？”他问了一声，伸手去够台灯。台灯却不在原处；有人把它挪走了。这一下他看清了，一眨眼便翻身下床。

他听见门边一阵响动，拔腿扑了过去。有人落进了他的掌握，那人在他手里扭动挣扎；跟着他倒抽一口气，松开了手。是个女人——直觉告诉他，那是塔莉娅·德拉蒙德。

他缓缓伸出手去，摸索电灯开关；屋里顿时大放光明。

===Original===

It was Thalia﻿—Thalia as white as death and trembling. Thalia who held something behind her and met his pained gaze with a tragic attempt at defiance.

“Thalia!” he groaned, and sat down.

Thalia in his room! What had she been doing?

“Why did you come?” he asked shakily, “and what are you concealing?”

===Chinese===

果然是塔莉娅——塔莉娅面白如纸，浑身发颤。她把一样东西藏在身后，迎着他痛楚的目光，强撑出一副凄然的挑衅神色。

“塔莉娅！”他呻吟一声，坐了下来。

塔莉娅，在他的房间里！她方才在干什么？

“你到这儿来做什么？”他颤着声问，“你藏的是什么？”

===Original===

“Why did you bring those papers up to your room?” she asked almost fiercely. “If you had left them in your safe﻿—oh, why didn’t you leave them in your safe?”

And now he saw that she held the sealed packet containing the photograph of the execution.

“But﻿—but, Thalia,” he stammered, “I don’t understand you. Why didn’t you tell me﻿—”

“I told you not to look at the picture. I never dreamt you would bring it here. They have been here tonight searching for it.”

===Chinese===

“你为什么把这些文件带上楼、拿进你自己的房间？”她问得近乎凶狠。“你要是把它们留在保险柜里——唉，你为什么不把它们留在保险柜里呢？”

这时他才看清，她手里攥着的那只密封封套，装着的正是那张行刑照片。

“可是——可是，塔莉娅，”他结结巴巴地说，“我不明白你的意思。你为什么不早告诉我——”

“我嘱咐过你，别去看那张照片。我做梦也想不到，你会把它带到这儿来。今夜他们已经来过这儿搜它了。”

===Original===

She was breathless, on the verge of tears that were not all anger.

“Been here tonight?” he said slowly. “Who have been here?”

“The Crimson Circle. They knew you had that photograph, and they came and burgled your library. I was in the house when they came, and prayed﻿—prayed”﻿—she wrung her hands and he saw the look of anguish on her face. “I prayed that they would find it, and now they will think you have seen the picture. Oh, why did you do it?”

He reached for his dressing-gown, realising that his attire was somewhat scanty, and in the warm folds he felt a little more assurance.

===Chinese===

她气喘吁吁，眼泪已经涌到了眼边——那泪水又不全是气出来的。

“今夜来过？”他缓缓地说。“谁来过？”

“红圈。他们知道你手里有那张照片，便闯来把你的书房盗了个遍。他们来的时候，我正在这座宅子里，我一路祷告——祷告”——她绞着双手，他看见她脸上那副痛楚万状的神情。“我一路祷告，求他们把它找到；可如今，他们就要当你已经看过那张照片了。唉，你何苦来呢？”

他这才觉出自己身上穿得未免太单薄，便伸手抓过晨衣；裹在暖烘烘的衣褶里，他心里才多少有了些底气。

===Original===

“You are still talking Greek to me,” he said. “The thing I understand perfectly is that my house has been burgled. Will you come with me?”

She followed him down the stairs and into his library. She had spoken the truth. The door of the safe hung drunkenly upon its hinges. A hole had been cut through the shutter and it was open. The contents of the safe lay upon the floor; the drawers of his desk had been forced open and apparently a search had been made amongst the papers on the desk. Even the wastepaper basket had been turned out and searched.

“I can’t understand it,” he muttered. He was pulling the heavy curtains across the window.

“You will understand better, though I hope you do not understand too well,” she said grimly. “Now, please take a sheet of paper and write as I dictate.”

===Chinese===

“你这话说得，我听着还是天书。”他说。“有一件事我倒明白得透透的：我家遭了贼。你肯随我来一趟么？”

她跟着他下了楼，进了书房。她说的句句是实。保险柜的门歪歪斜斜地挂在铰链上；柜门挡板被割开了一个窟窿，敞在那里。柜中之物全都摊在地板上；书桌的抽屉被撬开了，桌上的文件看来也翻检过一通。连字纸篓都倒了个底朝天，细细搜过。

“我实在弄不懂。”他喃喃道，一面把厚重的窗帘往窗前拉拢。

“你会明白得更多的——不过我但愿你别明白得太透。”她冷冷地说。“现在，请你取一张纸来，我说，你写。”

===Original===

“To whom must I write?” he asked in surprise.

“Inspector Parr,” she said. “Say ‘Dear Inspector.﻿—Here is the photograph which my father received the day before his death. I have not opened it, but perhaps it may interest you.’ ”

Meekly he wrote as she ordered and signed the letter, which, with the photograph, she put into a large envelope.

“And now address it,” she said. “And write on it on the top left-hand corner, ‘From John Beardmore,’ and put after that ‘Photograph, very urgent.’ ”

===Chinese===

“写给谁？”他愕然问道。

“帕尔探长。”她说。“这么写：‘亲爱的探长。——这张照片，是我父亲去世的前一天收到的。我没有拆看，不过它或许会让您感兴趣。’”

他乖乖地照她的口授写了，又签上名；她把信连同那张照片一起装进一只大信封。

“现在写地址。”她说。“在信封左上角写上‘约翰·比德莫尔寄’，后面再添一句‘照片，十万火急’。”

===Original===

With the envelope in her hand she walked to the door.

“I shall see you tomorrow, Mr. Beardmore, if you are alive.”

He would have laughed, but there was something in her drawn face, some message in her quivering lips, that checked the laughter on his.

===Chinese===

她手拿那只信封，径直向房门走去。

“我明天再来见你，比德莫尔先生——如果你还活着的话。”

他本想笑一笑，可她那张紧绷的脸上有什么东西、她那颤抖的双唇里含着什么话，把笑意生生煞在了他自己的唇边。

## XXXVIII / 三十八

===Original===

The Arrest of Thalia

===Chinese===

塔莉娅被捕

===Original===

It was the seventh day following the meeting of the Cabinet, and, so far from agreeing with the terms of the Crimson Circle, the Government had made it known, in the most unmistakable terms, that it refused to deal with the Circle or its emissaries.

That afternoon Mr. Raphael Willings prepared for a visitor. His house in Onslow Gardens was one of the show places of the country. His collection of antique armour and swords, his priceless intaglios and his rare prints were without equal in the world. But he had no thought of his visitor’s antiquarian interests when he made his preparations, and he was less deterred than stimulated by a confidential document which had come to him, intimating in plain language the character which Thalia Drummond bore.

Thief she might be﻿—well, she could take any sword in the armoury, any print on the wall, the rarest intaglio among his show cases, so long as she was pleasant and complacent.

When Thalia came she was admitted by a foreign-looking footman and remembered that Raphael Willings had only Italian servants in the house.

Warily she surveyed the room into which she was ushered. There were open windows at each end﻿—which surprised her. She had expected to find a little tête-à-tête tea table. That was missing, and yet in this room was the cream of his collection, as she could see at a glance.

===Chinese===

内阁会议之后的第七天，政府非但没有应允红圈的条款，反而以最明白无误的措辞公然昭告天下：拒绝同红圈或其使者打任何交道。

那天下午，拉斐尔·威林斯先生为接待一位来客做了准备。他在昂斯洛花园（Onslow Gardens）的宅邸是全国屈指可数的观赏名宅之一。他收藏的古董盔甲和刀剑、他那些价值连城的凹雕宝石、他罕有的版画珍品，在世上无出其右。可他张罗准备时，心里想的并不是来客的考古雅兴；一份送到他手里的机密文书，用直白的言语道破了塔莉娅·德拉蒙德的品行底细——这与其说吓退了他，不如说更撩起了他的兴致。

小偷么，她兴许是的——好啊，只要她和颜悦色、心满意足，兵器室里随便哪柄剑、墙上随便哪幅版画、陈列柜里最稀罕的凹雕宝石，她尽管拿去便是。

塔莉娅到时，开门迎她进去的是个一副异国面孔的男仆；她记起来，拉斐尔·威林斯宅里用的全是意大利仆人。

她被引进屋里，警觉地四下打量。房间两头都开着窗——这让她暗暗称奇。她原以为会见到一张两人对坐的小茶桌，却不见踪影；然而正如她一眼便看出的，这间屋里陈列的尽是他藏品中的精华。

===Original===

Willings came in a few seconds later, and greeted her warmly.

“Eat, drink, and be merry, for tomorrow we die; perhaps today,” he said melodramatically. “Have you heard the news?”

She shook her head.

“I am the newest victim of the Crimson Circle,” he said gaily enough. “You probably read the newspapers, and know all about that famous company. Yes,” he went on with a laugh, “of all my colleagues I have the honour to be the first chosen for sacrifice; *pour encourager les autres*.”

She could not help wondering how, in these circumstances, Ralph Willings could preserve so unruffled a mien.

===Chinese===

几秒钟后威林斯走了进来，热络地招呼她。

“吃吧，喝吧，及时行乐吧，因为明天我们就死了——兴许还是今天。”他拿腔作势地说。“你听到消息了吗？”

她摇了摇头。

“我可是红圈最新的一位受害者。”他说得相当轻快。“你大概常读报纸，对那家鼎鼎大名的公司知之甚详。是啊，”他笑着接了下去，“在我的一班同僚当中，我有幸头一个被挑中做牺牲——正所谓‘杀一儆百’。”

她不禁暗自纳闷：处此境地，拉尔夫·威林斯怎么还能保持这样一副纹丝不乱的气度。

===Original===

“As the tragedy is due to occur in this house some time this afternoon,” he was continuing, “I must ask you to extend your kindness﻿—”

There was a tap at the door, and a servant came in to say something in Italian, which the girl did not understand.

Raphael nodded.

“My car is at the door, if you would honour me, we will have tea at my little place in the country. We shall be there in half-an-hour.”

===Chinese===

“既然这出悲剧定于今天下午某个时辰在本宅上演，”他正接着往下说，“我只好求您再行个方便——”

门上轻轻敲了两下，一个仆人进来用意大利语说了句什么，姑娘没有听懂。

拉斐尔点了点头。

“我的车子就在门口，倘若您肯赏光，我们就到乡下我那处小宅子里去用茶。咱们半个钟头就能到。”

===Original===

This was a development she had not looked for.

“Where is your little place in the country,” she asked.

It was, he explained, between Barnet and Hatfield, and expatiated on the loveliness of Hertfordshire.

“I prefer to have tea here,” she said, but he shook his head.

“Believe me, my dear young lady,” he said earnestly, “the threat of the Crimson Circle distresses me not at all. Onslow Gardens is ‘paradise enow’ with so delightful a guest, but the police have been to see me this afternoon, and have changed all my plans. I told them that I had a friend coming to tea, and they suggested a more public rendezvous. The police, however, quite approve of my alternative scheme. Now, Miss Drummond, you are not going to spoil a very happy afternoon? I owe you a thousand apologies, but I shall be very disappointed if you refuse: I have sent two of my servants down to have everything in readiness, and I hope to be able to show you one of the loveliest little houses within a hundred miles of London.”

===Chinese===

这是她始料未及的一变。

“您乡下那处小宅子在哪里？”她问。

据他说明，就在巴尼特（Barnet）和哈特菲尔德（Hatfield）之间；跟着他又对赫特福德郡（Hertfordshire）风光的可爱大发议论。

“我宁愿就在这里用茶。”她说。他却摇了摇头。

“相信我，亲爱的小姐，”他诚恳地说，“红圈的威胁一点也不让我犯愁。有您这样一位赏心悦目的嘉宾在座，昂斯洛花园便是‘天堂足矣’。只是今天下午警察来探望过我，把我全盘计划都打乱了。我告诉他们有位朋友要来用茶，他们建议换个更公开些的会面去处。不过，警察对我这个替代方案倒是十分赞成。瞧，德拉蒙德小姐，您总不至于要搅黄一个多么快活的下午吧？我欠您一千个不是，可您若是不肯，我可要大失所望了：我已先打发两个仆人下去把一切收拾停当，还指望能领您开开眼，看看伦敦方圆百英里之内数得着的一座最可爱的小宅子呢。”

===Original===

She nodded.

“Very well,” she said, and when he had gone, she strolled through the room examining its fascinating contents with every appearance of interest.

He came back wearing his greatcoat, and found her looking at a section of the wall which was covered with beautiful examples of the Eastern swordmaker’s art.

“They’re lovely, aren’t they? I’m so sorry I can’t explain the history of them,” he said, and then in a changed tone: “Who has taken the Assyrian dagger?”

There was undoubtedly a blank space in the wall where a weapon had hung, and a little label beneath the empty space was sufficient to call attention to its absence.

===Chinese===

她点了点头。

“好吧。”她说。他出去之后，她便在屋里信步浏览，对那些迷人的藏品摆出一副十足有兴致的模样。

他回来时已穿上厚呢大衣，见她正望着墙上的一处——那里挂满了东方铸剑师手艺的精美杰作。

“它们多可爱，是不是？真抱歉，我没法给您讲讲它们的来历。”他说着，忽而换了一副声口：“那柄亚述匕首是谁拿走了？”

墙上分明空出一块原本挂着兵器的地方，空位下方一张小小的标签，正足以引人留意它已不翼而飞。

===Original===

“I was wondering the same thing,” said the girl.

Mr. Willings frowned.

“Perhaps one of the servants have taken it down,” he suggested. “Though I have given them strict instructions that they are not to be cleaned except under my personal directions.” He hesitated, and then: “I’ll see about that when I come back,” he said, and he ushered her out of the room into the waiting limousine.

She could see that the loss of his precious trophy had disturbed him, for some of his animation had departed.

“I can’t understand it,” he said as they were passing through Barnet. “I know the dagger was there yesterday, because I was showing it to Sir Thomas Summers. He is keenly interested in Eastern steel work. None of the servants would dare touch the swords.”

===Chinese===

“我也正纳闷这件事呢。”姑娘说。

威林斯先生皱起了眉头。

“许是哪个仆人把它摘下来了。”他揣测道。“虽说我严令过：不经我亲自指点，谁也不许擦拭它们。”他迟疑了一下，又说：“这事等我回来再查究。”说罢便引她出了房间，坐进候在外头的大型轿车。

她看得出，失掉这件珍贵战利品让他心绪不宁，他身上那股活泛劲儿已消退了几分。

“我实在想不通。”车子正穿过巴尼特时，他说。“我知道昨天匕首还挂在那儿，因为我正拿给托马斯·萨默斯爵士（Sir Thomas Summers）看。他对东方钢铁工艺极感兴趣。仆人们谁也不敢碰这些刀剑。”

===Original===

“Perhaps you’ve had strangers in the room.”

He shook his head.

“Only the gentleman from police headquarters,” he said, “and I’m quite sure he wouldn’t have taken it. No, it is a little mystery which we can put on one side at the moment.”

For the rest of the journey he was attentive, polite, and mildly amusing. Not once did he give the slightest hint that he entertained any other emotion towards her than that of a well-bred man for a respected guest.

He had not exaggerated the charms of his “little place” on the Hatfield Road. In truth, it lay nearly three miles from the main road, and was delightfully situated in the midst of rolling and wooded country.

===Chinese===

“兴许有生人进过那间屋子。”

他摇了摇头。

“只有警察总部来的那位先生。”他说，“而且我敢保他绝不会拿。不，这是桩小小的疑案，眼下尽可搁在一旁。”

一路上余下的时光，他殷勤备至、彬彬有礼，还颇有几分风趣。他自始至终没有露出过丝毫别的情绪，俨然只是一位教养良好的绅士对待一位受敬重的宾客。

他在哈特菲尔德路那处“小宅子”的好处，他并没有夸大。实际上，它离大路差不多有三英里，坐落在丘峦起伏、林木葱茏的乡野中心，位置再宜人不过。

===Original===

“Here we are,” he said, as he led her through a panelled hall into an exquisitely decorated little drawing-room.

Tea was laid, but there was no servant in sight.

“And now, my dear,” said Willings, “we are alone, thank heaven.”

His tone, his very manner had changed, and the girl knew that the critical moment was at hand. Yet her hand did not tremble as she filled the teapot from the steaming kettle, seemingly oblivious to all that he was saying. She had poured out the tea and was setting his cup in its place, when, without preliminary, he stooped over her and kissed her; another second, and she was in his arms.

===Chinese===

“到了。”他说着，引她穿过一间镶板门厅，走进一座陈设精美的小客厅。

茶点已经摆好，却不见一个仆人的影子。

“这下好了，亲爱的，”威林斯说，“只剩咱们两个了，感谢上帝。”

他的语调、他整个人的做派都变了，姑娘知道紧要关头已经临头。然而她从嘶嘶冒着热气的水壶里向茶壶注水时，手一丝儿也不发颤，他说的那些话，她似乎全然不闻不问。茶已斟出，她正把他的茶杯端放到位，他毫无铺垫地俯身吻了她；再一转眼，她已被他搂进了怀里。

===Original===

She did not struggle, but her grave eyes were fixed steadfastly on his, and she said quietly:

“I have something I’d like to say to you.”

“Well, you can say anything you wish, my dear,” said the amorous Willings, holding her tightly, and looking into her unflinching eyes.

Before she could speak again his mouth was against hers. She tried to get her arm between them, and to exercise the jujitsu trick she had learnt at school, but he knew something of that science. She had seen on entering the room that at one end was a curtained recess, and toward this he was half-lifting, half-carrying her. She did not scream, indeed, to Raphael, she seemed more yielding than he had dared to hope. Twice she tried to speak, and twice he stopped her. She struggled nearer and nearer to the curtained brocade.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

她没有挣扎，只把她那双凝重的眼睛牢牢盯住他的眼睛，静静地说：

“我有一句话想跟你说。”

“好啊，亲爱的，你想说什么都行。”情热的威林斯说着，把她搂得紧紧的，直望进她那双毫不退缩的眼睛。

她还来不及再开口，他的嘴已经堵上了她的嘴。她想把胳膊挤进两人中间，使出上学时学来的那手柔术招数，可这门功夫他也懂得几分。进屋时她就看见，房间一头有一处挂帘的凹室；他正是半搀半抱，把她往那儿送去。她没有喊叫——在拉斐尔看来，她简直比他斗胆指望的还要温顺。她两次想开口，两次都被他堵了回去。她越挣，离那幅织锦帘幕越近。……

===Original===

The two Italian servants were in the kitchen which was somewhat removed from the room, but they heard the scream and looked at one another, and then with one accord they flew into the hall. The door of the drawing-room was unlocked: they flung it open. Near by the curtain Raphael Willings lay on his face, three inches of Assyrian dagger in his shoulder, and standing by him, staring down at him was a white-faced girl.

One of the men jerked the dagger from his master’s back, and lifted him groaning to a sofa, whilst the other rushed to the telephone. In his agitation the Italian who was endeavouring to staunch the flow of blood from the wound, jabbered unintelligibly at the girl, but she did not hear him, and would not have understood him if she had.

Like one in a dream she walked slowly from the room, through the hall, and into the open.

Raphael Willings’s car was drawn up some distance from the front of the house, and the chauffeur had left it unattended.

===Chinese===

两名意大利仆人正在厨房里，那厨房离客厅颇有几步路；可他们听见了那声尖叫，彼此对望一眼，便不约而同地飞奔进门厅。客厅的门没有上锁：他们一把推开。帘幕近旁，拉斐尔·威林斯面朝下躺着，肩头插着三英寸的亚述匕首；立在他身边、低头瞪视着他的，是一个面无人色的姑娘。

一个仆人猛地从主人背上拔出匕首，把他哼哼着抬上沙发；另一个奔去打电话。那个意大利人一面竭力按住伤口止血，一面激动得冲着姑娘叽里咕噜说个不停，可她根本没听见他，就算听见了也听不懂。

她像梦游的一般，缓缓走出客厅，穿过门厅，来到露天旷地上。

拉斐尔·威林斯的汽车停在离宅子正门有一段路的地方，司机撂下车子无人照看。

===Original===

She looked round; there was nobody in sight; then all her energies awakened, and she sprang into the driver’s seat and pressed the plug of the starter. With a whine and a splutter the engines started up, and she sent the car flying down the drive﻿—but here was an obstacle. The iron gates at the end were closed, and she remembered that the chauffeur had had to get down to unlock them. There was no time to be lost. She backed the car, then sent it full speed at the gates. There was a smashing of glass, a crash as the gates broke, and she was in the road with a damaged radiator, lamps twisted beyond recognition, and a mudguard that hung in shreds. But the car was moving, and she set it spinning in the direction of London.

The hall porter of the flats in which she lived did not recognise her, she looked so wild and changed.

“Aren’t you well, miss?” he asked as he took her up in the lift.

She shook her head.

===Chinese===

她四下一望，一个人影也不见；顿时浑身劲力一齐醒了过来。她跳进司机座，按下启动钮。发动机呜咽一声、噼啪一阵响，转了起来；她驱动汽车顺着车道疾飞而下——可是这里有道障碍。尽头的大铁门关着，她记起司机方才还得下车来开锁。分秒也耽搁不得。她把车倒回去，随即全速朝铁门撞去。玻璃哗啦粉碎，铁门轰然撞开，她已经上了大路——散热器撞坏了，车灯拧得面目全非，一块挡泥板吊着条条碎片。可车子还能动，她便催着它朝伦敦的方向飞驰而去。

她住的那幢公寓的门房竟没有认出她来——她那模样太狂乱、变得太厉害了。

“您不大舒服吗，小姐？”他送她乘电梯上楼时问。

她摇了摇头。

===Original===

Once behind the door of her flat she went straight to the telephone and gave a number, and to the man who answered, she poured forth such a wild, incoherent story, a story so punctuated by sobs, that he found it difficult to discover exactly what had happened.

“I’m through, I’m through,” she gasped. “I can do no more! I will do no more! It was horrible, horrible!”

She hung up the receiver, and staggered to her room, feeling that she was going to faint unless she took tight hold of herself; hours passed before she was normal.

===Chinese===

一进自家公寓、掩上房门，她便直奔电话，拨了一个号码；对着来接电话的那个男人，她倾倒出一篇狂乱而语无伦次的叙述，句句夹着呜咽，叫他实在难以弄清究竟出了什么事。

“我不干了，我不干了，”她喘着气说。“我再也做不到了！我也不愿再做了！太可怕了，太可怕了！”

她挂上听筒，踉踉跄跄走回自己房里，只觉得若不紧紧把持住自己，马上就要晕倒；过了好几个钟头，她才恢复如常。

===Original===

And it was in that condition that Mr. Derrick Yale found her when he called that evening﻿—her old calm, insolent self.

“This is an unexpected honour,” she said coolly, “and who is your friend?”

She looked at the man who was standing behind Yale.

“Thalia Drummond,” said Yale sternly. “I have a warrant for your arrest.”

===Chinese===

当晚德里克·耶尔先生登门来访时，见到的是那副状态的她——还是她那套老样子，镇定自若、倨傲不逊。

“这真是意外的荣幸。”她淡淡地说，“您这位朋友是哪位？”

她望着站在耶尔身后的那个男人。

“塔莉娅·德拉蒙德，”耶尔声色俱厉地说，“我手里有你的逮捕证。”

===Original===

“Again?” she raised her eyebrows. “I seem always to be in the hands of the police. What is the charge?”

“Attempted murder,” said Yale. “The attempted murder of Mr. Raphael Willings. I caution you that what you now say may be taken down, and used in evidence against you.”

The second man stepped forward and took her arm.

Thalia Drummond spent that night in the cells of Marylebone Police Station.

===Chinese===

“又来？”她挑起了眉毛。“我好像总在警察手里打转。这回是什么罪名？”

“谋杀未遂，”耶尔说，“谋杀拉斐尔·威林斯先生未遂。我提醒你，你现在所说的话都可能被记录下来，用作对你不利的证据。”

第二个男人上前一步，抓住了她的手臂。

塔莉娅·德拉蒙德在玛丽勒本警察局的拘留室里度过了那一夜。

## XXXIX / 三十九

===Original===

A Prison Diet

===Chinese===

监狱伙食

===Original===

“As to what happened, I have yet to learn,” said Derrick Yale to a silent but attentive Inspector Parr. “I arrived at Onslow Gardens just after Willings had taken the girl away. The servants at the house were rather reluctant about giving me information, but I soon discovered that she had been taken to Willings’s house in the country. Whether she enticed him or he lured her is a matter for discovery. Probably he is under the impression that she went against her will. All along I have suspected Thalia Drummond as being something more than a servant of the Crimson Circle; naturally I was a little alarmed and flew off to Thetfield, arriving at the house just after she had left. She escaped in Willings’s car, smashing the lodge gates en route; by the way﻿—that girl has got nerve.”

“How is Willings?”

“He will recover; the wound is superficial, but what is significant is the proof that the crime was premeditated. Willings only missed the dagger with which he was stabbed this afternoon, after he had left the girl alone in his armoury whilst he put on his overcoat. He thinks she must have carried it in her muff, and that, of course, is very likely. He gives me no very clear account of what were the events which immediately preceded the stabbing.”

===Chinese===

“究竟出了什么事，我也有待查明。”德里克·耶尔对一言不发、却听得分外仔细的帕尔探长说。“我赶到昂斯洛花园时，威林斯刚把姑娘带走不久。宅里的仆人相当不情愿向我吐露消息，不过我很快就打听出来，她被带到威林斯乡下的宅子里去了。是她诱了他去，还是他拐了她去，这还有待查证。多半他还只当她是违了本心去的呢。我从头起就疑心，塔莉娅·德拉蒙德在红圈里绝不止是个供驱遣的仆役；自然，我心里有点发急，连忙飞车赶往蒂特菲尔德（Thetfield），到那宅子时她刚离开不久。她是开威林斯的车逃的，一路撞碎了带门房的铁门——顺便说一句，那姑娘可真有胆量。”

“威林斯怎么样了？”

“他死不了；伤口不深。要紧的是，这证明了此案乃蓄谋而为。威林斯把姑娘独自留在兵器室里、自己去穿大衣，过后才发现那柄当天下午刺进他身体的匕首不见了。他认定她准是把它藏在皮手筒里带进去的，这当然大有可能。行凶之前紧接着究竟出了什么事，他没能给我说出个所以然来。”

===Original===

“H’m,” said Inspector Parr. “What sort of a room was it? I mean, the room where this nearly﻿—occurred?”

“A pretty little drawing-room communicating with what Willings calls his Turkish room. It is a marvellous replica of an Eastern interior, and I should imagine the scene of more or less disreputable happenings﻿—Willings hasn’t the best of reputations. It is only separated from the drawing-room by a curtain, and it was near the curtain that he was found.”

Mr. Parr was so absorbed in his meditation that his companion thought he had gone to sleep. But the inspector was not asleep; he was very wide awake. He was conscious of the appalling fact that once more whatever kudos attached to the latest of the Crimson Circle’s outrages went to his companion, and yet he did not grudge him the honour.

Without warning he delivered himself of a sentiment which seemed to have no bearing whatever upon the matter they were discussing.

===Chinese===

“哼。”帕尔探长说。“那是间什么样的屋子？我是说，这件险些——出了的事，是在怎样的屋子里？”

“一间精致的小客厅，连通着威林斯自称的‘土耳其式房间’。那是东方内室惟妙惟肖的仿造，依我想，也是种种不大体面的勾当上演之处——威林斯的名声可算不得好。它同客厅之间只隔着一道帘子，他就是在帘子近旁被发现的。”

帕尔先生沉思得出神，他的同伴还当他睡着了。可探长并没有睡；他清醒得很。他心里明白一桩骇人的事实：红圈最新一桩暴行带来的一切功名，这一回又要尽数归于同伴头上；然而他并不眼红他这份荣耀。

他冷不丁地发表起一番感想来，这话似乎同他们正谈论的事毫无干系。

===Original===

“All great criminals come to grief through trifling errors of judgment,” he said oracularly.

Yale smiled.

“The error of judgment in this case, I presume, being that they didn’t kill our friend Willings﻿—he is not a nice man, and I should imagine of all the members of the Cabinet he could best be spared. But I for one am very grateful that these devils did not get him.”

“I am not referring to Mr. Willings,” said Inspector Parr rising slowly. “I am referring to a stupid little lie told me by a man who really should have known better.”

And with this cryptic utterance, Mr. Parr went off to break the news to Jack Beardmore.

===Chinese===

“一切大奸巨恶之辈，都栽在鸡毛蒜皮的失算上。”他一副先知般的口吻说。

耶尔微微一笑。

“照这么说，本案里的失算，就在于他们没把咱们这位朋友威林斯结果掉——他不是个好东西，而且依我想，内阁全体成员之中，数他最可有可无。不过就我个人而言，这帮恶魔没能要了他的命，我倒感激得很。”

“我指的不是威林斯先生。”帕尔探长说着，缓缓站起身来。“我指的是一个小小的蠢谎，撒谎的那一位，本该更明白些事理才是。”

丢下这句没头没脑的话，帕尔先生便动身去向杰克·比德莫尔报信了。

===Original===

It was typical of him that Jack was the first person who came to his mind when he learnt of Thalia Drummond’s arrest. He was fond of the boy, fonder than Jack could guess, and he knew, even better than Yale, how heavily the weight of Thalia Drummond’s guilt would lie upon the man who loved her.

Jack had already received his shock. The news of the girl’s arrest had been published in the stop-press columns of the late editions, and when Parr arrived he was the picture of desolation.

“She must have the best lawyers procurable,” he said quietly. “I don’t know that I ought to take you into my confidence, Mr. Parr, because you naturally will be on the other side.”

“Naturally,” said the inspector, “but I’ve got a sneaking regard for Thalia Drummond, too.”

===Chinese===

他一听说塔莉娅·德拉蒙德被捕，头一个想起的便是杰克，这正是他的为人。他疼这孩子，疼爱的程度远非杰克所能揣度；而且他比耶尔更明白，塔莉娅·德拉蒙德的罪名将何等沉重地压在那个爱她的男人心上。

杰克的那记打击已经挨过了。姑娘被捕的消息登在晚版报纸的截稿快讯栏里；帕尔到时，他那副模样，简直成了愁苦凄惶的化身。

“务必给她请最好的律师，能请到多好的就请多好的。”他低声说。“我不知道该不该把心里话向您和盘托出，帕尔先生，因为您自然是要站在对面一边的。”

“自然。”探长说，“不过对塔莉娅·德拉蒙德，我心里也暗暗存着几分好感。”

===Original===

“You?” said Jack in astonishment. “Why, I thought﻿—”

“I’m human,” said the inspector. “A criminal to me is just a criminal. I have no personal grudge against the men I have arrested. Truland, the poisoner, whom I sent to the gallows, was one of the nicest fellows I’ve ever met, and I got quite fond of him after a bit.”

Jack shuddered.

“Don’t talk of poisoners and Thalia Drummond in the same breath,” he said testily. “Do you honestly believe she is the leading spirit of the Crimson Circle?”

Mr. Parr pursed his thick lips.

===Chinese===

“您？”杰克愕然道。“咦，我还当——”

“我也是个凡人。”探长说。“在我眼里，罪犯就是罪犯。对我逮捕过的人，我并无私怨。那个下毒犯特鲁兰（Truland），我送他上了绞架，可他是我平生遇到的最和气的人之一，处得久了，我竟相当喜欢起他来。”

杰克打了个寒噤。

“别把下毒犯和塔莉娅·德拉蒙德相提并论。”他没好气地说。“您当真相信她是红圈的首脑？”

帕尔先生抿起他那两片厚嘴唇。

===Original===

“If somebody came to me and told me the Archbishop was the leading light, I shouldn’t be surprised, Mr. Beardmore,” he said. “By the time this Crimson Circle business is settled, we are all going to have shocks. I started my investigations prepared to believe that anybody might be the Crimson Circle﻿—you, or Marl, the Commissioner or Derrick Yale, Thalia Drummond﻿—almost anybody.”

“And you still hold that opinion?” asked Jack with an attempt at a smile. “For the matter of that, Mr. Parr, you yourself might be the villain of the piece.”

Mr. Parr did not deny the possibility.

“Mother thinks﻿—” he began, and this time Jack did actually laugh.

“Your grandmother must be a remarkable personality; has she views on the Crimson Circle?”

===Chinese===

“倘若有谁来告诉我，说大主教才是那头号人物，我也不会吃惊，比德莫尔先生。”他说。“等这桩红圈公案了结的时候，咱们个个都要大吃几惊。我着手查案之初，便打定主意，谁都有可能是红圈——你，或者马尔，总监或者德里克·耶尔，塔莉娅·德拉蒙德——几乎随便哪一个都行。”

“那您如今还抱着这个看法？”杰克勉强堆出一丝笑意，问道。“说起来，帕尔先生，您本人也未尝不可能就是这出戏里的反派。”

帕尔先生并不否认有此可能。

“‘妈妈’认为——”他起了个头，这一回杰克当真笑出声来。

“您的祖母想必是位了不起的人物；她对红圈也有高见？”

===Original===

The inspector nodded vigorously.

“She always has had, since the first murder. She put her finger down on the very spot, Mr. Beardmore, but mother always could do that sort of thing. I’ve had my best inspirations from her; in fact, all the﻿—” He stopped himself.

Jack was amused, but he was pitying, too. This man, so ill-equipped by nature for his work, had probably won himself a high place in the police service by dogged unimaginative persistence. In every service men had reached near to the top with no other merit than their seniority. It was just a little fantastic at this moment, when the keenest brains were exercised to lay this bizarre association by the heels, to hear this stout man talking solemnly of the advice he had received from his grandmother!

“I must come along and renew my acquaintance with your aunt,” said Jack.

“She has gone into the country,” was the reply, “and I’m all alone. A woman comes in every morning to clean the place, but there’s nobody there evenings﻿—it doesn’t seem like home to me now.”

===Chinese===

探长使劲点了点头。

“打头一桩凶案起，她就一直有高见。她一指头就点中了要害，比德莫尔先生，而‘妈妈’向来有这份本事。我最妙的灵感都得自于她；说实在的，所有那——”他猛地收住了话头。

杰克觉得好笑，可又觉得怜悯。这个天生不宜干这一行的人，多半就是凭着一股死心塌地、不知变通的韧劲，才在警界挣下了这般高位。无论哪一行，都有人单凭资历便爬到了近乎顶尖的地方。此时此刻，全城最锋利的头脑都在绞尽脑汁要把这个怪诞透顶的团伙擒拿归案，却听这位胖先生一本正经地大谈他从祖母那里领来的高见——这实在有几分离奇！

“我可得登门去，同您的姑母续一续旧谊。”杰克说。

“她下乡去了。”回答是这么一句，“家里就剩我一个人。每天早晨有个女人来打扫屋子，一到晚上便一个人也没有——如今我竟不觉得那像个家了。”

===Original===

It was a relief to Jack to get on to the subject of Mr. Parr’s domestic affairs. Their very unimportance was a sedative to his racked mind. He felt that an evening spent with the inspector’s knowledgeable grandparent might even restore him to something like normality.

Parr himself led the conversation back to more serious channels.

“Drummond will be brought up tomorrow and remanded,” he said.

“Is there any hope of getting bail for her?”

===Chinese===

话头转到帕尔先生的家务事上，杰克倒松了口气。这些事的无足轻重，正给他那备受煎熬的心一帖镇静剂。他甚至觉得，同探长那位无所不晓的祖母消磨一个晚上，兴许能叫他恢复几分常态。

还是帕尔自己，把话头引回了正经题目。

“德拉蒙德明天提堂，还押。”他说。

“有没有希望给她办下保释？”

===Original===

Parr shook his head.

“No. She’ll have to go to Holloway, but that won’t do her much harm,” he said, heartlessly, as Jack thought. “It is one of the best prisons in the country, and maybe she’ll be glad of the rest.”

“How came Yale to arrest her? I should have thought that was your job?”

“I instructed him,” said Parr. “He has now the status of a regular police officer, and as he had been in the case earlier in the day, I thought I would let him continue it to the end.”

Just as the inspector had foreshadowed, the police-court proceedings of the next day were confined only to evidence of arrest, and Thalia Drummond was remanded in custody.

===Chinese===

帕尔摇了摇头。

“没有。她得进霍洛韦监狱，不过那也伤不着她什么。”他说——在杰克听来，这话未免狠心。“那是全国数一数二的监狱，没准她还乐得歇上一歇呢。”

“怎么会是耶尔去逮捕她的？我还当那是您的差事呢。”

“是我派他去的。”帕尔说。“他如今有正式警官的身份，况且当天早些时候他就已介入此案，我想索性让他办到底。”

果然不出探长所料，次日的警务法庭程序只限于逮捕经过的举证，塔莉娅·德拉蒙德被当庭还押。

===Original===

The court house was packed, and a big crowd, attracted by the sensational character of the charge, filled all the roads approaching the court.

Mr. Willings was not well enough to attend, but well enough to send his resignation to the Cabinet in response to the Prime Minister’s suggestion, contained in a letter couched in such unpleasant terms﻿—and the acidulated vocabulary of the Prime Minister was notorious﻿—that even he, the thick-skinned Willings, was pained.

Whatever happened, he was everlastingly disgraced; even the thick and thin supporters of his policy would be revolted by the evidence he must give. He had taken the girl﻿—a comparative stranger﻿—to his country house, made violent love to her, and had been stabbed. There could be no romantic version of that unpleasant story; and he heartily cursed himself for his stupidity.

===Chinese===

法院里座无虚席，一大群人被这条罪名的轰动性引来，把通向法院的条条道路挤得水泄不通。

威林斯先生的身体还不宜出庭，却还撑得住遵照首相的示意，向内阁递上辞呈；那示意载在一封信里，措辞刻薄得很——这位首相尖酸的字眼本是出了名的——连脸皮厚如威林斯者，也觉着受了刺痛。

无论结局如何，他已永远身败名裂；连他那一套政策最死心塌地的拥趸，听了他势必给出的证词，也要心生憎恶。他把一个近乎素昧平生的姑娘带到乡间宅子里，强行向她求欢，还挨了一刀。这段难堪的往事，断然翻不出什么浪漫的讲法；他把自己那股蠢劲，痛痛快快骂了个够。

===Original===

Parr made one call upon the girl whilst she was in prison. She refused to see him in her cell, and insisted upon the interview taking place in the presence of a wardress. She explained her attitude when they sat together in the big gaunt waiting-room of the gaol, he at one end of the table and she at the other.

“You must excuse my not seeing you in my apartment, Mr. Parr,” she said. “But so many promising young emissaries of the Crimson Circle have met with an untimely end through interviewing policemen in their cells.”

“The only one I can recall,” said Parr stolidly, “is Sibly.”

“Who was a shining example of indiscretion.”

She showed her even white teeth in a smile.

===Chinese===

姑娘坐牢期间，帕尔去探望过她一回。她拒绝在牢房里见他，执意要有女看守在场才肯会面。两人在监狱那间空落落的大候见室里对面坐定——他占着桌子一头，她占着另一头——这时她才说明了自己这般做派的缘由。

“请恕我没有在自己的‘寓所’里接待您，帕尔先生。”她说。“只是红圈里那么多前程无量的青年使者，都因为在牢房里会见警察而死于非命了。”

“我记得起来的，只有一个西布利。”帕尔不动声色地说。

“他正是行事不谨的光辉典范。”

她莞尔一笑，露出一口整齐的白牙。

===Original===

“Now what do you want of me?”

“I want you to tell me what happened when you called at Onslow Gardens.”

She gave him a faithful and a detailed account of that afternoon visit.

“When did you discover the dagger was gone?”

“When I was looking round the room whilst Willings was putting on his coat. How is Lothario?”

“He’s all right,” said Parr. “I am afraid he will recover﻿—I mean,” he added hastily, “I am glad to say he’ll get better. Was that the first time Willings noticed the absence of the dagger?”

===Chinese===

“好了，您要我做什么？”

“我要你告诉我，你上昂斯洛花园登门拜访时出了什么事。”

她把那天下午那趟造访的经过，原原本本、详详细细地讲给了他听。

“你是什么时候发现匕首不见了？”

“威林斯穿大衣的时候，我正环顾那间屋子，就是那时候发现的。洛萨里奥（Lothario，情场浪子的代称）他怎么样了？”

“他没事。”帕尔说。“恐怕他是要痊愈的——我是说，”他慌忙补上一句，“我很高兴地告诉你，他会好起来的。那是不是威林斯头一回察觉匕首不见了？”

===Original===

She nodded.

“Did you carry a muff?”

“Yes,” she said. “Is that the place where the deadly weapon was supposed to be concealed?”

“Did you have your muff in your hand when you went into his house at Hatfield?”

She thought a moment.

“Yes,” she nodded.

===Chinese===

她点了点头。

“你带了皮手筒吗？”

“带了。”她说。“那件致命的凶器，照他们的说法，就是藏在那里面的吧？”

“你进他哈特菲尔德那所宅子的时候，手里拿着皮手筒吗？”

她想了一会儿。

“拿着。”她点头道。

===Original===

Inspector Parr rose.

“You’re getting all the food you require?”

“Yes: from prison,” she said emphatically. “Prison food will suit me very well, thank you, and I do not want anybody, out of mistaken kindness, to send in luscious dishes from outside, as I understand prisoners on remand are allowed.”

He scratched his chin.

“I think you’re wise,” he said.

===Chinese===

帕尔探长站起身来。

“你要的吃食都齐全吗？”

“齐全——吃的就是监狱的饭。”她加重语气说。“监狱的伙食很合我的胃口，谢谢。我不愿有谁出于好心办了错事，再从外面给我送美味可口的东西进来——据我了解，还押的犯人是有这个便利的。”

他挠了挠下巴。

“我看你这是明智之举。”他说。

## XL / 四十

===Original===
The Escape

===Chinese===
越狱

===Original===
The outrage upon Raphael Willings had produced something like a panic in the Cabinet.

Mr. Parr learnt how profound was the concern when he returned to headquarters. And the Prime Minister was justified in his anxiety. The Crimson Circle had not stated when the next blow would fall, or upon whom.

The inspector was sent for to Downing Street, and was closeted with the Prime Minister for two hours. It was the first personal consultation he had had, and it was followed by a meeting of the inner Cabinet, a fact that was duly recorded in the newspapers.

It was said, but without authority, that the life of the Prime Minister had been threatened, and this statement was neither denied nor affirmed.

===Chinese===
对拉斐尔·威林斯下手的这桩暴行，在内阁里掀起了一阵近乎恐慌的风潮。

帕尔先生回到警察总部，才明白这份忧惧有多深重。而首相的焦虑也不无道理。红圈并没有言明，下一击将于何时落下，又将落在谁的头上。

探长奉召前往唐宁街（Downing Street），同首相闭门密谈了两个钟头。这是他头一回与首相当面磋商，随后又举行了内阁核心会议——此事也照例见诸各报。

有传言说，连首相的性命也受到了威胁，不过此说查无实据；而对这一说法，官方既不否认，也不证实。

===Original===
Derrick Yale, returning to his flat that night, found Inspector Parr waiting on the doormat.

“Is anything wrong?” he asked quickly.

“I want your help,” said Parr, and did not speak again until he was sitting in a comfortable chair before the fire in Yale’s sitting room.

===Chinese===
当天夜里，德里克·耶尔回到公寓，发现帕尔探长正候在门前的擦鞋垫上。

“出什么事了？”他急忙问。

“我要你帮个忙。”帕尔说。直到坐进耶尔起居室炉火前一把舒适的椅子里，他才重新开口。

===Original===
“You know, Yale, that I’ve got to go, and the Prime Minister is considering the advisability of my going a little sooner than I had expected. There has been a Cabinet committee appointed, and they are calling into question the methods which headquarters are employing and I have been asked by the Commissioner to attend an informal meeting at the Prime Minister’s house tomorrow evening.”

“What is the idea?” asked Yale.

“I’m to give a sort of lecture,” said Parr gloomily, “and explain to the members of the Cabinet the methods I have employed against the Crimson Circle. You probably know that I have been given unusual powers, and that I have not been asked to tell the Government all I know. I intend doing that on Friday evening, and I want your help.”

“My dear chap, you have it before you ask it,” said Yale warmly, and Parr went on.

===Chinese===
“你是知道的，耶尔，我这身官服总归是要脱下来的，首相正在斟酌，要不要让我走得比原先预想的再早一点。内阁已经指派了一个委员会，正对警察总部采用的种种手段逐一诘问；总监要我出席明晚在首相府举行的一场非正式会议。”

“这是什么名堂？”耶尔问。

“要我来讲一堂课似的东西，”帕尔闷闷不乐地说，“向内阁的成员们讲解我对付红圈用过的种种手段。你大概知道，人家给了我非常的权力，也从没要我把自己知道的一切向政府和盘托出。我打算在星期五晚上就这么办——我要你帮忙。”

“亲爱的老兄，还没等你开口，这忙就已经帮定啦。”耶尔热诚地说。帕尔接着往下讲。

===Original===
“There is still a lot about the Crimson Circle that is a mystery to me, but I am piecing it together. At the moment I am under the impression that there is somebody at police headquarters who is working with them.”

“That is my view, too,” said Yale quickly. “Why do you say that?”

“Well,” said the slow Parr, “I’ll give you an instance. Young Beardmore had a photograph that he found in his father’s papers and this was posted to me. It arrived all right, with the seal of the envelope intact, but when I opened it, there was a blank card. I have since discovered that he gave that card to Thalia Drummond to post; he swears he stood on the doorstep and watched her slip it into the letter-box on the opposite side of the road. If that is the case, the envelope must have been tampered with after it reached headquarters.”

===Chinese===
“关于红圈，还有好些事在我仍是谜，不过我正在一点点把它拼拢来。眼下的印象是：警察总部里有个人，在同他们串通。”

“这也是我的看法。”耶尔应声道，“你为什么这么说？”

“唔，”说话慢条斯理的帕尔说，“我给你举一个例子。小比德莫尔在他父亲的文件里翻出过一张照片，把它寄给了我。东西完好寄到，信封的封口原封未动，可等我拆开一看，里面只有一张空白卡片。我后来查明，那张卡片是他交给塔莉娅·德拉蒙德去寄的；他赌咒发誓，说自己就站在门阶上，眼看着她把它投进了马路对面的邮筒。倘若果真如此，那么这信封必定是在到了警察总部之后，才被人动过手脚的。”

===Original===
“What kind of a photograph?” asked the other curiously.

“It was either a picture of an execution or the condemned man Lightman, for I think it was taken on the occasion when they tried to execute Lightman and failed. It came to old man Beardmore the day before his death﻿—a great number of things seem to have happened to the victims of the Crimson Circle the day before their death﻿—and was found by Jack and, as I say, sent on﻿—”

“By Thalia Drummond!” said Yale significantly. “My view is that you can exonerate the people at headquarters. This girl is deeper in the Crimson Circle than you imagine. I searched her house tonight﻿—that is where I’ve been, and this is what I found.”

===Chinese===
“一张什么样的照片？”对方好奇地问。

“不是一张行刑的相片，就是死刑犯利特曼的相片，因为我想，那是在他们试图处决利特曼而未得手的那一回拍下的。它是在老比德莫尔死的前一天寄到他手里的——红圈的受害者们，似乎总有许许多多的事，偏偏都发生在他们死的前一天——后来让杰克翻了出来，又照我方才说的，寄了过来——”

“偏偏是经塔莉娅·德拉蒙德的手寄的！”耶尔意味深长地说。“依我看，警察总部里那些人倒可以撇清干系了。这姑娘在红圈里陷得比你想象的要深。我今晚搜了她的住处——我方才就是打那儿来——这就是我搜到的东西。”

===Original===
He went out into the hall and returned with a brown paper parcel, opened it, and the inspector stared.

A gauntlet glove and a long bright-bladed knife were exposed when Yale cut the string and stripped away the paper wrapping.

“This glove is a fellow to that which was found in Froyant’s study. The knife is an exact pair to the other.”

===Chinese===
他出门到门厅里，捧回来一个牛皮纸包裹，动手打开，探长不由得瞪直了眼。

耶尔铰断捆绳、揭去纸皮，一只护腕手套和一柄长刃雪亮的刀子露了出来。

“这只手套，同弗鲁瓦扬书房里搜到的那只正是一对；这把刀，也跟另一把一模一样。”

===Original===
Parr took up the gauntlet and examined it.

“Yes, this is the left hand, and the one on Froyant’s desk was the right,” he agreed. “It is a worn motor-glove. Who was the owner? Try your psychometric powers, Yale.”

“I’ve already tried,” said the other, shaking his head, “but the glove has passed through so many hands that the impressions I receive are very confused. At any rate, this discovery confirms the theory that Thalia Drummond is in the business up to her neck. As to the other matter you were speaking about,” he said, as he wrapped the knife and glove carefully in the paper, “I shall be most happy to assist you.”

===Chinese===
帕尔拿起那只护腕手套，细细察看。

“不错，这只是左手的，弗鲁瓦扬书桌上那只是右手的。”他表示赞同。“这是只用旧的驾车手套。物主是谁？耶尔，用你的触物感应试试。”

“我已经试过了。”对方摇着头说，“只是这手套经手的人太多，我接收到的印象乱作一团。无论如何，这一发现坐实了那个推断：塔莉娅·德拉蒙德在这桩买卖里，已经陷到了脖子根。至于你方才提到的另一件事，”他一面把刀和手套仔细包回纸里，一面说，“我极愿效劳。”

===Original===
“What I want from you,” said Parr, “is that you shall fill in the spaces which I cannot fill,” he shook his head. “I only wish mother could be there,” he said regretfully.

“Mother?” said the astonished Yale.

“My grandmother,” said Mr. Parr soberly. “The only detective in England﻿—bar you and I.”

It was the first time that Derrick Yale ever had reason to suspect that Mr. Parr possessed a sense of humour.

===Chinese===
“我要你做的，”帕尔说，“是替我把我填不上的那些空子填上。”他摇了摇头。“我惟愿‘妈妈’也能在场才好。”他怅然说。

“‘妈妈’？”耶尔吃了一惊。

“我的祖母。”帕尔先生一本正经地说。“英格兰独一无二的大侦探——只除了你我两个。”

这是德里克·耶尔生平头一回，有理由疑心帕尔先生竟也有几分幽默感。

---

===Original===
It was typical of that period of excitement, when the name of the Crimson Circle was on every tongue, that sensation should follow sensation. But probably no incident created so much excitement as that which, in scrawling headlines, greeted Derrick Yale as, sitting in bed sipping his tea, he read the newspaper the following morning!

Thalia Drummond had escaped!

People escape from prison in works of fiction; they have been known to make a temporary getaway from dread Dartmoor, but never before in the history of the prison service had a woman escaped from Holloway. And yet the wardress unlocking the door of Thalia Drummond’s cell in the morning found it empty.

===Chinese===
时局鼎沸，红圈之名挂在人人嘴上，轰动接着轰动，这本是那一时期的常例。可是大概没有哪一桩事情，激起过如此这般的骚动——第二天早晨，德里克·耶尔坐在床上呷着茶读报，一行行歪斜粗大的标题，就这样劈面迎来！

塔莉娅·德拉蒙德越狱了！

小说里的人物越狱，屡见不鲜；也确曾有人从令人闻之色变的达特穆尔（Dartmoor）监狱短暂脱身过。可是在狱政史上，还从来没有一个女人从霍洛韦监狱逃出去过。然而早晨，女看守打开塔莉娅·德拉蒙德的牢门，里面却已空无一人。

===Original===
It took a great deal to shock Derrick Yale, but the news temporarily paralysed him. He read the account of the escape word by word, and in the end he was as mystified as ever.

But there it was in cold print, officially admitted, and communicated to the early morning press by the Government with unnatural haste.

Owing to the unusual importance of the prisoner, and the character of the offence alleged against her, extraordinary precautions were taken to guard her. The patrol which usually visits the ward in which her cell was situated, was doubled, and instead of hourly, half-hourly visits were paid by the officers on duty. It is not customary to look into every cell on these occasions, but at three o’clock this morning the wardress﻿—Mrs. Hardy﻿—looked through the observation hole and saw the prisoner was there. At six o’clock when the cell door was opened, Drummond was missing. The bars of the window were intact, and the door had not been tampered with.

===Chinese===
能叫德里克·耶尔大吃一惊的事本不多，可这条消息竟叫他一时动弹不得。他把那篇越狱纪事逐字逐句读了下去，读到末了，依旧如坠五里雾中。

可是白纸黑字就摆在那里：官方正式承认，并且由政府以一种反常的仓促，通报给了清晨印行的各报。

鉴于该犯身份非同小可，所控罪名的性质又极重大，狱方为看守她采取了非常的防范措施。通常巡查其牢房所在监区的巡班加了一倍，值班狱吏的察看也由每小时一次改为每半小时一次。这类察看望例并不逐间牢房查看，但今日凌晨三点，女看守——哈代（Hardy）太太——曾从观察孔张望，看见犯人还在里面。六点钟打开牢门时，德拉蒙德已经不见踪影。窗上的铁条完好无损，牢门也不曾被人动过手脚。

===Original===
A search of the prison grounds showed no trace of her footsteps, and it is almost impossible that she could have escaped over the wall. It is equally impossible that she could have left by the ordinary means, since it would have necessitated her passing through six separate doors, none of which had been forced, or through the gatekeeper’s lodge, which is occupied throughout the night.

This new proof of the Crimson Circle’s omnipotence and extraordinary powers is very disconcerting, coming, as it does, at a moment when the lives of Cabinet Ministers are threatened by this mysterious gang.

Yale glanced at the clock. It was half-past eleven. And then he looked at the newspaper and saw that his servant had brought him an early edition of one of the evening papers. He was out of bed in a second and, not waiting for breakfast, rushed off to headquarters, to find Inspector Parr in a very good humour, considering all the circumstances.

===Chinese===
搜遍监狱全境，不见她的一丝足迹；要说她是翻墙逃出去的，几乎不可能。说她是走寻常门径出去的，也同样不可能——那就非得穿过六道各自分开的门不可，而没有一道门曾被强行弄开；再不然，就得打整夜有人的门房里经过。

红圈无所不能、神通广大的这一最新明证，实在令人心慌意乱——它偏偏来得正是时候：内阁大臣们的性命，此刻正悬在这个神秘匪帮的威胁之下。

耶尔瞥了一眼时钟：十一点半。接着他再端详那报纸，才看出仆人拿来的是一家晚报的早印版。他转眼间便跳下床来，早饭也顾不得吃，直奔警察总部，却发现帕尔探长——就眼下种种情形而论——心情竟好得很。

===Original===
“But this is incredible, Parr, it is impossible! She must have friends in the prison!”

“That is my idea entirely,” said Parr. “I told the Commissioner in the identical words that she must have friends in the prison. Otherwise,” he said after a pause, “how did she get out?”

Yale looked at him suspiciously. It did not seem the moment or the occasion for flippant talk, and Inspector Parr’s tone was undoubtedly flippant.

===Chinese===
“可这真是难以置信，帕尔，这不可能！她在监狱里一定有自己人！”

“这恰恰正是我的看法。”帕尔说，“我对总监说的，也是一字不差的一句：她在监狱里一定有自己人。要不然，”他顿了一顿，说，“她是怎么出去的呢？”

耶尔狐疑地望着他。无论此时此际，似乎都不是打趣的时候，而帕尔探长的那副口气，分明透着轻佻。

## XLI / 四十一

===Original===
Who Is the Crimson Circle?

===Chinese===
红圈究竟是谁？

===Original===
Yale learnt no more details than those he had already read, and took a taxi to his city office, which he had not visited for two days.

The escape of Thalia Drummond was a much more important affair than Parr seemed to think. Parr! An awful thought occurred to Derrick Yale. John Parr! That stolid, stupid-looking man﻿—it was impossible! He shook his head, yet put his mind resolutely to the task of piecing together every incident in which Inspector Parr had figured, and in the end:

“Impossible!” he muttered again, as he walked slowly up the stairs to his office, declining the invitation of the lift-boy.

===Chinese===
耶尔并没有打听到比他已读到的更多的细节，便乘出租汽车去了他在城里的办公室——那地方他已有两天没光顾了。

塔莉娅·德拉蒙德的越狱，是桩比帕尔看上去所认为的重大得多的事件。帕尔！一个可怕的念头在德里克·耶尔脑中闪过。约翰·帕尔！那个木讷迟钝、一脸蠢相的人——不可能！他摇了摇头，却还是打定主意，把帕尔探长露过面的每一桩事件逐一拼凑起来，到末了：

“不可能！”他一边缓缓登上通往办公室的楼梯，一边又喃喃自语，谢绝了开电梯的小伙子的好意。

===Original===
The first thing he noticed when he unlocked the door was that the letter-box was empty. It was a very large letter-box, with a patent flap device, designed so that it was impossible for an outside pilferer to extract any of its contents. The wire cage reached almost to the floor, and letters that came through the slot in the door had to fall through revolving aluminium blades, which made the letter thief’s task a hopeless one. And the letter-box was empty! There was not so much as a tradesman’s circular.

He closed the door quietly and went into his own room. He took no more than a pace into the interior and then stopped. Every drawer in his desk was open. The little steel safe by the side of the fireplace, concealed from view by the wooden panelling, had been unlocked, and the door was now open. He looked under the desk. There was a tiny cupboard, which only an expert could have found, and here Derrick Yale had kept the more intimate documents connected with the Crimson Circle case.

He saw nothing but a broken panel and the mark of the chisel that had wrenched it free.

===Chinese===
他开锁进门，头一眼注意到的，就是信箱空了。那是个很大的信箱，装有专利的翻板机关，设计得叫外头的小贼休想从里面抽走任何东西。铁丝笼几乎直落到地板，从门上投信口塞进来的信件，必须先穿过旋转的铝制叶片才能落下——这叫偷信贼彻底断了念想。可这信箱竟然空空如也！连一张商家的广告传单也没有。

他轻轻掩上门，走进自己的里间。刚迈进屋里一步，他便站住了。写字台的每一个抽屉都敞开着。壁炉旁那口小小的钢制保险柜——外面用木质嵌板遮掩着，不露形迹——锁已被人打开，柜门此刻大敞。他朝写字台底下看了看。那儿有个小小的暗柜，只有行家才找得到，德里克·耶尔存放的与红圈一案关系更为紧要的文件，原本就锁在这里。

如今他看到的，只有一块被撬破的嵌板，和凿子把它撬下来时留下的痕迹。

===Original===
He sat for a long time in his chair, staring out of the window. There was no need to ask who was the artist. He could guess that. Nevertheless, he made a few perfunctory inquiries, and the lift boy supplied him with all the information he needed.

“Yes, sir, your secretary has been this morning, the pretty young lady. She came in soon after the offices were open. She was only here about an hour, and then she left.”

“Did she carry a bag?”

“Yes, sir. A little bag,” said the boy.

“Thank you,” said Derrick Yale, and went back to headquarters, to pour into the phlegmatic Mr. Parr’s ear a tale of rifled desk and stolen documents.

===Chinese===
他在椅子里坐了良久，两眼望着窗外。这番“手艺”出自谁的手笔，不问也知道。他猜得到。话虽如此，他还是例行公事地打听了几句，开电梯的小伙子便把他想知道的和盘托出。

“来过，先生，您的秘书今天早上来过，就是那位漂亮的小姐。各间办公室一开门没多久，她就到了。她只待了约莫一个钟头，然后就走了。”

“她手里提着提包吗？”

“提着，先生。一只小提包。”小伙计说。

“多谢。”德里克·耶尔说。随后他回到警察总部，把写字台被撬、文件失窃的经过，一五一十灌进了不动声色的帕尔先生的耳朵。

===Original===
“Now, I’m going to tell you, Parr, what I have told nobody else, not even the Commissioner,” said Yale. “We think of the Crimson Circle organisation as being run by a man. I happen to know that this girl has met the man who initiated her into the mysteries of the gang, whatever they are. But I also know that, so far from being the master, this mysterious gentleman in the motorcar, takes his orders, as everybody else does, from the real centre of the organisation﻿—which is Thalia Drummond!”

“Good Lord!” said the inspector.

“You wonder why I had her in my office? I told you it was because I thought she would bring us closer to the Circle. And I was right.”

“But why dismiss her?” asked the other quickly.

“Because she had done something which merited dismissal,” said Yale, “and if I had not fired her then and there, she would have known that I was keeping her in my office with an object. I might have saved myself the trouble, apparently,” he smiled, “because this morning’s work proves that she knew what my game was.” His thin, delicate face darkened, and then he said almost sharply: “When you have told your story tonight to the Prime Minister and his friends, I have a little story to tell which I think will surprise you.”

“Nothing you can say will ever surprise me,” said Mr. Parr.

===Chinese===
“帕尔，现在我要对你讲一件事，这件事我谁也没有告诉过，连总监也不例外。”耶尔说。“我们都以为红圈这个组织是由一个男人操纵的。我恰好知道，这姑娘见过那个引她入伙、领她见识这帮派种种内幕的男人——不管那些内幕究竟是什么。可我还知道，汽车里那位神秘的先生根本不是什么主子；他同旁人一样，也是奉命行事，而发号施令的，是这个组织真正的中枢——那就是塔莉娅·德拉蒙德！”

“我的老天！”探长说。

“你纳闷我为什么把她安插在我的办公室里？我告诉过你，那是因为我觉得她能领我们逼近红圈。而我没有想错。”

“那又为什么辞退她？”对方急忙问。

“因为她干了该当辞退的事。”耶尔说，“倘若我当时不当场开革她，她就会明白，我把她留在办公室里是别有所图。看来，这点工夫我原可省下的。”他微微一笑，“因为今天早晨这一手证明，我的把戏她早已识破。”他那张清瘦秀气的脸阴沉下来，随即近乎厉声地说：“今晚你把你的故事讲给首相和他的同僚们听罢之后，我也有个小故事要讲，我想，它准会让你吃一惊。”

“你说什么都吓不着我。”帕尔先生说。

===Original===
The third shock which Derrick Yale received that day came on his return home. The first half of his surprise was to find that his servant was out. The one woman he employed did not sleep on the premises, but she was supposed to remain in the flat until nine o’clock in the evening. It was exactly six when Derrick Yale came in to find the place in darkness.

He turned on the light and made a tour of the rooms. Apparently, the sitting-room was the only apartment which had been disturbed, but here, whoever the intruder had been and he could guess her name, she had been very thorough and painstaking. It was not necessary for him to seek out the servant and discover what had happened. She had been called away from the house by a message purporting to come from him﻿—he guessed that much. And whilst she was away Thalia Drummond had examined the contents of the flat at her leisure.

“A clever young woman!” said Derrick without malice, for he could admire even the genius which was employed against himself. She had lost no time. Within twelve hours she had broken gaol, ransacked both his office and his flat, and had removed documents which had a vital bearing upon the Crimson Circle.

He dressed himself leisurely, wondering what would be her next move. Of his own he was certain. Within twenty-four hours Inspector Parr would be a broken man. From a drawer in his dressing-room he took a revolver, looked at it for a moment speculatively, and slipped it into his hip pocket. There was going to be a startling and a sensational end to the chase of the Crimson Circle, an end wholly unforeseen by the spectators of the tragic game.

===Chinese===
德里克·耶尔这一天受到的第三次震动，是在回家的时候。这桩意外的头一半，是他发现仆人出去了。他雇的这惟一的女仆不在宅里过夜，但按规矩她该在公寓里一直待到晚上九点。德里克·耶尔进门时恰恰六点整，屋里却是一片漆黑。

他开了灯，把各房间巡视了一遍。看样子，起居室是惟一被人动过的屋子；而在这儿，不管那位不速之客是谁——她的名字他猜得到——她干得可是又彻底又细致。他犯不着去找女仆问个究竟。她是被一个冒他名义送去的口信支开的——这一点他也猜得到。趁她不在，塔莉娅·德拉蒙德从容不迫地把公寓里的东西翻检了一遍。

“真是个机灵的年轻女人！”德里克说这话并无恶意，因为即便是用来对付他自己的才干，他也能欣赏。她可是一刻也没耽搁。十二个钟头之内，她越了狱，把他的办公室和公寓都搜了个底朝天，还取走了那些与红圈干系重大的文件。

他不慌不忙地换着衣裳，心里揣摩她下一步棋会怎么走。至于他自己，那是拿定了主意的。不出二十四小时，帕尔探长就要身败名裂。他从更衣室的一个抽屉里取出一把左轮手枪，端详了片刻，便塞进了裤后袋。对红圈的追猎，就要迎来一个骇人听闻、轰动一时的结局——一个这出悲剧的看客们全然不曾料到的结局。

===Original===
In the wide lobby of the Prime Minister’s house he found a guest, the excuse for whose presence he could not fathom. Jack Beardmore had certainly been a sufferer from the activities of the Crimson Circle, but he had no part in the latter incidents.

“I suppose you are surprised to see me, Mr. Yale,” laughed Jack, as he took the other’s hand, “but you’re not more surprised than I am to be invited to a meeting of the Cabinet.”

He chuckled.

“Who invited you?﻿—Parr?”

“To be exact, the Prime Minister’s secretary. But I think Parr must have had something to do with the invitation. Don’t you feel scared in this company?”

“Not very,” smiled Derrick, slapping the other on the back.

===Chinese===
在首相宅邸宽敞的门厅里，他见到一位客人，此人到场凭什么名堂，他实在参不透。杰克·比德莫尔固然吃过红圈活动的苦头，但后来这几桩事里，他都不沾边。

“我猜你见到我准会奇怪，耶尔先生。”杰克握住对方的手，笑道，“不过要我列席内阁的会议，我自己比你更奇怪呢。”

他呵呵地笑了。

“谁请的你？——帕尔？”

“说得确切些，是首相的秘书。不过我想，这份请帖背后必有帕尔的一份。跟这帮人物坐在一处，你不觉得发怵吗？”

“不怎么怕。”德里克微笑着，拍了拍对方的背。

===Original===
A youthful private secretary bustled in and ushered them into the severe drawing-room, where a dozen gentlemen were talking in two groups.

The Prime Minister came forward to meet the detective.

“Inspector Parr has not arrived.” He looked questioningly at Jack. “I presume this is Mr. Beardmore?” he said. “The inspector particularly asked that you should be present. I suppose he has some light to throw upon poor James Beardmore’s death﻿—by the way, your father was a great friend of mine.”

The inspector came in at that moment. He wore a dress suit which had seen better days, a low collar with an awkwardly-tied bow, and he seemed an incongruous figure in that atmosphere of intellect and refinement. Following him came the grey-moustached Commissioner, who nodded curtly to his junior and led the Prime Minister aside.

===Chinese===
一位年轻的私人秘书疾步进来，引他们走进那间陈设庄重的客厅，十几位先生正分作两堆谈话。

首相迎上前来招呼这位侦探。

“帕尔探长还没有到。”他打量着杰克，眼里带着疑问。“我想，这位就是比德莫尔先生吧？”他说。“探长特别提出要您到场。我猜，他大概能对可怜的詹姆斯·比德莫尔之死作些说明——对了，令尊是我的一位至交。”

就在这时，探长进来了。他穿着一套昔日曾经体面过的晚礼服，低低的领子上打着个笨拙系成的蝴蝶结；在这满室才智与儒雅的氛围里，他显得格格不入。随后进来的是留着灰白胡子的总监，他朝自己的下级简慢地点了点头，便把首相引到一旁。

===Original===
The two were engaged in a whispered conversation for a little time, and then the colonel came across to where Yale was standing with Jack.

“Have you any idea what sort of a lecture Parr is going to give?” he said, a little impatiently. “I was quite under the impression that he was making a statement by invitation, but from what the Prime Minister tells me, it was Parr who suggested he should give the history of the Crimson Circle. I hope he isn’t going to make a fool of himself.”

“I don’t think he will, sir.” It was Jack’s quiet voice that had interrupted, and the Commissioner looked at him inquiringly until Yale introduced the young man.

“I agree with Mr. Beardmore,” said Derrick Yale. “I have not the slightest expectation of Mr. Parr making a fool of himself, in fact, I think he is going to fill up a number of gaps and bridge over seemingly irreconcilable circumstances, and I am ready to fill in a number of spaces which he may leave blank.”

===Chinese===
两人低声交谈了一阵，然后上校便走到耶尔和杰克站立的地方来。

“你可知道帕尔要讲的究竟是堂什么课？”他说，语气里带着几分不耐。“我先前的印象是，他是应邀前来作一番陈述的；可照首相对我说的，倒是帕尔自己主动提出，要讲一讲红圈的始末。但愿他别当众出丑才好。”

“我想他不会的，先生。”插话的是杰克平静的嗓音。总监望着他，面露询问之色，直到耶尔把这位年轻人介绍了出来。

“我同意比德莫尔先生的看法。”德里克·耶尔说。“帕尔先生会不会当众出丑，我丝毫不为此担心。事实上，我想他会把好些空子填上，把那些看似无法调和的情节一一接榫；而他万一留下一些空白，我也随时准备补上。”

===Original===
The company seated itself, and the Prime Minister beckoned the inspector forward.

“If you don’t mind, sir, I’ll stay where I am,” he said. “I’m not an orator, and I should like to tell this yarn as if I were telling it to any one of you.”

He cleared his throat and began speaking. At first his words were hesitant and he paused again and again to find the right phrase, but as he warmed to his subject he spoke more quickly and lucidly.

===Chinese===
众人落了座，首相招手请探长到前面去。

“您别见怪，先生，我就坐在原处讲吧。”他说。“我可不是什么演说家，这段故事，我想就像讲给你们中间随便哪一位听那样，平平静静地讲出来。”

他清了清嗓子，开了腔。起初他的话音迟疑，为了找到合适的字眼，他一遍又一遍地停顿；可随着话头渐入正题，他说得越来越快，也越来越明晰。

===Original===
“The Crimson Circle,” he began, “is a man named Lightman, a criminal who committed several murders in France, was condemned to death, but was saved by an accident from execution. His full name is Ferdinand Walter Lightman, and on the date of his attempted execution his age was twenty-three years and four months. He was transported to Cayenne, and escaped from that settlement after murdering a warder, and it is believed got away to Australia. A man answering his description, but giving another name, was working for a storekeeper in Melbourne for eighteen months, and was afterwards in the employment of a squatter named Macdonald for two years and five months. He left Australia in a hurry, a warrant having been issued against him by the local police for attempting to blackmail his employer.

===Chinese===
“红圈，”他开口道，“是一个姓利特曼的人，一个在法国犯下过数起命案的罪犯，被判了死刑，却因一场意外从处决中捡回性命。他的全名叫费迪南德·沃尔特·利特曼（Ferdinand Walter Lightman），行刑未成的那一天，他的年纪是二十三岁零四个月。他被流放到卡宴，杀死了一名看守后从那处流放地脱逃，据信辗转逃到了澳大利亚。有一个相貌与他吻合、却报了另一个姓名的人，在墨尔本替一家店铺老板做了十八个月的工，后来又受雇于一个名叫麦克唐纳（Macdonald）的牧场主，做了两年零五个月。再后来他仓皇离开了澳大利亚——当地警方因他企图敲诈雇主，已经对他发出了逮捕证。”

===Original===
“What happened to him subsequently we have not been able to trace until there appeared in England an unknown and mysterious blackmailer who signed himself the Crimson Circle, and who, by careful organisation and a display of remarkable patience and energy, gathered around him a large number of assistants, all of whom were unknown to one another. His modus operandi” (the inspector stumbled at the phrase) “was to find out somebody in a responsible position, who was either in need of money or in fear of prosecution for some offence which he or she had committed. He made the most careful inquiries before he approached his recruit, who was finally interviewed in a closed car driven by the Crimson Circle himself. Usually the rendezvous was one of the London squares which had the advantage of having four or five exits and a further advantage of being poorly lighted. You gentlemen are probably aware that the residential squares of London are the worst illuminated streets in the metropolis.

===Chinese===
“此后他的行踪，我们一直无从查考，直到英国冒出了一个无人知晓、行踪诡秘的勒索者，他署名‘红圈’。此人凭着缜密的组织和展现出的非凡耐心与精力，在自己身边聚拢了大批帮手，而这些帮手彼此素不相识。他的作案手法（modus operandi）”——探长念到这个字眼时还打了个磕绊——“就是先物色一个身居要职的人，这人要么缺钱，要么因自己犯下的某桩罪过而惧怕吃官司。在着手接近每个新收的人之前，他总要先做最周密的查访；到最后，那人会被领进一辆密闭的汽车里面谈，亲自驾车的，正是红圈本人。约见的地点通常选在伦敦的某座广场——这些广场的好处是有四五个路口可通，更进一步的好处是灯光昏暗。诸位先生想必都知道，伦敦的住宅广场，是这京城里照明最差的街道。”

===Original===
“Another class of recruit the Crimson Circle was very eager to secure was the convicted criminal. In this way he dragged in Sibly, an ex-sailor of a particularly low intelligence, who was already suspected of having committed murder, and who was the very man for the Crimson Circle’s purpose. In this way he secured Thalia Drummond﻿—” he paused﻿—“a thief, and an associate of thieves. In this way, too, he found the black man who murdered the railway director. For his own purpose he put in Brabazon the banker, and would have taken Felix Marl only, unfortunately for Marl, they had been associated together in the very crime for which Lightman nearly lost his life. More unfortunate still, Marl recognised Lightman when he met him in England, and this is the reason why Marl was eventually destroyed, the murderer employing perhaps the most ingenious method that has ever been used by a homicidal criminal.

===Chinese===
“红圈急欲罗致的另一类人马，是判过刑的罪犯。他就是这样把西布利拽下水的——一个智力格外低下的前水手，早已背着行凶杀人的嫌疑，正是红圈用得着的人。他也是这样网罗到了塔莉娅·德拉蒙德——”他顿了顿——“一个贼，一个与贼为伍的人。他同样循着这个路子，找到了那个谋害铁路董事的黑人。为了他自己的目的，他把银行家布拉巴宗拉了进来；本来也会把菲利克斯·马尔收归己用，只可惜——对马尔而言实在不幸——他们两个曾在同一桩罪行里搭过伙，而利特曼正是为了那桩案子差点送了命。更倒霉的是，马尔在英国遇见利特曼时，把他认了出来。这就是马尔终遭毁灭的缘由——行凶者所用的手法，也许是杀人凶犯有史以来所用过的最巧妙的一种。”

===Original===
“You can well understand, gentlemen,” he went on. They were following the little man with strained interest. “The Crimson Circle﻿—”

“Why did he call himself Crimson Circle?” It was Derrick Yale who asked the question, and for a little while the inspector was silent.

“He called himself Crimson Circle,” he said slowly, “because it was a name he had amongst his fellow convicts. About his neck was a red birthmark﻿—and I’ll blow the top of your head off if you move!”

===Chinese===
“诸位不难明白，”他接着往下讲。满座的人都屏息凝神，紧随着这个矮小的人。“红圈——”

“他为什么自称红圈呢？”发问的是德里克·耶尔。探长沉默了片刻。

“他自称红圈，”他缓缓地说，“因为这名字是他在苦役犯伙伴中间得来的。他的脖子上有一圈红色的胎记——你要是敢动一动，我就轰掉你的天灵盖！”

===Original===
The heavy calibre Webley he held in his hand covered Derrick Yale.

“Put your hands right up!” said the inspector, and then suddenly he reached out his hand and tore away the high white collar which covered Yale’s neck.

There was a gasp. Red, bloodred, as though it were painted by human agency, a circle of crimson ran about the throat of Derrick Yale.


===Chinese===
他手中那把大口径韦伯利左轮手枪，稳稳对准了德里克·耶尔。

“把双手高高举起！”探长说。接着他突然伸出手，一把扯下了掩着耶尔脖颈的那高高的白领。

满座倒抽了一口冷气。红的，血红血红的，仿佛是人工描画上去的一般，一圈猩红环绕在德里克·耶尔的喉咙上。

## XLII / 四十二

===Original===

Mother

===Chinese===

‘妈妈’

===Original===

In the room three men had mysteriously appeared﻿—the three who had captured Parr’s spy two nights before﻿—and in a second Yale was manacled hand and foot. A deft hand jerked the pistol that he carried from his pocket, a third man dropped a cloth bag over his head and face, and he was hurried from the room.

Inspector Parr wiped the perspiration from his streaming forehead, and faced his amazed audience.

“Gentlemen,” he said a little shakily, “if you will excuse me for tonight I will tell you the whole of this story tomorrow.”

They surrounded him, plying him with questions, but he could only shake his head.

“He’s had a very bad time,” it was the colonel’s voice, “and nobody knows it better than I. I should be very glad, Prime Minister, if you could accede to the inspector’s request, and allow the further explanation to stand over until tomorrow.”

===Chinese===

屋子里悄没声息地冒出了三条大汉——正是两夜之前擒获帕尔那名密探的三个——眨眼之间，耶尔便手脚皆被铐住。一只麻利的手把他随身携带的手枪从衣袋里猛地抽走，第三个人将一只布袋往他头脸上一罩，他便被人匆匆架出了房间。

帕尔探长抹去额上涔涔而下的汗水，面对满座惊愕的听众。

“诸位，”他说话时微微发颤，“今晚且请恕我失陪，明天我自会把这桩事的全部原委讲给你们听。”

众人把他团团围住，连声追问，他却只能摇头。

“他这一向的日子实在难熬，”说话的是上校，“再没有谁比我知道得更清楚了。首相，倘若您肯俯允探长所请，把进一步的解释展缓到明天，我将不胜感激。”

===Original===

“Perhaps the inspector will lunch with us,” said the Premier, and his Commissioner accepted on Parr’s behalf.

Gripping Jack’s arm Parr marched from the room and into the street. A taxicab was awaiting him and he bundled the young man in.

“I feel that I’ve been dreaming,” said Jack when he had found his voice. “Derrick Yale! Impossible! And yet﻿—”

“Oh, it is possible all right,” said the inspector with a little laugh.

“Then he and Thalia Drummond were working together?”

===Chinese===

“或者，请探长明天来与我们共进午餐。”首相说。他的总监当即代帕尔应承了下来。

帕尔攥住杰克的胳膊，大步走出房间，来到街上。一辆出租汽车正候在那里，他把这年轻人一把塞进了车厢。

“我觉得自己像是做了一场梦。”杰克好不容易才找回自己的声音，说道。“德里克·耶尔！不可能！可是——”

“哦，这可是不折不扣的可能。”探长轻笑着说。

“那么说，他和塔莉娅·德拉蒙德是联手做事的？”

===Original===

“Exactly,” was the reply.

“But, inspector, how did you get on to this story?”

“Mother put me on to it,” was the unexpected answer. “You don’t realise what a clever old lady mother is. She told me tonight﻿—”

“Then she’s come back?”

“Yes, she’s come back,” said the inspector. “I want you to meet her. She’s a bit dogmatic, and she is inclined to argue, but I always let her have her way in that respect.”

===Chinese===

“一点儿不错。”对方答道。

“可是探长，您是怎么摸到这桩事的底细的？”

“是‘妈妈’点醒了我。”这回答出人意料。“你可不晓得，‘妈妈’这位老太太有多精明。她今晚告诉我——”

“那么她回来了？”

“是的，她回来了。”探长说。“我要你去见见她。她有点儿固执己见，又爱同人争辩，不过在这一点上，我一向由着她。”

===Original===

“And you may be sure I shall, too,” laughed Jack, though he did not feel like laughing. “You really believe that the Crimson Circle is in your hands?”

“I am sure of it,” said the inspector. “As sure as I’m sitting in this taxicab with you, and as sure as I am that grandmother is the wisest old lady in the world.”

Jack maintained a silence until they were turning into the avenue.

“Then this means that Thalia is dragged a little lower?” he said quietly. “If this man Yale is, as you believe, the Crimson Circle, he will not spare her.”

===Chinese===

“您放心，我也一定事事由着她。”杰克笑着说，尽管他心里实在笑不起来。“您当真相信，红圈已经落在您的手心里了？”

“我拿得准。”探长说，“就像我拿得准自己此刻正同你坐在这辆出租汽车里一样，也像拿得准祖母是天底下最贤明的老太太一样。”

杰克一路默然不语，直到汽车拐进那条林荫大道。

“这就是说，塔莉娅又要被往泥潭深处拖一层了？”他轻声说。“倘若这个耶尔真如您所信，就是红圈，他是不会放过她的。”

===Original===

“I’m certain of that,” said the inspector; “but, lord bless you, Mr. Beardmore, why trouble your head about Thalia Drummond?”

“Because I love her, you damned fool!” said Jack savagely, and instantly apologised.

“I know I’m a bit of a fool,” the inspector spoke, between gusts of laughter, “but I’m not the only one in London, Mr. Beardmore, believe me. And if you’ll take my advice you’ll forget that Thalia Drummond ever existed. And if you’ve got any love to spare, why, give it to mother!”

Jack was about to say something uncomplimentary about this paragon of a grandmother, but suppressed his desire.

===Chinese===

“这一点我也拿得准。”探长说，“不过，老天爷，比德莫尔先生，你又何必为塔莉娅·德拉蒙德这样伤神呢？”

“因为我爱她呀，你这个该死的傻瓜！”杰克恶狠狠地说，随即又立刻赔了不是。

“我知道我是有几分傻气。”探长在一阵接一阵的大笑中说道，“可是请相信我，比德莫尔先生，伦敦城里犯傻的可不止我一个。你若肯听我的劝，就把塔莉娅·德拉蒙德这个人忘个一干二净。你那份情要是没处安放，嗐，就献给‘妈妈’吧！”

杰克正想对这位十全十美的祖母挖苦几句，话到嘴边，又把这点念头压了下去。

===Original===

The inspector’s maisonette was on the first floor, and he went up the stairs ahead, opened the door and stood for a moment in the doorway.

“Hello, mother,” he said. “I’ve brought Mr. Jack Beardmore to see you.”

Jack heard an exclamation.

“Come in, Mr. Beardmore, come in and meet mother.”

===Chinese===

帕尔探长那套独门小公寓在二楼，他抢先上了楼，打开房门，在门口站了片刻。

“喂，‘妈妈’。”他说，“我带杰克·比德莫尔先生来看您了。”

杰克听得屋里一声惊呼。

“请进，比德莫尔先生，请进来见见‘妈妈’。”

===Original===

Jack stepped into the room and stood as if he had been shot. Facing him was a smiling girl, a little pale and a little tired looking, but undoubtedly, unless he were mad or dreaming, Thalia Drummond!

She took his outstretched hand in hers and led him to the table, where a meal for three was laid.

“Daddy, you told me you were going to bring the Commissioner,” she said reproachfully.

===Chinese===

杰克跨进房间，呆立当场，像中了枪似的。迎面站着一位含笑的姑娘，脸色略微苍白，神情略微倦怠，可是不容置疑——除非他疯了，或者正在做梦——这正是塔莉娅·德拉蒙德！

她握住他伸出的手，把他领到桌前，桌上摆好了一桌三人份的饭菜。

“爸爸，您原说好要带总监来的。”她嗔怪道。

===Original===

“Daddy?” stammered Jack. “But you told me she was your grandmother.”

She patted his hand.

“Daddy has developed a sense of humour, which is very distressing,” she said. “I’m always called ‘mother’ at home, because I’ve mothered him ever since my own dear mother died. And that story about his grandmother is nonsense, but you must forgive him.”

“Your father?” said Jack.

===Chinese===

“爸爸？”杰克结结巴巴地说。“可是您明明跟我说过，她是您的祖母。”

她轻轻拍了拍他的手。

“爸爸近来添了一股幽默劲儿，实在叫人吃不消。”她说。“在家里，大家一向都喊我‘妈妈’，因为自从我亲爱的母亲去世，一直是我像母亲一样照管他。至于他那套祖母的说辞，全是无稽之谈，可您必须原谅他。”

“您的父亲？”杰克说。

===Original===

Thalia nodded.

“Thalia Drummond Parr, that is my name. Thank goodness, you aren’t a crime investigator, or you would have made inquiries and discovered my ghastly secret. Now eat your supper, Mr. Beardmore; I cooked it myself.”

But Jack could neither eat nor drink until he had learnt more, and she proceeded to enlighten him.

===Chinese===

塔莉娅点了点头。

“塔莉娅·德拉蒙德·帕尔——这才是我的名字。谢天谢地，您不是个罪案调查员，不然您只需稍加查访，就会发现我这个骇人的秘密了。好了，吃晚饭吧，比德莫尔先生；这是我自己下厨做的。”

可是杰克不把底细问个明白，便吃也吃不下、喝也喝不下，她便一五一十地讲给他听。

===Original===

“When the first of the Crimson Circle murders occurred and daddy was put into the case, I knew that he had a tremendous work in front of him and that the chances were he would fail. Daddy has a lot of enemies at headquarters, and our Commissioner asked him not to take the case, knowing how difficult it was going to be. You see, the Commissioner is my godfather,” she added smilingly, “and naturally he takes an interest in our affairs. But daddy insisted, though I think he regretted it the moment he had taken it on. I have always been interested in police work, and just as soon as father got behind the Crimson Circle organisation and knew the methods that the Circle employed to gather its recruits, I decided to start upon a career of crime.

===Chinese===

“红圈凶案的头一起发生、爸爸奉命接手的时候，我就知道摆在他面前的是一副千斤重担，而且十之八九他会败下阵来。爸爸在警察总部树敌不少，我们的总监深知这案子将何等棘手，曾劝他不要接手。您要晓得，总监是我的教父，”她含笑补了一句，“对我们家的事自然格外上心。可爸爸执意接了下来——不过我想，案子一到手他就懊悔了。我素来对警务工作怀有兴趣，等父亲钻进红圈组织内部、摸清了红圈网罗党羽的种种手腕，我便拿定主意，开始自己的犯罪生涯。”

===Original===

“Your father received the first threat three months before it was put into execution. It was two or three days afterwards that I secured a post as secretary to Harvey Froyant, for no other reason than that his estate adjoined yours. He was a friend of your father, and it gave me an opportunity of watching. I tried to get employment with your father. Perhaps you don’t know that,” she said quietly, “but I failed. Even more dreadful, I was in the wood when he was killed.” She squeezed his hand sympathetically. “I didn’t see who it was who fired the shot, but I flew forward to where your father was lying, only to discover that he was beyond help, and then, seeing you through the trees running across the meadows toward the wood, I thought I had better get away. The more so,” she added, “since I had a revolver in my hand at the time, for I had seen a man stalking in the wood and I had gone in to investigate.

===Chinese===
